# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 12 (Kannada → English translation)

## Sūkta 158 (Maṇḍala 1)

*The hundred and fifty-eighth sūkta. The supplied text begins at printed p. 2, in the middle of the Anuvāda of the introduction to this sūkta; printed p. 1 (title page, invocation and the beginning of the introduction, including Sāyaṇa's Sanskrit introduction) is not in the supplied text and is not translated.*

### Printed p. 2

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158, p. 2.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada), continued from the preceding page.** *[The page begins in mid-sentence: "…there are (ṛks)."]* The seer of this sūkta is Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya (printed "ಉಚಧ್ಯ", as supplied). As in the preceding sūkta, the deity of this sūkta too is the Aśvins. The metre is *triṣṭubh*; the last ṛk, that beginning दीर्घतमा मामतेय (*dīrghatamā māmateya*), is in *anuṣṭubh* metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: वसू अंत्यानुष्टुप् (*vasū aṃtyānuṣṭup*) [as supplied]. The special application (*viniyoga*) of this sūkta is *laiṅgika* (by indication).

**Sūkta—158**

*[Om sign ("॥ ಓಂ ॥") printed above the sūkta number, left out here and in the Kannada-script file as a sign only.]*

Maṇḍala 1 ‖ Anuvāka 22 ‖ Sūkta 158 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 3 ‖ Varga 1 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 6 ‖ *[a stray quotation mark precedes this line in the supplied text]*
Ṛṣi: Dīrghatamas Aucathya · Devatā: Aśvins · Chandas: Ṛks 1–4 *triṣṭubh*; Ṛk 6 *anuṣṭubh* *[as printed: Ṛk 5 is not mentioned in the Chandas line]*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वसू॑ रु॒द्रा पु॑रु॒मंतू॑ वृ॒धंता॑ दश॒स्यतं॑ नो वृषणाव॒भिष्टौ॑ ।
> दस्रा॑ ह॒ यद्रेक्ष॑ औच॒थ्यो वां॒ प्र यत्स॒साथे॒ अक॑वाभिरू॒ती। ॥ १ ॥

*[Printed line break inside the word ವೃಷಣಾವಭಿಷ್ಟೌ in the Saṃhitā block is joined in kn.md. The Saṃhitā text is kept as supplied; several words look misread (e.g. ರೇಕ್ಷ, ಪ್ರ ಯತ್ಸಸಾಥೇ) and are not corrected.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪವಪಾರಃ", for Pada-pāṭha); the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

> वसू इति । रुद्रा । पुरुमंतू इति पुरुमंतू । वृधंता । दशस्यतं । नः । वृषण् ।
> अभिष्टौ ।
> दस्रा । ह । यत् । रेक्णः । औचथ्यः । वां । प्र । यत् । सस्राथे इति । अकवाभिः ।
> ऊती ॥ १ ॥

> *vasū iti | rudrā | purumaṃtū iti purumaṃtū | vṛdhaṃtā | daśasyataṃ | naḥ | vṛṣaṇ |*
> *abhiṣṭau |*
> *dasrā | ha | yat | rekṇaḥ | aucathyaḥ | vāṃ | pra | yat | sasrāthe iti | akavābhiḥ |*
> *ūtī ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 3

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 3 (as supplied).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಾಯುಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sits inside the block; moved out and the standard label inserted.]*

> हे वृषण् कामानां वर्षितार् दस्रास्तद्विरोध्युपक्षपयितार् हे अश्विनौ
> युवं नोऽस्मभ्यं दशस्यतं । दत्तं दित्सितं फलं । कीदृशौ युवां । वसू वासयितारौ प्रजानां । वसुना धनेन तद्वंता वा ॥ मत्त्वर्थो लुष्यते ॥ रुद्रा रुद्रौ ।
> रुद्दुःखं तद्धेतुभूतं पापं ना । तस्य द्रावयितार् । संग्रामे भयंकरं शब्दयंता वा । रुद्रो रौतीति सतः । नि. १०. ५. । इति यास्कः । पुरुमंतू बहूनां
> ज्ञातार् ॥ मनेरौणादिकस्तुन्प्रत्ययः ॥ वृधंता वर्धमानौ स्तोत्रादिना अभिष्टावाभिमुख्येन पूजितौ । अभीष्टसाधकौ वा । हशब्द उक्तगुणप्रसिद्धिद्योतनार्थः । किमर्थं
> दीयत इति चेत् । यद्यस्मादौचथ्य उचथ्यस्य पुत्रो दीर्घतमा नां युवां
> रेक्नो धनं स्तुतिव्याजेन प्रार्थयते तदर्थं । यद्वा । अयं यजमानो यद्यस्माद्रॆक्ष्णो हविर्लक्षणमन्नं नां युवाभ्यां दास्यति ददातीति शेषः । यस्माच्चाकवाभिरकुत्सितैरूत्यूतिभी रक्षणैः ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति तृतीयायाः पूर्वसवर्णदीर्घः ॥
> प्र सस्राथे प्रसारयथः प्रकर्षेण दत्थः तस्माद्वशस्यतं । यस्मादभिमतप्रदौ यस्माच्छायं प्रार्थयते ददाति ना हविः तस्माद्वशस्यतमित्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* Aśvins, showerers of desires, grant us the fruit we seek; for Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya, asks you for wealth in praise, and you spread your protection widely.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

वृषण् (*vṛṣaṇ*)—showerers of desired objects; वसू (*vasū*)—(of the people) givers of dwelling-places, or possessors of wealth; रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—those who drive away (destroy) sorrow and sin, or those who make a fearful noise (in battle); पुरुमंतू (*purumaṃtū*)—knowers of many things; वृधंता (*vṛdhaṃtā*)—those who increase (through praise and the like); अभिष्टौ (*abhiṣṭau*)—those who satisfy by giving desired objects, or those who are worshipped face to face; दस्रा (*dasrā*)—those who perform deeds of valour such as destroying (enemies): O Aśvin gods! यत् (*yat*)—for which reason; औचथ्यः (*aucathyaḥ*)—Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya; वां (*vāṃ*)—from you; रेक्ष्णः (*rekṣṇaḥ*)—(with praise) asks wealth) or [(this sacrificer); यत् (*yat*)—for which reason; रेक्ष्णः (*rekṣṇaḥ*)—the food in the form of oblation; वां (*vāṃ*)—offers to you)] *[the brackets and parentheses are unbalanced in the supplied text and are kept as supplied]* (and for whatever reason you) अकवाभिः (*akavābhiḥ*)—by unobjectionable, that is, excellent; ऊती (*ūtī*)—protections; प्र सस्राथे (*pra sasrāthe*)—spread (your favour) extensively and liberally (for that reason); नः (*naḥ*)—to us; दशस्यतं (*daśasyataṃ*)—give all that is desired.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin gods, showerers of our desired objects, givers of dwelling-places, destroyers of sorrow and sin, knowers of many things, those who increase through praise and the like, those who satisfy by giving desired objects, and performers of deeds of valour such as destroying enemies! Because I, Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya (printed "ಉಚಧ್ಯ", as supplied), praise you and desire wealth from you, and because you too spread your favour liberally with your excellent protections, graciously grant us everything that is desired. *[The Bhāvārtha is broken across the page boundary inside the word ಇಷ್ಟಾರ್ಥ|ಗಳನ್ನು; read together here.]*

### Printed p. 4

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158, p. 4.*

**Printed English translation**

*[Printed heading "English Tranlation" [sic].]*

Dasras, showerers (of benefits), granters of dwellings, dispellers of sin, cognizant of many (things) increasing (through praise), and fulfillers (of desires), inasmuch as Anchattha offers you (sacrifical [sic]) wealth, and you protect (your worshippers) with unobjectionable aids, bestow upon us (our prayers).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

तुष्टाव चैव सूक्ताभ्यां अबोधीत्यश्विनावृषिः । (*tuṣṭāva caiva sūktābhyāṃ abodhītyaśvināvṛṣiḥ |*) (Bṛ. Ve. 4-26) *[reference as supplied; the abbreviation "ಬೃ. ವೇ." is not expanded here]*

Dīrghatamas, helped by the gods, composed the sūkta beginning with the ṛk अबोध्यग्निर्ज (*abodhyagnirja*) *[as supplied]* (1-157) and the present sūkta (1-158), and praised the Aśvin gods: so runs the sentence of the Bṛhaddevatā. In the Bṛhaddevatā the hardship that first befell Dīrghatamas is described, and how the gods removed it; then it is told that the seer praised Agni and the other gods. The birth of Dīrghatamas from Mamatā, wife of Ucathya, the outrage that befell Mamatā during her pregnancy, and the trouble that thereby came to Dīrghatamas, were told earlier, in another context, incidentally and briefly. Even so, here, in the context in which the seer's own work and its distinctive character are directly set forth, since he himself says औचथ्यः (*aucathyaḥ*) ("son of Ucathya"), the story of his birth as Ucathya's son is told as stated in the Bṛhaddevatā *[the Kannada has "ಉಚಥ್ಯಭಾರೈಯಾದ ಮಮತೆಯಲ್ಲಿ", "Mamatā, wife (printed ಭಾರೈ) of Ucathya", as supplied]*:

द्वावुचथ्यबृहस्पती ऋषिपुत्रौ बभूवतुः । (*dvāvucathyabṛhaspatī ṛṣiputrau babhūvatuḥ |*)
आसीदुचथ्यभार्या तु ममता नाम भार्गवी ॥ (*āsīducathyabhāryā tu mamatā nāma bhārgavī ‖*)
तां कनीयान्बृहस्पतिर्मैथुनायोपचक्रमे । (*tāṃ kanīyānbṛhaspatirmaithunāyopacakrame |*)
शुक्रस्योत्सर्गकाले तु गर्भस्तं प्रत्यभाषत ॥ (*śukrasyotsargakāle tu garbhastaṃ pratyabhāṣata ‖*)
इहास्मि पूर्वसंभूतो न कार्यः शुक्रसंकरः । (*ihāsmi pūrvasaṃbhūto na kāryaḥ śukrasaṃkaraḥ |*)
तच्चुक्तप्रतिषेधं तु न ममर्ष बृहस्पतिः ॥ (*taccuktapratiṣedhaṃ tu na mamarṣa bṛhaspatiḥ ‖*)
स व्याजहार त गर्भं तमस्ते दीर्घतमस्त्विति । (*sa vyājahāra ta garbhaṃ tamaste dīrghatamastviti |*)
स च दीर्घतमा नाम बभूवर्षिरुचथ्यजः ॥ (*sa ca dīrghatamā nāma babhūvarṣirucathyajaḥ ‖*)

*Sense (from the Sanskrit; the supplied text gives no Kannada rendering of these lines):* Ucathya and Bṛhaspati were two sons of a seer. Ucathya's wife was Mamatā, of the Bhārgava line. The younger one, Bṛhaspati, approached her for union. At the time of the emission of seed the child in the womb spoke to him: "I am here, first come; the mixing of seed must not be done." Bṛhaspati did not tolerate that prohibition of the seed, and spoke to the child in the womb with words containing "tamas … dīrghatamas" (the line reads "ಸ ವ್ಯಾಜಹಾರ ತ ಗರ್ಭಂ", with ತ as supplied, apparently a misreading). And he became the seer named Dīrghatamas, born of Ucathya. *[Text ends at the bottom of printed p. 4 with this verse; the passage may continue on p. 5.]*

### Printed p. 5

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 1.) Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 5 (the closing bracket is printed as a parenthesis, as supplied).* (The Bṛhaddevatā passage of the Special Topics, begun on p. 4, continues.)

स जातोऽभ्यतपद्देवानकस्मादंधतां गतः । (*sa jāto 'bhyatapaddevānakasmādaṃdhatāṃ gataḥ |*)
धदुर्देनास्तु तन्नेत्रे ततोऽनन्नो बभूव सः ॥ (*dhadurdenāstu tannetre tato 'nanno babhūva saḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. Pe. 4-11 to 15) *[reference as supplied; "ಪೇ" as printed, the earlier reference on p. 4 reads "ಬೃ. ವೇ."]*

There were two brothers, Ucathya and Bṛhaspati. Ucathya had a wife named Mamatā. While she was pregnant, Bṛhaspati, the younger brother of Ucathya, had intercourse with her. At the time of the emission of seed, the child within prayed that the emission of Bṛhaspati's seed should be held back. Angered by this, Bṛhaspati cursed the child in the womb that it should be born blind from birth. Because the child was born with a long darkness, it was named Dīrghatamas. When the seer's son, filled with darkness, performed austerity and won over the gods, they showed favour and made him gain sight. At once he became the seer of some sūktas. Thus (the passage) tells of the composition of the sūktas addressed to Agni and the other deities, and then continues the story that took place in the time of his blindness.

जीर्णं तु दीर्घतमसं खिन्नास्तत्परिचारिणः । (*jīrṇaṃ tu dīrghatamasaṃ khinnāstatparicāriṇaḥ |*)
दासा बद्ध्वा नदीतोये दृष्टिहीनमवादधुः ॥ (*dāsā baddhvā nadītoye dṛṣṭihīnamavādadhuḥ ‖*)
तत्रैकस्त्रैतनो नाम शस्त्रेणैनमपाहनत् । (*tatraikastraitano nāma śastreṇainamapāhanat |*)
शिरश्चांसावुरश्चैव स्वयमेव न्यकृंतत ॥ (*śiraścāṃsāvuraścaiva svayameva nyakṛṃtata ‖*)
हत्वा दीर्घतमास्तंतु पापेन महता वृतं । (*hatvā dīrghatamāstaṃtu pāpena mahatā vṛtaṃ |*)
आत्मांगान्यनुदच्चैव तत्रोदोन्मोहितो बृशं ॥ (*ātmāṃgānyanudaccaiva tatrodonmohito bṛśaṃ ‖*)
अंगदेशसमीपे तु तं नद्यः समुदक्षिपन् । (*aṃgadeśasamīpe tu taṃ nadyaḥ samudakṣipan |*)
अंगराजगृहे युक्तामुशिजं पुत्रकाम्यया ॥ (*aṃgarājagṛhe yuktāmuśijaṃ putrakāmyayā ‖*)
राज्ञा च प्रहितां दासीं भक्तां मत्वा महातपाः । (*rājñā ca prahitāṃ dāsīṃ bhaktāṃ matvā mahātapāḥ |*)
जनयामास चोत्थाय कक्षीवत्प्रमुखानृषीन् ॥ (*janayāmāsa cotthāya kakṣīvatpramukhānṛṣīn ‖*)
तुष्टाव चैव सूक्ताभ्यां अबोधीत्वश्विनावृषिः । (*tuṣṭāva caiva sūktābhyāṃ abodhītvaśvināvṛṣiḥ |*)

(Bṛhaddevatā 4-21 to 25)

Because Dīrghatamas remained blind for a very long time, his attendants, wearied of attending on him, bound him, worn out as he was, and threw him into the water of a river. Among them, one named Traitana, going to strike him with his sword, split his own head and arms himself. Thus that servant, who underwent the atonement for his own sin, Dīrghatamas killed outright, and, though bewildered by the force of the water, he tried to stretch out his hands and feet. Then the river current pushed him to the border of the neighbouring country of Aṅga and threw him on the bank. *[The sentence runs over the page break: "…pushed him … to the bank".]* The king of Aṅga sent a woman named Uśik, who was with him, to Dīrghatamas, desiring a son.

### Printed p. 6

*Running head: 6 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.*

Dīrghatamas, who had come out of the water, was pleased with her service and affection and caused sages such as Kakṣīvān to be born of her. Having told that story, (the Bṛhaddevatā) then tells that he composed sūktas addressed to the Aśvin deities and prayed to those gods in the present ṛks.

By the epithets वसू (*vasū*), रुद्रा (*rudrā*), पुरुमंतू (*purumaṃtū*), वृधंता (*vṛdhaṃtā*), वृषण् (*vṛṣaṇ*) and so on, the seer describes the nature of the Aśvin deities.

वसू (*vasū*)—वासयितार् प्रजानां । वसुना धनेन तद्वंतौ ना । (*vāsayitār prajānāṃ | vasunā dhanena tadvaṃtau nā |*) It is shown that this can be understood in two ways: as those who provide dwelling-places to the devotee people, or as those who possess wealth.

त्रिर्नो रयिं वहतमश्विना युवं त्रिर्देवताता त्रिरुतावतं धियः । (*trirno rayiṃ vahatamaśvinā yuvaṃ trirdevatātā trirutāvataṃ dhiyaḥ |*)
त्रिः सौभगत्वं त्रिरुत श्रवांसि नस्तिष्ठं वां सूरे दुहिता रुहद्रथं ॥ (*triḥ saubhagatvaṃ triruta śravāṃsi nastiṣṭhaṃ vāṃ sūre duhitā ruhadrathaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-34-5)

In such ṛks it is prayed that they should provide all wealth, food and the rest; and it is also suggested that they are the support of every kind of wealth. That they gave shelter to devotees such as Bhujyu, who were without refuge and had fallen into sorrow, and brought them to safe houses and the like, is described in many places (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-3 and others). Therefore whichever meaning is given to this epithet, it fits.

रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—रुत् दुःखं तद्भेतुभूतं पापं वा । तस्य द्रावयितारौ । संग्रामे (*rut duḥkhaṃ tadbhetubhūtaṃ pāpaṃ vā | tasya drāvayitārau | saṃgrāme*) भयंकरं शब्दयंत् वा । (*bhayaṃkaraṃ śabdayaṃt vā |*) *rut* means either sorrow or the sin that is its cause; (the word) is shown to be interpretable in one way as "those who destroy that sorrow or sin", or in another way as "those who make a fearful noise on the field of battle". And, quoting Yāska's sentence रुद्रो रौतीति सतः इति यास्कः । (*rudro rautīti sataḥ iti yāskaḥ |*) (*sic* ಸತಃ, as supplied; "ದಹರಿಸಿ" printed for "ಉದಹರಿಸಿ"), he has also given the derivation of this word. The author of the Nirukta, quoting the Kāṭhaka sentence and the Hāridravika sentence,

यदरुदत्तद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं (इति काठकं) । यवरोदीत्तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं (*yadarudattadrasya rudratvaṃ (iti kāṭhakaṃ) | yavarodīttadrudrasya rudratvaṃ*)
(इति हारिद्रविकं) ॥ (नि. १०-७) (*(iti hāridravikaṃ) ‖ (ni. 10-7)*)

(Ni. 10-7)

has shown that the name Rudra became famous from making sound in a roaring noise. That both qualities, the destroying of sorrow and the making of sound, are present in the Aśvin deities has been shown earlier with examples. *[The printed "ಅಶ್ವಿನೀವೇವತೆಗಳಲ್ಲಿ" is kept as supplied.]* Or,

### Printed p. 7

*Running head: [೨. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೧ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 7 (as supplied; "೨. ೨." printed for the Aṣṭaka).*

आ नो रत्नानि बिभ्रतावश्विना गच्छतं युवं । (*ā no ratnāni bibhratāvaśvinā gacchataṃ yuvaṃ |*)
रुद्रा हिरण्यवर्तनी जुषाणा वाजिनीवसू माध्वी मम श्रुतं हवं ॥ (*rudrā hiraṇyavartanī juṣāṇā vājinīvasū mādhvī mama śrutaṃ havaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-75-3)

उत नो रुद्रा चिन्मृळतामश्विना विश्वे देवासो रथस्पतिर्भगः । (*uta no rudrā cinmṛḷatāmaśvinā viśve devāso rathaspatirbhagaḥ |*)
ऋभुर्वाज ऋभुक्षणः परिज्मा विश्ववेदसः ॥ (*ṛbhurvāja ṛbhukṣaṇaḥ parijmā viśvavedasaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-93-7)

In such ṛks Sāyaṇa has interpreted the word रुद्रा (*rudrā*) as "the two sons of Rudra, the Aśvins" (रुद्रपुत्रौ अश्विन् (*rudraputrau aśvin*)). Along with it he has said that it may also mean स्तुत्या वा (*stutyā vā*), that is, "those worthy of praise". And since the word *rudra* is read among the names of praise (Ni. 3-19), he may have given the sense "worthy of praise" here. Therefore in the present ṛk there is room for all these meanings.

पुरुमंतू (*purumaṃtū*)—बहूनां ज्ञातारौ (*bahūnāṃ jñātārau*)—the meaning is: those with many kinds of knowledge. The wisdom of the Aśvin deities is highly praiseworthy. Therefore,

मेधां मे अश्विनावुभावाधत्तां । (*medhāṃ me aśvināvubhāvādhattāṃ |*)

(Trai. U. 4-16-5) *[abbreviation "ತ್ರೈ. ಉ." as supplied, not expanded]*

it is prayed: "May they bestow on me excellent wisdom." Also in the Ṛk-saṃhitā,

आ नूनं यातमश्विना रथेन सूरत्वचा । (*ā nūnaṃ yātamaśvinā rathena sūratvacā |*)
भुजी हिरण्यपेशसा कवी गंभीरचेतसा ॥ (*bhujī hiraṇyapeśasā kavī gaṃbhīracetasā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8 8-2) *[reference as supplied]*

in this ṛk the Aśvin deities are praised by the epithet गंभीरचेतसा (*gaṃbhīracetasā*). For this epithet Sāyaṇa has given the meaning प्रशस्तज्ञानौ (*praśastajñānau*), "having excellent knowledge".

रेक्षः (*rekṣaḥ*)—रेक्ष इति धननाम । रिच्यते प्रयतः । (*rekṣa iti dhananāma | ricyate prayataḥ |*) The authors of the Nirukta explain that wealth is called *rekṣaḥ* because, left behind by one who has died, it becomes a means of happiness to those who remain (Ni. 3-2). In the present ṛk Sāyaṇa says that this word may mean wealth or food in the form of oblation. If it is understood as wealth, स्तुतिव्याजेन प्रार्थयते (*stutivyājena prārthayate*), "he prays by means of praise"; and if it is understood as food in the form of oblation, हविर्लक्षणमन्नं (*havirlakṣaṇamannaṃ*) वां दास्यति ददातीति शेषः । (*vāṃ dāsyati dadātīti śeṣaḥ |*) — he explains that the purport is: "he will offer this food to you".

अकवाभिः (*akavābhiḥ*)—अकुत्सितैः (*akutsitaiḥ*) is explained as "not blameworthy, that is, not faulty", ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*) "by protections"; the meaning is "worthy of praise". The word कव (*kava*), formed from the root कु (*ku*), which gives the sense of "blameworthy", (means) small, despised, faulty, "thus the purport become…" *[the supplied text breaks off here, at the end of printed p. 7, in the middle of the word "ತಾತ್ಪರವಾಗು"; the sentence continues on p. 8, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 8

*Running head: 8 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.*

*[Continuation of the word broken at the end of p. 7: ತಾತ್ಪರವಾಗು|ತ್ತದೆ, "the purport becomes (so)".]* From this root कु (*ku*) both the forms कव (*kava*) and कवारि (*kavāri*) are formed. The word कवारि (*kavāri*) ordinarily means a man who is exceedingly selfish, miserly, and who lives a despicable life without performing sacrifice.

दैवी पूर्तिर्दक्षिणा देवयज्या न कवारिभ्यो नहि ते पृणंति । (*daivī pūrtirdakṣiṇā devayajyā na kavāribhyo nahi te pṛṇaṃti |*)
अथा नरः प्रयतदक्षिणासोऽवद्यभिया बहवः पृणंति ॥ (*athā naraḥ prayatadakṣiṇāso 'vadyabhiyā bahavaḥ pṛṇaṃti ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-107-3)

In this (ṛk) the word कवारिभ्यः (*kavāribhyaḥ*) has the meaning कुत्सितगंतृभ्यः (*kutsitagaṃtṛbhyaḥ*) अयष्टृभ्यः (*ayaṣṭṛbhyaḥ*), that is, selfish and miserly people who lead a despicable life without performing sacrifices and the like. This is one kind of usage of the word कवारि (*kavāri*).

मरुत्वंतं वृषभं वावृधानमकवारिं दिव्यं शासमिंद्रं । (*marutvaṃtaṃ vṛṣabhaṃ vāvṛdhānamakavāriṃ divyaṃ śāsamiṃdraṃ |*)
विश्वासाहमवसे नूतनायोग्रं सहोदामिह तं हुवेम ii (*viśvāsāhamavase nūtanāyograṃ sahodāmiha taṃ huvema ii*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-47-5) *[the end of the second line is printed "ii" for the double danda, as supplied]*

In this ṛk the word कव (*kava*) has the meaning "small, despised"; कवारि (*kavāri*) is a compound meaning "the enemy (*ari*) who is *kava*; a petty enemy". One who is not such a कवारि (*kavāri*) is आकवारि (*ākavāri*) *[so printed, with long ā]*, that is, an enemy with great power. Here this word is an epithet of Indra. The power of the demon Vṛtra is greater still; to subdue him the power of Indra, the enemy of Vṛtra, must be greater still, so this is an epithet showing Indra's valour. This is another kind of usage of the word कवार (*kavāra*) *[printed "ಕವಾರಯೆಂಬ ಪದ", as supplied]*.

In the present ṛk too the word कव (*kava*) has the meaning "small". What is not *kava* is *akava*. By such *akava* protections, that is, by exceedingly great protections, is the meaning indicated by the words अकवाभिः (*akavābhiḥ*) ऊती (*ūtī*).

दस्रा (*dasrā*)—अस्मद्विरोध्युपक्षपयितार् (*asmadvirodhyupakṣapayitār*) "those who harm and destroy our enemies": thus it describes the nature of the Aśvin deities. It was shown earlier that this word can have two meanings: destroyers of enemies, or those of beautiful form.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

वसू (*vasū*)—वसु अनयोः अस्ति इति वसू. (*vasu anayoḥ asti iti vasū.*) When the suffix *matup* comes, it is dropped in the Vedic language (*chāndasa*).

### Printed p. 9

*Running head: [ಅ ೨.ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 9 (as supplied).*

Sū. 2-179), सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39 and, at the end for ūtī, Pā. Sū. 3-1-39, as supplied), कंड्वादिभ्यो यक् (*kaṃḍvādibhyo yak*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-27), स्थायितंचिवंचि (*sthāyitaṃcivaṃci*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-170), आमंत्रितस्य (*āmaṃtritasya*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-198), तद्धितेष्टचामादेः (*taddhiteṣṭacāmādeḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-117), ऊतियूतिजूति (*ūtiyūtijūti*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-97) and, for औचथ्यः (*aucathyaḥ*), Pā. Sū. 4-1-92. The page ends with the whole-ṛk mark ॥ ೧ ॥ and a stray ornament line ":੦:", kept as supplied.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> को नां॑ दाशत्सुम॒तये॑ चिद॒स्यै वसू॒ यद्धेथे॒ नम॑सा॒
> प॒दे गोः ।
> जि॒गृ॒तम॒स्मे रे॒वतीः॒ पुरं॑धीः काम॒प्रेणे॑व॒ मन॑सा॒ चरं॑ता । ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಗದಗ ತಾ") and sits inside the Saṃhitā block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

> कः । वां । दाशत् । सुमतये । चित् । अस्यै । वसू इति । यत् । धेथे इति । नमसा ।
> पदे । गोः ।
> जिगृतं । अस्मे इति । रेवतीः । पुरंधीः । कामप्रेण इव । मनसा । चरंता ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sits inside the block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks inside the block are joined. The bhāṣya of this Ṛk is only transcribed; its gist is given with the Pratipadārtha, which follows on the next page.]*

> हे वसू वासयितारावश्विन् नां युवयोः संबंधिन्या अस्यॆ वक्ष्यमाणरूपायै सुमतये चित् शोभनबुद्धै ! चित् पूजायामप्यर्थे ना । स्वल्वेनैव
> हविषा महत्पलप्रदानरूपाया ईदृश्या अपि बुद्धै प्रीणनाय को दाशत् । को
> वद्यात् । दातुं शक्नुयात् । युवयोः प्रभावस्यातिमहत्वादिति भावः ॥ दाशतेर्लेट्यडागमः ॥ कथं मतेः सौष्टवमिति तदुच्यते । यद्यस्मात्कारणाद्योः पदे
> भूम्याः सर्वैर्गंतव्यायाः स्तुत्याया ना वेदिरूपायाः स्थाने । एतावती वै पृथिवी
> यावती वेदिरिति श्रुतेः । गौरिति भूनाम । तथा च निरुक्तं । गौरिति पृथिव्या नामधेयं यद्दूरं गता भवति यच्चास्यां भूतानि गच्छंति गातेर्वौकारो नामकरणः ।
> नि. २-५ । इति । तादृशे स्थानेऽस्माभिः प्रार्थितौ संतौ नमसा हविर्लक्षणेन यवमात्रप्रकारेणान्नेन धेथे धारयथो बहुप्रदानविषयां बुद्धिं !! दधातेर्लटि बहुलं
> छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् । धातोर्ह्रस्वत्वं । आतो जितः । पा. ७-२-८१ । इतीयादेशः ॥ यस्मादेवं तस्मादस्मे अस्मभ्यं रेवतीः क्षीरादिधनवतीः पुरंधीः शरीरधारिकाः शरीराभिवृद्धि हेतूर्गा जिगृतं । शब्दयतं । दत्तमित्यर्थः ॥ गृ शब्दे । भांदसः
> शपः श्लुः । अभ्यासस्यॆत्वं ह्रस्वत्वं च ii पुनस्तावेव विशेष्येते । कामप्रणेव । इवशब्द एवकारार्थः । कामपूरकेणैव मनसा सह चरंता चरंतौ । यजमानस्य कामाः
> पूरणीया इति कृतसंकल्पावित्यर्थः ॥ प्रा पूरणे । कामं प्रातीति कामप्रः । आतोनुपसर्गे कः ॥

> *he vasū vāsayitārāvaśvin nāṃ yuvayoḥ saṃbaṃdhinyā asyè vakṣyamāṇarūpāyai sumataye cit śobhanabuddhai ! cit pūjāyāmapyarthe nā | svalvenaiva*
> *haviṣā mahatpalapradānarūpāyā īdṛśyā api buddhai prīṇanāya ko dāśat | ko*
> *vadyāt | dātuṃ śaknuyāt | yuvayoḥ prabhāvasyātimahatvāditi bhāvaḥ ‖ dāśaterleṭyaḍāgamaḥ ‖ kathaṃ mateḥ sauṣṭavamiti taducyate | yadyasmātkāraṇādyoḥ pade*
> *bhūmyāḥ sarvairgaṃtavyāyāḥ stutyāyā nā vedirūpāyāḥ sthāne | etāvatī vai pṛthivī*
> *yāvatī vediriti śruteḥ | gauriti bhūnāma | tathā ca niruktaṃ | gauriti pṛthivyā nāmadheyaṃ yaddūraṃ gatā bhavati yaccāsyāṃ bhūtāni gacchaṃti gātervaukāro nāmakaraṇaḥ |*
> *ni. 2-5 | iti | tādṛśe sthāne 'smābhiḥ prārthitau saṃtau namasā havirlakṣaṇena yavamātraprakāreṇānnena dhethe dhārayatho bahupradānaviṣayāṃ buddhiṃ !! dadhāterlaṭi bahulaṃ*
> *chaṃdasīti vikaraṇasya luk | dhātorhrasvatvaṃ | āto jitaḥ | pā. 7-2-81 | itīyādeśaḥ ‖ yasmādevaṃ tasmādasme asmabhyaṃ revatīḥ kṣīrādidhanavatīḥ puraṃdhīḥ śarīradhārikāḥ śarīrābhivṛddhi hetūrgā jigṛtaṃ | śabdayataṃ | dattamityarthaḥ ‖ gṛ śabde | bhāṃdasaḥ*
> *śapaḥ śluḥ | abhyāsasyètvaṃ hrasvatvaṃ ca ii punastāveva viśeṣyete | kāmapraṇeva | ivaśabda evakārārthaḥ | kāmapūrakeṇaiva manasā saha caraṃtā caraṃtau | yajamānasya kāmāḥ*
> *pūraṇīyā iti kṛtasaṃkalpāvityarthaḥ ‖ prā pūraṇe | kāmaṃ prātīti kāmapraḥ | ātonupasarge kaḥ ‖*

### Printed p. 11

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 11 (printed "ಅ. ೨ .ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧. ]", as supplied).*

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 2 (the bhāṣya block closed on p. 10; this gist, my own short summary, is given here with the Pratipadārtha): O Aśvins, who could honour with oblation such goodwill as yours? Invoked at the altar, you grant us milk-rich, nourishing cows with willing minds.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

वसू (*vasū*)—O Aśvin deities, who provide dwelling-places. यत् (*yat*)—(because you wish to do good to us) for which reason. गोः पदे (*goḥ pade*)—in the place of the venerable altar. नमसा (*namasā*)—with food in the form of oblation (a meagre quantity) (in return for that). धेथे (*dhethe*)—(you hold a mind that gives to us abundantly) (and therefore). कामप्रेणेव (*kāmapreṇeva*)—with the (our) desires bound to be fulfilled. मनसा (*manasā*)—with a mind. चरंता (*caraṃtā*)—moving about. अस्मे (*asme*)—to us. रेवतीः (*revatīḥ*)—possessing wealth such as milk; पुरंधीः (*puraṃdhīḥ*)—and the cause of growth of the body: cows. जिगृतं (*jigṛtaṃ*)—you give (such). वां (*vāṃ*)—your. अस्यॆ सुमतये चित् (*asyè sumataye cit*)—to this excellent and gracious mind, (so abundantly as to satisfy it) *[the closing parenthesis has no opening one in the supplied text]*. कः (*kaḥ*)—which sacrificer indeed दाशत् (*dāśat*)—can offer?

*[Label garbled in the supplied text: "ಭಾಷಾರ್ಥ"; the standard Bhāvārtha label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, who provide dwelling-places! With the intention that you must do good to us, you stand at the place of the venerable altar and hold a gracious mind that gives us abundantly wealth and the like in return for the small oblation we offer. Therefore, moving with a mind set on fulfilling all our desires, you give us cows that possess milk and other wealth and are the cause of the growth of the body. Which sacrificer indeed can offer you oblation so abundantly as to satisfy such an excellent, gracious mind of yours? You must be satisfied with our small offering itself.

**Printed English translation**

Granters of dwellings, who may make you offerings so propitiatory of that your good will, that (invoked) by our adoration, to the site of the altar, you may entertain (favourable intentions towards us), and grant us cows rich in milk, coming with minds made up to grant the desires (of the worshipper).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

गोः पदे (*goḥ pade*)—भूम्याः सर्वैर्गंतव्यायाः स्तुत्याया वा वेदिरूपायाः (*bhūmyāḥ sarvairgaṃtavyāyāḥ stutyāyā vā vedirūpāyāḥ*) स्थाने । (*sthāne |*) This is explained as "on the earth in the form of the altar". One should first understand how the word गो (*go*) comes to have the name "earth", and then the identity (*tādātmya*) of the earth and the altar. The authors of the Nirukta have included the word गो (*go*) among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5) and have then given its derivation.

### Printed p. 12

*Running head: 12 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.*

गौरिति पृथिव्या नामधेयं । यद्दूरंगता भवति । यच्चास्यां भूतानि (*gauriti pṛthivyā nāmadheyaṃ | yaddūraṃgatā bhavati | yaccāsyāṃ bhūtāni*)
गच्छंति । गातेर्वौकारो नामकरणः । (*gacchaṃti | gātervaukāro nāmakaraṇaḥ |*)

(printed "ಸಿ-೨೫", i.e. Ni. 2-5 as in the sentence above; reference as supplied)

"Because it spreads out very widely (दूरंगता भवति (*dūraṃgatā bhavati*)); or because beings move about on it (भूतानि गच्छंति (*bhūtāni gacchaṃti*))": the word गौः (*gauḥ*) is a synonym of "earth". The authors of the Nirukta explain this synonym of the earth, saying that its derivation is possible also by adding the suffix औ (*au*) to the root गा (*gā*), and then say:

अधापि पशुनामेह भवत्येतस्मादेव ।: (*adhāpi paśunāmeha bhavatyetasmādeva |:*) *[the line ends in ".:" as supplied]*

Because it is formed from the root गम् (*gam*) or गा (*gā*) with the above sense, this name is well known also for the animal; and saying आदित्योऽपि गौरुच्यते, सर्वॆऽपि (*ādityo 'pi gaurucyate, sarvè 'pi*) रश्मयो गाव उच्यंते । (*raśmayo gāva ucyaṃte |*) they show that the word गो (*go*) is used also in the senses sun, ray and so on. In those contexts the meaning of the root and the derivation of गो (*go*) have been given, or will be given, in the respective contexts. In the present ṛk the meaning is "earth"; if this is extended further, the purport is "the earth in the form of the altar".

चंद्रमा एव प्रत्यक्षादादित्यो यजमानस्तस्येयमेव पृथिवी वेदिः । (*caṃdramā eva pratyakṣādādityo yajamānastasyeyameva pṛthivī vediḥ |*)

(Śa. Brā. 12-8-2-36)

In sentences like this the identity of the earth and the altar is stated. And when Śaṅkarācārya wrote the bhāṣya on the sentence होता वेदिषत् (*hotā vediṣat*) of the Karopaniṣad *[so printed]* (5-2), he wrote:

इयं वेदिः परोऽंतः पृथिव्याः । या यज्ञे प्रसिद्धा इयं वेदिः पृथिव्याः (*iyaṃ vediḥ paro 'ṃtaḥ pṛthivyāḥ | yā yajñe prasiddhā iyaṃ vediḥ pṛthivyāḥ*)
परोऽंतः परः स्वभाव इति वेद्याः पृथिवीस्वभावत्वकीर्तनात् पृथिवी वेदिशब्दवाच्या भवतीत्यर्थः । (*paro 'ṃtaḥ paraḥ svabhāva iti vedyāḥ pṛthivīsvabhāvatvakīrtanāt pṛthivī vediśabdavācyā bhavatītyarthaḥ |*) *[a hyphenated line break inside ವೇದಿ-ಶಬ್ದವಾಚ್ಯಾ is joined in kn.md]*

— thus giving a meaning that shows the identity of earth and altar. Moreover:

इयं वेदिः परो अंतः पृथिव्या अयं यज्ञो भुवनस्य नाभिः । (*iyaṃ vediḥ paro aṃtaḥ pṛthivyā ayaṃ yajño bhuvanasya nābhiḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-35)

there is a śruti sentence itself. In the present ṛk Sāyaṇa has explained it as "on the earth in the form of the altar, easily approached by all and worthy of praise".

नमसा (*namasā*)—हविर्लक्षणेन अन्नेन । (*havirlakṣaṇena annena |*) "By food in the form of oblation." The word नमः (*namaḥ*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of the thunderbolt (Ni. 3-11), and in the Ṛk-saṃhitā this word is used in both these senses, besides the established sense of salutation.

### Printed p. 13

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೧.] ಮುಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 13 (as supplied; "ಮುಗ್ವೇದ" for "ಋಗ್ವೇದ"). In the supplied text this head is merged into the last line of p. 12 and no page marker was supplied; the page break is inserted here.*

In some places there is also the meaning "praise, a means of salutation". In the present ṛk it means "by food in the form of oblation".

कामप्रेणेव (*kāmapreṇeva*)—इव शब्दः एवकारार्थः । कामपूरकेणैव मनसा (*iva śabdaḥ evakārārthaḥ | kāmapūrakeṇaiva manasā*) यजमानस्य (*yajamānasya*) कामाः पूरणीया इति कृतसंकल्पावित्यर्थः । (*kāmāḥ pūraṇīyā iti kṛtasaṃkalpāvityarthaḥ |*) This is explained as: having minds that have firmly resolved to obtain and give what the sacrificer's mind desires and so to satisfy him. Or it can also mean: having minds full of love, (thinking) that the desired object must be fulfilled. Because,

बृहस्पते प्रथमं वाचो अग्रं यत्पैरत नामधेयं वधानाः । (*bṛhaspate prathamaṃ vāco agraṃ yatpairata nāmadheyaṃ vadhānāḥ |*)
यदेषां श्रेष्ठं यदरिप्रमासीत्प्रेणा तदेषां निहितं गुहाविः॥ (*yadeṣāṃ śreṣṭhaṃ yadaripramāsītpreṇā tadeṣāṃ nihitaṃ guhāviḥ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 18-71-1)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 18-71-1) *[the reference is printed twice, with "೧೮" for the Maṇḍala number, as supplied]*

in this ṛk, for the word प्रेणा (*preṇā*) Sāyaṇa has explained मकारलोपश्छांदसः । प्रेम्नाविर्भवति (*makāralopaśchāṃdasaḥ | premnāvirbhavati*): in the word प्रेम्ना (*premnā*), being a Vedic word, the letter *m* is dropped; the meaning is "with love". Likewise in the present ṛk कामाभिपूरकप्रेम्ना इव । (*kāmābhipūrakapremnā iva |*) may also mean "with the pride (*abhimāna*) that the sacrificer's desired objects must be filled".

रेवतीः (*revatīḥ*)—क्षीरादिधनवतीः । (*kṣīrādidhanavatīḥ |*) The meaning is "possessing wealth such as milk". It is formed from the word रै (*rai*) read among the names of wealth (Ni 3-9, as printed). The word रेवति (*revati*) can mean either merely "wealthy" or "cattle that give milk and so on".

आ तू न इंदो शतदात्वश्वं सहस्रदातु पशुमद्धिरण्यवत् ॥ (*ā tū na iṃdo śatadātvaśvaṃ sahasradātu paśumaddhiraṇyavat ‖*)
उप मास्व बृहती रेवतीरिषोऽधि स्तोत्रस्य पवमान नो गहि ॥ (*upa māsva bṛhatī revatīriṣo 'dhi stotrasya pavamāna no gahi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-72-9)

Here for रेवतीः (*revatīḥ*) (Sāyaṇa gives) धनवंति यद्वा पयसो दातारः पशवः तद्वंति (*dhanavaṃti yadvā payaso dātāraḥ paśavaḥ tadvaṃti*), "wealthy or possessing animals that give milk": both meanings are given. It is an epithet of इषः (*iṣaḥ*) (food). The word is used in the same way in other places too.

पुरंधीः (*puraṃdhīḥ*)—शरीरधारिकाः शरीराभिवृद्धिहेतूः गाः । (*śarīradhārikāḥ śarīrābhivṛddhihetūḥ gāḥ |*) This is explained as "cows that are means of nourishing the body (are given)"; the purport lies in the nourishing quality of milk-wealth, "filled with milk". Although this word everywhere indicates "abundant, full, prosperous", since it is used in many contexts its meaning has to be explained according to each context, as is known from the sentence of the Nirukta author himself.

ते हि यज्ञेषु यज्ञियास ऊमाः सधस्थं विश्वे अभि संति देवाः । (*te hi yajñeṣu yajñiyāsa ūmāḥ sadhasthaṃ viśve abhi saṃti devāḥ |*)
ता अध्वर उशतो यक्ष्यग्ने श्रुष्टी भगं नासत्या पुरंधिं । (*tā adhvara uśato yakṣyagne śruṣṭī bhagaṃ nāsatyā puraṃdhiṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-39-4)

### Printed p. 14

*Running head: 14 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.*

*[In the supplied text the 7-39-4 reference is printed "ಮ. ಸಂ- ೭-೩೯-೪"; read Ṛ. Saṃ. by the rule of the user's reading of the abbreviation and changed in kn.md. The Kannada says "ಎಂಬ ಮಕ್ಕಿನಲ್ಲಿ" for "in that ṛk", as supplied.]* While explaining the meaning of the word पुरंधिं (*puraṃdhiṃ*) in that ṛk, (the Nirukta says):

पुरंधिर्बहुधीस्तत्कः पुरंधिर्भगः पुरस्तात्तस्यान्वादेश इत्येकं । इंद्र इत्यपरं (*puraṃdhirbahudhīstatkaḥ puraṃdhirbhagaḥ purastāttasyānvādeśa ityekaṃ | iṃdra ityaparaṃ*)
स बहुकर्मतः पुरां च दारयितृतमः । वरुण इत्यपरं तं प्रज्ञया स्तौति । (*sa bahukarmataḥ purāṃ ca dārayitṛtamaḥ | varuṇa ityaparaṃ taṃ prajñayā stauti |*)

(Ni. 6-13)

The word पुरंधि (*puraṃdhi*) means "one with abundant intelligence (*dhī*)". The question is: who is such a one? Since the word भग (*bhaga*) precedes it in the ṛk, some hold that it is an epithet of that word. Since the word धी (*dhī*) is read among the names of actions, पुरंधि (*puraṃdhi*) means either "one with many kinds of action" or "one who splits enemy cities", and so some hold that it is an epithet of Indra. Or, as in sentences such as इमामू नु कवितमस्य मायां (*imāmū nu kavitamasya māyāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-6) the excellent wisdom of Varuṇa is praised, in the present ṛk too (the 7-39-4 quoted by the Nirukta authors) it may be a synonym of Varuṇa, describing his wisdom, say still others: thus the Nirukta author states the differences of opinion. The Nirukta author shows with some more examples that this word is used also when the abundance of generosity shown by the gods towards sacrificers is shown, and when the abundance of oblations and the like offered by the sacrificers to the gods is indicated.

आदाय श्येनो अभरत्सोमं सहस्रं सना आयुतं च साकं । (*ādāya śyeno abharatsomaṃ sahasraṃ sanā āyutaṃ ca sākaṃ |*)
अत्रा पुरंधिरजहादरातीर्मदे सोमस्य मूरा अमूरः । (*atrā puraṃdhirajahādarātīrmade somasya mūrā amūraḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-26-2)

For the word in this ṛk, Durgācārya (Ni. 11-2) gives पुरंधिः बहु नः धनस्य दाता (*puraṃdhiḥ bahu naḥ dhanasya dātā*) *[so printed]*, that is, "one who gives much wealth", and Sāyaṇa gives बहुकर्म (*bahukarma*). It is an epithet of Indra. The purport is "generous".

नृधूतो अद्रिषुतो बर्हिषि प्रियः पतिर्गवां प्रदिव इंदुर्य्त्वियः । (*nṛdhūto adriṣuto barhiṣi priyaḥ patirgavāṃ pradiva iṃdurytviyaḥ |*)
पुरंधिवान्मनुषो यज्ञ साधनः शुचिर्धिया पवते सोम इंद्र ते ॥ (*puraṃdhivānmanuṣo yajña sādhanaḥ śucirdhiyā pavate soma iṃdra te ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-22-4)

In this ṛk बहुकर्मवान् मनुषः (*bahukarmavān manuṣaḥ*) is given the meaning "of the man who has many kinds of rites for the satisfaction of the gods".

पावीरवी तन्यतुरेकपादजो दिवो धर्ता सिंधुरासः समुद्रियः । (*pāvīravī tanyaturekapādajo divo dhartā siṃdhurāsaḥ samudriyaḥ |*)
विश्वे देवासः शृणवन् वचांसि मे सरस्वती सह धीभिः पुरंध्या ॥ (*viśve devāsaḥ śṛṇavan vacāṃsi me sarasvatī saha dhībhiḥ puraṃdhyā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-65-13)

### Printed p. 15

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 15 (printed "ಅ. ೨. ಅ ೩. ವ. ೧ ]", as supplied).* (Special Topics of Ṛk 2 continue.)

In that ṛk (the Sarasvatī verse quoted at the end of p. 14) the authors of the Nirukta (Ni. 12-30) explain this word as पुरंध्या स्तुत्या प्रयुक्तानि (*puraṃdhyā stutyā prayuktāni*), that is, वचांसि (*vacāṃsi*) "words accompanied by many kinds of praise". Sāyaṇa has explained बहुविधया (*bahuvidhayā*) प्रज्ञया सहिता सरस्वती । (*prajñayā sahitā sarasvatī |*) "Sarasvatī endowed with manifold wisdom", as an epithet of Sarasvatī. But from the explanation made by Durgācārya (Ni. 12-30), बहुधात्रा माध्यमिकया वाचा (*bahudhātrā mādhyamikayā vācā*), it is understood to be a particular deity similar to Sarasvatī. Likewise,

शं नो भगः शमु नः शंसो अस्तु शं नः पुरंधिः शमु संतु रायः । (*śaṃ no bhagaḥ śamu naḥ śaṃso astu śaṃ naḥ puraṃdhiḥ śamu saṃtu rāyaḥ |*)
शं नः सत्यस्य सुयमस्य शंसः शं नो अरमा पुरुजातो अस्तु । (*śaṃ naḥ satyasya suyamasya śaṃsaḥ śaṃ no aramā purujāto astu |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-35-2)

in such ṛks it is understood that Puraṃdhi is a separate deity; Sāyaṇa too has not made it an epithet of the words *bhaga* and so on in this ṛk. And

देवो भगः सविता रायो अंश इंद्रो वृत्रस्य संजितो धनानां । (*devo bhagaḥ savitā rāyo aṃśa iṃdro vṛtrasya saṃjito dhanānāṃ |*)
ऋभुक्षा वाज उत वा पुरंधिरवंतु नो अमृतासस्तुरासः ॥ (*ṛbhukṣā vāja uta vā puraṃdhiravaṃtu no amṛtāsasturāsaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-42-5)

here it is a synonym of Vibhvan.

पुराणी देवि युवतिः पुरंधिरनु व्रतं चरसि विश्ववारे । (*purāṇī devi yuvatiḥ puraṃdhiranu vrataṃ carasi viśvavāre |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-61-1)

In such places it is an epithet of Uṣas; the meaning is "having many hymns of praise". In many places this word is used for the wife or for women in general, and gives the meaning of women who are not barren, who are fit to obtain the wealth of progeny and are nourished.

अग्निः सप्तिं वाजंभरं ददात्यग्निर्वीरं श्रुत्यं कर्मनिष्ठां । (*agniḥ saptiṃ vājaṃbharaṃ dadātyagnirvīraṃ śrutyaṃ karmaniṣṭhāṃ |*)
अग्नी रोदसी वि चरत्समंजन्नग्निर्नारीं वीरकुक्षिं पुरंधिं ॥ (*agnī rodasī vi caratsamaṃjannagnirnārīṃ vīrakukṣiṃ puraṃdhiṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-80-1)

In such ṛks (1-106-17, 1-117-19) the meaning is a wife capable of bearing offspring, and

वायुर्युंक्ते रोहिता वायुररुणा वायू रथे अजिरा धुरि वोळ्हवे (*vāyuryuṃkte rohitā vāyuraruṇā vāyū rathe ajirā dhuri voḷhave*)
वहिष्ठा धुरि वोळ्हवे । (*vahiṣṭhā dhuri voḷhave |*)
प्र बोधया पुरंधिं जार आ ससतीमिव । (*pra bodhayā puraṃdhiṃ jāra ā sasatīmiva |*)
प्र चक्षय रोदसी वासयोषसः श्रवसे वासयोषसः ॥ (*pra cakṣaya rodasī vāsayoṣasaḥ śravase vāsayoṣasaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-134-3)

in such ṛks a woman fit for enjoyment is understood. Thus, when various qualities are described, the word पुरंधि (*puraṃdhi*) indicates the abundance of those qualities and real prosperity. In the present ṛk the meaning is given as cows that give abundant milk.

### Printed p. 16

*Running head: 16 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೮").*

जिगृतं (*jigṛtaṃ*)—शब्दयतं । (*śabdayataṃ |*) "Make a sound of acceptance", that is, give cows: this is the purport. In अविष्टं धियो जिगृतं (*aviṣṭaṃ dhiyo jigṛtaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-50-11) it has the meaning प्रबुध्यतं (*prabudhyataṃ*), "awaken". In the same way here too it can mean "make us aware in regard to cows", that is, "give us the knowledge that that wealth is ours".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained word for word):* the Kannada gives the grammatical analysis of दाशत् (*dāśat*), सुमतये (*sumataye*), वसू (*vasū*), धेथे (*dhethe*), पदे (*pade*), जिगृतम् (*jigṛtam*), अस्मे (*asme*) and रेवतीः (*revatīḥ*), with the suffix, the Vedic irregularity and the accent of each form, citing among others the sūtras लेटोऽडाट् (*leṭo 'ḍāṭ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-94), शृस्वस्निहित्रसि (*śṛsvasnihitrasi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-10), प्वादीनां ह्रस्वः (*pvādīnāṃ hrasvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-80), ऊडिदं पदादि (*ūḍidaṃ padādi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171), सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) and छंदसीरः (*chaṃdasīraḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-15); the printed "ಪಾ. ಸೂ. ೭.೨-೮೧" for आतोजितः (*ātojitaḥ*) and the sūtra number "೬-೧-೧೭೬-೧" broken across two lines are kept as supplied.

### Printed p. 17

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 17 (as supplied).*

*Grammar, continued:* कामप्रेण इव (*kāmapreṇa iva*) (root प्रा (*prā*), suffix *ka* by आतोनुपसर्गे कः (*ātonupasarge kaḥ*), Pā. Sū. 3-2-3, a third-case form) and चरंता (*caraṃtā*) (root चर (*cara*), participle suffix, *num* augment, सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 7-1-39 for the dual, the root accent remains). The grammar of Ṛk 2 ends with the mark ॥ ೨ ॥ and a stray ornament line ":0:", kept as supplied.

#### Ṛk 3

*[The Saṃhitā of this Ṛk is printed as ordinary lines, not as a block, in the supplied text, and its label is garbled as "ಸಂಕ್ಷಿಪ್ತವಾಗಿ"; the standard label is inserted, the garbled word kept in kn.md as a note, and the lines are made a block.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यु॒क्तो ह॒ यद्वां॑ तौ॒ग्राय॑ पे॒रुर्वि मध्ये॒ अर्ण॑सो॒॒॑
> धायि॑ प॒ज्रः ।
> उप॑ वा॒मवः॑ शर॒णं ग॑मेयं॒ शूरो॒ नाज्म॑ प॒तय॑द्धि॒रेवैः॑ । ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> युक्तः । ह । यत् । नां । तौग्त्याय । पेरुः । वि । मध्ये । अर्णसः । धायि । पज्रः ।
> उप । नां । अवः । शरणं । गमेयं । शूरः । न । अज्म । पतयेत्भिः । एवैः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अत्रेतिहासमाहुः । तौग्र्यनामकं राजानं युद्धे शत्रवः पराभाव्य पाशैर्दृढं बद्धा समुद्रमध्यॆ प्रचिक्षिपुः । स चोत्तरीतुमशक्तः सन्नश्विन्‌नौ तुष्टाव । तौ
> च तुष्टौ शीघ्रमेव रथमश्वैर्योजयित्वा समुद्रमागत्य तमुत्तार्यापालयतामिति । अयमर्थोऽजोहवीदश्विना तौग्र्यः । ऋ. १-११७-१५ । इत्यादिमंत्रांतरे
> प्रसिद्धः । सोऽत्रोच्यते ॥ हे अश्विन् नां युवयोः संबंधी पेरुः पारणकुशलो
> रथो युक्तो ह । अश्वैर्युक्तः खलु । हशब्दो मंत्रांतरप्रसिद्धिद्योतनार्थः ।
> स चोदाहृतः । कस्मै । तौग्रायैतन्नाम्ने राज्ञे तद्बंधमोचनार्थं । कुत्रॆति

*[The bhāṣya block breaks off at the end of printed p. 17 in the middle of a sentence ("…ಕುತ್ರೆತಿ"); it continues on p. 18, in the next window. Two stray signature lines at the foot of the page ("[2] Vol. 12", printed twice) are kept in kn.md as a note. Gist of what is given so far: enemies defeated King Taugrya in battle, bound him and threw him into the mid-ocean; he praised the Aśvins, who yoked horses to a chariot, came to the sea and brought him out; the bhāṣya says that this story is known from another mantra (Ṛ. 1-117-15) and is referred to here.]*

### Printed p. 18

*Running head: 18 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. "೫೮" (printed so for 158; the hundreds figure is missing in the supplied text).*

*(Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 3, continued from the end of p. 17; the block begins with the words "ತದುಚ್ಯತೇ" that complete the sentence "…ಕುತ್ರೆತಿ" of the previous window. A hyphenated line break in the block is joined in kn.md.)*

> तदुच्यते। अर्णसोऽंभसो मध्यॆ समुद्रस्य मध्यॆ। स च पज्रः पाजसा बलेन तीर्णः
> सन् वि धायि । धारितः । स्थापितः । यस्मादेवं तस्माद्वां युवयोरवो रक्षणं
> शरणमुप गमेयं । संप्राप्नुयां । ऐहिकदुःखाद्विमुक्तो युष्मत्स्थानं संभजेयं । गतौ दृष्टांतः । पतयद्भिरेवैरश्चैः शूरो न शूर इव । स यथाज्म जित्वा
> निर्भयं स्वगृहं प्राप्नोति तद्वत् ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 3 (my own short summary): Taugrya, thrown by enemies into mid-ocean, called on the Aśvins, whose horse-drawn chariot brought him out; the seer prays to reach their protection as a victorious hero reaches home.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे अश्विनौ (*he aśvinau*)—O Aśvin deities.) यत् (*yat*)—for which reason, that is, in which manner. वां (*vāṃ*)—your. पेरुः (*peruḥ*)—the chariot able to protect. तौग्राय (*taugrāya*)—for the son of Tugra. अर्णसः मध्यॆ (*arṇasaḥ madhyè*)—in the middle of the sea with deep water. युक्तो ह (*yukto ha*)—joined (with horses). पज्रः (*pajraḥ*)—(as) having the capacity to cross (the sea). विधायि (*vidhāyi*)—has it been established? ("ಸ್ಥಾಪಿಸಲ್ಪಟ್ಟಿದೆಯೋ"). (Therefore:) पतयद्भिः (*patayadbhiḥ*)—swiftly running; एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—with horses; शूरो न अज्म (*śūro na ajma*)—just as a valiant hero, victorious, reaches a well-protected house. वां (*vāṃ*)—your; अवः (*avaḥ*)—protection; शरणं (*śaraṇaṃ*)—and refuge; उप गमेयं (*upa gameyaṃ*)—I shall obtain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities! At the time of rescuing the son of Tugra who was sinking in the middle of the sea, your chariot, able to protect, joined with horses, showed its capacity to cross the sea. Therefore your refuge always gives protection. Just as a valiant hero returns victorious, with the help of horses, to a well-protected house, so I too obtain your protection and refuge.

**Printed English translation**

In like manner, Ashwins, as your strong-drawn(car) able to cross (the sea), was placed, harnessed, in the middle of the water, for (the rescue of) the son of Tugra, so may I attain to your preserving protection (with the same alacrity) as a victorious hero (returns home) with bounding steeds.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

A king named Taugrya was once defeated by enemies, who tied his hands and feet and threw him into the middle of the sea. Unable to cross the sea, he prayed to the Aśvin deities for help. At once they yoked horses to their chariot, drove it into the sea and brought him to the shore: thus Sāyaṇa has cited the earlier story. *[The sentence runs over the page break, "ಪ್ರಾರ್ಥಿ|ಸಿದನು".]*

### Printed p. 19

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 19 (as supplied).*

(Sāyaṇa has cited the story with the word "ದಹರಿಸಿದ್ದಾರೆ", printed for "ಉದಹರಿಸಿದ್ದಾರೆ", as supplied.) This son of Tugra is well known by the name Bhujyu. That the Aśvin deities rescued this same son of Tugra, who was sinking in the sea, is described by Kakṣīvān, son of Dīrghatamas, in sūktas 1-116 and 1-117, by Agastya Maitrāvaruṇi in sūkta 1-182, by Brahmātithi of the Kaṇva gotra in sūkta 8-5, and by Ghoṣā, daughter of Kakṣīvān, in 10-39. In sūkta 1-182 it is described how they sent boats and how the son of Tugra, filled with hope, clung to those boats.

युवमेतं चक्रथुः सिंधुषु प्लवमात्मन्यंतं दक्षिणं तौग्राय कं । (*yuvametaṃ cakrathuḥ siṃdhuṣu plavamātmanyaṃtaṃ dakṣiṇaṃ taugrāya kaṃ |*)
येन देवत्रा मनसा निरूहथुः सुपप्तनी पेतथुः क्षोदसो महः । (*yena devatrā manasā nirūhathuḥ supaptanī petathuḥ kṣodaso mahaḥ |*)
अवविद्धं तौग्रमप्पन्तरनारंभणे तमसि प्रविद्दं । (*avaviddhaṃ taugramappantaranāraṃbhaṇe tamasi praviddaṃ |*)
चतस्रो नावो जठलस्य जुष्टा उदश्चिभ्यामिषिताः पारयंति ॥ (*catasro nāvo jaṭhalasya juṣṭā udaścibhyāmiṣitāḥ pārayaṃti ‖*)
कः स्विद्वक्षो निष्ठितो मध्यॆ अर्णसो यं तौग्रो नाधितः पर्यषस्वजत् । (*kaḥ svidvakṣo niṣṭhito madhyè arṇaso yaṃ taugro nādhitaḥ paryaṣasvajat |*)
पर्णा मृगस्य पतरोरिवारभ उदश्विना ऊहथुः श्रोमताय कं ॥ (*parṇā mṛgasya patarorivārabha udaśvinā ūhathuḥ śromatāya kaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-182-5 to 7)

O Aśvin deities! For Bhujyu, son of Tugra, you made this boat, endowed with life and with wings. For the son of Tugra who had fallen and was sinking in the unfathomable sea filled with darkness, you sent four boats. It is praised (by asking) from which tree you made the parts of the boat that was the support and protector of Bhujyu sinking in the sea, like the wings that are the support of flying birds in the sky. The same point is indicated in the present ṛk too. And this refuge and protection are described along with comparisons.

पेरुः (*peruḥ*)—उपद्रवेभ्यः पारयिता । (*upadravebhyaḥ pārayitā |*) "One who carries across from calamities." As in places such as शं नो अपां (*śaṃ no apāṃ*) नपात्सेरुरस्तु (*napātserurastu*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-35-13) it is an epithet of Agni and others, here it is an epithet of the chariot of the Aśvin deities.

पज्रः (*pajraḥ*)—The word *pajra* is well known as the name of the family of Kakṣīvān; its details were given earlier. As the word पज्र (*pajra*) in the ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-63-12 was explained as वेगवान् बलवान् ना (*vegavān balavān nā*) *[so printed]*, the same meaning is given in the present ṛk. पाजसा बलेन तीर्ण (*pājasā balena tīrṇa*)स्सन् । (*ssan |*) "having crossed by its own strength, it stands": thus it shows the strength of the chariot *[printed "ಶಥದ ಶಕ್ತಿ", for "ರಥದ" presumably; kept as supplied]*.

### Printed p. 20

*Running head: 20 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.*

एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—कामैरयनैरवनैर्वा (*kāmairayanairavanairvā*) (Ni. 12-21): this word shows free, unobstructed movement and also indicates the power of going. Ordinarily this word is an epithet of the Maruts, who are always moving and are friends of Indra. In the present ṛk it is a synonym for the swift and powerful horses of the hero.

अज्ज (*ajja*) *[so printed for ಅಜ್ಮ]*—This word is read among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of a house (Ni. 3-13). Besides the usages in these two senses,

उचध्ये ३ वपुषि यः स्वराळुत वायो घृतस्नाः । (*ucadhye 3 vapuṣi yaḥ svarāḷuta vāyo ghṛtasnāḥ |*)
अश्वेषितं रजेषितं शुनेषितं प्राज्ञ तदिवं नु तत् ॥ (*aśveṣitaṃ rajeṣitaṃ śuneṣitaṃ prājña tadivaṃ nu tat ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-46-28)

in this (ṛk) the word अज्ज (*ajja*) *[so printed]* is explained as "food".

अच्युता चिद्वो अज्मन्ना नानदति पर्वतासो वनस्पतिः। भूमिर्या मेषु रेजते। (*acyutā cidvo ajmannā nānadati parvatāso vanaspatiḥ| bhūmiryā meṣu rejate|*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-20-5)

Here अज्मनि गमने सति । (*ajmani gamane sati |*) is given, and it is explained as a usage in the sense of going.

ये पातयंते अज्मभिर्गिरीणां स्नुभिरेषां । (*ye pātayaṃte ajmabhirgirīṇāṃ snubhireṣāṃ |*)
यज्ञं महिष्वणीनां सुम्मं तुविष्टणीनां प्राध्वरे ॥ (*yajñaṃ mahiṣvaṇīnāṃ summaṃ tuviṣṭaṇīnāṃ prādhvare ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-46-18)

In this place अज्मभिः बलैः बलकरैरुदकैः सह (*ajmabhiḥ balaiḥ balakarairudakaiḥ saha*) is explained as "strengths, together with strength-giving waters". Thus this word is used in various senses. In the present ṛk the meaning is "a house that gives comfort and protection".

शूरः (*śūraḥ*)—शूरः शवतेर्गतिकर्मणः । (*śūraḥ śavatergatikarmaṇaḥ |*) Formed from the root शु (*śu*) meaning "to go", it gives the purport "one who has valour" (Ni. 4-13).

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada begins the grammatical analysis with पेरुः (*peruḥ*) (root पि (*pi*), suffix *ru* by मिपिभ्यां रुः (*mipibhyāṃ ruḥ*), Uṇ. Sū. 4-541) and अर्णसः (*arṇasaḥ*) (root ऋ (*ṛ*), suffix *asun* with the augment *nuṭ* by उदकेनुट् च (*udakenuṭ ca*), Uṇ. Sū. 4-639); the analysis continues on the next page.

### Printed p. 21

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 21 (as supplied).*

*Grammar of Ṛk 3, continued (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses धायि (*dhāyi*) (root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*), aorist, no augment because Vedic), अवः (*avaḥ*) (root अव (*ava*), Uṇādi suffix *asun*), गमेयम् (*gameyam*) (optative of the root गमल् (*gamal*), first person), अज्ज (*ajja*) *[so printed for ಅಜ್ಮ]* (root अज (*aja*), suffix *manin*), पतयद्भिः (*patayadbhiḥ*) (root पत्ल्‌ (*patl*), causative, participle suffix, instrumental plural) and एवै (*evai*) (root इण् (*iṇ*), suffix *van* by the sūtra Uṇ. Sū. 1-150, accent on the first syllable), and ends with the mark ॥ ೩ ॥. A stray ornament line ":C:" follows, kept as supplied.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उप॑स्तुतिरौच॒थ्यमु॑रुष्ये॒न्मा मामि॒मे प॑त॒त्रिणी॒ वि दु॑ग्धां ।
> मा मामेधो॒ दश॑तयश्चि॒तो धा॒क् प्र यद्वां॑ ब॒द्धस्मनि॒ खाद॑ति॒ क्षां ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उपस्तुतिः । औचथ्यं । उरुष्येत् । मा । मां । इमे इति । पतत्रिणी इति । वि ।
> दुग्धां ।
> मा । मां । एधः । दशतयः । चितः । धाक् । प्र । यत् । वां । बद्धः । त्मनि । खादति।
> क्षां ॥ ४ ॥

> *upastutiḥ | aucathyaṃ | uruṣyet | mā | māṃ | ime iti | patatriṇī iti | vi |*
> *dugdhāṃ |*
> *mā | māṃ | edhaḥ | daśatayaḥ | citaḥ | dhāk | pra | yat | vāṃ | baddhaḥ | tmani | khādati|*
> *kṣāṃ ‖ 4 ‖*

### Printed p. 22

*Running head: 22 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22 (printed "ಆ. ೨೨"), Sū. 158.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕಾಯಶಭಾಷ್ಯ c") and sits inside the block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks inside the block are joined.]*

> अत्राख्यानमाहुः । जराझुर्झरितगात्रं जात्यंधं दीर्घतमसं मामतेयं
> वरिवसितुमशक्नुवानाः स्वगर्भदासा अग्नौ प्रदाहाय प्रचक्षिपुः । तत्र क्षिप्रोऽश्विनावस्तावीत् । तौ चैनमरक्षतां । ततोऽप्यमियमाणमुदकेष्वपातयन् । तत्र निमग्नः
> पुनरश्विन् तुष्टाव । तुष्टौ संतौ तौ जलादुदहार्ष्टां । एवमवध्यं तं त्रैतनो
> नामकः कश्चिद्दासोऽस्य शिरो वक्षश्च व्यतक्षत् । ततोऽप्यपालयतामिति । तदिदमृग्धयाभ्यामुच्यते । हे अश्विनौ उपस्तुतिर्युवां बुद्धोपेत्य क्रियमाणा स्तुतिरौचथ्यमुचथस्य पुत्रं दीर्घतमसं मामुरुष्येत् । रक्षेद्वाहाम्य पद्रवात् । उरुष्यती रक्षाकर्मति यास्कः । नि. ५. २३. । एवं सामान्येनोक्ता विशेषेण प्रत्युपद्रवपरिहारं प्रार्थयते । इमे प्रसिद्धे पतत्रिणी पतनरीले पुनरावर्तनशीले अहोरात्रे मां मा वि दुग्धां । विशेषेण दोहगतसारं मा कार्ष्टां । प्राणनिर्गमनानुकूल्यं मा कुरुतामित्यर्थः । किंच मां दशतयो वशवारश्चितः संचितः संपादित
> एधः प्रज्वालितेंधनसंघो मा धाक् । मा धाक्षीत् ॥ दहतेर्लुजि सिचि हलंतलक्षणा
> वृद्धिः । बहुलं छंदसीतीडभावः । हल्बादिसंयोगांतलोप् ॥ दुःसहदुप्राप्तिं
> दर्शयति । यद्यस्माद्वां युवयोः संबंध्ययुं जनो बद्धः पाशैर्गाढं वेष्टित
> सन् त्मन्यात्मन्यात्मना क्षां भूमिं खादति भक्षयति । गंतुमशक्तः सन् भूमौ परिलुततीत्यर्थः । तस्मादुपस्तुतिरौचथ्यमुरुष्येदिति ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): blind, aged Dīrghatamas, cast by servants into fire and water, prays: may your praise protect me, may days, nights and fire not consume me.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರ. ಶ್ರೀಧರ್") and the Pratipadārtha sits inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे अश्विनौ (*he aśvinau*)—O Aśvin deities.) औचथ्यं (*aucathyaṃ*)—made by Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya; उपस्तुतिः (*upastutiḥ*)—(addressed to you) this hymn; मां (*māṃ*)—me; उरुष्यत् (*uruṣyat*)—may it protect. इमे पतत्रिणी (*ime patatriṇī*)—these days and nights, moving by recurrence; वि मग्धां (*vi magdhāṃ*) *[so printed; the Saṃhitā has ವಿ ದುಗ್ಧಾಂ]*—(causing me old age) may they not make me wither. दशतयः (*daśatayaḥ*)—ten times; चितः (*citaḥ*)—kindled (and flaming); विधः (*vidhaḥ*) *[so printed; the Saṃhitā has ಏಧಃ]*—the mass of sparks of fire; मां (*māṃ*)—me; मा धाक् (*mā dhāk*)—may it not burn. यत् (*yat*)—for which reason (that is, in which manner); वां (*vāṃ*)—I, belonging to you; बद्धः (*baddhaḥ*)—(the whole body) bound; त्मनि (*tmani*)—by myself; क्षां (*kṣāṃ*)—the earth; खादति (*khādati*)—(crawling) I lick (knowing this, protect me).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities! May this fire, kindled ten times by enemies, not burn me. May the days and nights, which move by recurrence, in the same way not make me old and decrepit. May this praise, made by me, the son of Ucathya, satisfy you and protect me. Knowing my wretched state, bound by enemies, crawling by myself and licking the earth, protect me.

### Printed p. 23

*Running head: [ಅ ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 23 (as supplied).* (The Bhāvārtha, begun at the foot of p. 22, is given above in full.)

**Printed English translation**

*[Printed heading "English Translation."]*

May the praise addressed to you, Ashwins, preserve the son of Uchatthya: let not these revolving (days and nights) exhaust me : let not the ten times kindled fire consume me: let it not be, that one who is your (dependant), bound (with bonds), should of himself bite the earth.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Dīrghatamas had a body worn out by old age and had been blind from birth. His attendants, disgusted at serving him, bound his hands and feet and threw him into fire. When he praised the Aśvin deities, they protected him from the fire. The attendants then pushed him, who had not died, into the water. Even then, praised and invoked by him, the Aśvin deities protected him. One slave named Traitana, with his own strength, struck him on the head and chest, wounded him and ran away. Sāyaṇa has told the earlier story, saying that this occasion is spoken of in these two ṛks. (See Ṛ. Saṃ. part 5, page 231 — so printed: "ಋ. ಸಂ. ಭಾಗ ೫. ಪೇಜು 231 ನೋಡಿ".)

उरुष्येश् (*uruṣyeś*) *[so printed]*—उरुष्यती रक्षाकर्म (*uruṣyatī rakṣākarma*) (Ni. 5-23): it is used in the sense of protection. The authors of the Nirukta

स नो बोधि श्रुधी हवमुरुष्या णो अघायतः समस्मात् । (*sa no bodhi śrudhī havamuruṣyā ṇo aghāyataḥ samasmāt |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-24-3)

quote this ṛk and give the meaning of the word उरुष्य (*uruṣya*) as "protect us from all who wish us evil". Sāyaṇa has said that the meaning is the same in the present ṛk.

पतत्रिणी (*patatriṇī*)—पतनशीले पुनरावर्तनशीले । (*patanaśīle punarāvartanaśīle |*) It is used as a synonym for the days and nights, which move by recurrence (being born, setting) and by an uninterrupted succession. Although this word too shows the sense "moving", the authors of the Nirukta (quote):

यो होतासीत्पथमो देवजुष्टो यं समांजन्नाज्येना वृणानाः । (*yo hotāsītpathamo devajuṣṭo yaṃ samāṃjannājyenā vṛṇānāḥ |*)
स पतत्रीत्वरं स्था जगद्यच्छात्रमग्निरकृणोज्जातवेदाः । (*sa patatrītvaraṃ sthā jagadyacchātramagnirakṛṇojjātavedāḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-4)

### Printed p. 24

*Running head: 24 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158.* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue.)

Quoting this ṛk (Ni. 5-3), (the authors of the Nirukta) have shown the difference between the flying movement of birds and the movement of all other moving beings. Comparing the movements of days and nights with the flight of birds is very fitting from the point of view of figures of speech. The comparison in this metaphor suits both the speed and the beauty and attractiveness.

दशतयः (*daśatayaḥ*) *[the supplied text has "ದಶತೆಯರಿ" here]*—दशवाररि । (*daśavārari |*) "ten times".

क्षां (*kṣāṃ*)—भूमिं । (*bhūmiṃ |*) It is read among the names of the earth: गौः, ग्मा, ज्मा, क्ष्मा, क्षौ । (*gauḥ, gmā, jmā, kṣmā, kṣau |*) and so on (Ni. 2-5). क्षा क्षियतेर्निवासकर्मणः (*kṣā kṣiyaternivāsakarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 2-6): formed from the root क्षी (*kṣī*), which gives the sense "to dwell", it means "the earth on which one dwells".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses उपस्तुति (*upastuti*) (root ष्टुञ् (*ṣṭuñ*), suffix *ktin*; accent by Pā. Sū. 5-2-50), उरुष्येत् (*uruṣyet*) (optative; with the Nirukta definition "ಉರುಷ್ಯತೀ ರಕ್ಷಾ ಕರ್ಮಾ"), पतत्रिणी (*patatriṇī*) (suffix *atrin* by Uṇ. Sū. 4-509, or *atran* by Uṇ. Sū. 3-385), दुग्धाम् (*dugdhām*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-32 for the change of the aspirate), दशतयः (*daśatayaḥ*) (suffix *tayap*), चितः (*citaḥ*) (suffix *kta*), and, on the next page, धाक् (*dhāk*) (aorist; Pā. Sū. 3-3-175), बद्धः (*baddhaḥ*), त्मनि (*tmani*) (loss of the initial ā by Pā. Sū. 6-4-141) and खादति (*khādati*), with the suffixes, Vedic irregularities and accents of each.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> न मा॑ गरन्न॒द्यो॑ मा॒तृत॑मा दा॒सा यदीं॒ सुस॑मुब्धम॒वाधुः॑ ।
> शिरो॒ यद॑स्य त्रैत॒नो वि॒तक्ष॑त्स्व॒यं दा॒स उरो॒ अंसा॒वपि॑ ग्ध । ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> न । मा । गरन् । नद्यः । मातृतमाः । दासाः । यत् । ईं । सुसमुब्धं । अव्वअधुः ।
> शिरः । यत् । अस्य । त्रैतनः । वितक्षत् । स्वयं । दासः । उरः । अंसौ । अपि । ग्धेति ग्ध । ॥ ५ ॥

*[The label of the bhāṣya is garbled in the supplied text ("ಚಾಯುಧಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sits inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the bhāṣya are joined.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अस्याः पंचम्या विनियोगमाह शौनकः । आततायिनमायांतं दृष्ट्वा
> व्याघ्रमथो वृकं । नमा गरन्निति जपंस्तेभ्य एव प्रमुच्यते । त्रिरात्रोपोषितो

> *asyāḥ paṃcamyā viniyogamāha śaunakaḥ | ātatāyinamāyāṃtaṃ dṛṣṭvā*
> *vyāghramatho vṛkaṃ | namā garanniti japaṃstebhya eva pramucyate | trirātropoṣito*

### Printed p. 26

*Running head: 26 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158 (the bhāṣya block continues from p. 25).*

> रात्र्य जपेदासूर्यदर्शनात् । आस्प्लुत्य प्रयतः सूर्यमुपतिष्ठेद्दिवाकरं । पश्यंति
> तस्करा नैनं तथान्ये पापबुद्धयः । एकः शतानि त्रायेत तस्करेभ्यश्चरन्पथि ॥ ऋग्वि.
> १. २५. । इति ॥
> नद्यो नदनशीला मातृतमा मातृवज्जगतां हितकारिण्य आपो मा मां
> दीर्घतमसं गरन् । न गिरेयुः । निमग्नं मा कुर्युः ॥ गृ निगरणे । लेटि व्यत्ययेन शप् ॥ गिरणपाप्तिं दर्शयति । यद्यस्माद्दासा अस्मदुपक्षपयितारो मदीयगर्भदासा ईमेनं दीर्घतमसं मां सुसमुब्धं सुष्टु संकुचितसर्वांगं ॥ स्वती
> पूजायां । पा- २-२-१८ । इति प्रादिसमासेऽव्ययपूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ अवाधुः
> अवाङ्मुखमपातयन् । किंचास्य मम शिरः स त्रैतन एतन्नामको दासोऽत्यंतनिर्घृणः सन् यद्यस्माद्वितक्षत् विविधं तष्टवान् तस्मात्स दासः स्वयं स्वकीयमेव
> शिरस्तक्षतु । न केवलं शिर एव अपि तु मदीयमुरो वक्षस्थलमंसौ च गृ । हतवान् ।
> विदारितवानित्यर्थः ॥ हंतेर्लुजः छांदसमेतद्रूपं ॥ ततः स्वकीयमुरोऽंसावपि
> स्वशस्त्रेणैव तथा कृतवान् । तम्मुवयोर्माहात्ममिति भावः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Śaunaka prescribes this ṛk against attackers; Dīrghatamas, bound and thrown into the rivers by his slaves, asks that they not swallow him; Traitana, who struck him, wounded himself.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यत् (*yat*)—for which reason; दासाः (*dāsāḥ*)—(my) servants; सुसमुब्धं (*susamubdhaṃ*)—with a decrepit body, or with hands and feet folded and bound; ईं (*īṃ*)—me; अवाधुः (*avādhuḥ*)—having made me face downwards threw (into the river) (so, being helpless); मातृतमाः (*mātṛtamāḥ*)—most beneficial to the world like a mother; नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*)—rivers; मा (*mā*)—me; न गरन् (*na garan*)—may they not make sink. यत् (*yat*)—for which reason; अस्य (*asya*)—of this me; शिरः (*śiraḥ*)—the head; त्रैतनः (*traitanaḥ*)—my servant named Traitana; वितक्षत् (*vitakṣat*)—struck (with a sword) (immediately); दासः (*dāsaḥ*)—that servant; स्वयं (*svayaṃ*)—of his own accord; उरः (*uraḥ*)—(his own) "ಪಕ್ಷಸ್ಸನ್ನೂ" *[so printed; chest (ವಕ್ಷಸ್ಸು) is presumably meant]*; अंसौ अपि (*aṃsau api*)—his own arms also; गृ (*gṛ*) *[so printed]*—cut himself (that is your greatness).

*[The Bhāvārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("॥ ಭವಾನಾಥ ॥"); the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

My servants threw me, whose body was decrepit with old age, face downwards into the river. Knowing that I was helpless, may the rivers, which are most beneficial to the world like a mother, not make me sink. When Traitana, one of my servants, wounded me on the head and shoulder with a sword, by your (the Aśvin deities') grace he struck his own head and shoulder himself and ran away.

### Printed p. 27

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨ ಅ. .೩ ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 27 (printed so, as supplied).*

**Printed English translation**

Let not the maternal waters swallow me, since the slaves hurled down this decrepit (old man); in like manner as Traitana wounded his head, so has the slave wounded his own, and has struck his breast and shoulders,

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The application (*viniyoga*) of this fifth ṛk, नमा गरन् (*namā garan*) *[so printed]*, is stated by the great sage Śaunaka in his work called Ṛgvidhāna as follows: if a murderous and violent man, a tiger or a wolf comes face to face during a journey, then, if this mantra न मा गरन् (*na mā garan*) is chanted, the fear that might arise from them is removed. "Three nights" means: fast for three days, begin to chant this mantra at night and continue chanting until sunrise, then bathe in a river, stand facing the sun and praise it; then thieves and sinful men do not come near such a man, and when he travels on the road such a man alone can protect even a hundred people who are with him: this is stated in Ṛgvidhāna 1-25. Here, for the word आततायि (*ātatāyi*),

अग्निदो गरदश्चैव शस्त्रोन्मत्तो धनापहः । (*agnido garadaścaiva śastronmatto dhanāpahaḥ |*)
क्षेत्रदारहरश्चैतान् षड्विद्यादाततायिन ॥ (*kṣetradāraharaścaitān ṣaḍvidyādātatāyina ‖*)

(Śukranīti, printed "ಶುಕ್ರಸೀತಿ" *[so printed]*)

there is the following explanation in the Śukranīti: one who sets fire to a house, one who kills by giving poison, one who holds a sharp weapon in his hand and causes fear to others, one who seizes the wealth of others, one who deceitfully seizes land such as paddy-fields, fields and gardens, and one who abducts another man's wife: these six are called *ātatāyins*. Śaunaka has said that if one sees such sinners and recites the ṛk न मा गरन् (*na mā garan*), that sin is removed.

सुसमुब्धं (*susamubdhaṃ*)—सुष्टु संकुचितसर्वांगं । (*suṣṭu saṃkucitasarvāṃgaṃ |*) This is explained as "having a body made contracted". Whether it is contracted because it is worn out by old age, or because the servants tied his hands and feet, is not explained. It is also difficult to say from which root this word is formed. The scholar Monier Williams, in his Sanskrit-English Dictionary, explains it as सम्+उभ् (उंभाति) (*sam+ubh (uṃbhāti)*) "to cover up, confined", saying that it is formed from the root उभ् (*ubh*). With this help, Sāyaṇa's word "contracted" (संकुचित (*saṃkucita*))

### Printed p. 28

*Running head: 28 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೮").*

means "having a body made contracted because the servants bound it". But the scholar Wilson has translated this word as "decrepit"; then it must be understood that the body is worn out by old age and contracted. Monier Williams has clearly said that the meaning of सुसमुब्धं (*susamubdhaṃ*) is "well-bound, feltered" *[so printed; Latin letters in the supplied text]* (bound with nooses). In the Ṛk-saṃhitā, apart from this ṛk, the word is used in two other places: in one place merely as उब्बं (*ubbaṃ*) and in another as समुब्धं (*samubdhaṃ*).

ते गव्यता मनसा दृढमुब्बं गा येमानं परि षंतमद्रिं । (*te gavyatā manasā dṛḍhamubbaṃ gā yemānaṃ pari ṣaṃtamadriṃ |*)
दृळ्हं नरो वचसा दैवेयन व्रजं गोमंतमुशिजो वि ववुः ॥ (*dṛḷhaṃ naro vacasā daiveyana vrajaṃ gomaṃtamuśijo vi vavuḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-1-15)

In this ṛk, for the word उब्धं (*ubdhaṃ*) (Sāyaṇa) gives first the meaning समुब्धं संहितं (*samubdhaṃ saṃhitaṃ*), "collected, brought together" (here too the sense is contraction), and then says that, यद्वा उभिः पूर्णार्थः (*yadvā ubhiḥ pūrṇārthaḥ*) गोभिः पूर्णं । (*gobhiḥ pūrṇaṃ |*), formed from the root उभ् (*ubh*) in the sense of fullness, it may also mean "filled (with cows)". And,

कुमारं माता युवतिः समुब्बं गुहा बिभर्ति न ददाति पित्रे । (*kumāraṃ mātā yuvatiḥ samubbaṃ guhā bibharti na dadāti pitre |*)
अनीकमस्य न मिनज्जनासः पुरः पश्यंति निहितमरतौ ॥ (*anīkamasya na minajjanāsaḥ puraḥ paśyaṃti nihitamaratau ‖*)

(ಮ್ಯ. ಸಂ. 5-2-1) *[the printed abbreviation "ಮ್ಯ. ಸಂ." is kept; probably Ṛ. Saṃ., not expanded]*

here too, for the word समुब्बं (*samubbaṃ*) *[so printed]* he gives first चक्रेण हतं (*cakreṇa hataṃ*) and the second time निगूढं (*nigūḍhaṃ*). So Sāyaṇa too has not given an exact meaning. In the present ṛk the contraction of the body is the purport; it may be due to old age or to binding.

नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*)—नद्यः कस्मान्नदना इमा भवंति शब्दवत्यः । (*nadyaḥ kasmānnadanā imā bhavaṃti śabdavatyaḥ |*) (Ni. 2-24) The word is formed from the root नद् (*nad*), which gives the sense "to make a sound". For these rivers there is the epithet मातृतमाः (*mātṛtamāḥ*), explained as "very beneficial, like a mother". The rivers not only are the support of food in the form of plants and the foundation of the life of the world, but also show their unearthly love like a mother to the whole world and do good. In this context the authors of the Nirukta have cited a story to show the maternal love of the river:

विश्वामित्र ऋषिः सुदासः पैजवनस्य पुरोहितो बभूव । स वित्तं गृहीत्वा (*viśvāmitra ṛṣiḥ sudāsaḥ paijavanasya purohito babhūva | sa vittaṃ gṛhītvā*)
विपाट्टतुद्रो संभेदमाययौ । अनुययुरितरे । स विश्वामित्रो (*vipāṭṭatudro saṃbhedamāyayau | anuyayuritare | sa viśvāmitro*)

### Printed p. 29

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 29 (as supplied).*

नदीस्तुष्टाव ' गाधा भवत ' इति । (*nadīstuṣṭāva ' gādhā bhavata ' iti |*)

(Ni. 2-24)

Viśvāmitra the seer was the family priest (*purohita*) of the king Sudās, son of Pijavana. Once he collected wealth for a sacrifice and came to the confluence of the rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī, and praised them with the ṛk quoted below, praying that they would make it easy for him to cross.

रमध्वं मे वचसे सोम्याय ऋतावरीरुप मुहूर्तमेवैः । (*ramadhvaṃ me vacase somyāya ṛtāvarīrupa muhūrtamevaiḥ |*)
प्र सिंधुमच्छा बृहती मनीषावस्युरह्वे कुशिकस्य सूनुः ॥ (*pra siṃdhumacchā bṛhatī manīṣāvasyurahve kuśikasya sūnuḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33-5)

"I, the son of Kuśika, pray with the most excellent praise. Stop your flow for a moment and make it convenient for our passage." When he prayed thus, the rivers (said):

इंद्रो अस्मा अरदद्वज्रबाहुरपाहन्वृत्तं परिधिं नदीनां i (*iṃdro asmā aradadvajrabāhurapāhanvṛttaṃ paridhiṃ nadīnāṃ i*)
देवोऽनयत्सविता सुपाणिस्तस्य वयं प्रसवे याम उर्वीः ॥ (*devo 'nayatsavitā supāṇistasya vayaṃ prasave yāma urvīḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33-6)

"Indra of the thunderbolt arm made a path for our flow. Savitṛ of beautiful hands brought us here. By his impulsion we flow; yet" —

अ ते कारो शृणवामा वचांसि ययाथ दूरादनसा रथेन । (*a te kāro śṛṇavāmā vacāṃsi yayātha dūrādanasā rathena |*)
नि ते नंस्यॆ पीप्यानेव योषा मर्यायेव कन्या शश्वचै ते । (*ni te naṃsyè pīpyāneva yoṣā maryāyeva kanyā śaśvacai te |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33-10)

"we have accepted your prayer. As a mother bends lovingly so that her child may suckle, and as a young woman bends for the embrace of her beloved, so we bend and make a way for you": thus the authors of the Nirukta have cited the story in which the rivers show their maternal love. The same point,

सूक्ते प्रेति तु नद्यश्च विश्वामित्रः समूदिरे ॥ (*sūkte preti tu nadyaśca viśvāmitraḥ samūdire ‖*)
पुरोहितः सन्निज्यार्थं सुदासा सह यन्नृषिः । (*purohitaḥ sannijyārthaṃ sudāsā saha yannṛṣiḥ |*)
विपाट्टु तुद्रोः संभेदं शमित्येते उवाच ह ॥ (*vipāṭṭu tudroḥ saṃbhedaṃ śamityete uvāca ha ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 4-105-6) *[printed "ಬೃ. ದೇ. ೪-೧೦೫-೬"]*

in these sentences the authors of the Bṛhaddevatā too indicate the occasion on which Viśvāmitra praised the rivers and won their maternal love. Besides, the comparison of the help of the rivers to the loving help of mothers can be seen in the Ṛk-saṃhitā.

### Printed p. 30

*Running head: 30 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 158 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೮").* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 5 continue.)

अभि त्वा सिंधो शिशुमिन्न मातरो वाश्रा अर्षंति पयसेव धेनवः । (*abhi tvā siṃdho śiśuminna mātaro vāśrā arṣaṃti payaseva dhenavaḥ |*)
राजीव युध्वा नयसि त्वमित्सिचौ यदासामग्रं प्रवतामिनक्षसि ॥ (*rājīva yudhvā nayasi tvamitsicau yadāsāmagraṃ pravatāminakṣasi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-75-4) *[printed "ಮ- ಸಂ." with a hyphen; read Ṛ. Saṃ. by the abbreviation rule and changed in kn.md]*

In that (ṛk) it is described that, as a mother cow runs, making a sound, to her calf in order to give milk, the river rushes forward in the form of a flood, making a sound, in order to help the people. And,

ओमानमापो मानुषीरमृक्तं धात तोकाय तनयाय शं योः । (*omānamāpo mānuṣīramṛktaṃ dhāta tokāya tanayāya śaṃ yoḥ |*)
यूयं हि ष्ठा भिषजो मातृतमा विश्वस्य स्थातुर्जगतो जनित्रीः ॥ (*yūyaṃ hi ṣṭhā bhiṣajo mātṛtamā viśvasya sthāturjagato janitrīḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-50-7)

in this ṛk it is described that to the whole world, moving and unmoving, (the waters) give benefits greater even than those of mothers.

अंबितमे नदीतमे देवितमे सरस्वति । (*aṃbitame nadītame devitame sarasvati |*)
अप्रशस्ता इव स्मसि प्रशस्तिमंब नस्कृधि ॥ (*apraśastā iva smasi praśastimaṃba naskṛdhi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-41-16)

In this ṛk Sarasvatī, the best among the rivers, is praised as the best among mothers. Thus, as the loving help of the rivers is described in many places, in the present ṛk too Dīrghatamas, praising that quality, prays that they protect him without making him sink.

गरन् (*garan*)—Formed from the root गृ (*gṛ*), which gives the sense "to swallow", it gives here the sense "to make sink". न गरन् (*na garan*), "do not make me sink": thus it is prayed.

यथा ना शीर्णे गरमभिनिदध्यादेवं (*yathā nā śīrṇe garamabhinidadhyādevaṃ*)

(Śa. Brā. 11-5-8-6)

In sentences such as this the word गर (*gara*) is explained as an intoxicating substance.

ग्ध (*gdha*)—It is formed from the root हन् (*han*), which gives the sense "to injure". हतवान् (*hatavān*): "struck himself" is the meaning. When (the slave) set out to strike Dīrghatamas with a sword, the Aśvin deities favoured Dīrghatamas and made those blows fall on Dīrghatamas's own slave Traitana, on his neck and arms; the seer says this with gratitude.

### Printed p. 31

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨ ಆ ೩. ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 31 (as supplied).*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses गरन् (*garan*) (root गृ (*gṛ*), *let* third person plural, suffix *jhi*, the *śap* augment by the Vedic rule, इतश्चलोपः (*itaścalopaḥ*) Pā. Sū. 3-4-97), दासा (*dāsā*) (root दसि (*dasi*), suffix *ṭa*/*ṭan* by Uṇ. Sū. 688), सुसमुब्बम् (*susamubbam*) *[so printed]* (root उब्ब (*ubba*) *[so printed]*, suffix *kta*, compound accent), अव अधुः (*ava adhuḥ*) (prefix अव (*ava*), root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*), aorist; गातिस्थाघु (*gātisthāghu*) Pā. Sū. 2-4-77, Pā. Sū. 3-4-110, Pā. Sū. 6-1-96, तिजिचोदात्तवति (*tijicodāttavati*) Pā. Sū. 8-1-71), वितक्षत् (*vitakṣat*) (root तक्षू (*takṣū*), no augment, Pā. Sū. 6-4-75), अंसौ (*aṃsau*) (root अम (*ama*), suffix *san* by Uṇ. Sū. 699) and गृ (*gṛ*) *[so printed, for ಗ್ಧ]* (root हन (*hana*), aorist), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೫ ॥. Odd printed forms (e.g. "ದಾಸಾ:", "ಪಾ ಸೂ. ಮ. ೨-೨-೧೮-೪") are kept as supplied.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> दी॒र्घत॑मा मामते॒यो जु॑जु॒र्वान्द॑श॒मे यु॒गे ।
> अ॒पामर्थं॑ य॒तीनां॑ ब्र॒ह्मा भ॑वति॒ सार॑थिः ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಹದಪಾರಃ") and sits inside the Saṃhitā block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

> दीर्घतमाः । मामतेयः । जुजुर्वान् । दशमे । युगे ।
> अपां । अर्थं । यतीनां । ब्रह्मा । भवति । सारथिः ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[In the bhāṣya a Latin "s" stood in the Kannada text for the avagraha ("ಯುಗೇs-ತೀತೇ"); it is written letter for letter as ಽ in kn.md, and the hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> दीर्घतमा एतन्नामा महर्षिः स च मामतेयो मामतायाः पुत्रो
> दशमे युगे दशयुगपर्यंतं महानुभावयोरश्विनोः प्रभावात्पूर्वोक्ताद्दुःखाद्विमुक्तोऽत्यंतसुखी स्वपुत्रभार्यादिभिः सहितो जीवितवान् । दशमे युगेऽतीते सति जुजुर्वाञ्चीर्णो वलीपलितगात्रो बभूव । एवंभूतः सन्नर्थं पुरुषैरर्थ्यमानं कर्मफलं स्वर्गादिकं यतीनां प्राप्नुवतीनामपामस्कार्याणां प्रजानां । यद्वा । अपामपसां कर्मणामर्थं प्रयोजनं यतीनां तासां प्रजानां ।
> ब्रह्मा ब्रह्मसदृशः परिवृद्धो भवति । किंच सारथिः तद्वन्निर्वाहको भवति । देवो
> भवतीत्यर्थः । एवमस्य वृत्तांतं मंत्रोऽनुवदति । स्वयं ना स्वात्मानं पारोक्ष्येणाश्विनोर्महानुभावत्वं प्रकटयितुं ब्रवीति ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Dīrghatamas, freed from his suffering by the Aśvins' power, lived happily ten yugas, then aged, and became like Brahmā and a guide for the people's rites.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರ. ಶಿಪದಾರ್ಥ") and the Pratipadārtha sits inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

मामतेयः (*māmateyaḥ*)—the son of Mamatā; दीर्घतमाः (*dīrghatamāḥ*)—the seer named Dīrghatamas; दशमे युगे (*daśame yuge*)—(in his life) in the tenth yuga; जुजुर्वान् (*jujurvān*)—became one with a body worn out by old age; अपां (*apāṃ*)—for sacrifices and other rites; अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)—in order to obtain their desired objects in the form of fruit; यतीनां (*yatīnāṃ*)—for all the people who perform rites; ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)—became Brahman in form (became a leader of the sacrificers); सारथिः (*sārathiḥ*)—(as there is a charioteer to drive a chariot) becoming fit to carry out sacrifices and the rest; भवति (*bhavati*)—became.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Dīrghatamas, son of Mamatā, through the favour of the Aśvin deities, experienced a happy life for the yugas of his life, and when the tenth yuga had passed, he became one with a body weakened by old age… *[the supplied text of p. 32 breaks off here, in the middle of the sentence ("ಶಿಥಿಲಿತವಾದ", "weakened"); the Bhāvārtha continues on p. 33, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 33

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 33 (printed so: "ವ. ೨." although Ṛk 6 is in Varga 1; as supplied).* (The Bhāvārtha of Ṛk 6, begun at the foot of p. 32, continues.)

*[The sentence begun on p. 32 continues with "ದೇಹವುಳ್ಳವನಾದನು", "became one with a body (weakened by old age)".]* From his excellent life, he obtained the position of Brahman, a helper in performing rites so that the desired objects are fulfilled for all the people who, expecting fruit, perform sacrifices and the like. As there is a charioteer to drive a chariot, he became capable of conducting sacrifices and the rest. *[The final syllable of "ಅದನು" is on the last line of the page: "ಆದವನಾಗಿ / ಅದನು", as supplied.]*

**Printed English translation**

Dirghatamas, the son of Mamata, has grown old after the tenth yuga (has passed): he is the Brahma of those who seek to obtain the object of their (pious) works: he is their chariotcer [sic].

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Dīrghatamas, who had become unable to lead even an ordinary life, overcame, with the help of the Aśvin deities, all the kinds of calamity that had befallen him, and not only enjoyed for a very long time a life full of happiness, with the wealth of sons, grandsons and so on, but also, by his excellent life, in which he performed sacred rites accompanied by faith and devotion, obtained the position of Brahman, the leader of all sacrificers: thus his worth is told.

ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)—ब्रह्मसदृशः परिवृढो भवति । (*brahmasadṛśaḥ parivṛḍho bhavati |*) "He became one with power equal to Brahman." What this power is, the authors of the Nirukta (explain), citing this reasoning *[the supplied "ದಹರಿಸಿ" stands for "ಉದಹರಿಸಿ"]* from the ṛk

ऋचां त्वः पोषमास्ते पुपुष्वान् गायत्रं त्वो गायति शक्वरीषु । (*ṛcāṃ tvaḥ poṣamāste pupuṣvān gāyatraṃ tvo gāyati śakvarīṣu |*)
ब्रह्मा त्वो वदति जातविद्यां यज्ञस्य मात्रां वि मिमीत उ त्वः ॥ (*brahmā tvo vadati jātavidyāṃ yajñasya mātrāṃ vi mimīta u tvaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-21-11)

and explaining the nature of the position of Brahman:

ब्रह्मैको जाते जाते विद्यां वदति । ब्रह्मा सर्वविद्यः सर्वं वेदितुमर्हति । (*brahmaiko jāte jāte vidyāṃ vadati | brahmā sarvavidyaḥ sarvaṃ veditumarhati |*)
ब्रह्मा परिवृळ्हः श्रुततो ब्रह्म परिवृळ्हं सर्वतः ॥ (*brahmā parivṛḷhaḥ śrutato brahma parivṛḷhaṃ sarvataḥ ‖*)

(Ni. 1-8)

"Brahman (being a knower) expounds the truths of all things; he is one who knows all. Because he knows the truth he is the most excellent of all": thus the authors of the Nirukta have stated the high worth of Brahman. It is said that Dīrghatamas obtained such a position.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada begins with मामतेयः (*māmateyaḥ*) (suffix *ḍhak*; *eya* substituted by Pā. Sū. 7-1-2; initial vṛddhi by Pā. Sū. 7-2-118, and loss of the ā by Pā. Sū. 6-8-148, all as printed) and, on the next page, जुजुर्वान् (*jujurvān*) (root जृष् (*jṛṣ*), perfect, *kvasu* by Pā. Sū. 3-2-107), दतमे (*datame*) *[so printed for ದಶಮೇ]* (suffix *maṭ* by Pā. Sū. 5-2-49), अपाम् (*apām*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171) and यतीनाम् (*yatīnām*) (root इण् (*iṇ*); Pā. Sū. 6-1-81, 4-1-6 and 6-1-173), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೬ ॥.
