## Sūkta 159 (Maṇḍala 1)

*"ನೂರ ಇವತ್ತೊಂಭನೆತ್ತಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು" — printed so (garbled; "the hundred and fifty-ninth sūkta").*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; the sense is in the Anuvāda below):

> प्र द्यावेति पंचर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं दैर्घतमसं जागतं द्यावापृथिव्यं ।
> अनुक्रांतं च। प्र द्यावा पंच द्यावापृथिव्यं तु जागतं त्विति ॥ अग्निष्टोमे पृष्ठस्य
> षष्ठेऽहनि च द्यावापृथिव्यनिविद्वानमेतत्तूक्तं । तथा च सूत्रं ! प्र द्यावेति दैर्घततमसं । आ. ५-१८ । इति ॥ आद्या द्यावापृथिव्ये पशौ हविषो याज्या । तथा च सूत्रितं ।
> प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः पृथिवी ऋतावृधा मृळा नो रुद्रोत नो मयस्कृधीति द्वै ।
> आ ३-८ । इति ॥

> *pra dyāveti paṃcarcaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sūktaṃ dairghatamasaṃ jāgataṃ dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ |*
> *anukrāṃtaṃ ca| pra dyāvā paṃca dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ tu jāgataṃ tviti ‖ agniṣṭome pṛṣṭhasya*
> *ṣaṣṭhe 'hani ca dyāvāpṛthivyanividvānametattūktaṃ | tathā ca sūtraṃ ! pra dyāveti dairghatatamasaṃ | ā. 5-18 | iti ‖ ādyā dyāvāpṛthivye paśau haviṣo yājyā | tathā ca sūtritaṃ |*
> *pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ pṛthivī ṛtāvṛdhā mṛḷā no rudrota no mayaskṛdhīti dvai |*
> *ā 3-8 | iti ‖*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning प्र द्यावा (*pra dyāvā*) is the third sūkta in the twenty-second anuvāka. It has five ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya (printed "ಉಚಧ್ಯ", as supplied). Heaven and Earth are the deity. The metre is *jagatī*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्र द्यावा पंच द्यावा (*pra dyāvā paṃca dyāvā*)

### Printed p. 35

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ ೨ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 35 (as supplied).*

पृथिव्यं तु जागतं तु (*pṛthivyaṃ tu jāgataṃ tu*) *[the Anukramaṇikā words are broken across the page, "ದ್ಯಾವಾ-ಪೃಥಿವ್ಯಂ"; the supplied text has "ತು" twice where the Sanskrit of the introduction has "ತ್ವಿತಿ"]*. In the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice called Pṛṣṭha, the application of this sūkta is in the recitation of the *nivid-dhāna* mantra related to Heaven and Earth on the sixth day, as determined by the sūtra प्र द्यावेति दैर्घतमसं (*pra dyāveti dairghatamasaṃ*) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 5-18). And in the animal sacrifice, in the oblation made to the deities Heaven and Earth, the first ṛk of this sūkta is to be used as the *yājyā* mantra, as laid down by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra

प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः पृथिवी ऋतावृधा मृळा नो रुद्रोत नो मयस्कृधीति द्वे (*pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ pṛthivī ṛtāvṛdhā mṛḷā no rudrota no mayaskṛdhīti dve*)

(Ā. 3-8)

**Sūkta—159**

*[Om sign ("॥ ಓಂ ॥") printed above the sūkta number, left out here and in the Kannada-script file as a sign only.]*

Maṇḍala 1 ‖ Anuvāka 22 ‖ Sūkta 159 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 3 ‖ Varga 2 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 5 ·
Ṛṣi: Dīrghatamas Aucathya · Devatā: Heaven and Earth (द्यावापृथिवौ (*dyāvāpṛthivau*)) · Chandas: *jagatī*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> प्र द्यावा॑ य॒ज्ञैः पृ॑थि॒वी ऋ॑ता॒वृधा॑ म॒ही स्तु॑षे वि॒दथे॑षु॒॑
> प्रचे॑तसा ।
> दे॒वेभि॒र्ये दे॒वपु॑त्रे सु॒दंस॑से॒त्था धि॒या वार्या॑णि॒॑
> प्रभूषतः ॥ १ ॥

> *pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ pṛthivī ṛtāvṛdhā mahī stuṣe vidatheṣu*
> *pracetasā |*
> *devebhirye devaputre sudaṃsasetthā dhiyā vāryāṇi*
> *prabhūṣataḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 36

*Running head: 36 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪದರಃ") and stands as the first line of the Pada block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note. The Pada text is kept as supplied (e.g. "ಋತ್ತ್ವವೃಧಾ").]*

> प्र । द्यावा । यज्ञैः । पृथिवी इति । ऋत्त्ववृधा । मही इति । स्तुषे । विदथेषु ।
> प्रचेतसा ।
> देवेभिः । ये इति । देवपुत्रे इति देवपुत्रे । सुदंससा । इत्था । धिया ।
> वार्याणि । प्रभूषतः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[In the bhāṣya a Latin "s" stood for the avagraha ("ಕೋs-ತಿಶಯ"); it is written letter for letter as ಽ in kn.md, and the hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The word "ಯತಾವೃಧಾ" in the block is printed so, as supplied.]*

> द्यावा पृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ ॥ दिवो द्यावेति द्यानादेश आमृदात्तः । प्रथिवीशब्दो जीषंतोऽंतोदात्तः । देवताद्वंद्वे चेत्युभयपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । समासमध्ये पदांतरपाठश्चांदसः ॥ अहं यजमानो यज्ञैर्यागैस्तत्पदानैर्निमित्तभूतैर्हविष्प्रदानार्थं प्र स्तुषे । प्रकर्षेण सौमि ॥ पुरुषव्यत्ययः । कीवृशौ ते ।
> यतावृधा यज्ञस्य वर्धयित्र्यौ मही महत्यौ विदथेषु यागेषु प्रचेतसा प्रकर्षणास्मांश्चेतयित्र्यौ आस्माभिश्चेतयितव्ये वा । ईदृश्यौ स्तुषे । अनयोः कोऽतिशय इति उच्यते । ये द्यावापृथिव्यौ देवेभिर्यजमानैर्देवपुत्रे देवा यजमानाः पुत्रस्थानीयाः शुश्रूषकाययोस्ते तादृश्यौ । यद्वा । देवेभिर्विशिष्टे
> सुदंससा । दंस इति कर्मनाम । शोभनकर्मोपेते इत्था सत्यं । इत्येति सत्यनाम
> सत्रेत्थेति तन्नामसु पाठात् । सत्यं धियास्मदनुग्रहबुद्ध्या वार्याणि वरणीयानि
> धनान्यस्मभ्यं प्रभूषतः अलंकुरुतः । वत्त इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । वार्याणि हवींषि
> धिया कर्मणा निमित्तेन कर्मसमाप्त्यर्थं प्रभूषतः प्रकर्षेण स्वीकुरुतः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): I praise great Heaven and Earth, who nurture the sacrifice; with gracious mind they accept our desired oblations or grant our desired wealth.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕ್ರಿ,ತಿಪದಾರ್ಥ") and the Pratipadārtha sits inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*)—those who make the sacrifice increase; मही (*mahī*)—those of great form; विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—in sacrifices; प्रचेतसा (*pracetasā*)—who give us consciousness, or who are to be worshipped by us with faith; द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—the deities Heaven and Earth; यज्ञैः (*yajñaiḥ*)—with oblations and the like, which are the means of sacrifice, or for the sake of offering oblations; स्तुषे (*stuṣe*)—I praise. देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*)—having the sacrificers themselves as sons, or having the gods as sons. ये (*ye*)—which Heaven and Earth; देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—performed by the sacrificers; सुदंससा (*sudaṃsasā*)—endowed with excellent works; इत्था (*itthā*)—certainly; धिया (*dhiyā*)—with a gracious mind; वार्याणि (*vāryāṇi*)—the desired oblations; प्रभूषतः (*prabhūṣataḥ*)—adorn, that is, accept. [Or वार्याणि (*vāryāṇi*)—the desired objects wished for by the sacrificers; प्रभूषतः (*prabhūṣataḥ*)—provide.]

### Printed p. 37

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 37 (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I praise and pray, for the sake of offerings of oblations and the like, to Heaven and Earth, who make the sacrifice increase, who have a great form and are praised in the sacrifices. Heaven and Earth, who have the gods themselves as sons, endowed with the excellent rites performed by the sacrificers, fulfil the desired objects of the sacrificers with their gracious mind.

**Printed English translation**

*[Printed heading "English Tranlation" [sic]; the Latin text breaks "augment-ers" over a line, joined here.]*

I glorify with sacred rites the mighty Heaven and Earth, the augmenters of sacrifice, who (are to be contemplated) with devotion at holy ceremonies; those two, who, cherishing their worshippers as children, are venerated by the devout, and therefore with favour bestow (upon us) desirable blessings.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—Except for one sūkta, beginning with the ṛk बळित्था पर्वतानां (*baḷitthā parvatānāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-84), which has the Earth as its deity, there is no sūkta that has the Earth alone, or Heaven alone, as its deity. Heaven and Earth are referred to together in the other places. When they are so referred to together, in the matter of which of the two has the greater precedence, the authors of the Nirukta, anticipating that doubt, have explained as follows on the basis of the Ṛk-saṃhitā:

द्यावापृथिवीनामधेयान्युत्तराणि चतुर्विंशतिः । (*dyāvāpṛthivīnāmadheyānyuttarāṇi caturviṃśatiḥ |*) "स्वधे, पुरंधी, धिषणे (*svadhe, puraṃdhī, dhiṣaṇe*) रोदसी (*rodasī*) and so on are the twenty-four names of Heaven and Earth" (Ni. 3-21). तयोरेषा भवति । (*tayoreṣā bhavati |*)

कतरा पूर्वा कतरापरायोः कथा जाते कवयः को विवेद । (*katarā pūrvā katarāparāyoḥ kathā jāte kavayaḥ ko viveda |*)
विश्वं त्मना बिभृतो यद्ध नाम वि विर्तेते अहनी चक्रियेव ॥ (*viśvaṃ tmanā bibhṛto yaddha nāma vi virtete ahanī cakriyeva ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-185-1)

कतरा पूर्वा कतरापरा । एनयोः कथं जाते कवयः क एने विजानाति । (*katarā pūrvā katarāparā | enayoḥ kathaṃ jāte kavayaḥ ka ene vijānāti |*)
सर्वमात्मना बिभृतो यद्धैनयोः कर्म विवर्तेते चैनयोरहनी (*sarvamātmanā bibhṛto yaddhainayoḥ karma vivartete cainayorahanī*)
अहोरात्रे चक्रियेव चक्रयुक्ते इवेति द्यावापृथिव्योर्महिमानमाचष्ट (*ahorātre cakriyeva cakrayukte iveti dyāvāpṛthivyormahimānamācaṣṭa*)
आचष्टे । (*ācaṣṭe |*)

(Ni. 3-22)

"Which of these two deities is the earlier? What is the order of their origin? O seers, which of you knows this secret? They are the support of all the beings of the world; like the two wheels of a chariot, both are praised as running in succession": thus, quoting the Ṛk-saṃhitā, (the Nirukta authors) have explained. *[The sentence runs over the page break, "ರಥದ | ಎರಡು ಚಕ್ರಗಳಂತೆ".]*

### Printed p. 38

*Running head: 38 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೯").*

The same ṛk is quoted in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa also. Concerning the origin of these deities, wonder at times and an uncertain conjecture at other times are natural.

किं स्विध्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आस यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । (*kiṃ svidhvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsa yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ |*)
संतस्थाने अजरे इतऊती अहानि पूर्विरुषसो जरंत ॥ (*saṃtasthāne ajare itaūtī ahāni pūrviruṣaso jaraṃta ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 11 31-7) *[reference as printed, "೧೧ ೩೧-೭"; the ṛk is quoted again below as Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-31-7]*

"While innumerable days and dawns are passing, from what forest and from what trees were these Heaven and Earth, which stand imperishable, unbroken and firm, built?": thus the seer expresses his wonder, full of praise.

The inquiry expressed in the ṛk किं स्विध्वनं (*kiṃ svidhvanaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-31-7) is indicated in many other places too; in some places an apt answer can also be seen. For example,

किं स्विदासीदधिष्ठानमारंभणं कतमत्स्वित्कथासीत् । (*kiṃ svidāsīdadhiṣṭhānamāraṃbhaṇaṃ katamatsvitkathāsīt |*)
यतो भूमिं जनयन् विश्वकर्मा वि द्यामौर्णोन्महिना विश्वचक्षाः ॥ (*yato bhūmiṃ janayan viśvakarmā vi dyāmaurṇonmahinā viśvacakṣāḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-81-2)

it asks Viśvakarman, who created the earth, made the atmosphere spread and has all-seeing eyes, what the support was for their creation. As the answer to it,

किग्स् स्विद्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आसीत् । यतो द्यावा पृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । मनिषि- (*kigs svidvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsīt | yato dyāvā pṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ | maniṣi-*)
णो मनसा पृच्छतेम तत् । यदध्यतिष्ठद्भवनानि धारयन् । ब्रह्म वनं (*ṇo manasā pṛcchatema tat | yadadhyatiṣṭhadbhavanāni dhārayan | brahma vanaṃ*)
ब्रह्म स वृक्ष आसीत् । यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । मनिषिणो मनसा (*brahma sa vṛkṣa āsīt | yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ | maniṣiṇo manasā*)
विब्रवीमि वः । ब्रह्माध्यतिष्ठद्भवनानि धारयन् ॥ (*vibravīmi vaḥ | brahmādhyatiṣṭhadbhavanāni dhārayan ‖*)

(Tai. Brā. 2-8-9-6, 7) *[printed "ತೈ. ಬ್ರಾ."; the first word is printed "ಕಿಗ್ಸ್" in the supplied text]*

"For the creation of these Heaven and Earth Brahman (the imperishable thing called Brahman) itself is the forest and Brahman itself is the tree; from it they were made": thus it is said in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa.

इयं पृथ्वी भूतस्य प्रथमजा । (*iyaṃ pṛthvī bhūtasya prathamajā |*)

(Śa. Brā. 14-1-2-11)

"This earth was born first of all beings": this is the statement of the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa. In the present ṛk the greatness of Heaven and Earth is praised by the epithets मही (*mahī*), ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*) and so on.

### Printed p. 39

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 39 (as supplied).*

ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*)—ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य सत्यस्य वा वर्धयित्र्यौ । (*ṛtasya yajñasya satyasya vā vardhayitryau |*) The word ऋत (*ṛta*) has the meanings "sacrifice" and "truth"; and since the sacrifice is performed and truth established only with the help of Heaven and Earth, the epithets ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*) and ऋतावरी (*ṛtāvarī*) refer to Heaven and Earth *[printed "ವ್ಯಾವಾಪೃಥಿವಿಗಳ", for ದ್ಯಾವಾಪೃಥಿವಿಗಳ, as supplied]*.

मही (*mahī*)—महत्यौ । (*mahatyau |*) "Those of great form." प्रथनात्पृथिवीत्याहुः क एनामप्रथ (*prathanātpṛthivītyāhuḥ ka enāmapratha*)यिष्यत् किमाधारश्चेत्यथ वै दर्शनेन पृथुरप्रथिता चेदप्यन्यैः (*yiṣyat kimādhāraścetyatha vai darśanena pṛthuraprathitā cedapyanyaiḥ*) (Ni. 1-14): the name Pṛthivī is from being spread out widely. If it is asked who spread it out, it is hard to give the answer; even if no one had spread it, it is spread out of itself.

आपो वा इदमग्रे सलिलमासीत्तस्मिन्जापतिर्वायुर्भूत्वाचरत्स इमामप- (*āpo vā idamagre salilamāsīttasminjāpatirvāyurbhūtvācaratsa imāmapa-*)
श्यत्तां वराहो भूत्वाहरत्तां विश्वकर्माभूत्वा व्यमार्ट्सा प्रथत सा (*śyattāṃ varāho bhūtvāharattāṃ viśvakarmābhūtvā vyamārṭsā prathata sā*)
पृथिव्यभवत्तत्पृथिव्यै पृथिवित्वं । (*pṛthivyabhavattatpṛthivyai pṛthivitvaṃ |*)

(ಕೃ. ಸಂ. 7-1-5-1) *[abbreviation "ಕೃ. ಸಂ." as printed, not expanded; probably the Taittirīya Saṃhitā or Ṛk-saṃhitā]*

Here too the same quality is stated, and the greatness of the earth is shown.

विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—यज्ञेषु । (*yajñeṣu |*) "In sacrifices", that is, at the places of sacrifice. By वेदयत्सु (*vedayatsu*) स्तोत्रेषु निमित्तभूतेषु (*stotreṣu nimittabhūteṣu*) the derivation of the word विदथ (*vidatha*) is shown. This word is read among the names of sacrifice, यज्ञः वेनः (*yajñaḥ venaḥ*) and so on (Ni. 3-16). And,

होता देवो अमर्त्यः पुरस्तादेति मायया । विदथानि प्रचोदयन् ॥ (*hotā devo amartyaḥ purastādeti māyayā | vidathāni pracodayan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-27-7)

quoting this ṛk, the authors of the Nirukta show that the word विदथ (*vidatha*) is formed from the root विद् (*vid*), which gives the sense of knowledge. The opinions of the scholars who tried to give the exact meaning of this word and stated their own views were given in the earlier part (that is, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, part 11, pages 321 to 325, "ಸೇಜು" printed for the page). Max Muller has explained the word as "sacrificial assembly", Oldenberg as "the sacrifice prescribed by the śāstra", and Bloomfield as "house"; this was shown earlier by quoting their own sentences. Since the different meanings "house", "sacrifice" and "assembly" are all possible for this word, all fit according to the context; and it was also shown earlier, with examples, that all these meanings occur in Sāyaṇa's bhāṣya itself. In the present ṛk the meaning विदथेषु यागेषु (*vidatheṣu yāgeṣu*), "in sacrifices", is given.

देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*)—देवाः सर्वे पुत्रस्थानीया ययोस्ते द्यावापृथिव्यौ । (*devāḥ sarve putrasthānīyā yayoste dyāvāpṛthivyau |*) "Heaven and Earth, who have all the deities as sons."

### Printed p. 40

*Running head: 40 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 1 continue.)

इमौ वै लोकौ सहास्तां तौ व्यैतां नावर्षन्न समतपत्ते पंचजना न सम- (*imau vai lokau sahāstāṃ tau vyaitāṃ nāvarṣanna samatapatte paṃcajanā na sama-*)
जानत तौ देवाः समनयंस्तौ संयंतावेतं देवविवाहं व्यवहेतां । (*jānata tau devāḥ samanayaṃstau saṃyaṃtāvetaṃ devavivāhaṃ vyavahetāṃ |*)

(Ai. Brā. 4-27)

The conjugal relationship of Heaven and Earth described in this sentence, and the point that these two are the support of the activity of all the worlds, were shown earlier.

य इमे द्यावाप्पथिवी जनित्रि रूपैरपिंशद्भवनानि विश्वा । (*ya ime dyāvāppathivī janitri rūpairapiṃśadbhavanāni viśvā |*)
तमद्य होतरिषितो यजीयान् देवं त्वष्टारमिह यक्षि विद्वान् ॥ (*tamadya hotariṣito yajīyān devaṃ tvaṣṭāramiha yakṣi vidvān ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-9)

In this ṛk, for the word जनित्री (*janitrī*) it is said विश्वस्य जनयित्र्य । (*viśvasya janayitrya |*): they are the producers of all beings, gods, men and the rest. And,

देवी देवस्य रोदसी जनित्री बृहस्पतिं वावृधतुर्महित्वा । (*devī devasya rodasī janitrī bṛhaspatiṃ vāvṛdhaturmahitvā |*)
दक्षाय्याय दक्षता सखायः करद्ब्रह्मणे सुतरा सुगाधा ॥ (*dakṣāyyāya dakṣatā sakhāyaḥ karadbrahmaṇe sutarā sugādhā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-97-8)

here it is said that they nourished their own son Bṛhaspati; and

यं त्वा द्यावापृथिवी यं त्वापस्त्वष्टा यं त्वा सुजनिमा जजान । (*yaṃ tvā dyāvāpṛthivī yaṃ tvāpastvaṣṭā yaṃ tvā sujanimā jajāna |*)
पंथामनु प्रविद्वान्पि तृयाणं द्युमदग्ने समिधानो वि भाहि ॥ (*paṃthāmanu pravidvānpi tṛyāṇaṃ dyumadagne samidhāno vi bhāhi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-2-7)

here that they are the parents of Agni *[a second reference "(ಖ. ಸಂ. ೧೦-೨-೭)" is printed after it in the supplied text; "ಖ. ಸಂ." is not expanded]*. In some places they are said to be, individually, the producers of Agni and the other deities, and in still others the parents of all the deities. Therefore,

उपहूते द्यावापृथिवी पूर्वजे ऋतावरी देवी देवपुत्रे इति । (*upahūte dyāvāpṛthivī pūrvaje ṛtāvarī devī devaputre iti |*)

(Śa. Brā. 1-8-1-29)

in all such sentences the epithet देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*) is established for Heaven and Earth as there referred to *[printed "ದ್ಯಾವ್ಯಾಸೃಥಿಪಿಗಳಿಗೆ", as supplied]*.

देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—देवो दानाद्वा दीपनाद्वा । (*devo dānādvā dīpanādvā |*) Because it is said that this epithet is "giver" and "illuminator" (Ni. 7-5), and because the sacrificer makes the gods shine by gifts of oblations and the like, the word देव (*deva*) is sometimes explained as "sacrificer"; in the same way in the present ṛk too it is explained as यजमानैः (*yajamānaiḥ*).

सुदंससा (*sudaṃsasā*)—दंस इति कर्मनाम । शोभनकर्मोपेते । (*daṃsa iti karmanāma | śobhanakarmopete |*) "Endowed with excellent rites": it is shown that this is an epithet of Heaven and Earth.

इत्था (*itthā*)—Since it is read among the names of truth, बट् (*baṭ*), श्रत् (*śrat*) and so on (Ni. 3-13), in this ṛk too it is explained as "truly".

### Printed p. 41

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨.) Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 41 (the closing bracket is printed as a parenthesis, with tab spacing, as supplied).*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) (the substitution दिवो द्यावा (*divo dyāvā*) Pā. Sū. 6-3-29, the accent by देवताद्वंद्वे च (*devatādvaṃdve ca*) Pā. Sū. 6-2-141, with the Vedic break inside the compound), ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*) (root वृधु (*vṛdhu*), suffix *kvip*; सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 7-1-39; Pā. Sū. 6-2-49), स्तुषे (*stuṣe*) (थासः से (*thāsaḥ se*) Pā. Sū. 3-4-80), विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*), प्रचेतसा (*pracetasā*), देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*), सुवंससा (*suvaṃsasā*) *[so printed for ಸುದಂಸಸಾ; नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) Pā. Sū. 6-2-172]* and प्रभूषतः (*prabhūṣataḥ*) (तिजुचोदात्तवति (*tijucodāttavati*) Pā. Sū. 8-1-71), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧ ॥. A stray line follows, kept as a note in kn.md: "This image contains no text. It is a decorative graphic design consisting of swirls and floral elements."

### Printed p. 42

*Running head: 42 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

*[A hyphenated line break inside the Saṃhitā block ("ತದ್ಧವೀ-ನುಭಿಃ", "ವರೀ-ಮಭಿಃ") is joined in kn.md; the words are kept as supplied.]*

> उ॒त म॑न्ये पि॒तुर॒द्रुहो॒ मनो॑ मा॒तुर्मुहि॒ स्वत॑व॒स्तद्धवी॑नुभिः ।
> सु॒रेत॑सा पि॒तरा॒ भूम॑ चक्रतुरु॒रु प्र॒जाया॑ अ॒मृतं॒ वरी॑मभिः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕಡತ") and sits inside the Saṃhitā block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

> उत । मन्ये । पितुः । अद्रुहः । मनः । मातुः । महि । स्वतवः । तत् । हवीमभिः ।
> सुरेतसा । पितरा । भूम । चक्रतुः । उरु । प्रजायाः । अमृतं । वरीमभिः ॥२॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸ ಹರಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sits inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> उतापि च पितुः पितृस्थानीयायाः पात्रा अद्रुहोऽद्रोग्नुः । न हि कदाचित्पिता स्वपुत्रादये द्रुह्यति । तादृश्याः पृथिव्याः स्वतवः स्वायत्तं बलं स्वयमेवास्मदनुग्रहाय त्वरमाणं आत एव महि महत् तत्तादृशमनुग्रहयुक्तं मनश्चित्तं
> हवीमभिर्हवनैराह्वानैर्मन्ये । जानामि । प्रसादयामीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । तत्तादृशमुक्तलक्षणं मनो हवीमभिर्हवनैर्महि मन्ये । अतिमहत्पृथ्वं मन्ये । तथा
> मातुः सर्वस्योत्पादयितुरंतरिक्षस्य द्युलोकस्य । मातांतरिक्षं निर्मियंतेऽस्मिन्भूतानि ॥ नि. २-८ । इति निरुक्तं । यद्वा । पितुर्म्यलोकस्य मातुर्भूम्याः ।
> दौर्वः पिता पृथिवी माता । ऋ १- ९१-६ । इति श्रुतेः । तत्तादृशमुक्तलक्षणं मनो
> मन्ये । द्यावापृथिव्योर्मनोऽस्मदाह्वानैः प्रीणयामीत्यर्थः । किंच पितरा माता
> च पिता च पितरौ द्यावापृथिव्यौ सुरेतसा शोभनसामर्थ्येन प्रजाया यजमानादिरूपाया वरीमभिर्वरिष्यॆ रक्षणविशेषैर्भूम बहुतरमुरु विस्तीर्णममृतममररणभूतमभिलषितं चक्रतुः । कुरुतः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): by invocations I win the gracious, mighty minds of Earth and Heaven, our parents, who with ample protection give their children the wished-for undying wealth.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उत (*uta*)—and; अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*)—who never does harm; पितुः (*pituḥ*)—of the Earth, who stands in the place of a father; स्वतवः (*svatavaḥ*)—with strength under her own control; महि (*mahi*)—(therefore) great; तत् (*tat*)—famous, together with grace; मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind. (Likewise) मातुः (*mātuḥ*)—of the heaven-world, who is in the form of a mother, … *[the supplied text breaks off here in the middle of a word ("ದ್ಯುಲೋಕಾಧಿ"); the Pratipadārtha continues on p. 43, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 43

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨ .ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 43 (as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 2, broken off at the end of p. 42 in the word "ದ್ಯುಲೋಕಾಧಿ", continues: "…ದ್ಯುಲೋಕಾಧಿ|ದೇವತೆಯ", "…of the presiding deity of the heaven-world".)

(मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind.) हवीमभिः (*havīmabhiḥ*)—by hymns of praise accompanied by prayer; मन्ये (*manye*)—I know, that is, I make (it) satisfied. [Or: अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*)—who never does harm; पितुः (*pituḥ*)—of the presiding deity of the heaven-world, who is in the form of a father; स्वतवं (*svatavaṃ*) *[so printed]*—with strength under her own control; महि (*mahi*)—(therefore) great; तत् (*tat*)—famous, together with grace; मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind; (likewise) मातुः (*mātuḥ*)—of the Earth, who is in the form of a mother; मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind (endowed with the qualities stated above); हवीमभिः (*havīmabhiḥ*)—by hymns of praise accompanied by prayer; मन्ये (*manye*)—I know, that is, I satisfy.] पितरा (*pitarā*)—Heaven and Earth, the parents of the whole world; सुरेतसा (*suretasā*)—by their excellent capacity; प्रजायाः (*prajāyāḥ*)—of the people in the form of sacrificers (their desired objects); भूम (*bhūma*)—as very abundant; उरु (*uru*)—as extensive; अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—as free from death; वरीमभिः (*varīmabhiḥ*)—by their help of protection; चक्रतुः (*cakratuḥ*)—made.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

With hymns accompanied by prayer I honour and satisfy the mind, strong with its own power and therefore great and gracious, of the presiding deity of the heaven-world, who never does harm and is in the form of a father, and the mind of the Earth, in the form of a mother, who has the same qualities. Heaven and Earth, the parents of the whole world, by their excellent capacity make the desired objects of the people in the form of sacrificers abundant, extensive and eternal, by their help of protection.

**Printed English translation**

Verily I propitiate, by my invocations, the mind of the benevolent father, and the great and spontaneous (affection) of the mother (of all beings): The parents, with kindness, have secured, by their excellent protections, the vast and manifold immortality of their progeny.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk Heaven and Earth are designated by the word पितरा (*pitarā*) *[printed "ನಿರ್ದೇಶಿಸದೆ", which would read "does not designate"; the sense requires "designates"; kept as supplied]*. But from this ṛk it cannot be known exactly which is the father and which the mother. Sāyaṇa has explained it in two ways: पितुः पितृस्थानीयायाः पृथिव्याः (*pituḥ pitṛsthānīyāyāḥ pṛthivyāḥ*), मातुः (*mātuḥ*)—मातांतरिक्षं निर्मियंते अस्मिन् भूतानि (*mātāṃtarikṣaṃ nirmiyaṃte asmin bhūtāni*) (Ni. 2-8). Formed from the root पा (*pā*), which means "to protect", the earth, which protects all beings, is called "father"; and since all beings are formed in the atmosphere, the atmosphere is called "mother": thus, quoting the statement of the Nirukta authors, he has given the first meaning.

### Printed p. 44

*Running head: 44 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.*

This too is correct in one way from the point of view of derivation, but cannot accord with the traditional meaning. Ordinarily the sentences that designate the atmosphere as "father" and the earth as "mother" are the well-known ones.

तन्नो वातो मयोभु वातु भेषजं तन्माता पृथिवी तत् पिता द्यौः । (*tanno vāto mayobhu vātu bheṣajaṃ tanmātā pṛthivī tat pitā dyauḥ |*)
तद्गावाणः सोमसुतो मयोभुवस्तदश्विना शृणुतं धिष्ण्या युवं ॥ (*tadgāvāṇaḥ somasuto mayobhuvastadaśvinā śṛṇutaṃ dhiṣṇyā yuvaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-4)

Here the earth is designated "mother" and the atmosphere "father".

प्रैषः स्तोमः पृथिवीमंतरिक्षं वनस्पतीरोषधी राये अश्याः । (*praiṣaḥ stomaḥ pṛthivīmaṃtarikṣaṃ vanaspatīroṣadhī rāye aśyāḥ |*)
देवोदेवः सुहवो भूतु मह्यं मा नो माता पृथिवी मर्मत् धात् । (*devodevaḥ suhavo bhūtu mahyaṃ mā no mātā pṛthivī marmat dhāt |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-42-16)

उप द्यां स्कंभथुः स्कंभनेनाप्रथतं पृथिवीं मातरं वि । (*upa dyāṃ skaṃbhathuḥ skaṃbhanenāprathataṃ pṛthivīṃ mātaraṃ vi |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-72-2)

दौ ३ प्पितः पृथिवि मातरध्रुगन्ने भ्रातर्वसवो मृळता नः । (*dau 3 ppitaḥ pṛthivi mātaradhruganne bhrātarvasavo mṛḷatā naḥ |*)
विश्व आदित्या अदिते सजोषा अस्मभ्यं शर्म बहुलं वि यंत ॥ (*viśva ādityā adite sajoṣā asmabhyaṃ śarma bahulaṃ vi yaṃta ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-51-5)

In many such ṛks (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-90-7, 1-185-11, 6-20-6 and others) the atmosphere is addressed as "father" and the earth as "mother". Thus, not only in the sentences of the Saṃhitā but also in

द्याः पिता॥ पृथिवी माता (*dyāḥ pitā‖ pṛthivī mātā*) (Tai. Brā. 3-7-5-4-5)

and in Brāhmaṇa sentences such as

अथ यत्राहोपहूते द्यावापृथिवी इति । तदग्नीध आ दधाति तदग्नीत्पाश्ना- (*atha yatrāhopahūte dyāvāpṛthivī iti | tadagnīdha ā dadhāti tadagnītpāśnā-*)
त्युपहूता पृथिवी मातोप मां पृथिवी माता ह्वायतामग्निराग्निध्रात्सा- (*tyupahūtā pṛthivī mātopa māṃ pṛthivī mātā hvāyatāmagnirāgnidhrātsā-*)
होपहूता द्यौष्टितोप मां द्यौष्टिता । (*hopahūtā dyauṣṭitopa māṃ dyauṣṭitā |*)

and in Upaniṣad sentences such as मधु द्यौरस्तु नः पिता । (*madhu dyaurastu naḥ pitā |*) (Bṛ. U. 6-3-6), the epithet "father" is established for the presiding deity of the heaven-world; therefore in the present ṛk the meaning that Sāyaṇa gave the second time, with यद्वा (*yadvā*), is the proper one.

सुरेतसा (*suretasā*)—For this word he has given the meaning शोभनसामर्थ्येन (*śobhanasāmarthyena*). Although the meaning "capacity for procreation" is ordinarily well known for this word, the word is used in other senses too.

### Printed p. 45

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ಪ. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 45 (printed "ಪ. ೨." for the Varga; as supplied).*

The authors of the Nirukta have included this word among अर्णः (*arṇaḥ*), क्षोदः (*kṣodaḥ*) and the other names of water (Ni. 2-27, printed "ನಿ- ೨-೨೭").

दिवो न यस्य रेतसो दुघानाः पंथासो यंति शवसापरीताः । (*divo na yasya retaso dughānāḥ paṃthāso yaṃti śavasāparītāḥ |*)
तरद्वेषाः सासहिः पौंस्येभिर्मरुत्वान्नो भवत्विंद्र ऊती ॥ (*taradveṣāḥ sāsahiḥ pauṃsyebhirmarutvānno bhavatviṃdra ūtī ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-100-3)

In such ṛks the meaning is "rain-water".

कथा महे पुष्टिंभराय पूष्टे कद्रुद्राय सुमखाय हविर्दे । (*kathā mahe puṣṭiṃbharāya pūṣṭe kadrudrāya sumakhāya havirde |*)
कद्विष्णव उरुगायाय रेतो ब्रवः कदग्ने शरवे बृहत्यै ॥ (*kadviṣṇava urugāyāya reto bravaḥ kadagne śarave bṛhatyai ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-3-7)

Here Sāyaṇa has explained the word रेतस् (*retas*) as "sin that is the cause of destruction". The use of this word in the sense of "procreative semen", and the description of the extraordinary power that semen has, are given at length in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (5-6-8).

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses मन्ये (*manye*), अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*) (root द्रुह (*druha*), suffix *kvip* by सत्तूद्विष (*sattūdviṣa*) Pā. Sū. 3-2-61), महि (*mahi*), हवीमभिः (*havīmabhiḥ*) (suffix *manin*, Vedic augment; Pā. Sū. 6-1-34), सुरेतसा (*suretasā*) (accent by Pā. Sū. 6-2-119), पितरा (*pitarā*) (the residue by माता च पिता च पितरा पितामात्रा (*mātā ca pitā ca pitarā pitāmātrā*) Pā. Sū. 1-2-70; सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 3-1-39 for the dual) and चक्रतु (*cakratu*) *[printed "ಚಕ್ರತು:"]* (root डुकृञ् (*ḍukṛñ*), perfect dual), with the accents; some sūtra numbers are broken across lines in the supplied text and are kept as supplied. The grammar of Ṛk 2 may continue on p. 46.

### Printed p. 46

*Running head: 46 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.* (The grammar of Ṛk 2 continues.)

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses प्रजायाः (*prajāyāḥ*) (root जनी (*janī*), suffix *ḍa* with the prefix *pra*, by Pā. Sū. 3-2-99), अमृतम् (*amṛtam*) (accent by नञोजरमर (*nañojaramara*), Pā. Sū. 6-2-116, as printed) and वरीमभिः (*varīmabhiḥ*) (root वृञ् (*vṛñ*), Uṇādi suffix *manin*; a sūtra number broken across two lines), and ends with the mark ॥ ೨ ॥.

#### Ṛk 3

No letter was left unsettled.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ते सू॒नवः॒ स्वप॑सः सु॒दंस॑सो म॒ही ज॑ज्ञुर्मा॒तरा॑ पू॒र्वचि॑त्तये ।
> स्था॒तुश्च॑ स॒त्यं जग॑तश्च॒ धर्म॑णि पु॒त्रस्य॑ पाथः प॒दमद्व॑याविनः । ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ते । सूनवः । सुअपसः । सुदंससः । मही इति । जज्ञुः । मातरा । पूर्वचित्तये ।
> स्थातुः । च । सत्यं । जगतः । च । धर्मणि । पुत्रस्य । पाथः । पदं । अद्वयाविनः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label printed before the bhāṣya is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಾಯುಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sat inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the blocks are joined.]*

> ते प्रसिद्धाः सूनवो युवाभ्यामुत्पन्नाः पुत्रस्थानीयाः प्रजाः स्वपसः शोभनकर्माणः सुदंससः शोभनदर्शना युवां मही महत्यौ मातरा मातृवद्धितकारिण्यौ
> जज्नुः । जानंति । महानुभावतामवगच्छंति । किमर्थं । पूर्वचित्तये प्रथमबुद्धयेऽनुग्रहरूपाय्कॆ । किंच ते द्यावापृथिव्यॆ स्थातुः स्थावरस्य जगतो जंगमस्य
> पुत्रस्य पुत्रस्थानीयस्योभयविधस्य जगतोऽद्वयाविनो न विद्यते द्वयं यस्य
> तादृशस्य । स्वजीवनाय युष्मद्व्यतिरिक्तमजानत इत्यर्थः । ईदृशस्य धर्मणि धारणे
> रक्षणे निमित्तभूते सति पदं स्थानं सत्यमबाध्यं पाथः । सक्षथः दत्थ इत्यर्थः । यद्वा।

> *te prasiddhāḥ sūnavo yuvābhyāmutpannāḥ putrasthānīyāḥ prajāḥ svapasaḥ śobhanakarmāṇaḥ sudaṃsasaḥ śobhanadarśanā yuvāṃ mahī mahatyau mātarā mātṛvaddhitakāriṇyau*
> *jajnuḥ | jānaṃti | mahānubhāvatāmavagacchaṃti | kimarthaṃ | pūrvacittaye prathamabuddhaye 'nugraharūpāykè | kiṃca te dyāvāpṛthivyè sthātuḥ sthāvarasya jagato jaṃgamasya*
> *putrasya putrasthānīyasyobhayavidhasya jagato 'dvayāvino na vidyate dvayaṃ yasya*
> *tādṛśasya | svajīvanāya yuṣmadvyatiriktamajānata ityarthaḥ | īdṛśasya dharmaṇi dhāraṇe*
> *rakṣaṇe nimittabhūte sati padaṃ sthānaṃ satyamabādhyaṃ pāthaḥ | sakṣathaḥ dattha ityarthaḥ | yadvā|*

### Printed p. 47

*Running head: [ಅ.೨.ಆ. ೩ ವ. ೨ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 47 (as supplied).* (The bhāṣya continues from p. 46 after "ಯದ್ವಾ".)

> ते प्रसिद्धाः सुनव उत्पाद्यमानाः स्वपसः शोभनप्रकाशादिकर्माणः सुदंससः शोभनदर्शनाः सुषूपक्षयंतो वात्मानं स्वव्यापाराय ईदृशा रश्मयो मही महत्यौ
> द्याव्यापृथिव्य पूर्वचित्तये प्राणिनां पूर्वज्ञानाय जज्जुः । जातवंतः । द्यावापृथिव्योरंतराले व्याप्ता इत्यर्थः । किंच स्थातुर्जगतश्च धर्मणि धारणे तदर्थमद्वयाविनो मार्गद्वयरहितस्य ॥ बहुलं छंदसीति द्वयशब्दान्मत्त्वर्थीयो विनिः ।
> अन्येषामपि दृश्यत इति दीर्घः ॥ पुत्रस्य युष्मत्पुत्रस्थानीयस्यादित्यस्य पदं मार्गं
> सत्यं पाथः । अवश्यं रक्षथः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): your sacrificer-sons recognise you as great parents; you truly guard the path of your son Āditya, whose rays pervade heaven and earth.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ते (*te*)—well-known; स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*)—those with excellent rites; सुदंसनः (*sudaṃsanaḥ*) *[so printed]*—of excellent form; सूनवः (*sūnavaḥ*)—the sacrificers, in the position of sons; मही (*mahī*)—of great nature; मातरा (*mātarā*)—Heaven and Earth, beneficent like mothers; पूर्वचित्तये (*pūrvacittaye*)—for the first knowledge in the form of favour, (or) from former experiences (in which your favour was received); जज्जुः (*jajjuḥ*) *[so printed]*—know. (And you,) अद्वयाविनः (*advayāvinaḥ*)—who take in no second besides them, who trust wholly in them; स्थातुः (*sthātuḥ*) *[printed "ಸ್ಥಾಮಾತ್ಮಕವಾದುದೂ"]*—of the stationary; जगतश्च पुत्रस्य (*jagataśca putrasya*)—and of the people of the moving world; धर्मणि (*dharmaṇi*)—in protection; सत्यं (*satyaṃ*)—truly (without obstruction); पदं (*padaṃ*)—(their) place; पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)—you protect (you carry out the work of protection). [(Or) ते (*te*)—the world-famous *[printed "ಜಗತ್ಪ ಸಿದ್ಧವಾದವೂ"]*; स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*)—having excellent works such as illumination; सुदंसनः (*sudaṃsanaḥ*)—of excellent form; सूनवः (*sūnavaḥ*)—the born (rays of Āditya); मही (*mahī*)—Heaven and Earth, of great shape; पूर्वचित्तये (*pūrvacittaye*)—for the first knowledge (of the beings of the world); जज्जुः (*jajjuḥ*)—know, that is, pervade all of Heaven and Earth. And स्थातुः (*sthātuḥ*)—of the stationary; जगतश्च (*jagataśca*)—and of the moving world; धर्मणि (*dharmaṇi*)—in the work of upholding, or for the fulfilment of the duty of upholding; अद्वयाविनः (*advayāvinaḥ*)—who always follows one path, without two paths; पुत्रस्य (*putrasya*)—of your son, Āditya; पदं (*padaṃ*)—the path; सत्यं (*satyaṃ*)—truly; पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)—(you) protect.]

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The world-famous rays of Āditya, who have excellent works such as illumination, have an attractive form and are born from Āditya, pervade all of Heaven and Earth in order to awaken the world. O Heaven and Earth! For the fulfilment of the duty of upholding the world, stationary and moving, you truly protect the path of your son Āditya, who always follows a single path.

### Printed p. 48

*Running head: 48 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. "೧೩೯" (printed so for 159; spelled "ಸಾಯಣಭಾವ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ", "ಸು."). In the supplied text this head is merged into the last line of p. 47, and no page marker was supplied; the page break is inserted in kn.md and pages.json.*

**Printed English translation**

These, your children, the performers of good works, and of goodly appearance, recognize you as their great parents, through experience of former (kindness): preserve uninterrupted stability in the functions of your progeny, whether stationary or moving, (depending for existence) on none other than you.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The two kinds of meaning given to this ṛk have been shown in the Pratipadārtha. First: the sacrificers know the nature of Heaven and Earth, and Heaven and Earth protect all such people. Second: the rays of Āditya, pervading Heaven and Earth, give the sacrificers in the world prior knowledge of their duties, and Heaven and Earth protect the steady path of their son Āditya and make it favourable to the sacrificers' performance of rites. In the first meaning स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*), सुदंससः (*sudaṃsasaḥ*) and सूनव (*sūnava*) are epithets of the sacrificers.

स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*)—The word अपस् (*apas*) is read among the names of action: अपः (*apaḥ*), अप्पः (*appaḥ*) (Ni. 3-1). One meaning is "those with excellent rites such as sacrifice", another, "those with works such as illuminating the world (the rays of Āditya)", is possible.

सुदंससः (*sudaṃsasaḥ*)—The word दंस (*daṃsa*) too is read among the names of action. But since the word अपस् (*apas*) here denotes action, the word दंस (*daṃsa*) is explained as "form". Since the root is given as दसि दंशदर्शनयोः (*dasi daṃśadarśanayoḥ*), it can be explained as "good sight". Since this word is used more in the sense of sight than in the sense of action, it can be explained here as "having an attractive form".

उषस्तमश्यां यशसं सुवीरं दासप्रवर्गं रयिमश्वबुध्यं । (*uṣastamaśyāṃ yaśasaṃ suvīraṃ dāsapravargaṃ rayimaśvabudhyaṃ |*)
सुदंससा श्रवसा या विभासि पाजप्रसूता सुभगे बृहंतं ॥ (*sudaṃsasā śravasā yā vibhāsi pājaprasūtā subhage bṛhaṃtaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-8)

In this ṛk the word सुदंससा (*sudaṃsasā*) refers to Uṣas; it describes the form of Uṣas with its charming radiance. Likewise,

तं देवा बुद्धे रजसः सुदंससं दिवस्पृथिव्योररतिं नैरिरे । (*taṃ devā buddhe rajasaḥ sudaṃsasaṃ divaspṛthivyoraratiṃ nairire |*)
रथमिद वेद्यं शुक्रशोचिषमग्निं मित्रं न क्षितिषु प्रशंस्यं ॥ (*rathamida vedyaṃ śukraśociṣamagniṃ mitraṃ na kṣitiṣu praśaṃsyaṃ ‖*)

(ಜ. ಸಂ. 7-2-3) *[the printed abbreviation "ಜ. ಸಂ." is kept; probably Ṛ. Saṃ., not expanded]*

here also Sāyaṇa has explained the word सुदंससः (*sudaṃsasaḥ*) as शोभनदर्शनं (*śobhanadarśanaṃ*) *[the explanation runs over the page break]*

### Printed p. 49

*Running head: [೨ ೩. ೩ ವ. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 49 (as supplied; "೨ ೩. ೩" for the Aṣṭaka and Adhyāya).*

(of सुदंससः (*sudaṃsasaḥ*)): it describes the form of Agni. In the same way, in the present ṛk too the charming form of the rays of Āditya is described.

पूर्वचित्तये जग्मुः (*pūrvacittaye jagmuḥ*) *[so printed]*—for this (Sāyaṇa) explained प्राणिनां पूर्वज्ञानाय द्यावापृथिव्योरंत (*prāṇināṃ pūrvajñānāya dyāvāpṛthivyoraṃta*)राले व्याप्तः । (*rāle vyāptaḥ |*): "for the first knowledge of the beings, (the rays) pervaded the space between Heaven and Earth." The explanation tells that, in the morning itself, they entered Heaven and Earth for the awakening of the beings and for making known their duties; it tells the pervasion of the rays of Āditya.

आदित्यस्य रश्मय उभौ लोका गच्छंति । (*ādityasya raśmaya ubhau lokā gacchaṃti |*)

(ಭಾ. ಉ. 8-6-2) *[abbreviation "ಭಾ. ಉ." as printed, not expanded]*

As in such Upaniṣad sentences the pervasion of both Heaven and Earth by the rays of Āditya is described, so it is described here too.

पुत्रस्य (*putrasya*)—युष्मत्पुत्रस्थानीयस्य आदित्यस्य । (*yuṣmatputrasthānīyasya ādityasya |*) The meaning "of your son Āditya" fits better.

नमो मित्रस्य वरुणस्य चक्षसे महो देवाय तदृतं सपर्यत । (*namo mitrasya varuṇasya cakṣase maho devāya tadṛtaṃ saparyata |*)
दूरेदृशे देवजाताय केतवे दिवस्पुत्राय सूराय शंसत ॥ (*dūredṛśe devajātāya ketave divasputrāya sūrāya śaṃsata ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-37-1)

In such ṛks the description of the sun is indicated here. The order of the world-protection of this Āditya, who has no two paths, is weighty.

मह इत्यादित्यः । आदित्येन नाव सर्वे लोका महीयंते । (*maha ityādityaḥ | ādityena nāva sarve lokā mahīyaṃte |*)

(Tai. U. 1-5-2) *[printed "ತೈ. ಉ."]*

In sentences such as this, the growth and greatness of the worlds are described as possible only through the power of Āditya; this point is indicated here too, and Heaven and Earth are praised thus: "you protect the path of such an Āditya."

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada begins with स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*) (the accent of the compound by आद्युदात्तंद्व्यच् छंदसि (*ādyudāttaṃdvyac chaṃdasi*), a sūtra number broken across two lines) and says that the same compound accents occur in सुदंससः (*sudaṃsasaḥ*). A stray signature line at the foot of the page ("[ 4 ] Vol. 12") is kept in kn.md as a note.

*Running head: 50 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.* (The grammar of Ṛk 3 continues.)

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses जज्ञुः (*jajñuḥ*) (root ज्ञा (*jñā*), or the root जनी (*janī*); the verb accent), मातरा (*mātarā*) (सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 7-1-39), स्थातुः (*sthātuḥ*) (root ष्ठा (*ṣṭhā*), suffix *tṛc*; Pā. Sū. 6-1-111 and 8-2-24), धर्मणि (*dharmaṇi*) (root धृञ (*dhṛña*), suffix *manin*), पाथि (*pāthi*) *[so printed for ಪಾಥಃ]* (root पा (*pā*), present second person dual) and अद्वयाविनः (*advayāvinaḥ*) (suffix *vini* by the Vedic rule; Pā. Sū. 6-3-137 and 6-2-2), and ends with the mark ॥ ೩ ॥ and a stray ornament line ":੦:", kept as supplied.

> मिथुना समोकसा ।

> सुदीतयः ॥ ४ ॥

> *te māyino mamire supracetaso jāmī sayonī*
> *mithunā samokasā |*

### Printed p. 51

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ಪ. ೨] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 51 (as supplied; "ಪ. ೨" for the Varga).*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪದಹಾರಃ"), and stands as the first line of the Pada block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note. The Pada ends "॥ ೪ ii", the double danda printed as "ii", as supplied.]*

> ते । मायिनः । ममिरे । सुप्रचेतसः । जामी इति । सयोनी इति सयोनी ।
> मिथुना । संओकसा ।
> नव्यंनव्यं । तंतुं । आ । तन्नते । दिवि । समुद्रे । अंतरिति । कवयः । सुदीतयः ।
> ॥ ४ ii

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಾಯುಧಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sits inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined; a line end "ತದ್ವ." followed by "ಚನಃ" is joined as "ತದ್ವಚನಃ".]*

> ते प्रसिद्धे द्यावापृथिव्यौ मायिनः । माया प्रज्ञा । स्वविषयप्रज्ञावंतः
> प्रज्ञोपलक्षितकर्मवंतो ना सुप्रचेतसः सुष्टु प्रकर्षण चेतितुं शक्ता रश्मयो
> ममिरे । मिमते । प्रमाणेन परिछिंदंति । कीदृश्यौ ते । जामी परस्परं भगिनी ।
> एतत्कुत इति चेत् उच्यते । सयोनी समानोत्पत्तिस्थाने मिथुना प्राण्युत्पत्तये
> मिथुनभूते । परस्परमवियुक्ते इत्यर्थः । समोकसा समानस्थाने । द्युपृथिव्योरेकस्मिन्नेवावकाशरूप आकाशेऽवस्थानात् । समीचीनस्थाने वा । किंच कवयो
> मेधाविनः स्वव्यापारविषयज्ञानवंतः सुदीतयः सुष्ठुद्योतना रश्मयो दिवि
> द्योतमाने समुद्रेंतरिक्षेऽंतर्मध्यॆ । समुद्रशब्दोऽंतरिक्षनामसु पाठात्तद्वचनः । स चैवं यास्केन निरुत्तः । समुद्रः कस्मात्समुद्रवंत्यस्मादापः समभिद्रवंत्येनमापः संमोदंतेऽस्मिन्भूतानि समुदको भवति समुनत्तीति वा । नि. २-१० ।
> इति । नव्यं नव्यं तंतुं तननीयं विस्तारणीयं । जात्येकवचनं । रश्निना तन्नते ।
> समंताद्विस्तारयंति ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Heaven and Earth, sisters sharing one home, measure the wise rays; those bright rays stretch ever-new threads of light through the mid-space.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರತಿಪದ ರ್ಥ") and the Pratipadārtha sits inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जामी (*jāmī*)—sisters; सयोनी (*sayonī*)—having one place of origin; मिथुना (*mithunā*)—being together; समोकसा (*samokasā*)—having one dwelling-place in the form of space: ते (*te*)—those Heaven and Earth; मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*)—having (full) wisdom (in their own matter); सुप्रचेतसः (*supracetasaḥ*)—having an excellent power to give consciousness: the rays; ममिरे (*mamire*)—determine in the form of measure. कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—the wise, that is, those who know their duty well; सुदीतयः (*sudītayaḥ*)—shining excellently: the rays; दिवि (*divi*)—shining; समुद्रे अंतः (*samudre aṃtaḥ*)—in the middle part of the atmosphere; नव्यंनव्यं (*navyaṃnavyaṃ*)—exceedingly new; तंतुं (*taṃtuṃ*)—and spreading: their rays; आ तन्नते (*ā tannate*) *[so printed]*—spread all around.

### Printed p. 52

*Running head: 52 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Heaven and Earth, who are sisters, have one place of origin, are together and have one dwelling-place in the form of space, determine in the form of measure the rays, which have (full) wisdom in their own matter and an excellent power of giving consciousness. Those wise rays, that is, those that know their duty well and shine excellently, spread all around, in the middle part of the shining atmosphere, their exceedingly new and extending radiances.

**Printed English translation**

Those provident and intlligent [sic] sisters, (the rays of light), conceived of the same womb, (ever) united (with each other), and dwelling in the same abode, mete out (all things): knowing (their functions), and bright-shining, they spread in ever new (directions) throughout the radiant firmament.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

जामी (*jāmī*)—जामिरन्नेऽस्यां जनयंति जामपत्यं । (*jāmiranne 'syāṃ janayaṃti jāmapatyaṃ |*) जमतेर्वा स्याद्दतिकर्मणो (*jamatervā syāddatikarmaṇo*) निर्गमनप्राया भवति । (*nirgamanaprāyā bhavati |*) (Ni. 3-9). One explanation gives the idea that "the other makes children be born in her" and a derivation from the root जन् (*jan*), which means "to be born"; the other gives the idea "she who goes to her husband's house" and a derivation from the root जम् (*jam*), which means "to go". By both explanations it is understood, from the explanation of the authors of the Nirukta, that the word जामि (*jāmi*) means "sister". In support of this meaning,

न जामये तान्वो रिक्कमारैक्यकार गर्भः सनितुर्निधानं । (*na jāmaye tānvo rikkamāraikyakāra garbhaḥ saniturnidhānaṃ |*)
यदी मातरो जनयंत वह्निमन्यः कर्ता सुकृतोरन्य ऋंधन् ॥ (*yadī mātaro janayaṃta vahnimanyaḥ kartā sukṛtoranya ṛṃdhan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-21-2)

quoting this ṛk they have explained the word जामि (*jāmi*) as "sister". Likewise,

भगस्य स्वसा वरुणस्य जामिरुषः सूनृते प्रथमा जरस्व । (*bhagasya svasā varuṇasya jāmiruṣaḥ sūnṛte prathamā jarasva |*)
पश्चा स दध्या यो अघस्य धाता जयेम तं दक्षिणया रथेन ॥ (*paścā sa dadhyā yo aghasya dhātā jayema taṃ dakṣiṇayā rathena ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-123-5)

here too it is explained as "you who are in the position of a sister, like a sister to Varuṇa". This same meaning, extended, also becomes "kinsmen" for this word, as (was shown) earlier with examples… *[the supplied text of p. 52 breaks off here, at "ಹಿಂದೆ ಉದಾಹರಣೆಗಳೊಡನೆ", in the middle of a sentence; it continues on p. 53, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 53

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೨. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 53 (as supplied).* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue.)

*[The sentence broken off at the end of p. 52, "…ಹಿಂದೆ ಉದಾಹರಣೆಗಳೊಡನೆ", continues with "ತಿಳಿಸಿದೆ.": "…(as) was shown earlier with examples."]* In the present ṛk it is made clear by the epithets सयोनी (*sayonī*) and the rest that Heaven and Earth are sisters to each other.

सयोनी (*sayonī*)—समानोत्पत्तिस्थाने । (*samānotpattisthāne |*) It says "having one place of origin"; but this place of origin is not stated exactly. In other places, however, the origin of Heaven and Earth is spoken of:

जनिता दिपो जनिता पृथिव्याः पिबा सोमं मदाय कं शतक्रतो । (*janitā dipo janitā pṛthivyāḥ pibā somaṃ madāya kaṃ śatakrato |*)
यं ते भागमधारयन्विश्वाः सेहानः पृतना उरु ज्रयः समप्सुजिन्मरुत्वा (*yaṃ te bhāgamadhārayanviśvāḥ sehānaḥ pṛtanā uru jrayaḥ samapsujinmarutvā*)
इंद्र सत्पते ॥ (*iṃdra satpate ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-36-4)

in such ṛks Indra is said to be the creator of Heaven and Earth. But

मा नो हिंसीज्जनिता यः पृथिव्या यो दिवं सत्यधर्मा जजान । (*mā no hiṃsījjanitā yaḥ pṛthivyā yo divaṃ satyadharmā jajāna |*)
यश्चापश्चंद्रा बृहतीर्जजान कस्मै देवाय हविषा विधेम । (*yaścāpaścaṃdrā bṛhatīrjajāna kasmai devāya haviṣā vidhema |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-121-9)

here it is Hiraṇyagarbha, and

नाभ्या आसीदंतरिक्षं शीर्ष्णो द्यौः समवर्तत । (*nābhyā āsīdaṃtarikṣaṃ śīrṣṇo dyauḥ samavartata |*)
पद्भ्यां भूमिर्दिशः श्रोत्रात्तथा लोका अकल्पयन् । (*padbhyāṃ bhūmirdiśaḥ śrotrāttathā lokā akalpayan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-90-14)

in this ṛk the Puruṣa in the form of Virāṭ is said to have created Heaven and Earth. Thus, whenever the creation of the two is described, it is clearly stated that both have one origin; that is what this ṛk says with the epithet सयोनी (*sayonī*).

मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*)—माया प्रज्ञा । (*māyā prajñā |*) स्वविषयप्रज्ञावंतः । (*svaviṣayaprajñāvaṃtaḥ |*) It is explained as "having wisdom in the matter of their duty". मायेति प्रज्ञानाम । (*māyeti prajñānāma |*) The authors of the Nirukta have included माया (*māyā*), वयनं (*vayanaṃ*) and others among the names of wisdom (Ni. 3-13). Moreover,

मायामू तु यज्ञियानामेतामपो यत्तूर्णिश्चरति प्रजानन् । (*māyāmū tu yajñiyānāmetāmapo yattūrṇiścarati prajānan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-6)

quoting this ṛk and explaining स एव प्रज्ञां त्वेतां मन्यंते । (*sa eva prajñāṃ tvetāṃ manyaṃte |*) they have shown that the word माया (*māyā*) means "wisdom" (Ni. 7-27). And

### Printed p. 54

*Running head: 54 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೯").*

विश्वा हि माया अवसि स्वधावो भद्रा ते पूषन्निह रातिरस्तु । (*viśvā hi māyā avasi svadhāvo bhadrā te pūṣanniha rātirastu |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-58-1)

quoting this ṛk also (Ni. 12-17), they explained it as "wisdom" here too. Therefore in the present ṛk मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*) is an epithet of the rays, meaning "having wisdom".

समुद्रे अंतः (*samudre aṃtaḥ*)—समुद्रशब्दोऽंतरिक्षनामसु पाठात्तद्वचनः स चैवं (*samudraśabdo 'ṃtarikṣanāmasu pāṭhāttadvacanaḥ sa caivaṃ*) यास्केन निरुक्तः । (*yāskena niruktaḥ |*) Having said that the authors of the Nirukta have included the word समुद्र (*samudra*) among the names of the atmosphere, Sāyaṇa quoted the Nirukta sentence and also gave the derivation of the word समुद्र (*samudra*) *[a line-end "ಸಂಮೋದಂತೇs-ಸ್ಮಿನ್" in the supplied quotation, with a Latin "s" for the avagraha, is joined and written with ಽ in kn.md]*:

समुद्रः कस्मात्समुद्रवंत्यस्मादापः समभिद्रवंत्येनमापः संमोदंतेऽस्मिन् भूतानि समुदको भवति समुनत्तीति ना । (*samudraḥ kasmātsamudravaṃtyasmādāpaḥ samabhidravaṃtyenamāpaḥ saṃmodaṃte 'smin bhūtāni samudako bhavati samunattīti nā |*)

(Ni. 9-11) *[reference as printed here; the same derivation is cited below as Ni. 2-10]*

The word is formed: from the root द्रु (*dru*), which means "to move", together with the two prefixes सं (*saṃ*) and उद् (*ud*), which gives the sense "they flow upward, overflowing"; or, secondly, from the same root द्रु (*dru*) together with the prefixes सम् (*sam*) and अभि (*abhi*), giving the sense that the waters (of the rivers) flow toward it; or from the root मुद् (*mud*), which means "to rejoice", with the prefix सम् (*sam*), giving the sense that the aquatic creatures rejoice in it; or because it is the store (*nidhi*) of water; or from the root उद् (*ud*), which means "to be wet", with the prefix सम् (*sam*), giving the sense "to wet": thus the authors of the Nirukta have shown the derivation of the word समुद्र (*samudra*) (Ni. 2-10).

Thereafter, to show the difference between the earthly *samudra* and the *samudra* that is a synonym of the atmosphere, regarding those who interpret without knowing the difference and so give room for doubt, (the Nirukta says):

तत्र समुद्र इत्येतत्पार्थिवेन समुद्रेण संदिह्यते । (*tatra samudra ityetatpārthivena samudreṇa saṃdihyate |*)

Many people mistake the atmospheric *samudra* for the earthly one and interpret accordingly; that is not right; and in this context, to show the difference between the atmospheric *samudra* and the earthly *samudra*, they have also quoted a story:

तयोर्विभागस्तत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । देवापिश्चार्ष्टिषेणः शंतनुश्च कौरन्यौ (*tayorvibhāgastatretihāsamācakṣate | devāpiścārṣṭiṣeṇaḥ śaṃtanuśca kauranyau*)
भ्रातरा बभूवतुः । स शंतनुः कनीयानभिषेचयांचक्रे । देवापिस्तपःप्रतिपेदे । ततः शंतनो राज्ये द्वादशवर्षाणि देवो न ववर्ष । तमू- (*bhrātarā babhūvatuḥ | sa śaṃtanuḥ kanīyānabhiṣecayāṃcakre | devāpistapaḥpratipede | tataḥ śaṃtano rājye dvādaśavarṣāṇi devo na vavarṣa | tamū-*)
*[the word "ತಮೂ-" is broken across the page break; the quotation continues on p. 55]*

### Printed p. 55

*Running head: [ಅ ೩. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 55 (as supplied; "ಅ ೩." for the Aṣṭaka).*

चुब्राह्मणा अधर्मस्त्वयाचरितो ज्येष्ठं भ्रातरमंतरित्याभिषेचितं (*cubrāhmaṇā adharmastvayācarito jyeṣṭhaṃ bhrātaramaṃtarityābhiṣecitaṃ*)
तस्मात्ते देवो न वर्षतीति । स शंतनुर्देवापिं शिशिक्ष राज्येन । तमुवाच (*tasmātte devo na varṣatīti | sa śaṃtanurdevāpiṃ śiśikṣa rājyena | tamuvāca*)
देवापिः पुरोहितस्तेऽसानि याजयानि च त्वेति तस्यैद्वर्षकामसक्त तस्यैषा (*devāpiḥ purohitaste 'sāni yājayāni ca tveti tasyaidvarṣakāmasakta tasyaiṣā*)
भवति । (*bhavati |*)

Devāpi and Śantanu were sons of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, of the Kuru line; Śantanu, the younger of the two, had himself consecrated and became king of that kingdom. Devāpi went to the forest to practise austerities. From then on, for twelve years there was no rain in Śantanu's kingdom. Then the brahmins said to Śantanu: "By sending away your elder brother to the forest and becoming king yourself, you have done a sinful act; therefore the rain god has not rained in your kingdom." When Śantanu then tried to have Devāpi consecrated king, Devāpi said: "I shall be your priest and perform the sacrifice for you", and had the sacrifice performed for the sake of rain. Then rain fell from the upper *samudra* into the lower *samudra*: showing thus the difference between the two seas,

आर्ष्टिषेणो होत्रमृषिर्निषीवन् देवापिर्देवसुमतिं चिकित्वान् । (*ārṣṭiṣeṇo hotramṛṣirniṣīvan devāpirdevasumatiṃ cikitvān |*)
स उत्तरस्मादधरं समुद्रमपो दिव्या असृजद्वर्ष्या अभि ॥ (*sa uttarasmādadharaṃ samudramapo divyā asṛjadvarṣyā abhi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-98-5)

quoting this ṛk, they have shown the difference between the upper sea and the lower sea that are described here. Here "upper sea" means the atmosphere and "lower sea" means the earthly sea. Thus the meaning of this word has to be given according to the context. But

यथा वातो यथा वनं यथा समुद्र विजति । (*yathā vāto yathā vanaṃ yathā samudra vijati |*)
एवा त्वं दशमास्य सहावेहि जरायुणा ॥ (*evā tvaṃ daśamāsya sahāvehi jarāyuṇā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-78-8)

in such ṛks it is hard to say whether it is the atmospheric synonym or the earthly sea.

या आपो दिव्या उत वा स्रवंति खनित्रिमा उत वा याः स्वयंजाः । (*yā āpo divyā uta vā sravaṃti khanitrimā uta vā yāḥ svayaṃjāḥ |*)
समुद्रार्था याः शुचयः पावकास्ता आपो देवीरिह मामवंतु ॥ (*samudrārthā yāḥ śucayaḥ pāvakāstā āpo devīriha māmavaṃtu ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-49-2)

In many such ṛks it can be said definitely that it is the earthly sea. In some places this word is used as an adjective and gives the sense "flowing".

### Printed p. 56

*Running head: 56 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159 (printed with a full stop after "೧೫೯").* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue.)

अद्भिर्याति वरुणः समुद्रैर्युष्मा इच्छंतः शवसो नपातः । (*adbhiryāti varuṇaḥ samudrairyuṣmā icchaṃtaḥ śavaso napātaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-161-14)

In this ṛk समुद्रैः समुंदनस्वभावैः अद्भिः (*samudraiḥ samuṃdanasvabhāvaiḥ adbhiḥ*) *[so printed]* means "with waters whose nature is to flow". In the present ṛk the meaning is "atmosphere"; it tells of the pervasion of the rays of Āditya throughout the interior region of the atmosphere.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses मायिनः (*māyinaḥ*) (suffix *ini* by the sūtra on व्रीह्यादिभ्यश्च (*vrīhyādibhyaśca*), Pā. Sū. 5-2-116), ममिरे (*mamire*) (root माज् (*māj*), perfect in a present sense), सयोनी (*sayonī*) (the substitution of *sa* for *samāna*; a sūtra number broken across lines), मिथुना (*mithunā*) (suffix *unan*, Uṇ. Sū. 335), समोकसा (*samokasā*) (the root उच (*uca*); the rule is cited as "ಕಾ. ೭-೩-೬೪", as printed), नव्यंनव्यम् (*navyaṃnavyam*) (doubling, Pā. Sū. 8-1-4), तन्वते (*tanvate*) and सुदीतयः (*sudītayaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೪ ॥ and a stray ornament line ":C:", kept as supplied.

### Printed p. 57

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೨ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 57 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

*[A hyphenated line break "ಮನಾ-ಮಹೇ" in the Saṃhitā is joined in kn.md.]*

> तद्राधो॑ अ॒द्य स॑वि॒तुर्वरे॑ण्यं व॒यं दे॒वस्य॑ प्रस॒वे म॑नामहे ।
> अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ द्यावापृथिवी सुचे॒तुना॑ र॒यिं ध॑त्तं॒ वसु॑मंतं॒॑
> शतग्विनं ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तत् । राधः । अद्य । सवितुः । वरेण्यं । वयं । देवस्य । प्रसवे । मनामहे ।
> अस्मभ्यं । द्यावापृथिवी इति । सुचेतुना । रयिं । धत्तं । वसुमंतं । श तग्विनं ॥५॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> वयं यजमानाः सवितुः सर्वस्य प्रसवितुर्देवस्यादित्यस्यांतर्यामिरूपिणः प्रसवेऽनुज्ञायां सत्यां तत्तादृशं वरेण्यं वरणीयं राधः पुरुषार्थसाधनसमर्थं गवादि धनं मनामहे । याचामहे ॥ मन्यतिर्याज्ञाकर्मा ।
> छांदसः शप् ॥ यद्वा । पुनःपुनरभ्यस्यामः । लप्सीमहीति यावत् ॥ म्या अभ्यासे ।
> व्यत्ययेनात्मनेपदं । शपि पाघ्रादिना मनादेशः ॥ किंचास्मभ्यं द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ सुचेतुना शोभनचेतनेनास्मदनुग्रहबुद्ध्या रयिं धनं धत्तं । दत्तं ।
> कीदृशं । वसुमंतं निवासयोग्यगृहादिविशिष्टं शतग्विनं शतसंख्याकगवादिविशिष्टं ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): we sacrificers, under Savitṛ's impulse, ask desirable wealth; may Heaven and Earth, with gracious mind, give us wealth with dwellings and a hundred cows.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we, the sacrificers; सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—of the one who showers all desired objects; देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the venerable Āditya; प्रसवे (*prasave*)—when his favour is there; वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*)—exceedingly desired; राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—wealth in the form of cows and the like; मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—we ask, or (from him) obtain at every step; (and) अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us; द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—Heaven and Earth; सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*)—with a mind kind towards us; वसुमंतं (*vasumaṃtaṃ*)—endowed with dwelling-places such as houses; शतग्विनं (*śatagvinaṃ*)—endowed with hundreds of cows; रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth; धत्तं (*dhattaṃ*)—may (they) give.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

*[The Bhāvārtha runs over the page break, "ಪದೇ | ಪದೇ"; given here in full.]*

With the favour of Āditya, who provides all desired objects and is of divine nature, we ask for the wealth in the form of cows and the like that we exceedingly desire, or (we) obtain it from him at every step. And may Heaven and Earth, with a mind kind towards us, give wealth endowed with dwelling-places such as houses and endowed with hundreds of cows.

### Printed p. 58

*Running head: 58 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 159 (printed "ಸೂ ೧೫೯.").*

**Printed English translation**

We solicit to-day of the divine sun, his favour being propitiated, that wealth which is desirable, Benignant Heaven and Earth, bestow upon us rieches [sic], (consisting of) habitations and hundreds (of cattle and the like).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—सविता सर्वस्य प्रसविता । (*savitā sarvasya prasavitā |*) "The one who impels all": the nature of Savitṛ stated thus was explained earlier.

राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—It is read among the names of wealth, मघं (*maghaṃ*), रेक्णः (*rekṇaḥ*) and so on (Ni. 3-9). राध इति (*rādha iti*) धननाम । राध्नु वंत्यनेन । (*dhananāma | rādhnu vaṃtyanena |*) Sāyaṇa has shown the derivation of this word: wealth is well known by the name राधः (*rādhaḥ*) because by it people win over or appease.

मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—मन्यतिः याज्ञाकर्म । (*manyatiḥ yājñākarma |*) *[so printed]* याचामहे (*yācāmahe*), "we ask": this is one meaning; and यद्वा पुनःपुनरभ्यास्यामः । (*yadvā punaḥpunarabhyāsyāmaḥ |*) लप्सीमहीति यावत् । (*lapsīmahīti yāvat |*) "or at every step we obtain": this is the second meaning which he has given. The purport is either "having obtained the favour of Savitṛ we ask wealth of him", or "we obtain wealth from him again and again".

शतग्निनं (*śatagninaṃ*) *[so printed for ಶತಗ್ವಿನಂ]*—शतसंख्याकगवादिविशिष्टं । (*śatasaṃkhyākagavādiviśiṣṭaṃ |*) "Having cows in the hundreds": an epithet of the word रयि (*rayi*).

शनैश्चिध्यंतो अद्रिपोऽश्वावंतः शतग्विनः । विरक्षणा अनेहसः । (*śanaiścidhyaṃto adripo 'śvāvaṃtaḥ śatagvinaḥ | virakṣaṇā anehasaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-45-11)

In such ṛks, for the word शतग्विनः (*śatagvinaḥ*) only "possessing much wealth" has been given as the meaning.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada begins with राधः (*rādhaḥ*) (root राध (*rādha*), Uṇādi suffix *asun*) and सवितुः (*savituḥ*) (root षु (*ṣu*), suffix *tṛc*; genitive singular), with the accents; the analysis continues on the next page.

### Printed p. 59

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೩. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 59 (printed "ವ. ೩." although Ṛk 5 of Sūkta 159 is still in Varga 2; as supplied).* (The grammar of Ṛk 5 continues.)

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada continues with प्रसवे (*prasave*) (suffix *ac*, compound accent), मनामहे (*manāmahe*) (root मन (*mana*) used in the sense of "asking"; the lengthening by अतो दीर्घो यञि (*ato dīrgho yañi*), a sūtra number broken across two lines; and, in the second explanation, the root म्या (*myā*)), सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*) (नञ् सुभ्याम् (*nañ subhyām*) Pā. Sū. 6-2-70, as printed), धत्तम् (*dhattam*) (root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*), imperative second dual) and शतग्विनम् (*śatagvinam*) (suffix *ini* in a possessive compound), and ends with the mark ॥ ೫ ॥.

*Here the "ಗೀರ್ನೇ" sūkta is ended: "ಇಲ್ಲಿಗೆ ಗೀರ್ನೇ ಸೂಕ್ತವು ಮುಗಿದುದು." (printed so; the 159th sūkta is meant).*

## Sūkta 160 (Maṇḍala 1)

*"ನೂರ ಅರವತ್ತನೆಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು" — "the hundred and sixtieth sūkta".*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; the sense is in the Anuvāda below):

> तेहीति पंचर्चं चतुर्थं सूक्तं दैर्घतमसं जागतं द्यावापृथिव्यं ।
> ते हीत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ चतुर्विंशेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे द्यावापृथिव्यनिविद्धानमेतत्तूक्तं
> ते हि द्यावापृथिवी यज्ञस्य वो रथ्यमिति वैश्वदेवं । आ. ७-४ । इति सूत्रितत्वात् ॥
> तथा पृष्ठ्याभिप्लवषडहयोर्द्वितीयेऽहनि वैश्वदेवे द्यावापृथिव्यं निविद्धानीयं
> चातुर्विंशिकं तृतीयसवनं । आ. ७-६ । इत्याभिप्लविके द्वितीयेऽहन्यतिदिष्टत्वात् ।
> आभिप्लाविकाद्वितीयादह्नः पृष्यस्य द्वितीयेऽहन्येतत्तूक्तमतिदेशतः प्राप्तं
> पृष्यस्याभिप्लवेनोक्ते अहनी आद्ये आद्याभ्यां । आ. ७-१० । इति सूत्रितत्वात् ॥
> प्रथमाश्विनशस्त्रे विनियुक्ता । संस्थितेश्वाश्विनायेत्यत्र सूत्रितं । ते हि द्यावापृथिवी
> विश्वशंभुवा विश्यस्य देवीमृचयस्य । आ. ६-५ । इति ॥

> *tehīti paṃcarcaṃ caturthaṃ sūktaṃ dairghatamasaṃ jāgataṃ dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ |*
> *te hītyanukrāṃtaṃ ‖ caturviṃśe 'hani vaiśvadevaśastre dyāvāpṛthivyanividdhānametattūktaṃ*
> *te hi dyāvāpṛthivī yajñasya vo rathyamiti vaiśvadevaṃ | ā. 7-4 | iti sūtritatvāt ‖*
> *tathā pṛṣṭhyābhiplavaṣaḍahayordvitīye 'hani vaiśvadeve dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ nividdhānīyaṃ*
> *cāturviṃśikaṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ | ā. 7-6 | ityābhiplavike dvitīye 'hanyatidiṣṭatvāt |*
> *ābhiplāvikādvitīyādahnaḥ pṛṣyasya dvitīye 'hanyetattūktamatideśataḥ prāptaṃ*
> *pṛṣyasyābhiplavenokte ahanī ādye ādyābhyāṃ | ā. 7-10 | iti sūtritatvāt ‖*
> *prathamāśvinaśastre viniyuktā | saṃsthiteśvāśvināyetyatra sūtritaṃ | te hi dyāvāpṛthivī*
> *viśvaśaṃbhuvā viśyasya devīmṛcayasya | ā. 6-5 | iti ‖*

### Printed p. 60

*Running head: 60 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning "ತೇ ಹೀ ದ್ಯಾವಾಪೃಥಿವೀ" *[so printed, "ಹೀ"]* is the fourth sūkta in the twenty-second anuvāka. It has five ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya (printed "ಉಚಥ್ಯ", as supplied). Heaven and Earth are the deity. The metre is *jagatī*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said to begin with ते हि (*te hi*). On the twenty-fourth day of the sacrifice, when the mantras of the Vaiśvadeva-śastra are recited, the application of this sūkta is for the recitation of the *nivid-dhāna* mantras related to Heaven and Earth, as explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra ते हि द्यावापृथिवी यज्ञस्य वो रथ्यमिति वैश्वदेवं (*te hi dyāvāpṛthivī yajñasya vo rathyamiti vaiśvadevaṃ*) (Ā. 7-4). In the sacrifices called *pṛṣṭha* and *abhiplavaṣaḍaha* (printed "ಪೃಷ್ಟ", "ಅಭಿಪ್ಲವಪಡಹ"), on the second day, for the *nivid-dhāna* mantras related to Heaven and Earth when the Vaiśvadeva-śastra mantras are recited, the application of this sūkta is indicated both by the sūtra चातुर्विंशिकं तृतीयसवनमित्याभिप्लविके (*cāturviṃśikaṃ tṛtīyasavanamityābhiplavike*) द्वितीयेऽहस्यतिदिष्टत्वात् (*dvitīye 'hasyatidiṣṭatvāt*) (Ā. 7-6) and by the sūtra अभिप्लविकाद्वितीयादह्नः (*abhiplavikādvitīyādahnaḥ*) पृष्टस्य द्वितीयेऽहन्येतत्तूक्तमतिदेशतः प्राप्तं पृष्ट स्याभिप्लवेनोक्ते अहनी (*pṛṣṭasya dvitīye 'hanyetattūktamatideśataḥ prāptaṃ pṛṣṭa syābhiplavenokte ahanī*) आद्ये आद्याभ्यां (*ādye ādyābhyāṃ*) (Ā. 7-10); and that the first ṛk of this sūkta is applied in the Āśvina-śastra mantras also is shown by the sūtra ते हि द्यावापृथिवी विश्वशंभुवा विश्वस्य देवीमृचयस्य (*te hi dyāvāpṛthivī viśvaśaṃbhuvā viśvasya devīmṛcayasya*) in the place संस्थितेष्णाश्विनाय (*saṃsthiteṣṇāśvināya*) (Ā. 6-5).

**Sūkta—160**

*[Om sign ("॥ ಓಂ ॥") printed above the sūkta number, left out here and in the Kannada-script file as a sign only.]*

Maṇḍala 1 ‖ Anuvāka 22 ‖ Sūkta 160 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 3 ‖ Varga 3 ‖
*[the line "೧ ಸೂಕ್ತದಲ್ಲಿರುವ ಮುಕ್ಸಂಖ್ಯೆ-೫" is printed so, with a stray "೧" and "ಮುಕ್ಸಂಖ್ಯೆ" for ಋಕ್ಸಂಖ್ಯೆ]* Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 5 ·
*[printed "ಮುನಿಃ" (muni) for ṛṣi]* Ṛṣi: Dīrghatamas Aucathya *[printed "ಔಚಧ್ಯಃ"]* · Devatā: Heaven and Earth *[printed "ದ್ಯಾವಾಪೃಥಿವ್ಯೈ"]* · Chandas: *jagatī*.

No letter was left unsettled.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ते हि द्यावा॑पृथि॒वी वि॒श्वशं॑भुव ऋ॒ताव॑री॒ रज॑सो धार॒यत्क॑वी ।
> सु॒जन्म॑नी धि॒षणे॑ अं॒तरी॑यते दे॒वो दे॒वी धर्म॑णा॒ सूर्यः॒ शुचिः॑ ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ते इति । हि । द्यावापृथिवी इति । विश्वशंभुवा । ऋतवरी इत्यृतवरी । रजसः ।
> धारयत्कवी इति धारयत्कवी ।
> सुजन्मनी इति सुजन्मनी । धिषणे इति । अंतः । ईयते । देवः । देवी इति । धर्मणा ।
> सूर्यः । शुचिः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label printed before the bhāṣya is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sat inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The bhāṣya of this Ṛk continues on p. 62, in the next window; its gist will be given with its end.]*

> ते हि ते खलु प्रसिद्धे द्यावापृथिवी अंतस्तयोरंतराले शुचिः शुद्धो
> विश्वस्य शोचयिता वा देवो दीप्यमानः सूर्यो धर्मणा प्रकाशोदक्कदानादिधारणेन युक्तः सन्नीयते । सर्वदा गच्छति ॥ ईज् गत् दैन्वादिकः ॥ तादृशं कर्म युवयोरनुग्रहादिति स्तुतिः । कीदृत्यौ ते । विश्वशंभुना । विश्वं सुखभावयित्य ययोस्ते
> विश्वशंभुना । विश्वस्य सुखयित्रावित्यर्थः । ऋतावरी ऋतवत्यौ ॥ ऋतशब्दाच्छंदसीवनिपाविति वनिप् । वनो र चेति जिब्रोफौ ॥ रजस उदकस्योदकोत्पत्तौ । उदकं
> रज उच्यत इति निरुक्तं । ४-१९ । धारयत्कवी अकृच्छ्रेण धारकं कवि ययोस्ते
> तादृश्यौ । उदकोत्पादनाया प्रयत्नवत्यावित्यर्थः । यद्वा । धारयत्कविर्मनीष्यादित्यो ययोस्ते तादृश्यौ । वृष्टु दकधारयत्सूर्योपेते इत्यर्थः । सुजन्मनी
> शोभनजन्मवत्यौ धिषणे धर्षणोपेते । स्वव्यापारेषु प्रगल्पे इत्यर्थः । देवी
> द्योतमाने । अत्र यद्यपि धिषणे इत्येतत् धिषणे रोदसी इति तन्नामसूक्तत्वाद्द्यावापृथिवीनाम तथापि द्यावापृथिवी इत्यस्य विद्यमानत्वाद्योगिकं द्रष्टव्यं ॥

> *te hi te khalu prasiddhe dyāvāpṛthivī aṃtastayoraṃtarāle śuciḥ śuddho*
> *viśvasya śocayitā vā devo dīpyamānaḥ sūryo dharmaṇā prakāśodakkadānādidhāraṇena yuktaḥ sannīyate | sarvadā gacchati ‖ īj gat dainvādikaḥ ‖ tādṛśaṃ karma yuvayoranugrahāditi stutiḥ | kīdṛtyau te | viśvaśaṃbhunā | viśvaṃ sukhabhāvayitya yayoste*
> *viśvaśaṃbhunā | viśvasya sukhayitrāvityarthaḥ | ṛtāvarī ṛtavatyau ‖ ṛtaśabdācchaṃdasīvanipāviti vanip | vano ra ceti jibrophau ‖ rajasa udakasyodakotpattau | udakaṃ*
> *raja ucyata iti niruktaṃ | 4-19 | dhārayatkavī akṛcchreṇa dhārakaṃ kavi yayoste*
> *tādṛśyau | udakotpādanāyā prayatnavatyāvityarthaḥ | yadvā | dhārayatkavirmanīṣyādityo yayoste tādṛśyau | vṛṣṭu dakadhārayatsūryopete ityarthaḥ | sujanmanī*
> *śobhanajanmavatyau dhiṣaṇe dharṣaṇopete | svavyāpāreṣu pragalpe ityarthaḥ | devī*
> *dyotamāne | atra yadyapi dhiṣaṇe ityetat dhiṣaṇe rodasī iti tannāmasūktatvāddyāvāpṛthivīnāma tathāpi dyāvāpṛthivī ityasya vidyamānatvādyogikaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ ‖*

### Printed p. 62

*Running head: 62 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 16 (printed "ಸೂ. ೧೬." for 160; the final digit is missing in the supplied text).* (The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 1 ended on p. 61.)

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 1 (my own short summary): radiant, pure Sūrya moves between Heaven and Earth, which give happiness, uphold water, are nobly born and skilled in their tasks.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ते हि द्यावापृथिवी (*te hi dyāvāpṛthivī*)—the well-known Heaven and Earth; विश्वशंभुवा (*viśvaśaṃbhuvā*)—givers of happiness to all the worlds; ऋतावरी (*ṛtāvarī*)—those who keep the rule of *ṛta*; रजसः (*rajasaḥ*)—water; धारयत्कवी (*dhārayatkavī*)—having the capacity to hold (it); सुजन्मनी (*sujanmanī*)—of excellent birth; धिषणे (*dhiṣaṇe*)—skilled in their duty; देवी (*devī*)—shining, or of divine nature (they are); अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—between these Heaven and Earth; शुचिः (*śuciḥ*)—pure-souled; देवः (*devaḥ*)—shining; सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—the Sun; धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)—endowed with works such as holding water and illuminating the world; ईयते (*īyate*)—moves.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The world-famous Heaven and Earth give happiness to all the worlds, keep the rule of *ṛta*, have the capacity to hold water, are skilled in their duty and are shining. In the interval between these Heaven and Earth, the Sun (printed "ಸೂರನು"), pure-souled and shining, moves, endowed with works such as holding water and illuminating the world.

**Printed English translation**

Those two, the divine Heaven and Earth. are the diffusers of happiness on all, encouragers of truth, able to sustain the water (of the rains), auspicious of birth, and energetic (in action) : in the interval between whom proceeds the pure and divine Sun for (the discharge of his) duties.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

रजसः धारयत्कवी (*rajasaḥ dhārayatkavī*)—those who have the power to hold water. Moreover, together with विश्वशंभुवा (*viśvaśaṃbhuvā*), those who, by gifts of water and the like, increase the wealth of food and give happiness to the whole world, the power and the generosity of Heaven and Earth are stated together. It is well known that the cooperation and the relation of Heaven and Earth are the cause both of the holding of the water in the form of rain and of its giving.

स यदि वृष्टिकामः स्यात् । एतेनैवाददीत संजानाथां द्यावापृथिवी इति यदा (*sa yadi vṛṣṭikāmaḥ syāt | etenaivādadīta saṃjānāthāṃ dyāvāpṛthivī iti yadā*)
वै द्यावापृथिवी संजानाथे अथ वर्षति तस्मादाह संजानाथां द्यावापृथिवी (*vai dyāvāpṛthivī saṃjānāthe atha varṣati tasmādāha saṃjānāthāṃ dyāvāpṛthivī*)
इति ॥ (*iti ‖*)

(Śa. Brā. 1-8-3-12)

Since rain arises from the union of Heaven and Earth, their harmony and cooperation are prayed for in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa.

### Printed p. 63

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೩ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 63 (as supplied).*

पृथिवी पूर्वरूपं द्यौरुत्तररूपं वृष्टिः संधिः पर्जन्यः संधाता । तदुतापि (*pṛthivī pūrvarūpaṃ dyauruttararūpaṃ vṛṣṭiḥ saṃdhiḥ parjanyaḥ saṃdhātā | tadutāpi*)
तत्रैतद्वलवदनुद्गळ्हन् संदधदहोरात्रे वर्षति द्यावापृथिव्यौ समधाता- (*tatraitadvalavadanudgaḷhan saṃdadhadahorātre varṣati dyāvāpṛthivyau samadhātā-*)
मित्युताप्र्याहुः ॥ (*mityutāpryāhuḥ ‖*)

(Aitareya Āraṇyaka 3-1-2)

In these Āraṇyaka mantras too it is said, as above, that from the junction of Heaven and Earth there is the falling of water in the form of rain, and that when, by the gathering of waters, rain falls, immediately everyone says that Heaven and Earth have met. And,

असौ हि त्रिष्टुप्तदेने सयुजौ करोति तस्मादिमे संभुंजातेऽनयोरनु (*asau hi triṣṭuptadene sayujau karoti tasmādime saṃbhuṃjāte 'nayoranu*)
संभोगमिमाः सर्वाः प्रजा अनु संभुंजते ॥ (*saṃbhogamimāḥ sarvāḥ prajā anu saṃbhuṃjate ‖*)

(Śa. Brā. 1-7-2-16)

after oblations and the like have been offered to these Heaven and Earth together, they pour rain, and food becomes available to all beings. [The rain is the food of the earth, and the food, Produced thereby, in its turn furnishes food for the sky in the form of oblations.] *[this bracketed English sentence stands in the Kannada text as supplied]* Thus it describes that they are the cause of the wealth of food that gives happiness to the whole world. They are not only increasers of the wealth of food; they are also *ṛtāvarīs*, who keep the rule in the functioning of the world.

In this context, showing the relation between the course of Sūra, the eye of the world, and the rule of *ṛta* of Heaven and Earth, it is described, शुचिः सूरः अंतः ईयते (*śuciḥ sūraḥ aṃtaḥ īyate*), that the shining Sūra moves between these Heaven and Earth. The revolution of Sūra, in accordance with the rule and between Heaven and Earth, is described in the same way in many places.

अहमपो अपिन्वमुक्षमाणा धारयं दिवं सदन ऋतस्य । (*ahamapo apinvamukṣamāṇā dhārayaṃ divaṃ sadana ṛtasya |*)
ऋतेन पुत्रो अदितेर्यतावोत त्रिधातु प्रथयद्वि भूम ॥ (*ṛtena putro aditeryatāvota tridhātu prathayadvi bhūma ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-42-4)

Here Varuṇa has declared that he established the atmosphere in the seat of *ṛta*.

कविर्नृचक्षा अभि सीमचष्ट ऋतस्य येना विघृते मदंती । (*kavirnṛcakṣā abhi sīmacaṣṭa ṛtasya yenā vighṛte madaṃtī |*)
नाना चक्राते सदनं यथा वेः समानेन क्रतुना संविदाने ॥ (*nānā cakrāte sadanaṃ yathā veḥ samānena kratunā saṃvidāne ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-54-6)

In this ṛk it is said that Sūrya, moving in the interval between Heaven and Earth, which rejoice in the womb of *ṛta*, looks upon them; and

### Printed p. 64

*Running head: 64 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160.*

परिक्षिता पितरा पूर्वजावरी ऋतस्य योना क्षयतः समोकसा । (*parikṣitā pitarā pūrvajāvarī ṛtasya yonā kṣayataḥ samokasā |*)
द्यावापृथिवी वरुणाय सव्रते धृतवत्पयो महिषाय पिन्वतः ॥ (*dyāvāpṛthivī varuṇāya savrate dhṛtavatpayo mahiṣāya pinvataḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-65-8)

in such ṛks it is also said that the womb of *ṛta* is the common dwelling-place of Heaven and Earth. All the points of these descriptions are indicated in the present ṛk too.

धिषणे (*dhiṣaṇe*)—It is read among the names of Heaven and Earth, स्वधे, पुरंधी (*svadhe, puraṃdhī*) and so on (Ni. 3-21). For this word, धिषणा वाक् धिषेर्दधात्यर्थे । (*dhiṣaṇā vāk dhiṣerdadhātyarthe |*) धीसादिनीति वा । (*dhīsādinīti vā |*) (printed "ನಿ ೮-೩"): it is said that it may be formed from the root धिष् (*dhiṣ*), which gives the sense "to hold"; धीसानिनीति वा (*dhīsāninīti vā*) *[so printed]*: "it extends the intelligence", that is, it explains the meaning clearly: such is the derivation of the authors of the Nirukta.

द्यावापृथिवी गच्छ स्वाहेति । प्रजापतिवै प्रजाः सृष्टा ता द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां (*dyāvāpṛthivī gaccha svāheti | prajāpativai prajāḥ sṛṣṭā tā dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ*)
परगृह्णात्ता इमा द्यानापृथिवीभ्यां परिगृहीताः । (*paragṛhṇāttā imā dyānāpṛthivībhyāṃ parigṛhītāḥ |*)

(ಶ. ಪ್ರಾ. 3-8-4-17) *[the abbreviation "ಶ. ಪ್ರಾ." is printed so, for Śa. Brā.; not expanded]*

Here it is said that Heaven and Earth seized all the beings created by Prajāpati and kept them under their own rule; so the name may be well known as formed from the root धिष् (*dhiṣ*), "to hold", indicating this sense.

रजसः (*rajasaḥ*)—For the word रजस् (*rajas*) the meaning "water" is given here. रजः । (*rajaḥ |*) रजो रजतेर्ज्योती (*rajo rajaterjyotī*) रज उच्यते उदकं रज उच्यते । लोका रजांस्युच्यंते । (*raja ucyate udakaṃ raja ucyate | lokā rajāṃsyucyaṃte |*) (Ni. 4-19): formed from the root रंज् (*raṃj*), which gives the sense "to shine", the word रजस् (*rajas*) means "light", "water" and "worlds". Moreover there is a statement of the Nirukta that it is also a synonym for blood and for the days: असृगहनी रजसी उच्येते (*asṛgahanī rajasī ucyete*). The authors of the Nirukta quote the ṛk

अज्जमुक्थैरहिं गृणीषे बुद्धे नदीनां रजःसु पीदन् । (*ajjamukthairahiṃ gṛṇīṣe buddhe nadīnāṃ rajaḥsu pīdan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-34-16)

and, as there रजस्सु (*rajassu*) is explained as "in the waters", the meaning "water" is given in the present ṛk too. The other meanings of the word रजस् (*rajas*) are given in their respective contexts.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*[The heading is garbled in the supplied text ("ವ್ಯಾಕರಣ, ಕ್ರಿಯಾ"); the standard heading is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

Sū. 6-2 104, as printed); the analysis continues on the next page.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सु॒धृष्ट॑मे वपु॒ष्ये॒३॒॑ ३ न रोद॑सी पि॒ता यत्सी॑म॒भि रू॒पैरवा॑सयत् ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सुदृष्टमे इति सुधृष्टमे । वपुष्ये ३ इति । न । रोदसी इति । पिता । यत् । सीं ।
> अभि । रूपैः । अवासयत् ॥ २ ॥
