*Running head: 134 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).* (Ṛk 12: the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya continues from the previous window.)

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (continued)**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> तात्या तात् तायमाने वृष्टुदके । यद्वा । तात्या तासु वर्षासु ॥ छांदसस्यप्रत्ययो दकारस्यात्वं च ॥ अथवा पितृविशेषणं । तात्या तत्कालीनौ पितरा जगतः
> पालकौ सूर्याचंद्रमसौ क्व स्विदासतुः । कुत्र खलु तिष्ठतः ॥ अस्तेर्लिटि व्यत्ययेन
> भूभावाभावः । यद्वा । असतेर्गत्यर्थस्य लिट्येतद्रूपं ॥ अहोरात्रविभागेन
> प्रवर्षयथेत्यर्थः । किंचेदृशान्नो युष्मान् यः कोऽपि वृष्टिप्रतिबंधकारी
> राक्षसादिः करस्कं । बाहुनामैतत् करस्न बाहू इत्युक्तत्वात् अत्र त्वकारांतं ।
> युष्मदीयं हस्तवाददे गृह्णाति प्रतिरोधं करोति तमशपत । नाशयथ । यश्च
> प्राब्रवीत् प्रकर्षेण वाचा निरुंधे । तस्मै प्रो प्रैवाब्रवीतन । प्रकर्षेण भर्तृनादिशब्दं कुरुथ । यद्वा । यो नूनं स्तौति तं प्रकर्षेण स्तुतेत्यर्थः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): as rays the Ṛbhus cloud the world and rain; where are the parents then? Curse whoever seizes your arms; roar at him who reviles you.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays) यत् (*yat*)—when; भुवना (*bhuvanā*)—the beings; संमाल्य (*saṃmālya*) *[so printed]*—having covered (with clouds); पर्यसर्तत (*paryasartata*) *[so printed]*—you move about or shed rain, (then); तात्या (*tātyā*)—at the time when the rain is falling; वः (*vaḥ*)—your *[the printed text has an unmatched closing bracket after "ಜಗತ್ತಿನ", "your and the world's"]*; पितरा (*pitarā*)—the parents, the sun and the moon; क्वस्वित् (*kvasvit*)—where indeed; आसतुः (*āsatuḥ*)—are; यः (*yaḥ*)—any enemy whatsoever; वः (*vaḥ*)—your; करस्नं (*karasnaṃ*)—the rays in the form of arms; आददे (*ādade*)—seizes and obstructs, (him); अशपत (*aśapata*)—curse and destroy; यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever; प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—reviles and disparages; तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to him; प्रो अब्रवीतन (*pro abravītana*)—make a sound that causes fear. [Or यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever; प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—praises you; तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to him; प्रो अब्रवीतन (*pro abravītana*)—make an encouraging sound.]

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays, when you cover the beings with clouds and shed rain, where indeed are, at that time, the sun and the moon, your protectors and the parents of the world? Curse and destroy any enemy who seizes and obstructs your rays in the form of arms. Make a frightening sound at him who disparages you with improper words.

**Printed English translation**

As you glide along enveloping the regions (in clouds); where, then, are the parents (of the world)? curse him who arrests your arm: reply sternly to him who speaks disrespectfully (to you).

### Printed p. 135

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೬. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 135 (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ.").*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

तात्या—तायमाने वृष्टुदके ; यद्वा तासु वर्षासु । (*tātyā—tāyamāne vṛṣṭudake ; yadvā tāsu varṣāsu |*) It is explained as: when the rain-water falls uninterruptedly *[printed "ಅನುಸ್ಕೃತವಾಗಿಯೂ"]* and in streams. Monier Williams, in his Sanskrit-English Dictionary, explains this word as "tātya", relating to the father, gives the meaning "Fatherly", and shows the present ṛk and the ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-37-6 as examples. But since the word "pitarā" occurs again in this ṛk, it is not proper to take "tātyā" as "relating to the father". The meaning given by Sāyaṇa is the correct one.

Sāyaṇa, in the ṛk

वासयसीव वेधसस्त्वं नः कदा न इंद्र वचसो बुबोधः । (*vāsayasīva vedhasastvaṃ naḥ kadā na iṃdra vacaso bubodhaḥ |*)
अस्तं तात्या धिया रयिं सुवीरं पृक्षो नो अर्वा न्युहीत वाजी ॥ (*astaṃ tātyā dhiyā rayiṃ suvīraṃ pṛkṣo no arvā nyuhīta vājī ‖*) (ಋ ಸಂ, 7-37-6) *[reference as printed, with a comma]*

explains the word "tātyā" with तनोतेरिदं रूपं संततया (धिया) । (*tanoteridaṃ rūpaṃ saṃtatayā (dhiyā) |*) as the passive use of the root तनु (विस्तारे) (*tanu (vistāre)*), which gives the meaning "extended" *[printed "ವಿಸ್ತ್ರತವಾಗಿರುವುದು"]*; as it is there taken to mean uninterrupted praise, so in the present ṛk too the meaning is "by rain that falls without a break", that is, "at the time of rain". Or, षितृविशेषणं । तात्या तत्कालीनौ (*ṣitṛviśeṣaṇaṃ | tātyā tatkālīnau*) पितरा । (*pitarā |*) *[so printed, "ಷಿತೃ" for "ಪಿತೃ"; the sentence is split over two lines]* "relating to that time", it becomes an epithet of the word "pitarā": thus Sāyaṇa has given this second alternative meaning also.

करस्नं (*karasnaṃ*)—the word "karasna" is included among the names of the arms (Ni. 3-8). The Nirukta authors, having quoted the ṛk

बृबदुक्कं हवामहे सृप्रकरस्नमूतये । साधु कृण्वंतमवसे ॥ (*bṛbadukkaṃ havāmahe sṛprakarasnamūtaye | sādhu kṛṇvaṃtamavase ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-32-10)

have explained the word "karasna" as कर्मणां प्रस्नातारौ । (*karmaṇāṃ prasnātārau |*) *[the supplied text has "ಕರಸ್ಯ ಶಬ್ದಕ್ಕೆ" and "ಪ್ರಸ್ನಾತಾರೌ"]* and have shown that, since the arms are the promoters of works, this name is well known for them. (6-17, as printed without an abbreviation.)

प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—for this word both meanings, "reviles" and "praises", are given. If the prefix "pra" is added to the root "brū" the meanings are "pronounce loudly", "make known", "proclaim". This same point the scholar Wilson, speaking of the special points of this ṛk, has explained in the words "pra prefixed to brū, may mean, either to speak harshly or kindly; to censure or to praise" *[the supplied text has the Kannada letters "ಪ್ರ" and "ಬ್ರೂ" inside the English sentence]*. Because both meanings are possible, Sāyaṇa also, when he says तस्मै प्रो अब्रवीतन (*tasmai pro abravītana*), has explained that the meaning is: make a sound so that the one who reviles you is afraid, or say words of favour so that the one who praises you is encouraged.

पितरा-जगतः पालकौ । (*pitarā-jagataḥ pālakau |*) the sun and the moon, the protectors of the world.

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): resting in the Sun's orb, the Ṛbhus ask who impels them to rain; the Sun answers: Vāyu; after the year they light the world again.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays; सुषुप्वांसः (*suṣupvāṃsaḥ*)—resting, having taken support in the orb of the Sun; तत् (*tat*)—this question; अपृच्छत (*apṛcchata*)—ask (it is this); अगोह्य (*agohya*)—O Sun, whom no one can hide; इदं (*idaṃ*)—to this work of raining; नः (*naḥ*)—us; कः (*kaḥ*)—who; अबूबुधत् (*abūbudhat*)—has impelled; बस्तः (*bastaḥ*)—the Sun, who covers (everyone); श्वानं (*śvānaṃ*)—Vāyu; बोधयितारं (*bodhayitāraṃ*)—as the impeller; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said; संवत्सरे (*saṃvatsare*) *[printed with a double hyphen]*—the year

### Printed p. 138

*Running head: 138 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha continues.)

having been completed; अद्य (*adya*)—now; इदं (*idaṃ*)—this world; व्यख्यत (*vyakhyata*)—you made shine in manifold ways (he also said).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays, resting with the orb of the Sun as your support, ask the question: "O Sun, whom no one can hide, who has impelled us to this work of raining?" Then the Sun, who covers everyone, said that Vāyu is the impeller. He also said: "Afterwards, when the year has been completed, you made this world shine in manifold ways."

**Printed English translation**

Ribhus, reposing in the solar orb, you inquire, who awakens us, unapprehensible (Sun), to tbis [sic] office (of sending rain). The sun replies-The awakener is the wind ; and the year (being ended), you again to-day light up this (world).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

It is a well-known matter that the rays of the sun draw up water and perform the work of raining in the form of clouds. On careful examination, that work cannot be completely carried out by the sun's rays alone. To make clear that other help is also necessary, it is explained here in the form of question and answer. It is described here figuratively that the Sun says, in answer to the question of the rays, that Vāyu, who pervades the whole atmosphere and holds lordship of the middle world, is the chief helper in this work of raining.

Among Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, it is clear that Vāyu is the presiding deity of the middle world. For the sun's rays, which travel through the atmosphere, the impulse of this presiding deity of the atmosphere is indispensable for the shedding of rain. Vāyu is the lord of all the activities that take place in this world.

अथ विश्वज्योतिषमुपदधाति । नायुर्वै मध्यमा विश्वज्योतिर्नायुर्हैवांतरिक्षलोके विश्वं ज्योतिर्नायुमेवैतदुपदधाति ॥ (*atha viśvajyotiṣamupadadhāti | nāyurvai madhyamā viśvajyotirnāyurhaivāṃtarikṣaloke viśvaṃ jyotirnāyumevaitadupadadhāti ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 8-3-2-1) *[abbreviation as printed; the hyphenated line break "ನಾಯುರ್ಹೈ-ವಾಂತರಿಕ್ಷ" is joined; "ನಾಯು" is printed for "ವಾಯು"]*

Of the three universal lights, Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, Vāyu is the universal light of the middle world, and he is the one who illumines the whole atmosphere and rules it.

### Printed p. 139

*Running head: [ಅ.೨.ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೬.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 139 (as supplied).*

वेत्थ चत्वारि महांति वेत्थ चत्वारि महतां महांतीत्यग्निर्महांस्तस्य महतो (*vettha catvāri mahāṃti vettha catvāri mahatāṃ mahāṃtītyagnirmahāṃstasya mahato*)
महदोषधयश्च वनस्पतयश्च तद्ध्यस्यान्नं वायुर्महांस्तस्य महतो (*mahadoṣadhayaśca vanaspatayaśca taddhyasyānnaṃ vāyurmahāṃstasya mahato*)
महदापस्तद्ध्यस्यान्नमादित्यो महान् .... .... .... ॥ (*mahadāpastaddhyasyānnamādityo mahān .... .... .... ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 10-3-4-4) *[the last line has printed dots]*

The great things of the world are four: Agni, Vāyu, Āditya and Puruṣa. Among these four great things, for Vāyu, who stands in the atmosphere, the great water is food.

अयं वै वर्षस्येष्टे योऽयं पवते सोऽयमेक इवैव पवते सोऽयं (*ayaṃ vai varṣasyeṣṭe yo 'yaṃ pavate so 'yameka ivaiva pavate so 'yaṃ*)
पुरुषेंतः प्रविष्टः प्राज्ञ प्रत्यज्ज तानिमौ प्राणोदानौ प्राणोदानौ (*puruṣeṃtaḥ praviṣṭaḥ prājña pratyajja tānimau prāṇodānau prāṇodānau*)
वै मित्रावरुणौ तद्य एव वर्षस्येष्टे स त्वा वृष्ट्यावत्त्येवृतदाह तमेतेनैवाददीत यदा ह्येव कदा च वृष्टिः समिव समनक्त्याहुतिमेवैतत्करोत्याहुतिर्भूत्वा देवलोकं गच्छादिति ॥ (*vai mitrāvaruṇau tadya eva varṣasyeṣṭe sa tvā vṛṣṭyāvattyevṛtadāha tametenaivādadīta yadā hyeva kadā ca vṛṣṭiḥ samiva samanaktyāhutimevaitatkarotyāhutirbhūtvā devalokaṃ gacchāditi ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 1-8-3-12) *[the hyphenated line breaks "ತಮೇತೇ-ನೈವಾ" and "ಕರೋ-ತ್ಯಾಹುತಿ" are joined]*

Vāyu, who has entered within the Puruṣa and moves in two parts as prāṇa and udāna, pervades the atmosphere as one alone and is the sole lord of all rain. By the offering of an oblation useful to him he is helped to shed rain. By that rain he protects everyone.

तद्वायव्यं भवति । अयं वै वायुर्योऽयं पवत एष ना इदग्ं सर्वं (*tadvāyavyaṃ bhavati | ayaṃ vai vāyuryo 'yaṃ pavata eṣa nā idagṃ sarvaṃ*)
प्रप्याययति यदिदं किंच वर्षति वृष्टादोषधयो जायंते.... .... .... (*prapyāyayati yadidaṃ kiṃca varṣati vṛṣṭādoṣadhayo jāyaṃte.... .... ....*)
तस्माद्वायव्यं भवति ॥ (*tasmādvāyavyaṃ bhavati ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 2-6.-3-7) *[reference as printed, with a stop; the middle line has printed dots]*

The falling of rain is by the impulse of Vāyu. It is by the help of Vāyu, who is the cause and the increaser of rain, that plants and the like grow. (Therefore the Śākamedha oblation is to be offered to Vāyu.)

Thus all the śruti sentences say that Vāyu, who is in the atmosphere, is the impeller of the falling of rain; this is made clear here too, and it is shown that the sun's rays are the instrumental causes.

श्वानं—अंतरिक्षे श्वसंतं वायुं । (*śvānaṃ—aṃtarikṣe śvasaṃtaṃ vāyuṃ |*) Vāyu has this name because he has the chief breath in the atmosphere (Ni. 7-29).

संवत्सरे अद्य इदं व्यख्यत (*saṃvatsare adya idaṃ vyakhyata*)—it means that after the rainy season has passed, in the hot season the sun's rays again light up the world free of clouds.

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> दि॒वा यां॑ति म॒रुतो॒ भूम्या॒ग्निर॒यं वातो॑ अं॒तरि॑क्षेण
> याति ।
> अ॒द्भिर्या॑ति॒ वरु॑णः समु॒द्रैर्यु॒ष्मा इ॒च्छंतः॑ शवसो
> नपातः ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[Variants between Saṃhitā and Pada kept as supplied: the Pada has अत्भिः (*atbhiḥ*), समुद्रॆः (*samudrèḥ*) and शव सः (*śava saḥ*) (with a space) where the Saṃhitā has "ಅದ್ಭಿಃ", "ಸಮುದ್ರೈಃ" and "ಶವಸೋ".]*

> दिवा । यांति । मरुतः। भूम्या । अग्निः । अयं । वातः । अंतरिक्षेण । याति ।
> अत्भिः । याति । वरुणः । समुद्रॆः । युष्मान् । इच्छंतः । शव सः । नपातः ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The supplied text has "ಆಗ." with a stop for the hyphen at one line end ("ಆಗ.-ಚ್ಛಂತಿ"), kept as supplied, and a stray "॥ ॥ ೬ ॥" at the end of the bhāṣya, kept as supplied. The Pratipadārtha stood inside this block after the garbled label "ಪ್ರಸಿಪದಾರ್ಥ".]*

> हे शवसो वृष्टिकार्यस्य बलस्य नपातो नपातयितार ऋभवः युष्मानिच्छंतो वृष्ट्यनुकूलचिकीर्षया युष्मानेवेच्छंतः सर्वॆ देवा आयांति ।
> कस्मादिति तदुच्यते । मरुतो वृष्ट्यनुकूला देवा दिवा द्युलोकाद्यांति । आग.
> च्छंति ॥ सावेकाच इति तृतीयानिभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥ भूम्या भूमेः सकाशादग्निर्याति । आगच्छति । अयं वातः सर्वदा संचारी वायुरंतरिक्षेण याति । अंतरिक्षादायाति । तथा वरुण आवरको जलाभिमानी देवः समुद्रॆः समुंदनस्वभावैरद्भिः ताभ्य आयाति । यद्वा । पूर्व० वृष्टिसाहाय्यायागता एते देवाः कृतकार्याः संतः पुनर्युष्मान्कामयमानाः स्वस्वदेशं प्रति यांतीत्यर्थः ॥ ॥ ६ ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Ṛbhus, sons of strength: desiring you, the Maruts come from the sky, Agni from the earth, Vāyu from the atmosphere, Varuṇa with waters; or they go home.]*

*[The label of the Pratipadārtha is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರಸಿಪದಾರ್ಥ"); the standard label is inserted, the garbled word kept in kn.md as an italic note, and the text moved out of the bhāṣya block.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

शवसः नपातः (*śavasaḥ napātaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, who urge on the power that takes the form of rain (or, O Ṛbhus, sons of strength); युष्मान् इच्छंतः (*yuṣmān icchaṃtaḥ*)—desiring you; मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts; दिवा (*divā*)—from the heavenly world; यांति (*yāṃti*)—come; अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni; भूम्या (*bhūmyā*)—from the earth (comes); अयं वातः (*ayaṃ vātaḥ*)—this Vāyu; अंतरिक्षेण (*aṃtarikṣeṇa*)—from the atmosphere; याति (*yāti*)—comes; वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuṇa; समुद्रॆः (*samudrèḥ*) *[so printed]*—with the waters (of the atmosphere) that have a flowing nature; अद्भिः (*adbhiḥ*)—with waters; याति (*yāti*)—comes.

### Printed p. 142

*Running head: 142 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ- ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, sons of strength, desiring you, the Maruts come from the heavenly world, Agni from the earth, Vāyu from the atmosphere, and Varuṇa also, with the waters that are in the atmosphere.

**Printed English translation**

Sons of Strength, the Maruts, desirous of your coming, advance from the sky : Agni comes (to meet you) from the earth : the wind traverses the firmanent [sic] ; and Varuna comes with undulating waters.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

For the words "yāti" and "yānti" Sāyaṇa has given one meaning by adding the prefix "ā" and another without it. According to the first meaning, the gods named in the ṛk come, to help the shedding of rain, from various directions, desiring the company of the ray. According to the second explanation, since, by the sentence संवत्सर इदमद्या व्यख्यत (*saṃvatsara idamadyā vyakhyata*) of the previous ṛk, the rainy season has ended, all the gods set out for their own appointed places, so as to help the shining of the ray.

श्वानं बस्तो बोधयितारमब्रवीत् । (*śvānaṃ basto bodhayitāramabravīt |*) In the previous ṛk the chief help of Mātariśvan was told. In the present ṛk, for the same work of raining, the collective help of the gods Maruts, Agni, Vāta and Varuṇa is indicated. To know the relation between the fall of rain and these gods, one must know the individual nature of these gods and their mutual relation.

Mātariśvan, Vāyu and Maruts: in the conventional sense now current, these names are well known as mere synonyms of Vāyu. But in the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā these names have different significations. Those significations are examined here only from the point of view of the fall of rain described in the present ṛk.

The Ṛbhus (the rays of the sun) are the means of the fall of rain. Mātariśvan is their helper. Both these divine functions are transformations of Agni in the form of lightning. Since the Ṛbhus are plainly said to be the rays of the sun, there is no reason for doubt there. But as to what the relation is between Mātariśvan, a transformation of Vāyu, and Agni, in some ṛks the identity of the two deities is stated.

### Printed p. 143

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೬. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 143 (as supplied).*

तनूनपादुच्यते गर्भ आसुरो नराशंसो भवति यद्विजायते । (*tanūnapāducyate garbha āsuro narāśaṃso bhavati yadvijāyate |*)
मातरिश्वा यदमिमीत मातरि वातस्य सर्गो अभवत्सरीमणि ॥ (*mātariśvā yadamimīta mātari vātasya sargo abhavatsarīmaṇi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-29-11)

When Agni is in the form of an embryo he is called "tanūnapāt". When he forms and makes manifest his radiance in the atmosphere he is called "Mātariśvan": thus Mātariśvan is said to be a transformation of the Agni that is in the atmosphere.

स मातरिश्वा पुरुवारपुष्टिर्विदद्गातुं तनयाय स्वर्वित् । (*sa mātariśvā puruvārapuṣṭirvidadgātuṃ tanayāya svarvit |*)
विशां गोपा जनिता रोदस्योर्देवा अग्निं धारयन्द्रविणोदां ॥ (*viśāṃ gopā janitā rodasyordevā agniṃ dhārayandraviṇodāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-4)

In this, Sāyaṇa explains मातरि सर्वस्य जगतः निर्मातरि अंतरिक्षे श्वसन् वर्तमानः । (*mātari sarvasya jagataḥ nirmātari aṃtarikṣe śvasan vartamānaḥ |*) as the Agni in the atmosphere, who is the maker of the whole world; and Skandasvāmin has explained मातरिश्वसंबंधादग्निरेव मातरिश्वेत्युच्यते (*mātariśvasaṃbaṃdhādagnireva mātariśvetyucyate*) *[the hyphenated line break "ಮಾತರಿ-ಶ್ವೇತ್ಯುಚ್ಯತೇ" is joined]* as: because of connection with Mātariśvan (Vāyu), that is, because Mātariśvan is the cause of the manifestation of Agni, Agni too has the name Mātariśvan.

It was explained earlier that the god Vāyu, who is in the atmosphere and, as explained in the Special Topics of the previous ṛk, is the presiding deity of the atmosphere, is also only a transformation of Agni. The changed state of the Agni in the form of lightning that causes rain is called by names such as Mātariśvan and Vāyu.

वरुणः समुद्रॆरद्भिर्याति (*varuṇaḥ samudrèradbhiryāti*)—in saying that Varuṇa comes with the waters that are in the atmosphere, the mutual relation of water and Varuṇa is also stated. "Samudra" here means the atmosphere. (The explanation of this has been given in great detail earlier.) The relation of Varuṇa to rain will be told later.

In the present ṛk it is explained that the gods beginning with Mātariśvan, joining with Agni in the form of the sun's rays, provide rain at the time of rain and, when that has ended and the year is complete, the shining sun's ray for the world.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses दिना (*dinā*) *[so printed, for "ದಿವಾ"]* (Pā. Sū. 6-1-168) and यांति (*yāṃti*) (root या (*yā*)); the analysis of Ṛk 14 is cut off in the supplied text at the end of this window and continues in the next window.

*Running head: 144 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied; the head already names Sūkta 162).* (The grammar of Ṛk 14 of Sūkta 161 continues.)

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā), continued**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses भूम्या (*bhūmyā*) (root भू (*bhū*); with the rule ಉ. ಸೂ. 485 printed without a stop), अद्भिः (*adbhiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171), इच्छंतः (*icchaṃtaḥ*) (root इष (*iṣa*); Pā. Sū. 8-3-9) and नपातः (*napātaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-75 and 8-1-19), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧೪ ॥.

Here the 161st Sūkta ended. *[Kannada closing line of the supplied text.]*

## Sūkta 162 (Maṇḍala 1)

*[The Kannada heading of the supplied text reads "ನೂರ ಅರವತ್ತೆರಡನೆಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು", "The one hundred and sixty-second Sūkta".]*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction**

*[The introduction stood as plain lines in the supplied text and is made a block; the hyphenated line break "ನ್ಯಾಯೇ-ನಾಶ್ವೋ" is joined.]*

> मा नो मित्र इति द्वाविंशत्यृचं षष्ठं सूक्तं दैर्घतमसं । तृतीयाषष्टौ
> जगत्यौ । शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः । अश्वस्य स्तूयमानत्वाद्या तेनोच्यते सा देवतेति न्यायेनाश्वो देवता । अत्रानुक्रमणिका । मा नो द्वैधिकाश्वस्तुतिस्तु तृतीयाषष्टौ
> जगत्याविति ॥ अश्वमेधस्य मध्यमेऽहन्यध्रिगुप्रैषेऽध्रिगो शमीध्वमिति शिष्टा
> षड्विंशतिरस्य वंक्रय इत्यस्मात्पुरस्ताद्वैतत्तूक्तमावपेत् । त्रीणि सुत्यानि भवंतीति
> खंडे सूत्रितं । अद्रिगो शमीध्वमिति शिष्टा षड्विंशतिरस्य वंक्रय इति वा मा नो
> मित्र इत्यावपेत । आ १०-८ । इति ॥

**Anuvāda (Kannada rendering of the introduction)**

This sūkta मा नो मित्रः (*mā no mitraḥ*) is the sixth sūkta in the twenty-second anuvāka. It has twenty-two ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya. The third and the sixth ṛks of this sūkta are in the jagatī metre, the remaining ṛks in the triṣṭubh metre. Since the horse is praised in this sūkta, the praise of the horse is the deity of this sūkta; for the subject that is set forth in a sūkta is said to be the deity of that sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: मा नो द्वैधिकाश्वस्तुतिस्तु तृतीयाषष्टौ (*mā no dvaidhikāśvastutistu tṛtīyāṣaṣṭau*)

### Printed p. 145

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೭ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 145 (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ.").*

जगत्याविति (*jagatyāviti*). That this sūkta is to be used for the stomāvāpa (insertion of the stoma) on the middle day of the Aśvamedha sacrifice, at the time of the adhrigupraiṣa, is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra in the section that begins त्रीणि सुत्यानि (*trīṇi sutyāni*) भवंति (*bhavaṃti*), अध्रिगो शमीध्वमिति शिष्टा षड्विंशतिरस्य वंक्रय इति वा मा नो (*adhrigo śamīdhvamiti śiṣṭā ṣaḍviṃśatirasya vaṃkraya iti vā mā no*) मित्र इत्यावपेत (*mitra ityāvapeta*) (ಆ. 10-8, abbreviation as printed).

**Particulars of the sūkta**

Om. Sūkta 162. Maṇḍala 1, Anuvāka 22, Sūkta 162. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Vargas 7, 8, 9, 10. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 22. Seer: दीर्घतमा औचथ्यः (*dīrghatamā aucathyaḥ*). Deity: अश्वस्तुतिः (*aśvastutiḥ*) (praise of the horse). Metre (printed "ಲಂದಃ"): ṛks 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 22 त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*); ṛks 3 and 6 जगती (*jagatī*).

#### Ṛk 1

*[The label of the Saṃhitā is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕಂಠಪಾಠ"), and the Saṃhitā stood as plain lines; the standard label is inserted, the garbled word kept in kn.md as an italic note, and the lines made a block. The Pada has ऋभुषाः (*ṛbhuṣāḥ*) where the Saṃhitā has "ಋಭುಕ್ಷಾ"; kept as supplied.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> मा नो मित्रो वरुणो अर्यमायुरिंद्र ऋभुक्षा
> मरुतः परि ख्यन् ।
> यद्वा॒जिनो॑ दे॒वजा॑तस्य॒ सप्तेः॑ प्रव॒क्ष्यामो॑ वि॒दथे॒॑॑
> वीर्याणि ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> मा । नः। मित्रः। वरुणः । अर्यमा । आयुः । इंद्रः । ऋभुषाः । मरुतः । परि ।
> ख्यन् ।

### Printed p. 146

*Running head: 146 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨.] (as supplied).*

> यत् । वाजिनः । देवजातस्य । सप्तेः । प्रवक्ष्यामः । विदथे । वीर्याणि ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> नोऽस्थानश्वं स्तोतुं प्रवृत्तान्मित्रादयो देवा मा परि ख्यन् । परिख्यानं
> मा कुर्वंतु । परिख्यानं नाम निंदा । महानुभावं देवजातमश्वं तुच्छा नराः
> स्तुवंतीति मा कुर्वंतु ॥ ख्यातेर्माजि लुज्यस्य तिवक्तीत्यादिना चेरज् ॥ अहरभिमानी देवो मित्रो रात्र्यभिमानी वरुणः । अर्यमा तयोरंतरालाभिमान्यादित्यः । आयुः सततगंता वायुः ॥ वकारलोपो वा ॥ इंद्रः
> प्रसिद्धः । ऋभुक्षा देवानां निवासभूतः प्रजापतिः । यद्वा । एतनृहन्नामेंद्रविशेषणं ऋभक्षा उक्षेति तन्नामसु पाठात् । निंदायाः प्रसक्तिं दर्शयति । यद्यस्माद्वाजिनो वेजननतो बह्वन्नवतो वा देवजातस्य बहुदेवतास्वरूपेणोत्पन्नस्य । उषआदीनामस्य शिरआद्यवयवत्पादितिभावः । उषा ना अश्वस्य मेध्यस्य शिर
> इत्यादिश्रुतेः । यद्वा । देवेभ्यो जातस्य गंधर्वकुल उत्पन्नत्वात् । सप्तेः सर्पणशीलस्य । अश्वनामैतत् । एवंविधस्याश्वस्य महानुभावस्य वीर्याणि वीरकर्माणि
> सामर्थ्यानि विदथे वेदनसाधने यज्ञॆ प्रवक्ष्यामः प्रकर्षण ब्रूमः । अतो मा
> परि ख्यन् प्रत्युतानुगृह्णंतित्यर्थः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): may Mitra and the other gods not disparage us: we proclaim at the sacrifice the heroic deeds of the divine-born, swift horse.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यत् (*yat*)—when; विदथे (*vidathe*)—at the sacrifice; वाजिनः (*vājinaḥ*)—of the swift-going one; देव (*deva*)जातस्य (*jātasya*) *[the word is split over two lines]*—born of the gods (or born in the form of many gods); सप्तेः (*sapteḥ*)—of the horse; वीर्याणि (*vīryāṇi*)—the heroic deeds; प्रवक्ष्यामः (*pravakṣyāmaḥ*)—we proclaim and praise, (therefore); मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra; वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa; अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—Aryaman; आयुः (*āyuḥ*)—Vāyu, who moves constantly; इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra; ऋभुक्षा (*ṛbhukṣā*)—Ṛbhukṣan; मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts; नः (*naḥ*)—us; मा परिख्यन् (*mā parikhyan*)—may they not disregard (may they not disparage).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Because we proclaim and praise at this sacrifice the heroic deeds of this sacrificial horse, which is swift, born of the gods or existing in the forms of many gods, may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryaman, Vāyu who moves constantly, Indra, Ṛbhukṣan and the Maruts not disregard us (not disparage us).

### Printed p. 147

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೭. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 147 (as supplied).* (Sūkta 162, Ṛk 1.)

**Printed English translation**

Let neither Mitra nor Varuna, Aryaman. Ayu, Indra, Ribhukshin, nor the Maruts, censure us ; when we proclaim in the sacrifice the virtues of the swift horse sprung from the gods.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

(किमार्भवं) परे मा नो मेध्यस्याश्वस्य संस्तवः । (*(kimārbhavaṃ) pare mā no medhyasyāśvasya saṃstavaḥ |*)
ईर्मांतास इति त्वस्यां नीयमानं प्रशंसति ॥ (*īrmāṃtāsa iti tvasyāṃ nīyamānaṃ praśaṃsati ‖*) (ಬೃಹದ್ದೇವತಾ. 4-27)

The sūkta that begins with the ṛk मा नो मित्रो वरुणो (*mā no mitro varuṇo*) (1-162-1) and the sūkta that begins with the ṛk यदक्रंदः (*yadakraṃdaḥ*) प्रथमं जायमानः (*prathamaṃ jāyamānaḥ*) (1-163-1) are compositions in praise of the sacrificial horse. In ṛks such as ईर्मांतासः सिलिकमध्यमासः (*īrmāṃtāsaḥ silikamadhyamāsaḥ*) (1-163-10) there is a description of the well-grown sacrificial horse.

स्वयूथ्यास्तस्य चैवात्र बहवः संस्तुता हयाः । (*svayūthyāstasya caivātra bahavaḥ saṃstutā hayāḥ |*)
नियुक्ताश्चानियुक्ताश्च प्रसंगादनुकीर्तिताः ॥ (*niyuktāścāniyuktāśca prasaṃgādanukīrtitāḥ ‖*) (ಬೃ. ಪೇ. 4-28) *[abbreviation "ಬೃ. ಪೇ." as printed]*

Besides this sacrificial horse, other horses too that accompany it, whether for help or for ornament, whether appointed or not appointed, are also praised incidentally.

संज्ञप्तवदसंज्ञप्तं भविष्यं चाह भूतवत् । (*saṃjñaptavadasaṃjñaptaṃ bhaviṣyaṃ cāha bhūtavat |*)
तस्य मांसस्य सूनस्य चरूणां हविषस्तथा ॥ (*tasya māṃsasya sūnasya carūṇāṃ haviṣastathā ‖*)
वासोऽधिवाससोश्चात्र यद्विशस्यं च कीर्तितं । (*vāso 'dhivāsasoścātra yadviśasyaṃ ca kīrtitaṃ |*)
गात्रस्य शूलस्थूणानां स्वधितेश्च प्रकीर्तनं ॥ (*gātrasya śūlasthūṇānāṃ svadhiteśca prakīrtanaṃ ‖*)
भागस्य कीर्तनं चात्र इंद्रापूष्टोः सह स्तुतिः । (*bhāgasya kīrtanaṃ cātra iṃdrāpūṣṭoḥ saha stutiḥ |*) (ಬೃ. ದೇ. 4-29-31)

The horse that is to be sacrificed in the future is described as if it had already been offered as a sacrificial oblation. Its flesh, "sūna", "caru", "vāsa" and "adhivāsa", limbs, spit, post, knife and other special features are praised. The portion connected with this sacrifice (the goat) is also indicated. The author of the Bṛhaddevatā has given, for the sūktas 1-162 and 1-163, the explanation that the praise of Indra and of Pūṣan is included. Also in the present first ṛk,

### Printed p. 148

*Running head: 148 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨.] (as supplied; the sentence "ಪ್ರಕೃತವಾದ ಮೊದಲ-ನೆಯ ಋಕ್ಕಿನಲ್ಲಿಯೂ ಸಹ," is split across the page break).*

यद्वाजिनो देवजातस्य सप्तेः प्रवक्ष्यामो विदथे वीर्याणि । (*yadvājino devajātasya sapteḥ pravakṣyāmo vidathe vīryāṇi |*)

it is resolved: "We shall praise the heroic deeds of the horse born of the gods at this sacrifice." What is the nature of this sacrifice, what is the greatness of the horse appointed for it, and what is the special character of this sacrifice above the other sacrifices: this must be understood first.

राष्ट्रं वा अश्वमेधः । राष्ट्रे खलु ना एते न्यायच्छंते । (*rāṣṭraṃ vā aśvamedhaḥ | rāṣṭre khalu nā ete nyāyacchaṃte |*) (ತೈ. ಬ್ರಾ. 3-8-9-4) *[abbreviation as printed]*

The Aśvamedha is a very ancient and well-known sacrifice. This sacrifice, performed for the extension of the kingdom by a king who alone is qualified for the status of sovereign, has a national importance. Unlike the rites performed at the time of coronation or the Rājasūya sacrifice, it is not merely a personal display; it is impossible for petty kings to carry out, and is accomplished only by an emperor who shows his political authority as sovereign over many kings of kings and is not defeated by any king: this great sacrifice is a very laborious rite.

परा ना एष सिच्यते । योऽबलोऽश्वमेधेन यजते । (*parā nā eṣa sicyate | yo 'balo 'śvamedhena yajate |*) (ತೈ. ಬ್ರಾ. 3-8-9-5)

If any weak king tries to perform the Aśvamedha, thinking "I too will do it", it is certain that he will be driven out of his position.

यदमित्रा अश्वं विंदेरन् । हन्येतास्य यज्ञः । (*yadamitrā aśvaṃ viṃderan | hanyetāsya yajñaḥ |*) (ತೈ- ಬ್ರಾ- 3-8-9-5) *[reference as printed, with hyphens for stops]*

Thus, saying that enemies steal his horse and bring obstruction to his sacrifice, and so on, the qualification of the one who performs this sacrifice is fixed and its political significance and national importance are told. The horse capable of such a great undertaking must also be of great qualities. This horse must be swift. A black colour in front, a white colour behind and one black spot are its special qualities. On the eighth or ninth day of the month of Phālguna these rites begin. The king spends that night in the house of the Gārhapatya fire. The next day the horse is made ready. They have it bathed and so on. Then a dog with four eyes is cut up and let to roll under the horse. Then the sacrificer writes "He who confronts this horse confronts Varuṇa" and sticks it on. Then the horse comes near the altar of Agni. After the oblations to Savitṛ for three days

### Printed p. 149

*Running head: [೨೨ ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೭. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 149 (as supplied; "೨೨ ೨." is printed for "ಅ. ೨.").*

have been made, it is let loose freely. For its protection a hundred other horses, hundreds of youths and many warriors follow it. If the subordinate kings of him who is the sovereign are victorious in guarding it, their own sovereignty also remains safe. While that horse roams, the sacrificer and his priest sit on a golden seat. The hotṛ recites narratives and the like. The adhvaryu performs some rites for the victory of the horse. After it has returned, the sacrifice is performed.

In the Śatapatha and other texts the greatness of this Aśvamedha is much praised. ऋषभ एष यज्ञा (*ṛṣabha eṣa yajñā*)नां (*nāṃ*) ( ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-1-2-2) *[the word is split over two lines]*: among all sacrifices it is the one that is excellent like a strong bull; राजा ना एष यज्ञानां यदश्वमेधः (*rājā nā eṣa yajñānāṃ yadaśvamedhaḥ*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-2-2-1): it is the king of all sacrifices; and

एष वै प्रभूर्नाम यज्ञः .... .... .... एष वै विभूर्नाम यज्ञः .... .... .... एष (*eṣa vai prabhūrnāma yajñaḥ .... .... .... eṣa vai vibhūrnāma yajñaḥ .... .... .... eṣa*)
वै विधृतिर्नाम यज्ञः । .... .... .... एष वै प्रतिष्ठा नाम यज्ञः (*vai vidhṛtirnāma yajñaḥ | .... .... .... eṣa vai pratiṣṭhā nāma yajñaḥ*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-3-7) *[abbreviation "ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ." as printed; the lines have printed dots]*

In this and in other ways the special character of this sacrifice is praised. In the present ṛk there is praise of the horse that is the means of this great sacrifice. It is prayed that, if there is any deficiency in praising this horse, Mitra and the other gods may not, keeping it in view, blame us.

सप्तेः (*sapteḥ*)—the word "sapti" is read among the names of the horse (Ni. 2-27). The Nirukta authors, quoting the present ṛk itself (Ni. 9-3), have explained सप्तेः (*sapteḥ*) as सरणस्य (*saraṇasya*). Although this word means merely "moving", it indicates speed and power in movement.

वाजिनः (*vājinaḥ*)—for this word too the above meaning is current. But since both words occur here, it must have another meaning. Since "vāja" is read among the names of food, the word वाज (*vāja*) is taken to mean food and बह्वन्नवतः (*bahvannavataḥ*) has been explained as a horse having abundant food. But since the root ओविजी भय (*ovijī bhaya*) चलनयोः (*calanayoḥ*) has both meanings, fear and movement, one may say here that it is one that is to cause fear. The Nirukta authors, quoting the ṛk उत स्य वाजी क्षिपणिं तुरण्यति (*uta sya vājī kṣipaṇiṃ turaṇyati*) (the Ṛ. Saṃ. number is split over two lines in the print), have explained वाजी-वेजनवान् (*vājī-vejanavān*), and Durgācārya has explained it as भयवान् परेभ्यो भयदाता परेषां हि तं दृष्ट्वा भयमुत्पद्यते (*bhayavān parebhyo bhayadātā pareṣāṃ hi taṃ dṛṣṭvā bhayamutpadyate*): seeing the power of speed of this horse, fear arises in others; the purport is a horse that causes fear. The two words "sapti" and "vājī" are also used together in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, when the nature of the horse is described in अर्वासि सप्तिरसि वाज्यसि (*arvāsi saptirasi vājyasi*) (ತೈ. ಸಂ. 1-7-8, abbreviation as printed), and for the word "sapti" संग्रामेषु सहसा समवैति (*saṃgrāmeṣu sahasā samavaiti*) (षप समवाये (*ṣapa samavāye*)) in an instant (to the battlefield) *[the supplied text ends here, in the middle of the sentence; the rest is in the next window]*

### Printed p. 150

*Running head: 150 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ ೧೬೨.] (as supplied).* (Sūkta 162, Ṛk 1: the Special Topics continue; the first words complete the sentence about "sapti" in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā from the previous window.)

...: capable of reaching (the battlefield); and, as "vājī" is also explained as "swift" and "having food", it can be understood in the same way in the present ṛk.

देवजातस्य (*devajātasya*)—two kinds of meaning are given for this word. First, बहुदेवता (*bahudevatā*) स्वरूपेणोत्पन्नस्य । (*svarūpeṇotpannasya |*) *[split over two lines]* उषादीनामस्य शिर आद्यवयवत्वादिति भावः । (*uṣādīnāmasya śira ādyavayavatvāditi bhāvaḥ |*) Since the gods Uṣas, Sūrya and so on are said to be the limbs of this horse, this meaning; or, second, देवेभ्यो जातस्य गंधर्वकुले उत्पन्नत्वात् । (*devebhyo jātasya gaṃdharvakule utpannatvāt |*) since it was born from the gods in the family of the Gandharvas, it is explained as an epithet *[printed "ವೀಶೆಷಣ"]*. Both these explanations have full scope.

उषा वा अस्य मेध्यस्य शिरः । सूरश्चक्षुर्वातः प्राणो व्यात्तमग्निर्वैश्वानरः (*uṣā vā asya medhyasya śiraḥ | sūraścakṣurvātaḥ prāṇo vyāttamagnirvaiśvānaraḥ*)
संवत्सर आत्माश्वस्य मेध्यस्य । द्यौः पृष्ठमंतरिक्षमुदरं पृथिवी पाजस्यं (*saṃvatsara ātmāśvasya medhyasya | dyauḥ pṛṣṭhamaṃtarikṣamudaraṃ pṛthivī pājasyaṃ*)
दिशः पार्श्वे अवांतरदिशः पर्शव ऋतवोऽंगानि मासाश्चार्धमासाश्च पर्वाण्यहोरात्राणि प्रतिष्ठा नक्षत्राण्यस्थीनि नभो मांसानि ॥ (*diśaḥ pārśve avāṃtaradiśaḥ parśava ṛtavo 'ṃgāni māsāścārdhamāsāśca parvāṇyahorātrāṇi pratiṣṭhā nakṣatrāṇyasthīni nabho māṃsāni ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 10-6-4) *[abbreviation as printed; the hyphenated line break "ಪರ್ವಾ-ಣ್ಯಹೋರಾತ್ರಾಣಿ" is joined]*

The Dawn is the head of this sacrificial horse, the Sun the eye, Vāyu the breath, Vaiśvānara Agni the open mouth, the year the body, heaven the back, the atmosphere the belly, the earth the lower part, the directions the sides, and so on: thus the nature *[printed "ಸ್ವಶೂಪ"]* of this sacrificial horse is described.

देवेभ्यो जातस्य गंधर्वकुल उत्पन्नत्वात् । (*devebhyo jātasya gaṃdharvakula utpannatvāt |*) This is another meaning. In this meaning the horse has an indirect relation to the gods. Since in many places a direct relation of the horse with the gods is also stated, one may also say that "devajāta" means directly born of a god.

यत्पजापतिरालब्दोऽश्वोभवत् । तस्मादश्वः । यत्सद्यो मेधोऽभवत् । (*yatpajāpatirālabdo 'śvobhavat | tasmādaśvaḥ | yatsadyo medho 'bhavat |*)
तस्मादश्वमेधः । (*tasmādaśvamedhaḥ |*) (ತೈ. ಬ್ರಾ. 3-9-22-1) *[abbreviation as printed]*

Prajāpati, taken in the form of an animal, immediately became the all-pervading horse. On that same day that horse became the sacrifice in the form of "medha". Therefore the name "Aśvamedha" is well known. Moreover,

हयो भूत्वा देवानवहद्वाजी गंधर्वानर्नासुरानश्वो मनुष्यान् समुद्र (*hayo bhūtvā devānavahadvājī gaṃdharvānarnāsurānaśvo manuṣyān samudra*)
एवास्य बंधुः समुद्रो योनिः ॥ (*evāsya baṃdhuḥ samudro yoniḥ ‖*) (ಬೃ. ಉ. 1-1-2)

### Printed p. 151

*Running head: [ಅ.೨.ಅ ೩ ವ. ೭. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 151 (as supplied).*

this world-pervading sacrificial horse bears the gods in the form of "haya", the Gandharvas in the form of "vājī", the Asuras in the form of "arvan" and men in the form of "aśva". The atmosphere, the dwelling of the Gandharvas, is the kinsman and the birth-place of this horse. On the basis of such descriptions the second meaning given above for the epithet "devajāta" too becomes possible.

ऋभुक्षाः (*ṛbhukṣāḥ*)—for this word the Nirukta authors themselves have given two meanings: one, उरुक्षयणः इंद्रः (*urukṣayaṇaḥ iṃdraḥ*), and another, ऋभूनां राजा (*ṛbhūnāṃ rājā*). Either it is an epithet of Indra, "he who dwells in the wide atmosphere", or it means a king of the Ṛbhus, that is, of the shining gods. Or, as Sāyaṇa explains, since it is read among the names of "great" (Ni. 3-13), it may mean the great Indra.

मा परिख्यन् (*mā parikhyan*)—it is prayed: "let them not disparage". The meaning is: may Mitra and the other gods not reject us, thinking that there is no propriety in petty humans praising this horse, which is of divine nature.

विदथे—वेदनसाधने यज्ञे । (*vidathe—vedanasādhane yajñe |*) Sāyaṇa has explained it as "in a sacrifice that is a means of understanding". It is therefore understood that this sacrifice is also a means of acquiring spiritual knowledge. At the time of this sacrifice a brahmodya assembly takes place in connection with the adhvaryu, the hotṛ and the other priests, and a discussion on the principles of the world takes place in it.

अप वा एतस्मात् । तेजो ब्रह्मवर्चसं क्रामति योऽश्वमेधेन यजते (*apa vā etasmāt | tejo brahmavarcasaṃ krāmati yo 'śvamedhena yajate*)
होता च ब्रह्मा च ब्रह्मोद्यं वदत आग्नेयो वै होता बार्हस्पत्यो (*hotā ca brahmā ca brahmodyaṃ vadata āgneyo vai hotā bārhaspatyo*)
ब्रह्मा ब्रह्मा बृहस्पतिस्तेजश्चैवास्मिन्ब्रह्मवर्चसं च समीची धत्तो यूपोमभितो वदतो यजमानो वै यूपो यजमानमेवैतत्तेजसा च (*brahmā brahmā bṛhaspatistejaścaivāsminbrahmavarcasaṃ ca samīcī dhatto yūpomabhito vadato yajamāno vai yūpo yajamānamevaitattejasā ca*)
ब्रह्मवर्चसेन चोभयतः परिधत्तः । (*brahmavarcasena cobhayataḥ paridhattaḥ |*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ 13-2-6-9) *[the hyphenated line break "ಯೂಪೋ-ಮಭಿತೋ" is joined; the reference line is printed twice, the second time without the closing bracket "(ಶ. ಬ್ರ. ೧೩-೨-೬-೯" (abbreviation as printed); the second is kept in kn.md as an italic note]*

तेजसा वा विष .... .... .... ब्रह्मवर्चसमेवावरुंधे । (*tejasā vā viṣa .... .... .... brahmavarcasamevāvaruṃdhe |*) (ಶೈ. ಬ್ರಾ. 3-9-5) *[abbreviation "ಶೈ. ಬ್ರಾ." as printed; the line has printed dots]*

In such sentences a discussion of principles takes place among the priests, and, since it is a means of knowledge, Sāyaṇa explains that the place where this Aśvamedha is performed is called "vidatha". This matter will be told in the conclusion of this sūkta and of the next, when the nature of the Aśvamedha is explained from the point of view of the rite and of knowledge. But since here the word "vidatha" is explained as a sacrifice that is a means of knowledge, one can acquire a subtle acquaintance with the discussion on that knowledge. The brahmodya discussion that takes place between the hotṛ and the Brahmā is as follows.

कः स्विदेकाकी चरतीति । असौ वा आदित्य एकाकी चरत्येष ब्रह्मवर्चसं ब्रह्मवर्चसमेवास्मिन्पत्तः । (*kaḥ svidekākī caratīti | asau vā āditya ekākī caratyeṣa brahmavarcasaṃ brahmavarcasamevāsminpattaḥ |*) (ಶ ಬ್ರಾ. 13-2-6-10) *[the hyphenated line break "ಬ್ರಹ್ಮ-ವರ್ಚಸ" is joined]*

### Printed p. 152

*Running head: 152 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

In answer to the question "Who moves alone?", the distant Sun is the one who moves alone; he is the Brahman-radiance.

किग्ग स्विदासीद्भृहद्वय इति । अश्वो वै बृहद्वय आयुरेवावरुंधे । (*kigga svidāsīdbhṛhadvaya iti | aśvo vai bṛhadvaya āyurevāvaruṃdhe |*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-2-6-15) *[so printed, "ಕಿಗ್ಗ"]*

In answer to the question "Which is that great bird?", the sacrificial horse itself is that great "vaya"; it obtains the power of life. Thus the discussion of principles shows the nature of the brahmodya. Because of such discussion the Aśvamedha is a means of knowledge and is therefore named "vidatha". The same matter, in the Bṛhadāraṇyaka:

एष ह ना अश्वमेधो य एष तपति तस्य संवत्सर आत्मायमग्नि रर्कस्तस्येमेलोका आत्मानस्तावेतावर्काश्वमेधौ ॥ (*eṣa ha nā aśvamedho ya eṣa tapati tasya saṃvatsara ātmāyamagni rarkastasyemelokā ātmānastāvetāvarkāśvamedhau ‖*) (ಬೃ ಉ 1-2-7) *[the hyphenated line break "ತಸ್ಯೇಮೇ-ಲೋಕಾ" is joined]*

it shows the universal-self nature of the Aśvamedha. In the brahmodya there is a discussion explaining the nature of this universal self. Therefore the Aśvamedha, which has the brahmodya discussion, is named "vidatha", the means of knowledge.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses आयुः (*āyuḥ*) (root वा (*vā*); ಉ. ಸೂ. 1-1, abbreviation as printed; Pā. Sū. 7-3-33), ख्यन् (*khyan*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-175, 3-1-52, 6-4-64 and 6-4-74), देवजातस्य (*devajātasya*), प्रवक्ष्यामः (*pravakṣyāmaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71) and विदथे (*vidathe*) (root विद (*vida*)), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧ ॥. *[A stray line ":੦:" in Gurmukhi letters ends the supplied text; kept in kn.md as an italic note.]*

### Printed p. 153

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೭ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 153 (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ.").*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यन्नि॒र्णिजा॒ रेक्ष्ण॑सा॒ प्रावृ॑तस्य रा॒तिं गृ॑भी॒तां मु॑ख॒तो॑
> नयंति ।
> सुप्रा॑ज॒जो मेम्य॑द्वि॒श्वरू॑प इंद्रापू॒ष्टोः प्रि॒यमप्ये॑ति॒॑
> पाथः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[Variants between Saṃhitā and Pada kept as supplied: the Pada has रेक्णसा (*rekṇasā*) and इंद्रापूष्णोः (*iṃdrāpūṣṇoḥ*) where the Saṃhitā has "ರೇಕ್ಷ್ಣಸಾ" and "ಇಂದ್ರಾಪೂಷ್ಟೋಃ", and यॆत् (*yèt*) for "ಯತ್".]*

> यॆत् । निःनिजा । रेक्णसा । प्रावृतस्य । रातिं । गृभीतां । मुखतः । नयंति ।
> सुप्राज् । अजः । मेम्यत् । विश्वरूपः । इंद्रापूष्णोः । प्रियं । अपि । एति ।
> पाथः ॥२॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The Pratipadārtha stood inside this block after the garbled label "೧ ಪ್ರತಿಪದಾರ್ಥ ೧".]*

> यद्यस्मान्निर्णिजा । निर्णिगिति रूपनाम । रेक्षसा । रेक्ष इति धननाम । निर्णिग्वन्विरिति रेक्षो रिक्कमिति च तन्नामसूक्तत्वात् ॥ उभयत्र मत्त्वर्थो लुप्यते ॥
> रूपवता हिरण्यवताभरणेन प्रावृतस्य सर्वतो वेष्टितस्याश्वस्य मुखतः पुरस्तादृभीतां गृहीतां रातिं दातव्यां दातव्यत्वेन गृहीतामजां नयंत्यृत्तिजः । स तादृशोऽजः सुप्राज् सुष्टु प्रशस्यागमनो मेम्यद्धन्यमानः ॥ माञ् हिंसायां ।
> अस्माद्यज्नुगंतात्मर्मणि व्यत्ययेन शतृ ॥ यद्वा । तस्य शब्दानुकरणं मेमेशब्दं
> कुर्वन् । विश्वरूपो बहुवर्णोपेतः कृष्णग्रीवः कृष्णललामो ना । नैकवर्ण
> इत्यर्थः । एवंरूपोऽयमिंद्रापूष्टोः । इंद्रः प्रसिद्धः । पूषा पोषको
> देवोऽग्निः । तयोः प्रियं । आग्नेयः कृष्णग्रीव इत्युक्तत्पादग्नेः प्रियत्वं ।
> सर्वॆऽपींद्रस्येतींद्रप्रियत्वं । एवंरूपं पाथोऽन्नमप्येति । प्राप्पोतु ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): priests lead a goat before the adorned horse; the many-coloured, fine-gaited goat, bleating, becomes food dear to Indra and Pūṣan (Agni).]*

*[The label of the Pratipadārtha is garbled in the supplied text ("೧ ಪ್ರತಿಪದಾರ್ಥ ೧"); the standard label is inserted, the garbled words kept in kn.md as an italic note, and the text moved out of the bhāṣya block.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यत् (*yat*)—when; निर्णिजा (*nirṇijā*)—(adorned) with beautiful forms; रेक्णसा (*rekṇasā*)—with ornaments made of gold and the like; प्रावृतस्य (*prāvṛtasya*)—of the horse adorned all around; मुखतः (*mukhataḥ*)

### Printed p. 154

*Running head: 154 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha continues.)

—in front; गृभीतां (*gṛbhītāṃ*)—held; रातिः (*rātiḥ*)—the goat that is to be offered; नयंति (*nayaṃti*)—(the priests) bring, (then); विश्वरूपः (*viśvarūpaḥ*)—of various colours; सुप्राज् (*suprāj*)—having a fine gait; अजः (*ajaḥ*)—the goat; मेम्यत् (*memyat*)—making the sound "mē" *[the supplied text has "ಕೆಹಾಕುತ್ತಾ" after it, kept as supplied]*; इंद्रा पूष्णोः (*iṃdrā pūṣṇoḥ*)—for Indra and Pūṣan (or for Agni in the form of Pūṣan); प्रियं पाथः (*priyaṃ pāthaḥ*)—as dear food; अप्येति (*apyeti*)—becomes ready.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

When the priests bring in front of the horse, which is adorned all around with beautiful ornaments made of gold and the like, the goat that is held and is to be offered as an oblation, that goat, of various colours and fine gait, bleating "mē", becomes ready as food dear to Indra and to Pūṣan in the form of Agni.

**Printed English translation**

When they, (the priescs) [sic], bring the prepared offering to the presence (of the horse), who has been bathed and decorated with rich (trappings). the various-coloured goat going before him, bleating, becomes an acceptable offering to Indra and Pushan.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk are described the decoration of the sacrificial horse, and the placing, by the priests, in front of that horse, of the goat that is ready for the oblation and has become food dear to Indra and Pūṣan.

निर्णिजा—निर्णिक्, वप्रि इत्यादि रूपनामगळल्लि (*nirṇijā—nirṇik, vapri ityādi rūpanāmagaḷalli*) *[the Kannada continues "ಈ ಶಬ್ದವು ಪರಿತನಾಗಿದೆ", "this word is read"]* (Ni. 3-7). In general this word is used in two meanings: first, "cleansed", and second, "making manifest one's own nature"; besides these, it is also used in the meaning "well-grown".

अमृक्तेन रुशता वाससा हरिरमर्त्यो निर्णिजानः परि व्यत । (*amṛktena ruśatā vāsasā hariramartyo nirṇijānaḥ pari vyata |*)
दिवस्पृष्ठं बर्हणा निर्णिजे कृतोपस्तरणं चन्नोर्नभस्मयं ॥ (*divaspṛṣṭhaṃ barhaṇā nirṇije kṛtopastaraṇaṃ cannornabhasmayaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-69-5)

In ṛks of this kind, and in the Upaniṣad sentence

सर्वं पिबति निर्णिज्य कंसं चमसं वा । (*sarvaṃ pibati nirṇijya kaṃsaṃ camasaṃ vā |*) (ಛಾ. ತಾ. 5-2-7) *[reference as printed, "ಛಾ. ತಾ."]*

### Printed p. 155

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೭ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 155 (as supplied).*

the meanings "being purified" and "having washed" arise; the purport is "washed, purified".

यदी घृतेभिराहुतो वाशीमग्निर्भरत उच्चाव च । असुर इव निर्णिजं ॥ (*yadī ghṛtebhirāhuto vāśīmagnirbharata uccāva ca | asura iva nirṇijaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-19-23)

Here, where it is said that Agni makes his own nature manifest, "nirṇijam" means "the nature".

बिभ्रद्दापिं हिरण्ययं वरुणो वस्त निर्णिजं । परि स्पशो नि षेदिरे ॥ (*bibhraddāpiṃ hiraṇyayaṃ varuṇo vasta nirṇijaṃ | pari spaśo ni ṣedire ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-13)

Here the body of the god Varuṇa is described; "nirṇijam" here has the purport "a well-grown body". Since the word is thus used in many meanings, in the present ṛk it is explained, रूपवता (*rūpavatā*) आभरणेन (*ābharaṇena*), as "with an ornament of beautiful form".

रेक्णसा (*rekṇasā*)—this word is read among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). The Nirukta authors, quoting the ṛk

परिषद्यं ह्यरणस्य रेक्लो नित्यस्य रायः पतयः स्याम । (*pariṣadyaṃ hyaraṇasya reklo nityasya rāyaḥ patayaḥ syāma |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-4-2) *[a stray line "(ಋ." before the reference is kept in kn.md as an italic note]*

have shown with रेक्षः इति धननाम; रिच्यते प्रयतः । (*rekṣaḥ iti dhananāma; ricyate prayataḥ |*) (Ni. 3-2) that wealth has this name because it is a thing that is left behind by one who has died. Likewise, if in the present ṛk it is taken to mean wealth, the meaning of रेक्षसा (*rekṣasā*) is: the horse adorned with an ornament that has wealth in the form of gold, हिरण्यवताभरणेन (*hiraṇyavatābharaṇena*) *[the hyphenated line break is joined]*.

अजः प्रियं पाथः अप्येति (*ajaḥ priyaṃ pāthaḥ apyeti*)—it is said that the goat becomes food dear to Indra and Pūṣan. In some places it is said that when the sacrificial horse is brought adorned, there is a custom of giving a dog with four eyes as an oblation.

पाप्मा ना एतं भ्रातृव्य ईप्सति योऽश्वमेधेन यजेत वज्रोऽश्वः परो (*pāpmā nā etaṃ bhrātṛvya īpsati yo 'śvamedhena yajeta vajro 'śvaḥ paro*)
मर्तः परः श्वेति श्वानं चतुरक्षग्ं हत्वाधस्पदमश्वस्योपप्लावयति वज्रेणैवैनमवक्रामति नैनं पाप्मा भ्रातृव्य आप्नोति ॥ (*martaḥ paraḥ śveti śvānaṃ caturakṣagṃ hatvādhaspadamaśvasyopaplāvayati vajreṇaivainamavakrāmati nainaṃ pāpmā bhrātṛvya āpnoti ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-1-2-9) *[abbreviation as printed; the hyphenated line break "ವಜ್ರೇಣೈ-ವೈನಮ" is joined]*

If, out of envy, an enemy gives trouble to the sacrificer who is performing the Aśvamedha, then, killing a dog with four eyes and cursing the enemy, he offers it as an oblation below the horse *[the supplied text ends here, in the middle of the sentence; the rest is in the next window]*

### Printed p. 156

*Running head: 156 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).* (Sūkta 162, Ṛk 2: the Special Topics continue; the first words complete the sentence about the four-eyed dog from the previous window.)

...: thus even the enemy is destroyed by the stroke of the thunderbolt in the form of the horse. From these sentences, which have this purport, it is clear that this custom is a different one. In the present ṛk the word "aja" (goat) occurs. What is the nature of this goat?

विश्वरूपः—बहुवर्णोपेतं । कृष्णग्रीवः । कृष्णललामो वा । (*viśvarūpaḥ—bahuvarṇopetaṃ | kṛṣṇagrīvaḥ | kṛṣṇalalāmo vā |*) having a black neck or a black forehead-mark. Why so?

आग्नेयः कृष्णग्रीवः सारस्वती मेषी । (*āgneyaḥ kṛṣṇagrīvaḥ sārasvatī meṣī |*) (ತೈ. ಸಂ. 55-22) *[reference as printed, "೫೫-೨೨"; abbreviation as printed]*

Since it is said that the colour dear to Agni is black, and that the black colour should be especially near the neck, this meaning is given here.

Here, for the word पूषन् (*pūṣan*) the meaning पोषकः देवः अग्निः (*poṣakaḥ devaḥ agniḥ*) is given, that is, Agni, the nourisher, of divine nature. Moreover, at the time of the Aśvamedha,

इंद्राग्नी वै सर्वे देवास्तदेवैनान्यथादेवतं प्रिणातीति । (*iṃdrāgnī vai sarve devāstadevainānyathādevataṃ priṇātīti |*) (ಪ. ಪ್ರಾ. 13-5-2-12) *[abbreviation "ಪ. ಪ್ರಾ." as printed]*

Indra and Agni are joined with the Viśvedevas and their identity is stated.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses निर्णिजा (*nirṇijā*), प्रावृत्रस्य (*prāvṛtrasya*) *[so printed]* (Pā. Sū. 6-2-49), रातिम् (*rātim*) (Pā. ### Printed p. 158

*Running head: 158 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The Pada has पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*) where the bhāṣya has "ಪುರೋಡಾಶಂ"; kept as supplied.]*

> एष भागः शृंगरहितोऽजोऽश्वेन वाजिना शीघ्रव्यापकेनाश्वेन सह पूष्णः
> पोषकस्याग्नेर्भागो भजनीयो विश्वदेव्यः सर्वदेवार्हः । अग्नेः सर्वदेवात्मकत्वात् तदर्हत्वेन सर्वदेवप्रियत्वं । एवंविधोऽजः पुरः पुरस्तानीयते । प्राप्यते।
> यद्यस्मादेवं क्रियते तस्मात्प्रियं प्रीणयितारं पुरोडाशं पुरस्ताद्घातव्यमेनमजं त्वष्टा सर्वस्कोत्पादको देवः । त्वष्टा रूपाणि विकरोतीति श्रुतेः । तूर्णव्यापकोऽग्निर्वा । उभे त्वष्टुर्बिभ्यतुरित्युक्तत्वात् अहोरात्रे इति वारणी इति वेति
> यास्केनारणी बिभ्यतुरित्युक्तत्वात् । नि ८-१५ । अरणिसंबंधोऽग्नेरेवेति गम्यते
> अर्वतारणवताश्वेन सह सौश्रवसाय देवानां शोभनान्ननिमित्तमभि जिन्वति । प्रीतिहेतुकं करोति ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): this hornless goat, Agni's share fit for all gods, is led before the swift horse; Tvaṣṭṛ (or Agni) makes it a pleasing offering.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

विश्वदेव्यः (*viśvadevyaḥ*)—fit to be offered to all the gods; पूष्णः भागः (*pūṣṇaḥ bhāgaḥ*)—assigned to Agni, the nourisher (that which belongs to him); एषः भागः (*eṣaḥ bhāgaḥ*)—this goat; वाजिना (*vājinā*)—swift; अश्वेन (*aśvena*)—with the horse; पुरः (*puraḥ*)—in front; नीयते (*nīyate*)—is led; यत् (*yat*)—therefore; प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—giving pleasure; पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*)—to be offered first; एनं (*enaṃ*)—this goat; त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ or Agni; अर्वता (*arvatā*)—with the horse; सौश्रवसाय (*sauśravasāya*)—so that it becomes excellent food, and; अभि जिन्वति इत् (*abhi jinvati it*)—makes it be pleasing.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

This goat, fit to be offered to all the gods and belonging to the share of Agni, the nourisher, is led in front together with the swift horse. Therefore Tvaṣṭṛ, together with the horse, makes this goat, which gives pleasure and is to be offered first, be excellent food and pleasing.

**Printed English translation**

This goat, the portion of Pushan, fit for all the gods, is brought first with the fleet courser, so that Twashtri may prepare him along with the horse, as an acceptable preliminary offering for the (sacrifical) [sic] food.

### Printed p. 159

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೭. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 159 (as supplied).* (Sūkta 162, Ṛk 3.)

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In stating the rule by which, in the Aśvamedha, the goat in the form of the puroḍāśa is made dear to the gods, there is an epithet "viśvadevyaḥ" for this food in the form of a goat. In this context an objection arises, and the answer to it is as follows.

यत्पाजापत्योऽश्वोऽथ कथाप्यन्याभ्यो देवताभ्यः प्रोक्षतीति सर्वा वै (*yatpājāpatyo 'śvo 'tha kathāpyanyābhyo devatābhyaḥ prokṣatīti sarvā vai*)
देवता अश्वमेधेऽन्वायत्ता यदाह सर्वेभ्यस्त्वा देवेभ्यः प्रोक्षामीति (*devatā aśvamedhe 'nvāyattā yadāha sarvebhyastvā devebhyaḥ prokṣāmīti*)
सर्वा एनास्मिन्देवता अन्वायातयति तस्मादश्वमेधे सर्वा देवता अन्वायत्ताः ॥ (*sarvā enāsmindevatā anvāyātayati tasmādaśvamedhe sarvā devatā anvāyattāḥ ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 13-1-2-9) *[abbreviation as printed; the hyphenated line break "ಅನ್ವಾ-ಯತ್ತಾಃ" is joined]*

Since the sacrificial horse belongs chiefly to Prajāpati alone, this whole share of the sacrifice should belong to that deity alone. In such a case, to the question why the horse is sprinkled with reference also to the other deities, the answer is given that, since all the deities are connected with the Aśvamedha, all are addressed. According to that rule, the food in the form of the goat, described in the present ṛk and being in the form of the puroḍāśa, is also "viśvadevya".

पुरोळा (डा)शं-पुरस्ताद्दातव्यं । (*puroḷā (ḍā)śaṃ-purastāddātavyaṃ |*) *[so printed]* "that which is to be offered first".

स ना एभ्यस्तत्पुरोऽदाशयत् । य एभ्यो यज्ञं प्रारोचयत्तस्मात्पुरोदाशः पुरोदाशो ह वै नामैतद्यत्पुरोडाश इति ॥ (*sa nā ebhyastatpuro 'dāśayat | ya ebhyo yajñaṃ prārocayattasmātpurodāśaḥ purodāśo ha vai nāmaitadyatpuroḍāśa iti ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 1-6-2-5) *[the hyphenated line break "ಪುರೋ-ದಾಶಃ" is joined]*

The name "puroḍāśa" *[printed "ಪುರೋಡಾರ"]* is given to the preliminary offering that makes the sacrifice attractive and the sacrificial portion palatable. Here the offering of the goat is the one that precedes the offering of the horse. It is the puroḍāśa.

वाजिना, अर्वता (*vājinā, arvatā*)—the two words "vājī" and "arvā" are also read among the names of the horse (Ni. 2-27). When explaining the description of the horse called Dadhikrāḥ, the Nirukta authors, quoting the ṛk

आ दधिक्राः शवसा पंच कृष्टीः सूर‍्य इव ज्योतिषापस्ततान । (*ā dadhikrāḥ śavasā paṃca kṛṣṭīḥ sūrya iva jyotiṣāpastatāna |*)
सहस्रसाः शतसा वाज्यर्वा पृणक्तु मध्वा समिमा वचांसि ॥ (*sahasrasāḥ śatasā vājyarvā pṛṇaktu madhvā samimā vacāṃsi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-38-10)

have explained वाजी वेजनवान्, अर्वेरणवान् (*vājī vejanavān, arveraṇavān*) as having both speed and movement. But since the root "vī" (ओविजी भयचलनयोः) (*(ovijī bhayacalanayoḥ)*) has both the meaning of fear and the meaning of movement,

### Printed p. 160

*Running head: 160 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨.] (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ.").*

one may even say that the horse frightens (its enemies) by its swift gait.

त्वष्टा अभिजिन्वति (*tvaṣṭā abhijinvati*)—when it is said that Tvaṣṭṛ makes it pleasing, that is, makes it be satisfied, Sāyaṇa has first taken it as the god Tvaṣṭṛ, explaining त्वष्टा सर्वस्य उत्पादको देवः (*tvaṣṭā sarvasya utpādako devaḥ*), and afterwards, by तूर्णव्यापकोऽग्निर्वा (*tūrṇavyāpako 'gnirvā*), he has given the word "tvaṣṭṛ" the meaning "spreading quickly and pervading" and made it an epithet of Agni, so that he has said that "tvaṣṭṛ" may also mean Agni. To support this meaning,

अविष्टो वर्धते चारुरासु जिह्मानामूर्ध्वः स्वयशा उपस्थे। (*aviṣṭo vardhate cārurāsu jihmānāmūrdhvaḥ svayaśā upasthe|*)
उभे त्वष्टुर्बिभ्यतुर्जायमाना त्प्रतीची सिंहं प्रति जोषयेते ॥ (*ubhe tvaṣṭurbibhyaturjāyamānā tpratīcī siṃhaṃ prati joṣayete ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-5)

Sāyaṇa has explained that, having quoted this ṛk, the word "tvaṣṭṛ" is used as a synonym of Agni in the sentence त्वष्टुर्बिभ्यतुः (*tvaṣṭurbibhyatuḥ*). अरणी इति वा (*araṇī iti vā*) (Ni., the number printed "೮. ೧೫") इति यास्केन (*iti yāskena*) बिभ्यतुरित्युक्तत्वात् अरणिसंबंधः अग्नेरेवेति गम्यते । (*bibhyaturityuktatvāt araṇisaṃbaṃdhaḥ agnereveti gamyate |*) Sāyaṇa explains that, in giving the derivation of the word "tvaṣṭṛ", Yāska has also shown a derivation as a synonym of Agni.

जिन्वति—जिन्वतिः प्रीतिकर्मा । (*jinvati—jinvatiḥ prītikarmā |*) The word is used in the sense "cause satisfaction". *[In the supplied text this paragraph is printed twice; the second is kept in kn.md as an italic note.]*

भूमिं पर्जन्या जिन्वंति दिवं जिन्वंत्यग्नयः । (*bhūmiṃ parjanyā jinvaṃti divaṃ jinvaṃtyagnayaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-51)

The word is used in a context that indicates the same kind of satisfaction, as in "Parjanya, providing abundance of food, satisfies the earth, and the fires, providing food in the form of oblations, satisfy the gods".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses भागः (*bhāgaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-45 and 6-1-166; the Uṇādi-sūtra number is printed "ಉ ಸೂ. ೧-೧೨೧", not cited), भागः (*bhāgaḥ*) (root भज (*bhaja*); Pā. Sū. 7-2-116; the numbers of 7-3-52 and 6-1-156 are printed with a comma or split over lines, not cited), नीयते (*nīyate*) and, on p. 161, विश्वदेव्यः (*viśvadevyaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 5-1-67 and 6-2-106), सौश्रवसाय (*sauśravasāya*) and जिन्वति (*jinvati*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-58), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೩ ॥.
