### Printed p. 213
*Running head: ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೦. ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 213 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 19
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> एक॒स्त्वष्टु॒रश्व॑स्या विश॒स्ता द्वा यं॒तारा॑ भवत॒स्तथ॑ ऋ॒तुः ।
> या ते॒ गात्रा॑णामृतु॒था कृ॒णोमि॒ ताता॒ पिंडा॑नां॒ प्रजु॑होम्य॒ग्नौ ॥ १९ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> एकः । त्वष्टुः । अश्वस्य । विशस्ता । द्वा । यंतारा । भवतः । तथा । ऋतुः ।
> या । ते । गात्राणां । ऋतुथा । कृणोमि । ताता । पिंडानां । प्र । जुहोमि ।
> अग्नौ ॥ १९ ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> त्वष्टुरस्य दीप्तस्याश्वस्य विशस्ता विशसनस्य कर्तृकः । एक एव । स कः । ऋतुरेतदुपलक्षितः कालात्मा तस्यैव सर्वेषामपि पर्यवसितृत्वात् । यद्वा । ऋतुर्नि:-
> शंकविशसनाय गंता शमिता । तथा द्वा यंतारा नियमयितारावहोरात्रे देवौ
> द्यावापृथिव्य वा ऋत्विक्षु भवतः ॥ तथ ऋतुरित्यत्र ऋत्यक इति प्रकृतिभावो ह्रस्वत्वं
> च ii हे अश्व ते तव गात्राणां मध्यॆ या यानि हृदयाद्यवयवान्न्युतुथा काले
> कृणोमि छिनद्मि संपादयामि पिंडानां मध्यॆ ताता तानि तानि प्रज्ञानानुरोधेनाग्ना प्र जुहोमि । प्रकर्षण हुतं करोमि ॥
*Gist:* Of this radiant horse the one cutter-up is Time; two (day and night, or heaven and earth) restrain it; the limbs I prepare in season I offer in the fire as balls.
**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
त्वष्टुः (*tvaṣṭuḥ*)—radiant; अस्य अश्वस्य (*asya aśvasya*)—of this horse; विशस्ता (*viśastā*)—the cutter-up; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—one (who is that?); ऋतुः (*ṛtuḥ*)—Time, of the nature of the seasons (that brings everything to an end); यंतारा (*yaṃtārā*)—those who restrain it; द्वा (*dvā*)—two, having the forms of day and night, or of heaven and earth; भवतः (*bhavataḥ*)—there are; (हे अश्व (*he aśva*)—O horse); ते (*te*)—your; गात्राणां (*gātrāṇāṃ*)—among the limbs; या (*yā*)—which; ऋतुथा (*ṛtuthā*)—in accordance with the time of the sacrifice; कृणोमि (*kṛṇomi*)—I prepare; ताता (*tātā*)—all of them; पिंडानां (*piṃḍānāṃ*)—in the form of balls; अग्नौ (*agnau*)—in the fire; प्र जुहोमि (*pra juhomi*)—I offer as an excellent oblation.

### Printed p. 214
*Running head: 214 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨, ಸೂ. ೧೬೨.] (as supplied; a comma after ೨೨).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
Of this radiant horse there is one cutter-up. Who is that? It is Time, of the nature of the seasons, which brings everything to an end. Those who restrain it are two, having the forms of day and night, or of heaven and earth. O horse, whichever of your limbs I prepare in accordance with the time of the sacrifice, all of them I offer in the fire, in the form of balls, as an excellent oblation.

**Printed English**
There is one immolator of the radiant horse, which is Time: there are two that hold him fast : such of your limbs as I cut up in due season, I offer them, made into balls (of meat), upon the fire.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
The description in this Ṛk is not easily understood. A spiritual principle lies hidden in it. This description tells the nature of the sacrificial horse and its relation in two ways: in one way as the verse of the Śamitṛ engaged in the customary rite of cutting; in another way as the verse of Time, which holds the whole universe in its control and is the ender of all. ऋतुः एतदुपलक्षितः कालात्मा तस्यैव सर्वेषामपि पठ्यवसितृत्वात् । यद्वा ऋतुः, निःशंकविशसनाय गंता शमिता । (*ṛtuḥ etadupalakṣitaḥ kālātmā tasyaiva sarveṣāmapi paṭhyavasitṛtvāt | yadvā ṛtuḥ, niḥśaṃkaviśasanāya gaṃtā śamitā |*) [as supplied: *paṭhyavasitṛtvāt* for *paryavasitṛtvāt*; *niḥśaṅka* in the Bhāṣya reads *niśyaṅka* in the Kannada] — thus Sāyaṇa has shown the derivation in the two ways explained above.

ತ್ವಷ್ಟುಃ—Sāyaṇa explains this word as दीप्तस्य (*dīptasya*), "radiant". But since the preceding Ṛk says देवबंधोः (*devabaṃdhoḥ*), and since in many places the relation of begetter and begotten is stated between the sacrificial horse and Tvaṣṭṛ, it would not be wrong to give here the meaning "belonging to Tvaṣṭṛ".

श्वष्टा वीरं देवकामं जजान त्वष्टुरर्ना जायत आशुरश्वः ॥ (*śvaṣṭā vīraṃ devakāmaṃ jajāna tvaṣṭurarnā jāyata āśuraśvaḥ ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 5-1-11) [as supplied: *śvaṣṭā*, *arnā*]

In this Āprī-sūkta of the Aśvamedha it is clearly said that the sacrificial horse was produced from Tvaṣṭṛ [as supplied: *tṛṣṭavinindā*]. Moreover:

प्रजापतेरक्ष्यश्वयत् । तत्पारापतत्ततोऽश्वः समभवद्यदश्वयत्तदश्वस्माश्वत्वं तद्देवा अश्वमेधेनैव प्रत्यदधुरेष ह वै प्रजापतिग्ं सर्वं करोति योऽ श्वमेधेन यजते ॥ (*prajāpaterakṣyaśvayat | tatpārāpatattato 'śvaḥ samabhavadyadaśvayattadaśvasmāśvatvaṃ taddevā aśvamedhenaiva pratyadadhureṣa ha vai prajāpatigṃ sarvaṃ karoti yo ' śvamedhena yajate ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 13-3-1)

### Printed p. 215
*Running head: ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೦ ] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 215 (as supplied).*

Once the eye of Prajāpati swelled and fell out. From it the horse was produced. Therefore this name became famous for the horse. Afterwards the gods performed the Aśvamedha and restored it to its own place. Since it is said that he who performs the Aśvamedha makes Prajāpati complete and himself also becomes complete, it would not be wrong to say that here the word *tvaṣṭṛ* means "belonging to Prajāpati". Of such a horse, kinsman of the gods, it says विशस्ता एकः । विशसनकर्ता एकः । (*viśastā ekaḥ | viśasanakartā ekaḥ |*) "the cutter is one only", the one who cuts up. Who is this? It is indicated: ऋतुः (*ṛtuḥ*). The same matter is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

कस्त्वा भ्यति कस्त्वा वि शास्ति कस्ते गात्राणि शिम्यति । क उ ते शमिता कविः । ऋतवस्त ऋतुधा परुश्यमितारो वि शासतु । संवत्सरस्य धायसा शिमीभिशिम्यंतु त्वा । दैव्या अध्वरवस्ता भ्यंतु वि च शासतु । गात्राणि पर्वशस्ते शिमाः कृण्वंतु शिम्यंतः ॥ (*kastvā bhyati kastvā vi śāsti kaste gātrāṇi śimyati | ka u te śamitā kaviḥ | ṛtavasta ṛtudhā paruśyamitāro vi śāsatu | saṃvatsarasya dhāyasā śimībhiśimyaṃtu tvā | daivyā adhvaravastā bhyaṃtu vi ca śāsatu | gātrāṇi parvaśaste śimāḥ kṛṇvaṃtu śimyaṃtaḥ ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 5-2-12)

"O sacrificial horse, who cuts you up? Who, putting the cutting instrument into your limbs, separates them one from another?" Having asked in the form of questions, it then says: "May the seasons, spring and the rest, cut you up at their fixed times and separate your limbs; may they cut you up with the power of the year." This statement is the basis of Sāyaṇa's first explanation. Secondly, the statement "May the Adhvaryu of the world of the gods cut you up and separate your limbs" is favourable to Sāyaṇa's second explanation. यद्वा । ऋतुः निश्यंकविशसनाय गंता शमिता । (*yadvā | ṛtuḥ niśyaṃkaviśasanāya gaṃtā śamitā |*) [as supplied: *niśyaṅka*] — he has explained it as "the Śamitṛ who goes to cut up with a firm mind".

ದ್ವಾ ಯಂತಾರಾ—नियमितारौ अहोरात्रे देवौ द्यावापृथिव्यौ वा । (*niyamitārau ahorātre devau dyāvāpṛthivyau vā |*) It is explained as either day and night, or heaven and earth.

अहर्वा अश्वं पुरस्तान्महिमान्वजायत तस्य पूर्वे समुद्रे योन् रात्रिरेनं पश्चान्महिमान्वजायत तस्यापरे समुद्रे योनिरेत् वा अश्वं महिमानावभितः संबभूवतुः । (*aharvā aśvaṃ purastānmahimānvajāyata tasya pūrve samudre yon rātrirenaṃ paścānmahimānvajāyata tasyāpare samudre yoniret vā aśvaṃ mahimānāvabhitaḥ saṃbabhūvatuḥ |*) (Bṛ. U. 1-1)

"Day, in front of this horse, makes known its greatness. Night, behind it, makes known its greatness." This passage describes both the individual Aśvamedha and Prajāpati's universal sacrifice, famous as the Aśvamedha. If the individual sacrifice is examined: in front of the sacrificial animal a cup (*graha*) made of gold is placed, and behind it a cup made of silver; the meaning is that these two reflect day and night by their colours. From the spiritual point of view Śaṅkarācārya, explaining the meaning of this passage, says:

### Printed p. 216
*Running head: 216 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೦] (as supplied; ಆ. for ಅ., and ೧೬೦ where ೧೬೨ is expected).*

अहरश्वं पुरस्तान्महिमान्वजायतेति कथं ? अश्वस्य प्रजापतित्वात् प्रजापतिर्ह्यादित्यादि लक्षणोऽह्ना लक्ष्यते । (*aharaśvaṃ purastānmahimānvajāyateti kathaṃ ? aśvasya prajāpatitvāt prajāpatirhyādityādi lakṣaṇo 'hnā lakṣyate |*) (Śa. Bhā. 1-1)

"The horse belongs to Prajāpati. Prajāpati, having the characteristics of the sun and so on, is indicated by 'day'": explaining thus and so on, he says that the meaning is that day and night both make known the greatness of the horse. In Sāyaṇa's explanation also this same meaning comes.

ಋತುಥಾ ಕೃಣೋಮಿ—for the word *ṛtudhā* the meaning given is only "in season" (काले (*kāle*)). Its intention is clear in the passage of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

अर्धमासाः परूग्ंषि ते मासाश्च्यंतु शिम्यंतः । आहोरात्राणि मरुतो विलिष्टं सूदयंतु ते ॥ (*ardhamāsāḥ parūgṃṣi te māsāścyaṃtu śimyaṃtaḥ | āhorātrāṇi maruto viliṣṭaṃ sūdayaṃtu te ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 5-2-12) [as supplied: *ತ್ರೈ. ಸಂ*, without the full stop]

So it is stated. But in the present Ṛk, with *kṛṇomi* the sacrificer, making himself the cause of the cutting, speaks of himself.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammatical process, transcribed, not explained)**

ವಿಶಸ್ತಾ—root *śas* in the sense of injury. In the agent sense the suffix *tṛc*. As it has a *c*-marker, it becomes final-acute.

ಯಂತಾರಾ—root *yama* in the sense of stopping. As before, the suffix *tṛc*. As it has a *c*-marker, the final-acute accent comes. *Anusvāra* and homorganic nasal for the *m* of the root. By सुपां सुलुक्— (*supāṃ suluk—*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) the substitution of *ā* for the dual ending. In *dvā* also the *ā*-substitution is the same.

ತಥ ಋತುಃ—when it is *tathā + ṛtuḥ*, by ऋत्यकः (*ṛtyakaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 9-1-128 [as supplied; the print has 9 for the adhyāya]) the natural form (*prakṛtibhāva*) and shortening [as supplied: ಪ್ರಸ್ವ] come.

ಯಾ—by शेश्छंदसि बहुलम्— (*śeśchaṃdasi bahulam—*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70) elision of *śi*.

ಋತುಥಾ—by था हेत् च छंदसि (*thā het ca chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 5-3-29) the suffix *dhā* prescribed there comes, by *vyatyaya*, to the word *ṛtu* also.

ಕೃಣೋಮಿ—because of the connection with *yad* the *nighāta* accent does not come. By the accent of the verbal-class suffix it becomes middle-acute.

ತಾತಾ—when it is *tāni tāni*, by शेश्छंदसि बहुलम् (*śeśchaṃdasi bahulam*) elision of *śi*. In the sense of repetition (*vīpsā*) doubling comes. The following *āmreḍita* receives the unaccented accent.

ಜುಹೋಮಿ—root *hu* in the senses of giving and eating. *Śluk* for the verbal-class suffix; doubling and the rest for the root. Because it stands after a non-finite word [as supplied: *atijanta*], the *nighāta* accent comes. ॥ 19 ॥

### Printed p. 217
*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 10] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 217 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 20
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> मा त्वा॑ तपत्प्रि॒य आ॒त्मापि॒यंतं॒ मा स्वधि॑तिस्त॒न्व१॒॑ १ आ ति॑ष्ठिपत्ते ।
> मा ते॑ गृ॒ध्नुर॑विश॒स्ताति॒हाय॑ छि॒द्रा गात्रा॑ण्य॒सिना॒ मिथू॑ कः
> ॥२०॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> मा । त्वा । त प त् । प्रियः। आत्मा । अपियंतं । मा । स्वधितिः । तन्वः । आ । तिस्थि पत् ।
> ते ।
> मा । ते । गृध्नुः । अविशस्ता । अतिहाय । छिद्रा । गात्राणि । असिना । मिथु । करितिकः ॥ २० ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> हे अश्व त्वा त्वामपियंतं देवान्प्रति गच्छंतं प्रिय आत्मा भोगायतनत्वात्तव प्रियतरो देहो मा तसत् । तप्तं मा कार्षित् । वियोगजनिता व्यथा मा
> भूदित्यर्थः । स्वधितिः शस्त्रं ते तव तन्वस्तनूरंगानि मा तिष्ठिपत् । त्वयि चिरमास्थितानि
> मा कार्षित् ॥ स्थापयतेर्लुजि तिष्ठते रित् । पा. ७-४-५. । इतीत्वं ॥ तथा ते तव
> गात्राणि गृध्नुः केवलमांसग्रहणेप्सुरविशस्ता विशसनेकुशलः शमितातिहाय न्यूनातिरेकभावेन तत्तदंगमतिक्रम्य मध्यॆ मिथु मिथ्या व्यर्थमसिना छिद्रा छिद्राणि तिर्यक्छिन्नानि मा कः । मा कार्षित् ॥ करोतेर्लुजि मंत्रे घसेति चेर्लुक् । गुणः ।
> हल्जाब्बि इति तलोपः ॥
*Gist:* O horse, let neither your own dear body grieve, nor the knife linger in your limbs, nor the unskilled, greedy slaughterer hack your limbs.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
(हे अश्व (*he aśva*)—O horse); अपियंतं (*apiyaṃtaṃ*)—going towards the gods, to them; त्वा (*tvā*)—you; प्रियः (*priyaḥ*)—your beloved; आत्मा (*ātmā*)—body; मा तपत् (*mā tapat*)—let it not cause to grieve; स्वधितिः (*svadhitiḥ*)—the knife, the instrument of cutting; ते (*te*)—your; तन्नः (*tannaḥ*)—limbs (in the limbs) *[as supplied: ತನ್ನಃ; the bhāṣya has ತನ್ವಃ]*; मा आ अतिष्ठत् (*mā ā atiṣṭhat*)—let it not stay long; गृध्नुः (*gṛdhnuḥ*)—one desirous of flesh alone; अविशस्ता (*aviśastā*)—one without fine experience in cutting, the slaughterer (*śamitṛ*); अतिहाय (*atihāya*)—leaving the proper places, roughly; ते (*te*)—your; गात्राणि (*gātrāṇi*)—members; असिना (*asinā*)—with his knife; मिथु (*mithu*)—needlessly; छिद्रा (*chidrā*)—as pieces; माकः (*mākaḥ*)—let him not make—

### Printed p. 218
*Running head: 218 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, as you go towards the gods, let your beloved body not make you grieve; let the knife, the instrument of cutting, not linger long in your limbs. Let the slaughterer, who is desirous of flesh alone and without fine experience in cutting, not, leaving the proper places in your body, roughly make your members, with his knife, into needless pieces.

**Printed English**
Let not your precious body grieve you, who are going verily (to the gods): let not the axe linger in your body: let not the greedy and unskilful (immolator), missing the members, mangle your limbs needlessly with his knife.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
Because the horse is to go and join the gods, and because a place better than the earth will be obtained, it is suggested to it that it need not grieve at leaving its present body. Further, since pain to the body may arise at the time of cutting with the knife (*asi*), it is also prayed that no kind of injury may arise from the carelessness of the slaughterer, from his desire for flesh, or from his using the knife roughly and without understanding. The same is said in the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa also:

न वा उ एतन्म्रियते न रिष्यसीति । प्रश्वासयत्येवैनं तद्देवाग्० इदेषि पथिभिः सुगेभिरिति देवयानानेवैनं पथो दर्शयति यत्रासते सुकृतो यत्र ते ययुरिति सुकृद्भिरेवैनग्० सलोकं करोति तत्र त्वा देवः सविता दधात्विति सवितैवैनग्० स्वर्गॆ लोके दधाति प्रजापतये त्वा जुष्टं प्रोक्षामीत्युपाग्० श्वथोपगृह्णाति (*na vā u etanmriyate na riṣyasīti | praśvāsayatyevainaṃ taddevāg0 ideṣi pathibhiḥ sugebhiriti devayānānevainaṃ patho darśayati yatrāsate sukṛto yatra te yayuriti sukṛdbhirevainag0 salokaṃ karoti tatra tvā devaḥ savitā dadhātviti savitaivainag0 svargè loke dadhāti prajāpataye tvā juṣṭaṃ prokṣāmītyupāg0 śvathopagṛhṇāti*) (Śa. Brā. 13-2-7-12)

"O horse, there is no death for you (for your self). Let there be no injury to you" — reciting thus the sentence of the Ṛk-saṃhitā (1-162-21), it encourages the horse; showing "On easy paths you go to the world of the gods" it points out the easy way to the world of the gods; saying "Where the doers of good deeds dwell, where all the doers of good deeds go", it says that the world of the doers of good deeds will be obtained by the horse also; and uttering "the world to which Savitṛ carries you, you shall go", he sprinkles — thus the procedure stated above is conveyed. Agni, Vāyu and Sūrya having been sacrificial animals and all of them having obtained the highest world, the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa has shown this as an example also:

अग्निः पशुरासीत् । तेनायजंत । स एतं लोकमजयद्यस्मिन्नग्निः स ते लोको भविष्यति तं जेष्यसि पिबैता अप इति यावानग्निर्विजयो यावांल्लोको यावदैश्वर्यं तावांस्ते विजयस्तावां लोकस्तावदैश्वर्यं भविष्यतीत्येवैनं तदाह (*agniḥ paśurāsīt | tenāyajaṃta | sa etaṃ lokamajayadyasminnagniḥ sa te loko bhaviṣyati taṃ jeṣyasi pibaitā apa iti yāvānagnirvijayo yāvāṃlloko yāvadaiśvaryaṃ tāvāṃste vijayastāvāṃ lokastāvadaiśvaryaṃ bhaviṣyatītyevainaṃ tadāha*) (Śa. Brā. 13-7-2-13) *[as supplied: the reference reads 13-7-2-13, while the neighbouring references have a different pattern]*

"Agni was a sacrificial animal, and after he was offered as an oblation, he obtained all the worlds over which Agni now exercises lordship. O horse, that same world will be obtained by you too. As great as is Agni's victory, as extensive as is his lordship, just so great will be your victory and your lordship" — and:

### Printed p. 219
*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 10] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 219 (as supplied).*

वायुः पशुरासीत् । तेनायजंत स एतं लोकमजयद्यस्मिन्वायुः स ते लोको भविष्यति तं जेष्यसि पिबैता अप इति यावान्वायोर्विजयो (*vāyuḥ paśurāsīt | tenāyajaṃta sa etaṃ lokamajayadyasminvāyuḥ sa te loko bhaviṣyati taṃ jeṣyasi pibaitā apa iti yāvānvāyorvijayo*).... .... .... .... .... ॥ *[the ellipsis dots are as supplied]* (Śa. Brā. 13-2-7-14)

Vāyu too was a sacrificial animal. After that animal was offered in the sacrifice in the form of an oblation, he obtained the world over which Vāyu now exercises lordship. O horse, for you too that same victory and that same lordship will be obtained.

सूर्यः पशुरासीत् । तेनायजंत (*sūryaḥ paśurāsīt | tenāyajaṃta*) .................... । *[the ellipsis dots are as supplied]* (Śa. Brā. 13-2-7-15)

In the same way Sūrya too was an animal. To him also the position of being lord of all the worlds has now been obtained. Therefore, "you also, offered as an oblation in the sacrifice, obtain that same world and greatness" — the sentence thus encouraging the horse, with the mantra (1-162-21) of this same part of the Ṛk-saṃhitā recited, prays that no injury may befall the animal and that a good destination may be obtained.

आत्मा (*ātmā*)—this word is used here as a synonym for the body. It means a body dear as one's self.

गृध्नुः (*gṛdhnuḥ*)—it is an epithet of the *śamitṛ*. It prays that the slaughterer, who, not knowing the greatness of the sacrifice, desires only the flesh of the horse and does the cutting, be warned, and that he may not cut you roughly.

### Printed p. 220
*Running head: 220 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨ ಸೂ೧೬೦] (as supplied: the sūkta number is printed 160 here, and the abbreviation mark is missing).*

*Grammar (transcribed, not explained):*

तपत् (*tapat*)—root *tapa*, in the sense of heat (*saṃtāpa*). By स्मोत्तरेलज्च (*smottarelajca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-176) the *laṅ* that is prescribed comes even without connection with *sma*. Form ending in the third person singular. Because of connection with *māṅ* the augment *aṭ* does not come. As a finite verb, the *nighāta* accent comes.

अपियंतम् (*apiyaṃtam*)—root *iṇ* in the sense of going. *Śatṛ* substituted for *laṭ*. By इणो यण् (*iṇo yaṇ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-81) *yaṇ* is substituted for the root. The suffix has the first-acute accent. When it forms a *prādi* compound, the *kṛt*-final member has its own natural accent. Form ending in the accusative singular.

तन्वः (*tanvaḥ*)—the word *tanu*. When the accusative plural *śas* follows, by तन्नादीनां छंदस्युपसंख्यानम् (*tannādīnāṃ chaṃdasyupasaṃkhyānam*) *yaṇ* is substituted. By उदात्तस्वंतयोर्यणः (*udāttasvaṃtayoryaṇaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-4) the *svarita* final accent comes. *[as supplied: ತನ್ನಾದೀನಾಂ]*

तिष्ठिपत् (*tiṣṭhipat*)—root *ṣṭhā* in the sense of cessation of movement. To it, after the causative (*ṇijanta*), *luṅ*. In the third person singular *tip*. Elision of *i*; by आर्तिह्रीव्ली (*ārtihrīvlī*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-36) the augment *puk*. By तिष्ठतेरित् (*tiṣṭhaterit*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-5) *i* for the penultimate. *Caṅ* substituted for *cli*. By *caṅi* doubling. By शर्पूर्वाःखयः (*śarpūrvāḥkhayaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-61) the *ta* remains in the reduplicate. Because of connection with *māṅ*, the augment *aṭ* does not come. As a finite verb the *nighāta* accent comes.

गृध्नुः (*gṛdhnuḥ*)—root *gṛdhu* in the sense of desire. By तृसिगृधिधृषिक्षिपेः क्नुः (*tṛsigṛdhidhṛṣikṣipeḥ knuḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-140) the suffix *knu*. Because it has a *k*, the *guṇa* of the light penultimate does not come. By the accent of the suffix, it becomes final-acute.

अतिहाय (*atihāya*)—root *hāk* in the sense of abandoning. By समासेनञ् (*samāsenañ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-37) *lyap* is substituted for the suffix *ktvā*. Because it has a *p*, the accent of the root remains. In the compound, the *kṛt*-final member has its natural accent.

छिद्रा (*chidrā*)—root *chidir* in the sense of splitting in two. The Uṇādi suffix *rak*. In the neuter plural, by शेश्छंदसि बहुलम् (*śeśchaṃdasi bahulam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70) elision of *śi*.

कः (*kaḥ*)—root *ḍukṛñ* in the sense of doing. *Luṅ*, the suffix *tip* in the third person singular. By इतश्च (*itaśca*) elision of *i*. By मंत्रेघसहवर्ण (*maṃtreghasahavarṇa*) (Pā. Sū. 2-4-80) *luk* for *cli*. On account of *tip*, *guṇa* for the *i*-vowel of the root; it comes as *ra* [as supplied: ರಸರವಾಗಿ] *[sic]*. By हल्ज्याभ्यो (*haljyābhyo*) the *tip* that is attached is elided. *Visarga* for the *repha*. By नमाज्‌योगे (*namājyoge*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-74) the augment *aṭ* does not come. As a finite verb, the *nighāta* accent comes. ॥ 20 ॥

### Printed p. 221
*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 10] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 221 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 21
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> न वा उ॑ ए॒तन्म्रि॑यसे॒ न रि॑ष्यसि दे॒वा इदे॑षि प॒थिभिः॑ सु॒गेभिः॑।
> हरी॑ ते॒ युंजा॒ पृष॑ती अभूता॒मुपा॑स्थाद्वा॒जी धु॒रि रास॑भस्य ॥ २० ॥

> *na vā u etanmriyase na riṣyasi devā ideṣi pathibhiḥ sugebhiḥ|*
> *harī te yuṃjā pṛṣatī abhūtāmupāsthādvājī dhuri rāsabhasya ‖ 20 ‖*
*[As supplied: the verse-end number after the Saṃhitā text is printed ॥ ೨೦ ॥ (20), though the Pada-pāṭha that follows ends with ॥ ೨೧ ॥ (21).]*
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> न । वै । ऊं इति । एतत् । म्रियसे । न । रिष्यसि । देवान् । इत् । एषि । पथिभिः ।
> सुगेभिः ।
> हरी इति । ते ! युंजा । पृषती इति । अभूतां । उप । अस्फात् । वाजी । धुरि ।
> रासभस्य ॥ २१ ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> न वा उ नैव खल्वेतन्म्रियसे । वैशब्द एवकारार्थः । उ इत्यवधारणे । नैवेदानीमितराश्ववन्मृतो भवसि देवत्वप्राप्तेर्वक्ष्यमाणत्वात् । आत एव न रिष्यसि-। न
> हिंस्यसे । व्यर्थहिंसाया अभावात् । ननु प्रत्यक्षतो मृतिरवयवनाशश्च दृश्यते
> कथमेवमुच्यत इति उच्यते । सुगेभिः पथिभिः शोभनगमनसाधनैर्मार्गॆर्देवयानलक्षणैर्देवानिदेषि । देवानेव प्राप्नोषि । अतो युक्तैषोक्तिः । कैः
> साधनैरिति तत्राह । ते त्वां वोढुं हरी एतन्नामानाविंद्रस्याश्वा ते तव युंजा
> रथे युक्तावभूतां । भविष्यतः । आशंसारूपत्वादूतार्थनिर्देशः । हरी इंद्रस्यॆति
> निरुक्तं । तथा पृषती पृषत्यौ मरुतां वाहनभूतौ युंजाभूतां । युक्तौ भविष्यतः । पृषत्यो मरुतामिति च निरुक्तं ॥ युजेर्युत्विगित्यादिना क्विन् । युजेरसमासे । पा. ७-१- ७१ । इति नुम् । सुपां सुलुगित्याकारः । तथा रासभस्याश्विवाहनस्यॆतन्नामकस्याश्वस्य धुरि स्थाने वर्तमानः कश्चिद्वाज्युपास्थात् । उपस्थास्यति । वहतीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । रासभस्य भानोधुरि वर्त्मनि युक्तो वाज्यश्व उपस्थास्यति । देवत्वं
> गच्छतस्तव गमनायेंद्रादयः स्वस्ववाहनानि प्रेषयंतीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । तत्तद्देवभावमापन्नं त्वां तानि तानि वाहनानि वहंति ॥
*Gist:* You do not die; you are not harmed; by easy paths you go to the gods. Horses of Indra, the Maruts' mares, and another steed will be yoked to carry you.

### Printed p. 222
*Running head: 222 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
एतत् (*etat*)—at this time of the sacrifice; न वा उ म्रियसे (*na vā u mriyase*)—(as other horses meet death) you certainly do not meet death at all; न रिष्यसि (*na riṣyasi*)—you are not harmed either; सुगेभिः (*sugebhiḥ*)—suitable for easy travelling; पथिभिः (*pathibhiḥ*)—by paths, that is, by paths having the character of the *devayāna*; देवा इत् (*devā it*)—to the gods alone; एषि (*eṣi*)—you go; ते (*te*)—you (to carry); हरी (*harī*)—the two horses of Indra named *Harī*; युंजा (*yuṃjā*)—yoked together to the car; अभूतां (*abhūtāṃ*)—become; पृषती (*pṛṣatī*)—the vehicles of the Maruts (are also yoked to the car); रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*)—of the horse of this name, the vehicle of the Aśvin deities, that is yoked; धुरि (*dhuri*)—in the region of the yoke; वाजी (*vājī*)—another horse; उपास्थात् (*upāsthāt*)—yoked (carries you). [as supplied: the Kannada text writes ಮೈತಿಯನ್ನು for ಮೃತಿಯನ್ನು]

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, at this time of the sacrifice you do not meet a low death in the manner in which other horses meet death. And no injury will come to you from the cutting. By the *devayāna* path, suitable for easy travelling, you reach the gods alone. To carry you, the horses of Indra, named *Harī*, are yoked together to the car. The vehicle of the Maruts too is made ready, joined to the car. In the region of the yoke where the *rāsabha*, the vehicle of the Aśvin deities, is yoked, another horse, yoked, stands ready to carry you. *[as supplied: ಆಶ್ವಗಳು for ಅಶ್ವಗಳು]*

**Printed English**
Verily at this moment you do not die; nor are you harmed; for you go by auspicious paths to the gods. The horses of Indra, the steeds of the Maruts shall be yoked (to their cars), and a courser shall be placed in the shaft of the ass of the Aswins (to bear you to heaven).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
In this Ṛk two main points are indicated. First, after the sacrificial horse has been offered in the form of an oblation, the death that befalls other worldly animals does not befall it; a distinct state is obtained, one that makes it immortal along with the gods. As soon as the offering is made, its movement is attained on the path by which it goes and joins the gods. Here, for सुगेभिः पथिभिः (*sugebhiḥ pathibhiḥ*) the commentator has explained शोभनगमनसाधनैर्मार्गैः देवयानलक्षणैः (*śobhanagamanasādhanairmārgaiḥ devayānalakṣaṇaiḥ*) as "by paths suitable for the movement of the gods". This path of the *devayāna* … *[the printed page ends here; the sentence continues on the next page]*

### Printed p. 223
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. .೩ ವ. ೧೦.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 223 (as supplied).*

*[Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 21 from the previous page]* ...is not attainable by all beings; it is attainable only by the virtuous. For that very reason it is explained that Indra's Haris (bay horses), the Pṛṣatīs (spotted mares) of the Maruts, and the ass of the Aśvins, or its representative, take this horse along, and that it procures for it the most excellent station. Sāyaṇa, for रासभस्य धुरि (*rāsabhasya dhuri*), has given the meaning भानोः धुरि वर्त्मनि युक्तः वाजी अश्वः (*bhānoḥ dhuri vartmani yuktaḥ vājī aśvaḥ*), that is, "the courser, the horse, yoked on the path of the Sun's movement". If we examine what the Sun's path of movement is:

आ कृष्णेन रजसा वर्तमानो निवेशयन्नमृतं मर्त्यं च । हिरण्ययेन सविता रथेनादेवो याति भुवनानि पश्यन् (*ā kṛṣṇena rajasā vartamāno niveśayannamṛtaṃ martyaṃ ca | hiraṇyayena savitā rathenādevo yāti bhuvanāni paśyan*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-35-2)

याति देवः प्रवता यात्युद्वता याति शुभ्राभ्यां यजतो हरिभ्यां । आ देवो याति सविता परावतोप विश्वा दुरिता बाधमानः (*yāti devaḥ pravatā yātyudvatā yāti śubhrābhyāṃ yajato haribhyāṃ | ā devo yāti savitā parāvatopa viśvā duritā bādhamānaḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-35-3)

In Ṛks such as these the bright and eternal nature of the Sun's movement, as described there, is made known here also. This path of the Sun's movement is itself the path that carries the virtuous to Brahmaloka. In the Upaniṣads and similar texts it is called *devayāna*. In the Ṛk-saṃhitā, however, the word *devayāna* has the ordinary meaning "the path on which the gods travel"; only in some places is the idea described in the Upaniṣads also present.

परं मृत्यो अनु परेहि पंथां यस्ते स्व इतरो देवयानात् । चक्षुष्मते शृण्वते ते ब्रवीमि मा नः प्रजां रीरिषो मोत वीरान् (*paraṃ mṛtyo anu parehi paṃthāṃ yaste sva itaro devayānāt | cakṣuṣmate śṛṇvate te bravīmi mā naḥ prajāṃ rīriṣo mota vīrān*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-18-1)

In Ṛks such as these the *devayāna* is declared to be a path free from death. Since it is stated in the Ṛk-saṃhitā itself that on this path Savitṛ leads the virtuous to the deathless world of merit, this meaning also is fitting.

ये ते पंथाः सवितः पूर्व्यासोरेणवः सुकृता अंतरिक्षे । तेभिर्नो अद्य पथिभिः सुगेभी रक्षा च नो अधि च ब्रूहि देव (*ye te paṃthāḥ savitaḥ pūrvyāsoreṇavaḥ sukṛtā aṃtarikṣe | tebhirno adya pathibhiḥ sugebhī rakṣā ca no adhi ca brūhi deva*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-35-11)

यत्रासते सुकृतो यत्र ते ययुस्तत्र त्वा देवः सविता दधातु (*yatrāsate sukṛto yatra te yayustatra tvā devaḥ savitā dadhātu*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-17-4)

### Printed p. 224
*Running head: 224 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

In Ṛks such as these, since Savitṛ is the god who leads the virtuous along the good path, it is prayed that he lead the sacrificers who perform the sacrifice, and the animals that take part in the sacrificial rite, along that same path, which is pure, free from dust (*rajas*), and free from death. This same path is called in the Upaniṣads the *arcirādi* path. This path is described as one on which divine souls who reach Brahmaloka after death have no return.

तत्पुरुषोऽमानवः स एनान्ब्रह्म गमयत्येष देवपथो ब्रह्मपथ एतेन प्रति पद्यमाना इमं मानवमावर्तं नावर्तंते नावर्तंते (*tatpuruṣo 'mānavaḥ sa enānbrahma gamayatyeṣa devapatho brahmapatha etena prati padyamānā imaṃ mānavamāvartaṃ nāvartaṃte nāvartaṃte*)
(Bhā. U. 4-15-5)

*[Abbreviation "Bhā. U." as supplied.]* Just as it is proclaimed that there is no return for those who go to the world of the gods, having obtained such a superhuman (*amānuṣa*) spirit, the same idea is stated in the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad also:

सूर्यद्वारेण ते विरजाः प्रयांति यत्रामृतः स पुरुषो ह्यव्ययात्मा (*sūryadvāreṇa te virajāḥ prayāṃti yatrāmṛtaḥ sa puruṣo hyavyayātmā*)
(Muṃ. U. 2-11)

*[Abbreviation "Muṃ. U." as supplied.]* In passages such as these also the greatness of this path is stated. It is said that, leading the horse along such an excellent path, they will bring it to a good state. That is indicated here by सुगेभिः पथिभिः (*sugebhiḥ pathibhiḥ*).

रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*)—भानोः (*bhānoḥ*). On what authority the meaning "the Sun" has been stated for the word रासभ (*rāsabha*) is hard to show by giving its etymology. If proper authority is found, this word will be explained later.

When, after being offered in the form of an oblation, this horse is moving on the path of the gods, the question arises why Indra's vehicle, the Maruts' vehicle and the vehicle of the Aśvin gods should come to take it along. As the answer to this, Sāyaṇa says तत्तद्देवभावमापन्नं त्वां तानि तानि वाहनानि वहंति (*tattaddevabhāvamāpannaṃ tvāṃ tāni tāni vāhanāni vahaṃti*) (preceded by यद्वा (*yadvā*), "or"): "the vehicles of the respective gods carry you who have attained the very nature of those gods."

हरी इंद्रस्य ; रासभावश्विनोः ; पृषत्यो मरुतां (*harī iṃdrasya ; rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ ; pṛṣatyo marutāṃ*) (Ni. 2-28): thus the vehicles of Indra and the other gods are well known.

*Grammar (transcribed, not explained):*

म्रियसे (*mriyase*)—the root मृज् (*mṛj*) in the sense of giving up life (as printed); the *tudādi* class, *laṭ*, second person singular; when थास् (*thās*) comes, the substitute से (*se*); by तुदादिभ्यः (*tudādibhyaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-77) the vikaraṇa श (*śa*). The text then breaks at the page end *[printed: "ಇಂ" at the end of the line; the next page begins "ಪರವಾದಾಗ"]*.

### Printed p. 225
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೦. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 225 (as supplied).*

*Grammar (continued):* ...when it follows, by रिज्शयग्लिज्जु (*rijśayaglijju*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-28) the substitute रिज् (*rij*) for the root; when it comes as a final substitute, by अचिश्नुधातुभ्रुवां (*aciśnudhātubhruvāṃ*) the substitution “ಇಯಜಾದೇಶ” (as printed). The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

रिष्यसि (*riṣyasi*)—the root रिष (*riṣa*) in the sense of injuring; *divādi* class. By *vyatyaya* (interchange) the active suffix in the passive. The form is *laṭ*, second person singular. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

एषि (*eṣi*)—the root इण् (*iṇ*) in the sense of going. सिप् (*sip*) for *laṭ*. By आदिप्रभृतिभ्यःशपः (*ādiprabhṛtibhyaḥśapaḥ*) *luk* for the vikaraṇa. By reason of सिप् (*sip*), *guṇa* for the root. By आदेशप्रत्यययोः (*ādeśapratyayayoḥ*) the change to ष (*ṣa*). The *nighāta* accent results.

सुगेभिः (*sugebhiḥ*)—the root गम्‌ल्य (*gamlya*) *[as supplied]* in the sense of going. By सुदुरोरधिकरणे (*suduroradhikaraṇe*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-48-3, as supplied) the suffix ड (*ḍa*). By the force of the ड (*ḍa*)-marker, elision of the root's टि (*ṭi*). The *kṛd-uttarapada-prakṛti* accent results. When भिस् (*bhis*) follows, by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the substitution ऐस् (*ais*) is optional and therefore does not occur. The change to ए (*e*).

युञ्जा (*yuñjā*)—the root युजिर् (*yujir*) in the sense of joining. The root being by itself (without a prefix), by ऋत्विक् दधृक् (*ṛtvik dadhṛk*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-59) the suffix क्विन् (*kvin*). When this follows, by युजेरसमासे (*yujerasamāse*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-71) the augment (printed “ನುಮಾಗಮ”). Because it is marked with म् (*m*) it comes after the last vowel; for it, the *anusvāra* and the following homorganic (*parasavarṇa*). By सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) the substitute आ (*ā*) for the case-ending.

अस्थात् (*asthāt*)—the root ष्ठा (*ṣṭhā*) in the sense of cessation of motion. *Luṅ* in the Vedic manner. In the third person singular, elision of the इ (*i*) of तिप् (*tip*). When सिच् (*sic*) comes for “ಚ್ಚಿ” (as printed), by गातिस्थाघुपा (*gātisthāghupā*) (Pā. Sū. 2-4-77) *luk* for it. The augment (printed “ಅಡಾಗಮ”) for the stem. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*)—the root रासृ (*rāsṛ*) in the sense of sound. By रासिवल्लिभ्यां च (*rāsivallibhyāṃ ca*) (U. Sū. 4-405) the suffix अभच् (*abhac*). By चितः (*citaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. "ಟು೧-೧೬೩", as supplied; the number is garbled) the final-acute accent results. ॥ 21 ॥

*[As supplied, a garbled label: "। ಸಂಹಕಾರ ॥" (the script read it as the heading Saṃhitā-pāṭha).]*

#### Ṛk 22
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> सु॒गव्यं॑ नो वा॒जी स्वश्वं॑ पुं॒सः पु॒त्रौ उ॒त वि॑श्वा॒पुषं॒॑
> रयिं ।
> अ॒ना॒गा॒स्त्वं नो॒ अदि॑तिः कृणोतु क्ष॒त्रं नो॒ अश्वो॑ वनतां॒॑
> हविष्मान् ॥ २२ ॥
*[Stray line as supplied: [15]                                                                 Vol. 12]]*

### Printed p. 226
*Running head: 226 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied).*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> सुगव्यं । नः । वाजी । सुअश्वं । पुंसः । पुत्रान् । उत । विश्वपुषं । रयिं ।
> अनागात्वं । नः । अदितिः । कृणोतु । क्षत्रं । नः । अश्वः । वनतां । हविष्मान् ॥ २२ ॥
*[As supplied, a garbled label: "ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯ c" (the script read it as the heading Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya).]*
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> उपाकरणकालेवघ्राणावर्तने यद्यश्वो न कुर्यात् तदैषा नैमित्तिकी
> यजमानवाचनार्था भवति । तथा च सूत्रितं । सुगव्यं नो वाजी स्वश्वमिति यजमानं वाचयेत् । आ. १०-८ । इति ॥
> वाज्यत्व आलभ्यमानो नोऽस्माकं सुगव्यं शोभनेन गोसमूहेन युक्तं
> विश्वापुषं रयिं विश्वस्य पोषकं धनं कृणोतु । करोतु ॥ खलगोरथात् । सा
> ४-२-५० । इति समूहार्थे यत् । छांदसमुत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं । यद्वा । सामूहिकस्याणः प्रसंगे सर्वत्र गोरजादिप्रत्ययप्रसंग इति यत् । का. ४-३-१६० । तदंतस्याद्युदात्तं द्वच्छंदसीत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥ तथा स्वश्वं शोभनाश्वसमूहयुक्तं । तथा पुंसः पुत्रान् । पुत्रशब्दः स्त्रीपुमपत्यसाधारणः । अतः पुमपत्यानीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । पुंसो वध्वादीन् पुत्रान् पुरुरक्षकानात्मजान् । स्त्रैपत्यानि पुमपत्यानि चेत्यर्थः । उतापि च विश्वापुषं रयिं विश्वपोषणसमर्थं धनं । किंच
> नोऽस्मानदितिरदीनोऽश्वोऽनागास्त्वं कृणोतु । सर्वतो निष्पासत्वं करोतु -
> उक्तानां फलानां न्यूनातिरेकजनितपापक्षयाभावेऽसंभवादपापत्वं प्रार्थ्यते ।
> किंच हविष्मान् हविर्भूतावयवोपेतोऽश्वो नोऽस्माकं क्षत्रं बलं क्षात्रं
> तेजो वनतां संभजतां । कुरुतां ॥
*Gist:* If the horse does not sniff at the upākaraṇa, the sacrificer recites this Ṛk; may the horse give us cows, horses, sons, wealth, freedom from sin, and strength.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
वाजी (*vājī*)—this sacrificial horse; नः (*naḥ*)—to us; सुगव्यं (*sugavyaṃ*)—accompanied by excellent cows; स्वत्वं (*svatvaṃ*) *[as supplied]*—accompanied by excellent horses; पुंसः (*puṃsaḥ*)—of male form; पुत्रान् (*putrān*)—sons; उत (*uta*)—and; विश्वापुषं (*viśvāpuṣaṃ*)—nourishing all; रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth; कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—may it procure and give; अदितिः (*aditiḥ*)—possessing dignity; अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—the horse; नः (*naḥ*)—to us; अनागात्त्वं (*anāgāttvaṃ*)—the state free from sin; (कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—may it bring about); हविष्मान् (*haviṣmān*)—this horse, which is offered in the form of an oblation; नः (*naḥ*)—to us; क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—strength; वनतां (*vanatāṃ*)—may it supply.

### Printed p. 227
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೦.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 227 (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
May this sacrificial horse procure for us sons, accompanied by excellent cows, accompanied by excellent horses, and of male form, and wealth that nourishes all. May this horse, which possesses dignity, bring about for us a state free from sin. May this horse, which is offered in the form of an oblation, supply us with strength.

**Printed English**
May this horse bring to us all-sustaining wealth, with abundance of cows, of excellent horses, and of male offspring : may the spirited steed bring us exemption from wickedness: may this horse, offered in oblation, procure for us bodily vigor.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
If at the time of the upākaraṇa the sacrificial horse does not sniff (*avaghrāṇa*), then this Ṛk beginning सुगव्यं नो वाजीस्वश्वं (*sugavyaṃ no vājīsvaśvaṃ*) is to be recited by the sacrificer. This is set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra सुगव्यं नो वाजे।स्वश्वमिति यजमानं वाचयेत् (*sugavyaṃ no vāje|svaśvamiti yajamānaṃ vācayet*) *[as supplied: ವಾಜೇ।, where the Saṃhitā has ವಾಜೀ]* (Ā. 10-8).

Two points in this Ṛk are noteworthy. The first is the fruit of the Aśvamedha. The second is that this fruit must be joined with right action; if there is a deviation in the action, the fruit is not obtained. Therefore, having first prayed for wealth accompanied by cows and the like, for the wealth of affection and for the *kṣātra* power, it is prayed together with it also, अनागास्त्वं कृणोतु (*anāgāstvaṃ kṛṇotu*), "may it make us free from sin". The fruit to be obtained by performing the Aśvamedha is described in this manner:

आस्य यजमानस्य वीरो जायतामिति । यजमानस्यैव प्रजायां वीर्यं दधाति तस्मात्पुरेजानस्य वीरो जज्ञे । निकामे निकामे नः पर्जन्यो वर्ष त्विति । निकामे निकामे वै तत्र पर्जन्यो वर्षति यत्रैनेन यज्ञेन यजंते फलवत्यो न ओषधयः पच्यंतामिति फलवत्यो वै तत्रौषधयः पच्यंते यत्रैतेन यज्ञेन यजंते योगक्षेमो नः कल्पतामिति योग क्षेमो वै तत्र कल्पते यत्रैतेन यज्ञेन यजंते तस्माद्यतैतेन यज्ञेन यजंते कृप्तः प्रजानां योगक्षेमो भवति (*āsya yajamānasya vīro jāyatāmiti | yajamānasyaiva prajāyāṃ vīryaṃ dadhāti tasmātpurejānasya vīro jajñe | nikāme nikāme naḥ parjanyo varṣa tviti | nikāme nikāme vai tatra parjanyo varṣati yatrainena yajñena yajaṃte phalavatyo na oṣadhayaḥ pacyaṃtāmiti phalavatyo vai tatrauṣadhayaḥ pacyaṃte yatraitena yajñena yajaṃte yogakṣemo naḥ kalpatāmiti yoga kṣemo vai tatra kalpate yatraitena yajñena yajaṃte tasmādyataitena yajñena yajaṃte kṛptaḥ prajānāṃ yogakṣemo bhavati*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-9-9 to 10)

The same idea is stated in Tai. Brā. 3-8-13-3 also. Since it is established that a valiant son is born to one who has performed the Aśvamedha, let a valiant son be born to this sacrificer also. *[The sentence continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 228
*Running head: 228 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೨] (as supplied; "ಆ." where "ಅ." is usual).*

...Further, for such a sacrificer Parjanya pours rain and makes trees and the like heavy with fruit; and with no loss to the sacrificer's prosperity, all his wealth remains well protected. Therefore, since it is established that those who have performed this Aśvamedha obtain wealth in sons and wealth in fruit, and that all kinds of prosperity remain well protected, all these are prayed for here also.

क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—क्षत्रं बलं क्षात्रं तेजः (*kṣatraṃ balaṃ kṣātraṃ tejaḥ*). By this, the word is first given in general the meaning "strength", and then the distinctive feature of that strength is made known as *kṣātra* splendour. For performing the Aśvamedha also, complete *kṣātra* splendour is required, and the fruit of the Aśvamedha too is *kṣātra* splendour. One who lacks *kṣātra* splendour has no eligibility for the Aśvamedha at all; his former position also is lost.

राष्ट्रं वा अश्वमेधः । राष्ट्रे खलु ना एते न्यायच्छंते । योऽश्वं मेध्यग्ं रक्षंति । तेषां य उदृचं गच्छंति । राष्ट्रादेव ते राष्ट्रं गच्छंति । अथ य उदृचं न गच्छंति । राष्ट्रादेव ते व्यवच्छिद्यंते । परा वा एष सिच्यते । योऽबलोऽश्वमेधेन यजते यदमित्रा अश्वं विंदेरन् । हन्येतास्य यज्ञः (*rāṣṭraṃ vā aśvamedhaḥ | rāṣṭre khalu nā ete nyāyacchaṃte | yo 'śvaṃ medhyagṃ rakṣaṃti | teṣāṃ ya udṛcaṃ gacchaṃti | rāṣṭrādeva te rāṣṭraṃ gacchaṃti | atha ya udṛcaṃ na gacchaṃti | rāṣṭrādeva te vyavacchidyaṃte | parā vā eṣa sicyate | yo 'balo 'śvamedhena yajate yadamitrā aśvaṃ viṃderan | hanyetāsya yajñaḥ*)
(Tai. Brā. 3-8-9-4, 5)

The Aśvamedha is not a sacrifice of an individual. It is the emblem of the power of the nation, and is that which is to display the nation's power. The king who has the strength to protect the nation should perform this sacrifice. Until the time of the sacrifice he must be so strong as to be capable of protecting this horse. If a Kṣatriya has no such strength and other kings seize the horse, the sacrifice of such a Kṣatriya fails and his loss of position is certain: thus the necessity of *kṣatra* is stated. Since this is wholly identified with the strength of the Kṣatriya, its fruit also is united with *kṣātra* splendour alone.
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-6-3)

अधिपतिरस्यधिपतिं मा कुर्वधिपतिरहं प्रजानां भूयासमित्याह । अधिपतिमेवैनग्ं समानानां करोति (*adhipatirasyadhipatiṃ mā kurvadhipatirahaṃ prajānāṃ bhūyāsamityāha | adhipatimevainagṃ samānānāṃ karoti*)
(Tai. Brā. 3-9-16-2)

"O sacrificial horse, you are the overlord of all the sacrificers. By your grace may I also become the overlord of all my subjects": as it is prayed in this way, so here also it is prayed क्षत्रं नो वनतां (*kṣatraṃ no vanatāṃ*).

अनागास्त्वं करोतु (*anāgāstvaṃ karotu*)—may it make us free from sin. Since the Aśvamedha is an exceedingly difficult rite, a lapse in that rite may easily occur.

### Printed p. 229
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ಪ. ೧೦] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 229 (as supplied; "ಪ." where "ವ." is usual).*

*[Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 22 from the previous page]* The fragment printed at the head of this page, apparently the end of the Brāhmaṇa passage cited on the previous page and given without a reference, reads: प्र ना एष विभ्यो लोकेभ्यश्च वते । योश्वमेधेन यजते । (*pra nā eṣa vibhyo lokebhyaśca vate | yośvamedhena yajate |*) *[as supplied]*

Since many offences may occur in the conduct of this sacrifice, it is prescribed that, to avoid them, atonement be obtained by the immolation of the three animals beginning with the one for Agni. Moreover, in the mantras that serve as *yājyā* and *puronuvākyā* for the oblations known as the *mṛgāreṣṭi*, atonement for sin is also prayed for:

अग्नेर्मेन्वे प्रथमस्य प्रचेतसो .... .... .... .... स नो मुंचत्वग्‌हसः (*agnermenve prathamasya pracetaso .... .... .... .... sa no muṃcatvaghasaḥ*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 4-7-15)

In passages such as this, Agni and the other deities are prayed to make the sacrificers free from sin.

*Grammar (transcribed, not explained):*

सुगव्यम् (*sugavyam*)—शोभनानां गवां समूहः सुगव्यम् (*śobhanānāṃ gavāṃ samūhaḥ sugavyam*). By खल गोरथात् (*khala gorathāt*) (Pā. Sū. 4-2-50) the suffix यत् (*yat*) in the sense of collection. By वांतोयिप्रत्यये (*vāṃtoyipratyaye*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-79) the substitute printed “ಅವಾದೇಶ”. By यतोनावः (*yatonāvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-213) the initial-acute accent results. In a *prādi* compound the *uttarapada-prakṛti* accent results in the Vedic manner. Or: if अण् (*aṇ*) is obtained in the sense of collection, by गोरजादिप्रत्ययप्रसंगे (*gorajādipratyayaprasaṃge*) (Kā. 4-3-160) the suffix यत् (*yat*). When a word ending in यत् (*yat*) stands as the second member, by आद्युदात्तंद्व्यच्छंदसि (*ādyudāttaṃdvyacchaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-119) the accent on the first syllable of the second member results.

स्वश्यम् (*svaśyam*) *[as supplied]*—as before; when it is a *bahuvrīhi* compound the initial-acute accent of the second member results.

पुंसः (*puṃsaḥ*)—a form ending in the genitive singular. By सावेकाचस्तृतीयादिः (*sāvekācastṛtīyādiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-168) the case-ending receives the *udātta* accent.

विश्वापुषम् (*viśvāpuṣam*)—the root पुष (*puṣa*) in the sense of nourishing. The suffix क्विप् (*kvip*). By अन्येषामपिदृश्यते (*anyeṣāmapidṛśyate*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-137) lengthening of the first member at the time of the Saṃhitā. By गतिकारकोपपदात् कृत् (*gatikārakopapadāt kṛt*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-139) the *kṛd-uttarapada-prakṛti* accent results.

अनागास्त्वम् (*anāgāstvam*)—न विद्यते आगः यस्य अनागः (*na vidyate āgaḥ yasya anāgaḥ*), and the state of that is अनागास्त्वम् (*anāgāstvam*). By तस्यभावस्त्वतला (*tasyabhāvastvatalā*) (Pā. Sū. 5-1-119) the suffix त्व (*tva*). By the accent of the suffix the final-acute accent results.

वनताम् (*vanatām*)—the root वन (*vana*) in the sense of sharing. *Loṭ*, a form ending in the third person singular. Because it stands after a non-verb, the *nighāta* accent results.

### Printed p. 230
*Running head: 230 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied; "ಆ." where "ಅ." is usual).*

## Sūkta 163 (Maṇḍala 1)
*The hundred and sixty-third sūkta.*

Sāyaṇa's introduction: यदक्रंद इति त्रयोदशर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं दैर्घतमसं त्रैष्टुभं (*yadakraṃda iti trayodaśarcaṃ saptamaṃ sūktaṃ dairghatamasaṃ traiṣṭubhaṃ*) — "The sūkta beginning *yadakranda*, of thirteen Ṛks, the seventh sūkta, belonging to Dīrghatamas, in the Triṣṭubh metre. Because the horse is being praised, it has the horse as its deity. यदक्रंदः सप्नोनेत्यनुक्रमणिका (*yadakraṃdaḥ sapnonetyanukramaṇikā*) *[as supplied]* is the Anukramaṇikā." आश्वमेधिके मध्यमेऽहन्युपाकरणायावस्थितमश्वमाद्याभिरेकादशभिः स्तौति (*āśvamedhike madhyame 'hanyupākaraṇāyāvasthitamaśvamādyābhirekādaśabhiḥ stauti*) — "In the Aśvamedha, on the middle day, he praises with the first eleven Ṛks the horse that has been stationed for the upākaraṇa." And so it is stated in the sūtra: तथा च त्रिणि सुत्यानीति खंडे सूत्रितं । तमवस्थितमुपाकरणाय यदक्रंद इत्येकादशभिः स्तौत्यप्रणुवन् । (*tathā ca triṇi sutyānīti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ | tamavasthitamupākaraṇāya yadakraṃda ityekādaśabhiḥ stautyapraṇuvan |*) (Ā. 10-8), "in the section beginning 'three pressings': (the Hotṛ) praises that stationed (horse), for the upākaraṇa, with the eleven (Ṛks) beginning *yadakranda*; they respond."

Anuvāda: This sūkta, यदक्रंदः (*yadakraṃdaḥ*), is the seventh sūkta in the twenty-second anuvāka. It contains thirteen Ṛks. Its ṛṣi is Dīrghatamas, the son of उचध्य (*ucadhya*) *[as supplied; the particulars below read ಔಚಥ್ಯ]*. The metre is Triṣṭup. Since the horse is praised in this sūkta, praise of the horse is itself the deity of this sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said यदक्रंदः सप्तोनेति (*yadakraṃdaḥ saptoneti*) *[as supplied; differs from the Sanskrit introduction above]*. In the upākaraṇa rite that is to be performed on the middle day of the Aśvamedha sacrifice, the horse brought there and made to stand is to be praised with the first eleven Ṛks of this sūkta; this is expounded in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra of the section तथा च त्रीणि सुत्यानि (*tathā ca trīṇi sutyāni*), "tam avasthitam upākaraṇāya yadakranda ityekādaśabhiḥ …" (Ā. 10-8) *[the quotation as printed ends ಸೌತ್ಯಪ್ರಣುವನ್ here, differing from the Sanskrit above; kept as supplied]*.

॥ Oṃ ॥
Sūkta—163
॥ Maṇḍala 1 ॥ Anuvāka 22 ॥ Sūkta 163 ॥
॥ Aṣṭaka 2 ॥ Adhyāya 3 ॥ Varga 11, 12, 13 ॥
॥ Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 13 ॥
॥ महि (*mahi*)—Dīrghatamā Aucathya ॥ *[as supplied: "ಮಹಿ" where "ṛṣi" is expected]*
॥ वेषता (*veṣatā*)—Aśvastuti ॥ *[as supplied: "ವೇಷತಾ" where "devatā" is expected]*
॥ 80—Triṣṭup ॥ *[number as supplied]*

### Printed p. 231
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 231 (as supplied).*

*[The Saṃhitā and Pada blocks at the head of this page were supplied both as a picture and as text; the text transcription (b1, b2) is used, and the picture line was removed. The heading "ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" printed above the verse in the picture is the book's own.]*

#### Ṛk 1
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> यदक्रं॑दः प्रथ॒मं जाय॑मान उ॒द्यन्त॑मु॒द्रादु॒त वा॒॑
> पुरी॑षात् ।
> शै॒नस्य॑ प॒क्षा ह॑रि॒णस्य॑ बा॒हू उ॑प॒स्तुत्यं॒ महि॑ जा॒तं ते॑
> अर्वन् ॥ १ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> यत् । अक्रंदः । प्रथमं । जायमानः । उत्यन् । समुद्रात् । उत । वा ।
> पुरीषात् ।
> शैनस्य । पक्षा । हरिणस्य । बाहू इति । उपस्तुत्यं । महि । जातं । ते । अर्वन् ॥१॥
*[As supplied, a garbled label: "ಸಹಾಯಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" (the script read it as the heading Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya).]*
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> हे अर्वन्नरणकुशलाश्व ते तव जातं जन्म जननमुपस्तुत्यं । उपेत्य सर्वैः
> स्तोतव्यं ॥ स्तौतेरेतिस्तुशास्विति क्यप् । ततस्तुक् ॥ कथं स्तुत्यत्वमिति उच्यते । यद्यस्मात्समुद्रात् । अंतरिक्षनामैतत् । उदकसमुंदनापादानाद्यक्षगंधर्वादिसंमोदनाधिकरणाद्वांतरिक्षात्पथमं पूर्वं जायमान उत्पन्नः । यद्वा । समुद्र अदित्यः
> समुंदनाद्वृष्ट्या । तस्माद्वा जायमानः । सूरादश्वं वसवो निरतष्टेति वक्ष्यमाणत्वात् । उत वाधवा पुरीषात् सर्वकामानां पूरकादुदकात्पथममुद्यञ्चयमानः । स तादृशस्त्रं यद्यस्मादक्रंदः । महाशब्दमकरोर्यजमानमनुग्रहीतुं ।
> किंच ते पक्षा पतनसाधनौ पक्षौ श्येनस्य पक्षाविव । तौ यथा शीघ्रपतनसाधनौ तादृशावित्यर्थः । तव बाहू हरिणस्य बाहू इव । तौ यथा वेगवंतौ तादृशावित्यर्थः ।
> यस्मादेवं तस्मात्ते जन्म स्तुत्यमित्यर्थः ॥
*Gist:* Sāyaṇa: the horse's birth is praiseworthy, since it arose from the sky or the sun, or from water, neighed, and has falcon's wings and deer's limbs.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
अर्वन् (*arvan*)—O sacrificial horse; यत् (*yat*)—for which reason; (you—thou); प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*)—formerly; समुद्रात् (*samudrāt*)—from the firmament or from the sun; जायमानः (*jāyamānaḥ*)—having come into being; उ वा (*u vā*)—or; पुरीषात् (*purīṣāt*)—from water; उद्यन् (*udyan*)—having arisen; अक्रंदः (*akraṃdaḥ*)—because you made a neighing sound, and also because (for which reason); पक्षा (*pakṣā*)—your wings;

### Printed p. 232
*Running head: 232 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

शैनस्य (*śainasya*)—are like the wings of the falcon; बाहू (*bāhū*)—the limbs (legs); हरिणस्य (*hariṇasya*)—are like the legs of the deer (therefore); ते (*te*)—your; महि (*mahi*)—great; जातं (*jātaṃ*)—birth; उपस्तुत्यं (*upastutyaṃ*)—worthy of praise.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O sacrificial horse, because you, having first come into being from the sun in the firmament or from water, made your attractive neighing sound, and because your wings are like the wings of the falcon and your legs like the legs of the deer (swift), your great birth is worthy of praise.

**Printed English**
The great birth, O Horse, is to be glorified ; whether first springing from the firmament or from the water, inasmuch as you have neighed (auspiciously), for you have the wings of the falcon and the limbs of the deer.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
*[The print repeats the heading ಪ್ರತಿಪದಾರ್ಥ here, where the Special Topics begin.]*
In this Ṛk the origin and nature of the sacrificial horse are described and the greatness of its birth is praised. Since the horse's origin is stated in समुद्रात् उत वा पुरीषात् (*samudrāt uta vā purīṣāt*) as from the firmament or from water, it is evident that these two kinds of origin must be described in the Śruti. If the Śruti passages are examined, support is found for both these views.

अथ यो गर्भोऽंतरासीत् । सोऽग्निरसृज्यत स यदस्य सर्वस्याग्रमसृज्यतस्मादग्रिरग्रिर्ह वै तमग्निरित्याचक्षते परोऽक्षं परोक्षकामा हि देवा अथ यदश्रु संक्षरितमासीत्सोऽश्रुरभवदश्रुर्ह वै तमश्व इत्याचक्षते परोऽक्षं परोक्षकामा हि देवा अथ यदरसदिव स रासभोऽभवदथ यः कपाले रसो लिप्त आसीत्सोऽजोऽभवदथ यत्कपालमासीत्सा पृथिव्यभवत् (*atha yo garbho 'ṃtarāsīt | so 'gnirasṛjyata sa yadasya sarvasyāgramasṛjyatasmādagriragrirha vai tamagnirityācakṣate paro 'kṣaṃ parokṣakāmā hi devā atha yadaśru saṃkṣaritamāsītso 'śrurabhavadaśrurha vai tamaśva ityācakṣate paro 'kṣaṃ parokṣakāmā hi devā atha yadarasadiva sa rāsabho 'bhavadatha yaḥ kapāle raso lipta āsītso 'jo 'bhavadatha yatkapālamāsītsā pṛthivyabhavat*)
(Śa. Brā. 6-1-1-11)

*[The printed text reads ಅಗ್ರಿ(ರ್) where ಅಗ್ನಿ would be expected; kept as supplied.]* When Prajāpati wished to grow in the form of the creation of the world, an egg arose from the waters and from it Brahman, and this Brahman became the origin of the world and the rest (6-1-1-10). Since the egg that arose from the waters was the first of all creations, it became known as *āgri* *[as supplied]*.

### Printed p. 233
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. .೧೧ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 233 (as supplied).*

This same fire (*agni*) the gods, who love the hidden (*parokṣa*), addressed as *agni*. The *aśru* (tear) that had spread around the egg the gods, who love the hidden, called *aśva* (horse). The sound that arose when the egg split became known as *rāsabha* (the ass). The juice that was smeared on the shell of the egg became the *aja* (the unborn). That shell was transformed into the form of the earth. In this description there is an account of how Prajāpati created this horse out of the all-pervading waters. According to another description:

प्रजापतिर्वा इदमग्र आसीत् .................... निरमिमीत मनसः पुरुषं चक्षुषोऽश्वं (*prajāpatirvā idamagra āsīt .................... niramimīta manasaḥ puruṣaṃ cakṣuṣo 'śvaṃ*)
(Śa. Brā. 7-5-26) *[as supplied]*

It is said that this horse was created from Prajāpati's eye.

Just as the relation of the horse to Prajāpati is stated, in many places a very close relation of the horse to Varuṇa is also described.

वारुणो वा अश्वः । तं देवतया व्यर्धयति । यत्पाजापत्यं करोति । नमो राज्ञे नमो वरुणायेत्याह । वारुणो वा अश्वः (*vāruṇo vā aśvaḥ | taṃ devatayā vyardhayati | yatpājāpatyaṃ karoti | namo rājñe namo varuṇāyetyāha | vāruṇo vā aśvaḥ*)
(Kṛ. Brā. 3-F-16-1) *[reference as supplied]*

For this sentence Sāyaṇa has given the explanation अश्वस्याब्योनित्वाद्वरुणस्य चापां पतित्पादयमश्वो वारुणः (*aśvasyābyonitvādvaruṇasya cāpāṃ patitpādayamaśvo vāruṇaḥ*) *[as supplied]*: since the horse was born from the all-pervading waters, and Varuṇa is the lord of the waters, the sacrificial horse belongs to the deity Varuṇa. Discussing the question of the hidden meaning of this "horse of Varuṇa", the scholar Eggeling, who has translated the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa into English, has spoken thus:

"Varuna's horse is none other than the fiery races who Pursues his diurnal course across the all-encompassing arch of heaven, the sphere of Varuna, the all-ruler. It is in the form of the horse that the Sun is thus lauded in the hymn. Rigveda 1—163, received by the Hotri on the second Soma day of the Aswamedha."
(Sacred Books of the East. Vol. XL1V-P. XIX-XX ) *[as supplied: the English as printed, including "races", "Pursues", "XL1V", the missing opening quotation mark]*

What this horse of Varuṇa, the lord of all heaven and earth, is, is none other than the Sun going through his daily revolution. This very point the Hotṛ priest, reciting on the second day of the Aśvamedha the 163rd sūkta of the Ṛ. Saṃ., Maṇḍala 1 (the present sūkta), praises the Sun in the form of the horse (cf. Śa. Brā. 13-5-1-17 to 18). Sāyaṇa also, in the next Ṛk after the present one, *[the sentence continues on the next page]*

### Printed p. 234
*Running head: 234 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

...namely गंधर्वो अस्य रशनामगृभ्या श्चूरादश्वं वसवो निरतष्ट (*gaṃdharvo asya raśanāmagṛbhyā ścūrādaśvaṃ vasavo nirataṣṭa*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-163-2)

by explaining this sentence has shown that the origin of this horse is the Sun.

The relation, existing together with the horse, of Varuṇa and of Prajāpati is described in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā in this way:

प्रजापतिर्वरुणायाश्वमनयथ्व स्वां देवतामार्छत्स पर्यदीर्यत स एतं वारुणं चतुष्कपालमपश्यत्तं निरवपत्ततो वै स वरुणपाशादमुच्यत वरुणो वा एतं गृह्णाति योऽश्वं प्रतिगृह्णाति (*prajāpatirvaruṇāyāśvamanayathva svāṃ devatāmārchatsa paryadīryata sa etaṃ vāruṇaṃ catuṣkapālamapaśyattaṃ niravapattato vai sa varuṇapāśādamucyata varuṇo vā etaṃ gṛhṇāti yo 'śvaṃ pratigṛhṇāti*) *[printed with a dotted omission after the last words]*
(Tai. Saṃ. 2-3-12-1)

Prajāpati took this horse to Varuṇa. But the horse went to its own deity (to Prajāpati himself). Then, because Varuṇa was grieved, Prajāpati made an offering of a *catuṣkapāla* (cake on four potsherds) addressed to Varuṇa. Thereupon Prajāpati was freed from Varuṇa's noose. Thus it is explained.

यदक्रंदः (*yadakraṃdaḥ*)—महाशब्दमकरोः (*mahāśabdamakaroḥ*), "you made a great sound". This states the matter described in the passage "अथ यदरसदिव स रासभोऽभवत् (*atha yadarasadiva sa rāsabho 'bhavat*)" (Śa. Brā. 6-1-1-11) and so forth. This has been explained above.

पुरीषात् (*purīṣāt*)—सर्वकामानां पूरकात् उदकात् (*sarvakāmānāṃ pūrakāt udakāt*): from the water that fulfils all desires, is the meaning. The authors of the Nirukta, citing the Ṛk

देवानां माने प्रथमा अतिष्ठन् कृंतत्रादेषामुपरा उदायन् । त्रयस्तपंति पृथिवीमनूपा द्वा बृबूकं वहतः पुरीषं (*devānāṃ māne prathamā atiṣṭhan kṛṃtatrādeṣāmuparā udāyan | trayastapaṃti pṛthivīmanūpā dvā bṛbūkaṃ vahataḥ purīṣaṃ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-27-23)

have shown that there the word पुरीष (*purīṣa*) is derived, according to पुरीषं पृणातेः पूरयतेर्वा (*purīṣaṃ pṛṇāteḥ pūrayatervā*), from the root पृ (*pṛ*), which gives the meaning "fill" or "satisfy". (Ni. 2-22)

समुद्रात् (*samudrāt*)—two kinds of meaning have been given for this word: from the firmament, or from the Sun. How the meaning "firmament" is possible for the word समुद्र (*samudra*) has been explained, by citing the statements of the authors of the Nirukta, in the Special Topics of the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-159-4 of this same volume.

### Printed p. 235
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 235 (as supplied).*

*[Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 from the previous page]* Or, Sāyaṇa explains: समुद्रः आदित्यः समुंदनात् वृष्टा (*samudraḥ ādityaḥ samuṃdanāt vṛṣṭā*) *[as supplied]*, "the Sun is called *samudra* because he drenches (everything) with rain"; that is, because he makes everything wet with rain, the Sun also has the name "ocean" (*samudra*).

*[As supplied, a garbled label: "ಸ್ನಾಕರಣಪ್ರಕ್ರಿಯಾ" (the script read it as the heading Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā).]*

*Grammar (transcribed, not explained):*

अक्रंदः (*akraṃdaḥ*)—the root क्रदि आह्वाने रोदने च (*kradi āhvāne rodane ca*) (in the sense of calling and of weeping). *Laṅ*, second person singular, सिप् (*sip*). By इतश्च (*itaśca*) elision of the इ (*i*). The vikaraṇa शप् (*śap*). By इदितो नुम् धातोः (*idito num dhātoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-58) the augment नुम् (*num*) for the root. Because of the connection with यत् (*yat*) the *nighāta* accent does not occur. Since the past-tense augment is *udātta*, the form has the initial-acute accent.

जायमानः (*jāyamānaḥ*)—the root जनी प्रादुर्भावे (*janī prādurbhāve*) (in the sense of coming into being). The substitute participle suffix for *laṭ*. The vikaraṇa श्यन् (*śyan*). By the sūtra beginning ज्ञा (*jñā*)… (Pā. Sū. 7-3-79) the substitute जा (*jā*) for the root. By आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-82) the augment मुक् (*muk*) for the stem ending in अ (*a*). Since the *lasārvadhātuka* is unaccented and श्यन् (*śyan*) has the न् (*n*)-marker, the initial-acute accent results.

उद्यन् (*udyan*)—the prefix उत् (*ut*); the root इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*) *[as printed]*. The substitute participle suffix for *laṭ*. By इणोयण् (*iṇoyaṇ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-81) the substitute यण् (*yaṇ*) for the root. The *kṛd-uttarapada-prakṛti* accent results.

पुरीषात् (*purīṣāt*)—the root पृ पालनपूरणयोः (*pṛ pālanapūraṇayoḥ*) (in the sense of protecting and filling). By शृपृभ्यामिषन् (*śṛpṛbhyāmiṣan*) (U. Sā. 4-466, as supplied) the suffix ईषन् (*īṣan*). By इत्यादिर्नित्यम् (*ityādirnityam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-164) the initial-acute accent results.

पक्षा (*pakṣā*)—the root पण व्यवहारे स्तुतौ च (*paṇa vyavahāre stutau ca*) (in the sense of trade and of praise). By गृधिपण्योर्दकौ च (*gṛdhipaṇyordakau ca*) (U. Sū. 3-349) the suffix स (*sa*). For the ण (*ṇa*) of the root the substitute क (*ka*). By the accent of the suffix the final-acute accent results. By *supāṃ suluk* (the sūtra is split across lines in the print) the substitute आ (*ā*) for the dual.

उपस्तुत्यम् (*upastutyam*)—the root ष्टुञ् स्तुत् (*ṣṭuñ stut*) *[as printed]*. By एतिस्तुशास् (*etistuśās*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-109) the suffix क्यप् (*kyap*). Because it is प् (*p*)-marked, by ह्रस्वस्य पिति कृति तुक् (*hrasvasya piti kṛti tuk*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-71) the augment तुक् (*tuk*). Because the suffix is प् (*p*)-marked, the accent of the root remains. In a *prādi* compound the *kṛd-uttarapada-prakṛti* accent results.

जातम् (*jātam*)—the root जनी प्रादुर्भावे (*janī prādurbhāve*). The suffix क्त (*kta*) in the sense of the abstract. By the sūtra beginning *janasanakhanāṃ sanjhaloḥ* (Pā. Sū. 6-4-47) the substitution आ (*ā*) for the root. By the accent of the suffix the final-acute accent results. ॥ ೦ ॥ *[as supplied]*
