### Printed p. 236
*Running head: 236 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 2
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> य॒मेन॑ द॒त्तं त्रि॒त ए॑नमायुन॒गिंद्र॑ एणं प्रथ॒मो अध्य॑तिष्ठत् ।
> गं॒ध॒र्वो अ॑स्य रश॒नाम॑गृभ्णा॒त्सूरा॒दश्वं॑ वसवो॒ निर॑तष्ट ॥२॥
*[As supplied, a garbled label: "ಹಡಪಾರು" (the script read it as the heading Pada-pāṭha).]*
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> यमेन । दत्तं । त्रितः । एनं । अयुनक् । इंद्रः । एनं । प्रथमः । अधि । अतिष्ठत् ।
> गंधर्वः । अस्य । रशनां । अगृभ्णात् । सूरात् । अश्वं । वसवः । निः । अतष्ट ॥ २ ॥
*[As supplied, a garbled label: "ಶಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" (the script read it as the heading Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya).]*
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> यमेन नियामकेनाग्नि ना दत्तमेनमश्वं त्रितः पृथिव्यादिषु त्रिषु स्थानेषु
> वर्तमान स्तीर्णतमो वा वायुरयनक् । रथे योजितवान् । किंचैनमश्वमिंद्रः
> प्रथमः प्रतमः प्रकृष्टतमः । प्रथम इति मुख्यनाम । अध्यतिष्ठत् । अधिष्ठितवान् ।
> अस्य गंधर्वः सोमः । सोमेन समया विपृक्त इति वक्षमाणत्वात् । स चास्याश्वस्य
> रशनां नियमनरज्जु मगृभ्णात् । अग्रहीत् । ईवृशोऽश्वः कुतः समुत्पन्न इति चेत्
> उच्यते । हे वसवो रश्मयः यूयं सूरादादित्यादश्वमेवंमहानुभावं निरतष्ट ।
> निश्शेषेण साधु संपादितवंतः । यद्वा । वसुशब्देनोक्ता एव यमादयः परामृश्यंते । हे वसवः स्वस्वव्यापारेण सर्वस्याच्छादयितारः यूयं सूरादादित्यादश्वमेनं निरतष्ट । अतक्षत । निरित्येष समित्येतस्य स्थाने । तक्षतिः करोटिकर्मा । स च
> क्रियासामान्यवचनः । अत्रौचित्याद्धारणे वर्तते । सम्यग्धारितवंत इत्यर्थः ॥ तक्षेर्लजि छांदसः शपो लुक् । स्कोः संयोगाद्योरिति कलोपः ॥
*[As supplied, a garbled label: "೧ ಪ್ರತಿಪದಾರ್ಥ ೧" (the script read it as the heading Pratipadārtha).]*
*Gist:* Yama (the controlling Agni) gave the horse; Trita (the wind) yoked it; Indra mounted it; Gandharva (Soma) held the rein; the Vasus (rays) fashioned it from the Sun.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
यमेन (*yamena*)—by Yama; दत्तं (*dattaṃ*)—given; एनं (*enaṃ*)—this horse; त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—the wind that moves in the three places beginning with the earth *[printed ಪ್ರಥಿವ್ಯಾದಿ, as supplied]*; आयुनक् (*āyunak*)—harnessed to the car; एनं (*enaṃ*)—this horse; प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—the excellent; इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra; अध्यतिष्ठत् (*adhyatiṣṭhat*)—mounted and sat; गंधर्वः (*gaṃdharvaḥ*)—King Soma; अस्य (*asya*)—of this horse; रशनां (*raśanāṃ*)—the bridle; अगृभ्णात् (*agṛbhṇāt*)—held; वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—the rays of the Sun, or Yama and the others; अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—this horse; सूरात् (*sūrāt*)—from the Sun; निरतष्ट (*nirataṣṭa*)—produced.

### Printed p. 237
*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೧ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 237 (as supplied; "ಆ." where "ಅ." is usual).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
This horse, given by Yama, was harnessed to the car by the wind that moves in the three places beginning with the earth. The excellent Indra mounted and sat on this horse. King Soma held the bridle of this horse. The rays of the Sun, or Yama and the others, produced this horse from the Sun.

**Printed English**
Trita harnessed the horse which was given by Yama: Indra first mounted him, and Gandharva seized his reins. Vasus, you fabricated the horse from the sun.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
This Ṛk makes known the relation of the sacrificial horse to Agni, and likewise to Indra and the others.

यमेन दत्तं (*yamena dattaṃ*)—for this Sāyaṇa has explained the word *yama* as Agni: यमेन नियामकेन अग्निना (*yamena niyāmakena agninā*), "by Yama, that is, the controlling Agni".

यमो यच्छतीति सतः । अग्निरपि यम उच्यते (*yamo yacchatīti sataḥ | agnirapi yama ucyate*)
(Ni. 10-19)

The authors of the Nirukta say that Agni also has the name Yama because he has the power to restrain.

यमो ह जातो यमो जनित्वं जारः कनीनां पतिर्जनीनां (*yamo ha jāto yamo janitvaṃ jāraḥ kanīnāṃ patirjanīnāṃ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-66-8)

Citing this Ṛk, they have shown that there the word *yama* is a synonym of Agni. Sāyaṇa has given two kinds of meaning for this word in the Ṛk cited. यच्छति ददाति स्तोतृभ्यः कामानिति यमोऽग्नि रुच्यते (*yacchati dadāti stotṛbhyaḥ kāmāniti yamo 'gni rucyate*): Agni gets the name Yama because he gives the desired objects to the devotees—this is one explanation; or इंद्राग्न्यूर्युगपदुत्पन्न त्पादग्नेयमत्तं (*iṃdrāgnyūryugapadutpanna tpādagneyamattaṃ*) *[as supplied]*: because Indra and Agni were born together, Agni gets the name Yama—this is the other explanation. The statement of the authors of the Nirukta, यमो ह जात इंद्रेण सह संगतः (*yamo ha jāta iṃdreṇa saha saṃgataḥ*), supports this second meaning. Besides this,

बळित्था महिमा वामिंद्राग्नी पनिष्ठ आ । समानो वां जनिता भ्रातरा युवं यमाविहेहमातरा (*baḷitthā mahimā vāmiṃdrāgnī paniṣṭha ā | samāno vāṃ janitā bhrātarā yuvaṃ yamāvihehamātarā*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-59-2)

### Printed p. 238
*Running head: 238 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

in this, since युवं यमौ (*yuvaṃ yamau*) ("you two are twins") states the simultaneous birth of Indra and Agni, the second meaning also may be asserted.

Here it is hard to find proper authority to say precisely what the context is for such descriptions as "given by Yama" and "mounted by Indra". Sāyaṇa too has taken pains to explain this Ṛk. First he gives the meaning stated above in the Pratipadārtha, and afterwards यद्वा वसुशब्देनोक्ता एव यमादयः प्रतिपद्यंते । हे वसवः स्वस्वव्यापारेण सर्वस्याच्छादयितारः यूयं सूरादादित्यात् अश्वं अतक्षत (*yadvā vasuśabdenoktā eva yamādayaḥ pratipadyaṃte | he vasavaḥ svasvavyāpāreṇa sarvasyācchādayitāraḥ yūyaṃ sūrādādityāt aśvaṃ atakṣata*). "O Vasus (that is, Yama and the others mentioned in the Ṛk), you, each by your own activity pervading everything, fashioned the horse from the Sun and impelled it for the sacrifice": he has shown that this meaning also exists.

This context is described in subtle form in the Śatapatha in this way:

युंजंति ब्रध्नमरुषं चरंतमिति । असौ वा आदित्यो ब्रध्नोऽरुषोऽमुमेवात्मा आदित्यं युनक्ति स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य समष्टैः (*yuṃjaṃti bradhnamaruṣaṃ caraṃtamiti | asau vā ādityo bradhno 'ruṣo 'mumevātmā ādityaṃ yunakti svargasya lokasya samaṣṭaiḥ*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-2-6-1)

For the attainment of the world of heaven they yoke the ruddy (red-coloured) horse. The Sun himself is this horse.

तदाहुः । पराज् वा एतस्माद्यज्ञ एति यस्य पशुरुपा कृतोऽन्यत्र वेदेगेतीत्येतग्ग स्तोतरनेन पथा पुनरश्वमावर्तयासि न इति वायुवै स्तोता तमेवास्मा एतत्परस्ताद्वधाति तथा नात्येति (*tadāhuḥ | parāj vā etasmādyajña eti yasya paśurupā kṛto 'nyatra vedegetītyetagga stotaranena pathā punaraśvamāvartayāsi na iti vāyuvai stotā tamevāsmā etatparastādvadhāti tathā nātyeti*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-2-6-2)

If the horse is outside the altar, the sacrifice also (the horse) is not within the altar; therefore, "O praiser, make the horse stay in its appointed place" is the prayer. The wind is this praiser. As soon as he is prayed to, the wind makes the horse stay in its appointed place; he restrains its movement.

वसवस्तांजंतु गायत्रेण छंदसा (*vasavastāṃjaṃtu gāyatreṇa chaṃdasā*) and so on: thus the chief queen praises the horse. Although in this description the situation described in the present Ṛk is not suggested from the point of view of a narrative, there is an account of how the wind and the others make the sacrificial horse fit for sacrifice. This very matter is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

क्रम्येरत्यक्रमीद्वाजी विश्वैर्देव्येयज्ञॆय्सस्संविदानः (*kramyeratyakramīdvājī viśvairdevyeyajñèysassaṃvidānaḥ*) *[as supplied]*
(Tai. Saṃ. 5-7-24)

### Printed p. 239
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨ .ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 239 (as supplied).*

For this sentence it is explained: विश्वैर्देवैः यज्ञियैः यज्ञार्हैः संविदानः ऐकमत्यं गतः (*viśvairdevaiḥ yajñiyaiḥ yajñārhaiḥ saṃvidānaḥ aikamatyaṃ gataḥ*), "in agreement with all the gods worthy of sacrifice"; that is, it is shown as one with the Viśvedevas, which means that all their portions were contained in the horse. This very matter is described thus:

द्यास्ते पृष्ठं पृथिवी सधस्थमात्मांतरिक्षग्ं समुद्रो योनिस्सूर्यस्ते चक्षुर्वातः प्राणश्चंद्रमाश्क्रोत्रं (*dyāste pṛṣṭhaṃ pṛthivī sadhasthamātmāṃtarikṣagṃ samudro yonissūryaste cakṣurvātaḥ prāṇaścaṃdramāśkrotraṃ*)
(Kṛ. Saṃ. 5-8.25) *[reference as supplied]*

and so on.

त्रितः—पृथिव्यादित्रिषु स्थानेषु वर्तमानः तीर्णतमो ना वायुः (*tritaḥ—pṛthivyāditriṣu sthāneṣu vartamānaḥ tīrṇatamo nā vāyuḥ*) *[as supplied]*: it is explained that this name is well known either because he is present in the three places beginning with the earth, or because he is exceedingly swift. This epithet also makes known the nature of Indra. The authors of the Nirukta, in explaining the word *trita* in the Ṛk

पितुं नु स्तोषं महो धर्माणं तविषीं । यस्य त्रितो व्योजसा वृत्तं विपर्वमर्दयत् (*pituṃ nu stoṣaṃ maho dharmāṇaṃ taviṣīṃ | yasya trito vyojasā vṛttaṃ viparvamardayat*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-187-1)

have given the meaning ओजसा बलेन त्रितः त्रिस्थान इंद्रः (*ojasā balena tritaḥ tristhāna iṃdraḥ*), "by *ojas*, that is, strength; Trita, Indra of three places". There are contexts which state the identity of Indra and the wind. But since in the present Ṛk the name of Indra is clearly stated again, here it must be understood as the wind.

वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—for this word one meaning is given as the gods beginning with Yama (Agni and the rest), and another meaning as the rays of the Sun. The authors of the Nirukta give this etymology of the word:

वसवो यद्विवसते सर्वमग्निर्वसुभिर्वासव इति समाख्या, तस्मात्पृथिवीस्थानाः । इंद्रो वसुभिर्वासव इति समाख्या, तस्मान्मध्यस्थानाः । वसव आदित्यरश्मयो विवासनात्तस्माद्दुस्थानाः (*vasavo yadvivasate sarvamagnirvasubhirvāsava iti samākhyā, tasmātpṛthivīsthānāḥ | iṃdro vasubhirvāsava iti samākhyā, tasmānmadhyasthānāḥ | vasava ādityaraśmayo vivāsanāttasmāddusthānāḥ*)
*[reference as supplied: "೨ ೧೨-೪೧"]*

Because he envelops everything like a covering, Agni has the name *vāsava*; therefore it may be the name of a deity of the earth-region. Because Indra has the same quality, Indra also has the name *vāsava*; therefore it may be the name of a deity of the middle region. Because they shine exceedingly, the rays of the Sun also are called *vasus*; therefore it may be the name of a deity of the heaven-region. Having thus shown the extended meaning of this word, in support of this:

स्वगा वो देवास्सदनमकर्म य आजग्म सवनेदं जुषाणाः । जक्षिवाग्ंसः पपिवाग्ंसश्च विश्वेस्मे धत्त वसवो वसूनि (*svagā vo devāssadanamakarma ya ājagma savanedaṃ juṣāṇāḥ | jakṣivāgṃsaḥ papivāgṃsaśca viśvesme dhatta vasavo vasūni*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 1-4-44-2)

### Printed p. 240
*Running head: 240 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

citing this sentence, he has shown there the extended meaning that belongs to the word *vasu*. In the present Ṛk also Sāyaṇa has stated a purport of the same kind. Therefore it may also be said that the sacrificial horse exerted its influence in all three worlds and helped the affairs of the three worlds. The same idea is suggested in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā also by other sentences:

आक्रान्वाजी पृथिवीमग्निं युजमकृत वाज्यर्वाक्रान् वाज्यंतरिक्षं वायुं युजमकृत वाज्यर्वा द्यां वाज्याक्रग्गस्त सूर्यं युजमकृत वाज्यर्वाग्निस्ते वाजिन् युज्जनु त्वारभे स्वस्ति मा सं पारय वायुस्ते वाजिन् युज्जनु त्वारभे स्वस्तिमा सं पारय (*ākrānvājī pṛthivīmagniṃ yujamakṛta vājyarvākrān vājyaṃtarikṣaṃ vāyuṃ yujamakṛta vājyarvā dyāṃ vājyākraggasta sūryaṃ yujamakṛta vājyarvāgniste vājin yujjanu tvārabhe svasti mā saṃ pāraya vāyuste vājin yujjanu tvārabhe svastimā saṃ pāraya*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-5-19)

Since this idea is suggested in the next Ṛk also, the meaning of these Saṃhitā sentences will be explained in the next Ṛk.

*Grammar (transcribed, not explained):*

दत्तम् (*dattam*)—the root डुदाञ् दाने (*ḍudāñ dāne*) (in the sense of giving). By निष्ठा (*niṣṭhā*) the suffix क्त (*kta*). By दोदद्घोः (*dodadghoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-46) the substitute दत् (*dat*) for the root. By खरि च (*khari ca*) the change to च (*ca*)-class (*cartva*). By the accent of the suffix the final-acute accent results.

अयुनक् (*ayunak*)—the root युजिर् योगे (*yujir yoge*) (in the sense of joining). *Laṅ*, third person singular, the suffix तिप् (*tip*). By इतश्च (*itaśca*) elision of its इ (*i*). By रुधादिभ्यः श्नम् (*rudhādibhyaḥ śnam*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-78) the vikaraṇa श्नम् (*śnam*). Because it is म् (*m*)-marked it comes after the last vowel. By the sūtra हल्ज्याभ्योदी (*haljyābhyodī*) elision of the suffix. By चोःकुः (*coḥkuḥ*) the change of च (*ca*) to a velar (*kutva*). By the sūtra वावसाने (*vāvasāne*) the change to च (*ca*)-class (*cartva*). The augment the past-tense augment for the stem. Because it stands after a non-verb, the *nighāta* accent results.

अतिष्ठत् (*atiṣṭhat*)—the root ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ (*ṣṭhā gatinivṛttau*) (in the sense of cessation of motion). *Laṅ*, a form ending in the third person singular. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

रशनाम् (*raśanām*)—the root अशू व्याप्तौ (*aśū vyāptau*) (in the sense of pervading). By अशेरच (*aśeraca*) (U. Sū. 2-233) the suffix युच् (*yuc*). The substitute आश (*āśa*) for the stem. By युवोरनाकौ (*yuvoranākau*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-1) the substitute अन (*ana*); because it is च् (*c*)-marked, the final-acute accent results.

अगृभ्नात् (*agṛbhnāt*)—the root ग्रह उपादाने (*graha upādāne*) (in the sense of taking), *krayādi* class. A form of the third person singular. By *hṛgrahor bha chandasi* (the sūtra is split across lines in the print) the change of ह (*ha*) to भ (*bha*). The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results. *[The Grammar continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 241
*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ಪ. ೧೧.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 241 (as supplied).*

*Grammar (continuing from the previous Ṛk):*

सूरात् (*sūrāt*)—the root सूज् (*sūj*) प्राणिगर्भविमोचने (*prāṇigarbhavimocane*) (in the sense of releasing the embryo of living beings). By सुसूधागृधिभ्यः क्रन् (*susūdhāgṛdhibhyaḥ kran*) (U. Sū. 2-182) the suffix kran. Because it is k-marked, guṇa does not occur. Because it is n-marked, the initial-acute accent results.

अतष्ट (*ataṣṭa*)—the root तक्षू (*takṣū*) छेदने (*chedane*) (in the sense of cutting). For the second person plural of लज् (*laj*) (as supplied) the substitute ta by तस्थस्थ (*tasthastha*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-101). By बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the luk-elision of the śap. By स्कोस्संयोगाद्योः (*skossaṃyogādyoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-29) the loss of the k. The suffix takes the ṣṭutva change. The stem takes the aṭ augment. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results. ‖ 2 ‖

*[as supplied: the label "ಸಂಹಿತಾಕಾರರು" stands here, where the Saṃhitā-pāṭha label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

#### Ṛk 3
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> असि॑ य॒मो अस्या॑दि॒त्यो अ॑र्व॒न्नसि॑ त्रि॒तो गुह्ये॑न व्र॒तेन॑ ।
> असि॒ सोमे॑न स॒मया॒ विपृ॑क्त आ॒हुस्ते॒ त्रीणि॑ दि॒वि बंध॑नानि ।
> ॥ ३ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> असि । यमः । असि । आदित्यः । अर्वन् । असि । त्रितः । गुह्येन । व्रतेन ।
> असि । सोमेन । समया । विपृक्तः । आहुः । ते । त्रीणि । दिवि । बंधनानि ॥ ३ ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> हे अर्वन् त्वं यमोऽसि । नियमिताग्निरसि । तेन दत्तत्वात्तच्छब्दव्यपदेशः ।
> तथादित्योऽसि । सूरान्निरतष्टेत्युक्तत्वात् । तथा गुह्येन व्रतेन गोपनियेन दुर्निरूपेण वा कर्मणा । सर्वत्रव्याप्तिरूपेणेत्यर्थः । तेन त्रितस्त्रिषु स्थानेषु तायमानो वायुरसि । त्रित एनमायुनगित्युक्तत्वात्तद्रूपत्वं । अथवा गुह्येन व्रतेन
> गोपनीयेन कर्मणा योगादिसाधनरूपेण त्रित एतन्नामक ऋषिरसि । किंच हे
> अश्व त्वं सोमेन समया सह विपृक्तः । वीत्येष समित्येतस्मिन्नर्थे । संपृक्सोऽसि
> तेन ग्रहीतत्वात् । हे अश्वैवंरूप ते तव दिवि द्युलोक आदित्ये वा वर्तमानस्य
> त्रीणि बंधनान्युत्पत्ति कारणान्याहुः पुराविवः । वसव आदित्यो द्युस्थानं चेति त्रीणि ॥
*Gist:* Addressing the horse: you are Yama (Agni as controller), Āditya, Trita (the wind, or the sage), and united with Soma; three bindings of yours are in heaven.

*[stray line as supplied: [16] Vol. 12]]*
*[stray line as supplied: [16] Vol. 12]]*

### Printed p. 242
*Running head: 242 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
(हे (*he*)) अर्वन् (*arvan*)—O horse! (त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you); यमः (*yamaḥ*)—as Agni; असि (*asi*)—you are; आदित्यः (*ādityaḥ*)—as Āditya; असि (*asi*)—you are; गुह्येन (*guhyena*)—mysterious; व्रतेन (*vratena*)—by the act; त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—as the wind; असि (*asi*)—you are; सॊमेन समया (*sòmena samayā*)—with Soma; विपृक्तः (*vipṛktaḥ*)—connected; असि (*asi*)—you are; ते (*te*)—to you; दिवि (*divi*)—in the heaven-world; त्रीणिबंधनानि (*trīṇibaṃdhanāni*)—that there are three kinds of bindings; ते (*te*)—the knowers of the truth (as supplied); आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—say.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
*[The label is printed "ಭಾಷಾ" (Bhāṣā), as supplied.]*
O horse, you are of the form of Agni and of the nature of the wind; by your mysterious act you are connected with Soma; and you are of the form of Āditya. The knowers say that you have three kinds of bindings in the heaven-world.

**Printed English**
You, horse, are Yama : you are Aditya : you are Trita by a mysterious
act : you are associated with Soma. The sages have said there are three
bindings of you in heaven.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
Because it was given by Agni, the sacrificial horse is of the nature of Agni; because it was taken up by Āditya, it is of the nature of Āditya; and so on: identity with Agni and the others is stated. But along with this identity it is also said त्रीणि दिवि बंधनानि (*trīṇi divi baṃdhanāni*), that there are three kinds of bindings. This same mantra is also in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, and in explaining it Sāyaṇa has given this meaning:

ईदृशस्य ते तव द्युलोके बंधनानि बंधनसाधनानि दामानि त्रीणीत्येवमभिज्ञा आहुः । वणिजो हि शक्त्याधिक्येनात्युग्रमश्वं विक्रेतुं ग्रामांतरे नयंतस्त्रिभिर्दामभिर्बध्वंति । दक्षिणवामपार्श्वयोर्दामद्वयमुभौ पुरुषौ गृहीतः । यदा दक्षिणभागवर्तिनं द्रष्टुमुद्युंक्ते तदा वामभागवर्ति तमाकर्षति । तं चेद्द्रष्टुमुद्युंक्ते तदानीमितर आकर्षति । यदा सहसा पुरतः पलायनं करोति तदा तं वारयितुं गले बद्धमेकं दाम गृहीत्वा पृष्ठतः कश्चिदाकर्षति । यदिदं बंधनत्रयं । यद्वा शास्त्रसिद्धं किंचित्कारणत्रयं द्रष्टव्यं । तदनेन बंधनत्रयेणाधिकशक्तिरश्व इति स्तुतो भवति (*īdṛśasya te tava dyuloke baṃdhanāni baṃdhanasādhanāni dāmāni trīṇītyevamabhijñā āhuḥ | vaṇijo hi śaktyādhikyenātyugramaśvaṃ vikretuṃ grāmāṃtare nayaṃtastribhirdāmabhirbadhvaṃti | dakṣiṇavāmapārśvayordāmadvayamubhau puruṣau gṛhītaḥ | yadā dakṣiṇabhāgavartinaṃ draṣṭumudyuṃkte tadā vāmabhāgavarti tamākarṣati | taṃ ceddraṣṭumudyuṃkte tadānīmitara ākarṣati | yadā sahasā purataḥ palāyanaṃ karoti tadā taṃ vārayituṃ gale baddhamekaṃ dāma gṛhītvā pṛṣṭhataḥ kaścidākarṣati | yadidaṃ baṃdhanatrayaṃ | yadvā śāstrasiddhaṃ kiṃcitkāraṇatrayaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ | tadanena baṃdhanatrayeṇādhikaśaktiraśva iti stuto bhavati*)
(Tai. Saṃ. Bhā. 4-6-7) *[abbreviation as printed: ತೈ. ಸಂ. ಭಾ.]*

### Printed p. 243
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ ೩. ವ. ೧೧ ] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 243 (as supplied).*

The binding mentioned above may be compared with the binding of an ordinary horse. When a very powerful horse is being taken to another village for sale, it has restrainers on three sides. If it turns to the right side, the one on the left pulls; likewise, when it turns to the left, the one on the right pulls. If it suddenly tries to dash forward and run away, the one behind pulls. Thus even this horse has a threefold binding; having first stated this worldly comparison, and having then understood that it is not proper to compare the binding of this horse, born of a portion of the gods, with the binding of a worldly horse, he has stated another reason (kāraṇa).

But since there is no proper basis for knowing what the nature of this binding is, even he could not give a precise opinion on the matter, and so said यद्वा शास्त्रसिद्धं (*yadvā śāstrasiddhaṃ*) किंचित्कारणत्रयं द्रष्टव्यं (*kiṃcitkāraṇatrayaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ*): "some other cause enjoined by the scripture is to be understood."

The nature of this binding is described in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā itself, in the mantras quoted in the Special Topics of the preceding Ṛk: आक्रान्वाजी पृथिवीमग्निं (*ākrānvājī pṛthivīmagniṃ*) .... .... .... ........ आक्रान्वाज्यंतरिक्षं वायुं युजमकृत (*ākrānvājyaṃtarikṣaṃ vāyuṃ yujamakṛta*) .... .... .... ‖ (Tai. Saṃ. 7-5-19) and so on. Its meaning is this: this sacrificial horse, having traversed the earth, made Agni the binder in the form of its yoke for the terrestrial rite; that is, it rendered its service through Agni. Likewise this horse, pervading the atmosphere, made the wind its yoke-rope (and rendered its service). The same horse went to the heaven-world and, making Āditya its yoke-rope there, provided what was suitable for the affairs there. Therefore the prayer is made: "Grant us welfare with the help of terrestrial Agni, through the wind of the atmosphere, and by the power of Āditya in the heaven-world." One more point is to be noticed in this description: in the preceding Ṛk, on the authority of the words of the Nirukta-writers, the word वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) was explained as the deities pervading the three worlds, namely Agni, Vāyu or Indra, and Āditya, and it was said that those deities impelled this sacrificial horse. The same idea is indicated in these quoted passages of the Saṃhitā. From this the divine power of this horse and its activities beneficial to the world can be understood.

गुह्येन कर्मणा (*guhyena karmaṇā*)—गोपनीयेन दुर्निरूपेण वा कर्मणा । सर्वत्रव्याप्तिरूपेणेत्यर्थः (*gopanīyena durnirūpeṇa vā karmaṇā | sarvatravyāptirūpeṇetyarthaḥ*). The meaning is: by a secret act, or by an act that cannot be described. It may be said that this description is there both because the wind is all-pervading and because human beings of limited vision cannot describe the universal activities of the wind.

### Printed p. 244
*Running head: 244 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

असि (*asi*)—the root अस (*asa*) भुवि (*bhuvi*) (in the sense of being). *Laṭ*, second person singular; when sip follows, by तासस्त्योर्लोपः (*tāsastyorlopaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-50) the loss of the root's s. Because it stands at the beginning of a pāda, the *nighāta* accent does not result. Since sip is unaccented, the root accent remains.

यमः (*yamaḥ*)—यमयतीति यमः (*yamayatīti yamaḥ*) (he who restrains is Yama); the root यम (*yama*) उपरमे (*uparame*) (in the sense of ceasing). A root with the causative sense included, belonging to the *pacādi* class, hence the suffix ac. Because it is c-marked, it becomes final-acute.

गुह्येन (*guhyena*)—गुहायां भवम् गुह्यम् (*guhāyāṃ bhavam guhyam*) (what is in a cave is guhya); तेन (*tena*). By भवे छंदसि (*bhave chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 4-4-110) the suffix yat. By यस्यॆतिच (*yasyètica*) the loss of the ā-vowel. By यतोनावः (*yatonāvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-213) the initial-acute accent results.

समया (*samayā*)—the prefix सम् (*sam*). The root इण् (*iṇ*) गतौ (*gatau*) (in the sense of going). By आःसमिण् निकषिभ्याम् (*āḥsamiṇ nikaṣibhyām*) (U. Sū. 6-4 14, as supplied) the suffix ā when सम् (*sam*) is the preceding member. Taking that as the cause, guṇa for the i of the root; the substitute aya.

विपृक्तः (*vipṛktaḥ*)—the root पृची (*pṛcī*) संपर्के (*saṃparke*) (in the sense of contact). In the passive, by निष्ठा (*niṣṭhā*) the suffix kta. By चोःकुः (*coḥkuḥ*) the change to k of the root's c. By गतिरनंतरः (*gatiranaṃtaraḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-49) the udātta accent comes on the gati (vi).

आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—the root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*) व्यक्तायां वाचि (*vyaktāyāṃ vāci*) (in the sense of clear speech). *Laṭ*, third person plural, when jhi follows, by ब्रुवः पंचानामादित आहोब्रुवः (*bruvaḥ paṃcānāmādita āhobruvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-84) the substitute us for it. The substitute āha for the root. By उस्यपदांतात् (*usyapadāṃtāt*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-99) the following form (para-rūpa). Because it stands at the beginning of a pāda, the *nighāta* accent does not result. By the suffix accent it becomes final-acute.

दिवि (*divi*)—a locative-ending form. By ऊडिदं पदादि (*ūḍidaṃ padādi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171) the udātta accent comes on the case-ending.

बंधनानि (*baṃdhanāni*)—बंध (*baṃdha*) बंधने (*baṃdhane*), a root. In the sense of the action, the suffix lyuṭ. By युवोरनाक् (*yuvoranāk*) (Pā. Sū. 7 1-1, as supplied) the substitute ana for it. By लिति (*liti*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-193) the udātta accent comes on the syllable before the suffix. ‖ 3 ‖

#### Ṛk 4
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> त्रीणि॑ त आहुर्दि॒वि बंध॑नानि॒ त्रीण्य॒प्सु त्रीण्यं॒तः स॑मु॒द्रे ।
> उ॒तेव॑ मे॒ वरु॑णश्चंस्यर्व॒न्यत्रा॑ त आ॒हुः प॑र॒मं ज॒नित्रं॑ ।
> ॥ ४ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> त्रीणि । ते । आहुः । दिवि । बंधनानि । त्रीणि । अप्सु । त्रीणि । अंतरिति । समुद्रे ।
> उत्यइव । मे । वरुणः । छंत्सि । अर्वन् । यत्र । ते । आहुः । परमं । जनित्रं ॥ ४ ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> पूर्वं दिवि त्रीणि बंधनानीत्युक्तं । इदानीं स्थानत्रयेष्टपि त्रीणि बंधनानीत्याह । हे अर्वन् ते तव दिवि संबंधनानि त्रीण्याहुः । तानि पूर्वमुक्तानि । अप्पु ।
> अबुपजीवितत्वाद्दूलोकोऽप्यद्वेनाभिधीयते । पृथिव्यां त्रीणि बंधनान्याहुः ।
> आन्नं स्थानं बीजमिति त्रीणि । तथा समुद्रेंतः समुद्रमध्यॆ । समुद्रवणादपादानात्समुद्रमंतरिक्षं । तस्मिंस्त्रीणि वृष्टुत्पत्तिनिमित्तानि मेघो विद्यत् स्तनितमिति। उतेव।
> इवशब्दश्चार्थे । अपि च हे आर्वन्नश्व वरुणः पापस्य वारकः फलस्यावर्जयिता वा त्वं
> मे मह्यं छंत्सि । कथयसि । किं कथयसीति चेत् उच्यते । ते तव परमं जनित्रं
> निरतिशयं जन्म । जनेरौणादिक इत्रप्रत्ययः ॥ यत्र येषु त्रिषूक्तेषु स्थानेष्वाहुः
> कथयंति । यद्वा । यत्र यस्मिन्नादित्ये परमं जनित्रमाहुः सूरादश्वं वसवो निरतष्टेत्युक्तत्वात् । तादृशं प्रशस्यं जन्म यज्ञसाधनभावेन कथयसीत्यर्थः ॥
*Gist:* Three bindings are in heaven, three on earth (apas), three in the sea-like firmament; the horse, as Varuṇa, declares to me its supreme birth.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
(अर्वन् (*arvan*)—O horse *[as supplied: ಎಕೈ for ಎಲೈ]*); ते (*te*)—to you; दिवि (*divi*)—in the heaven-world; त्रीणि बंधनानि (*trīṇi baṃdhanāni*)—three bindings; अप्पु (*appu*) (as supplied)—on earth; त्रीणि (*trīṇi*)—three (bindings); समुद्रे अंतः (*samudre aṃtaḥ*)—in the middle of the firmament; त्रीणि (*trīṇi*)—three (bindings) there are; आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—(the knowers of the truth) say; उतेव (*uteva*)—not only do they say so; अर्वन् (*arvan*)—O horse; यत्र (*yatra*)—in which three places, or from which Āditya (arising); ते (*te*)—your; जनित्रं (*janitraṃ*)—birth; परमं (*paramaṃ*)—as most excellent; आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—they describe (of such a you, the nature of the

### Printed p. 246
*Running head: 246 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ.೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

*[Pratipadārtha continued from the previous page:]* birth); वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—you who ward off sin, or who give the fruit; मे (*me*)—to me; छन्ति (*chanti*) (as supplied)—you tell (you make known by your activities).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, the knowers of the truth say that you have three bindings in the heaven-world, three bindings on earth, and three bindings in the middle of the firmament. Not only do they say so: O horse, the nature of that birth of yours, arising from the Āditya from whom the knowers of the truth describe your birth as most excellent, you, who ward off sin, make known to me by your activities.

**Printed English**
They have said that three are your binds in heaven; three upon earth ; and three in the firmament. You declare to me, Horse, who are (one with) Varuna, that which they have called your most excellent birth.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
In the preceding Ṛk, by "त्रीणि बंधनानि (*trīṇi baṃdhanāni*)" the activities, beneficial to the world, of the horse, regularly performed through Āditya and the others, were described. Here it is said that there are three bindings in each world. A part of this was explained in the Special Topics of the preceding Ṛk.

अप्पु त्रीणि (*appu trīṇi*)—अबुपजीवितत्वात् भूलोकोऽपि अप्शब्देनाभिधीयते । पृथिव्यां (*abupajīvitatvāt bhūloko 'pi apśabdenābhidhīyate | pṛthivyāṃ*). Since it lives by the support of अप्पिन (*appina*) (as supplied; water), the word अप् (*ap*) is also a synonym for the earth-world. Therefore Sāyaṇa has explained that अप्पु (*appu*) here means "on earth". This meaning fits the context here. If the earth is to be included along with the heaven-world and the firmament, as in दिवि (*divi*) and समुद्रे (*samudre*), the word अप्पु (*appu*) must be given the meaning "on earth". But when Sāyaṇa commented on this same mantra in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, he gave अप्पु (*appu*) there the meaning "in the water". That commentary is as follows.

यथा द्युलोके तवोच्चैःश्रवोरूपस्य बंधनत्रयमभिज्ञा आहुतथैवाप्पु समुद्रे च । यथा वा वाजपेयेऽस्वंतरमृतमिति मंत्रेणाश्वानप्पु स्नापयंति तथापि पूर्वोक्तरीत्या बंधनत्रयमपेक्षितं तथा समुद्रमध्यॆ स्थिताया वडवाया बंधनत्रयं इत्येवमभिज्ञा आहुः (*yathā dyuloke tavoccaiḥśravorūpasya baṃdhanatrayamabhijñā āhutathaivāppu samudre ca | yathā vā vājapeye 'svaṃtaramṛtamiti maṃtreṇāśvānappu snāpayaṃti tathāpi pūrvoktarītyā baṃdhanatrayamapekṣitaṃ tathā samudramadhyè sthitāyā vaḍavāyā baṃdhanatrayaṃ ityevamabhijñā āhuḥ*).
(Tai. Saṃ. Bhā. 4-6-7) *[abbreviation as printed: ತ್ರೈ. ಸಂ ಭಾ.]*

### Printed p. 247
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಆ. .೩ ವ. ೧೧.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 247] (as supplied).*

*(Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 4.)* Here Sāyaṇa quotes the mantra used at the time of the Vājapeya, अप्पु । अंतरमृतमप्पु भेषजमपां (*appu | aṃtaramṛtamappu bheṣajamapāṃ*), and, just as it means "water" there, shows that the word in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā passage also means "water". For "sea" he says the conventional sea. Taking this commentary itself as the basis, Keith too has rendered that passage thus:

Three they say, are thy bonds in the sky, Three in the waters, three within the
occan [sic] ; And like Varuna to me thou appearest, where, they say, is thy highest, O steed,
birth place.
[Harvard oriental Series Vol. 19. P. 376]

Thus, since the Ṛg-Saṃhitā passage has been interpreted in one way, and the same mantra quoted in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā in another, it is difficult in such cases to know the exact intention. But in the present context the explanation given in the Ṛg-Saṃhitā mantra itself suits the context. In it, explaining what the threefold binding on earth is and what the nature of the threefold binding of the firmament is, he says: अप्पु अन्नं, स्थानं, बीजमिति त्रीणि । अंतरिक्षे वृष्टुत्पत्ति निमित्तानि मेघो, विद्युत्, स्तनितमिति (*appu annaṃ, sthānaṃ, bījamiti trīṇi | aṃtarikṣe vṛṣṭutpatti nimittāni megho, vidyut, stanitamiti*). That is, on earth the threefold binding of food, place and seed; and in the firmament the threefold binding of cloud, lightning and thunder, which are the causes of the production of rain. It seems that this passage must have a still more hidden intention.

वरुणक (*varuṇaka*) *[as supplied]*—this word here is a synonym of "horse". He has explained: पापस्य वारकः फलस्यावर्जयिता (*pāpasya vārakaḥ phalasyāvarjayitā*), that is, "one who wards off sin, or one who supplies the fruit". Or: just as in the earlier passage यमेन दत्तं (*yamena dattaṃ*) (1-163-2) the relation of "given by Agni" was stated, and therefore identity was stated in असि यमः अस्यादित्यः (*asi yamaḥ asyādityaḥ*) (1-163-3), so, since this same horse has a very close relation with Varuṇa too, identity with Varuṇa may have been stated here. The relation of this sacrificial horse with Varuṇa is shown thus:

वारुणो ना अश्वः । तं देवतया व्यर्धयति (*vāruṇo nā aśvaḥ | taṃ devatayā vyardhayati*)
(Śai. Brā. 3-9-16-1) *[abbreviation as printed: ಶೈ. ಬ್ರಾ-]*

This Brāhmaṇa passage has been quoted and this matter explained earlier. Not only this, but:

अथ श्वो भूते । सूतस्य गृहान्नरेत्य वारुणं यवमयं चरुं निर्वपति स वो वै सूतः स वो वै देवानां वरुणस्तस्माद्वारुणो भवत्येतद्वा (*atha śvo bhūte | sūtasya gṛhānnaretya vāruṇaṃ yavamayaṃ caruṃ nirvapati sa vo vai sūtaḥ sa vo vai devānāṃ varuṇastasmādvāruṇo bhavatyetadvā*)

### Printed p. 248
*Running head: 248 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

*(The Brāhmaṇa passage continues from the previous page:)* अस्यैकं रत्नं यत्तूतस्तस्मा एवैतेन सूयते तग्ग् स्वमनकक्रमिणं कुरुते तस्याश्वो दक्षिणा स हि वारुणो यदश्वः (*asyaikaṃ ratnaṃ yattūtastasmā evaitena sūyate tagg svamanakakramiṇaṃ kurute tasyāśvo dakṣiṇā sa hi vāruṇo yadaśvaḥ*)
(Śa.. Brā. 4-3-1-5) *[abbreviation as printed: ಶ.. ಬ್ರಾ.]*

In this passage identity of Varuṇa and the horse is stated, and in the Rājasūya there is the injunction that the barley, dear to the horse, is to be offered to Varuṇa. Since identity of this kind is stated in many other contexts as well, it would not be wrong to say that the same intention is indicated in the present Ṛk too.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

अप्पु (*appu*)—a form ending in the locative plural. By ऊडिदंपदादि (*ūḍidaṃpadādi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171) the udātta accent comes on the case-ending.

छन् त्सि (*chan tsi*)—छदि (*chadi*) संवरणे (*saṃvaraṇe*) (in the sense of covering), a root of the *curādi* class. Because it is i-marked, the optional ṇic does not occur. By इदितो नुम्‌धातोः (*idito numdhātoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-58) the num augment. *Laṭ*, second person singular, sip. By खरि च (*khari ca*) the change to a *car* letter (ca-class) of the root's d. Because it follows a non-finite verb, the *nighāta* accent results.

यत्रा (*yatrā*)—by यचितुनुघ (*yacitunugha*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-133) lengthening occurs in the Saṃhitā text.

जनित्रम् (*janitram*)—जनी (*janī*) प्रादुर्भावे (*prādurbhāve*) (in the sense of coming into being), a root. Since it belongs to the *aśitrādi* group, by अशित्रादिभ्य इत्रोत्थौ (*aśitrādibhya itrotthau*) (U. Sū. 4-612) the suffix itra. By the initial-acute accent of the suffix the word becomes middle-acute. ‖ 4 ‖

#### Ṛk 5
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> इ॒मा ते॑ वाजिन्नव॒मार्ज॑नानी॒मा श॒फानां॑ सनि॒तुर्नि॒धाना॑ ।
> अत्रा॑ ते भ॒द्रा र॑श॒ना अ॑पश्यमृ॒तस्य॒ या अ॑भि॒रक्षं॑ति गो॒पाः ।
> ॥ ५ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> इमा । ते । वाजिन् । अव्यमार्जनानि । इमा । शफानां । सनितुः । निधाना ।
> अत्र । ते । भद्राः । रशनाः । अपश्यं । ऋतस्य । याः । अभिरक्षंति । गोपाः ॥ ५ ॥

> *imā | te | vājin | avyamārjanāni | imā | śaphānāṃ | sanituḥ | nidhānā |*
> *atra | te | bhadrāḥ | raśanāḥ | apaśyaṃ | ṛtasya | yāḥ | abhirakṣaṃti | gopāḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

### Printed p. 249
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೧೧.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 249] (as supplied).*

*[as supplied: the label "ಸಾಯಾಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" stands here, where the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> हे वाजिन्नश्व ते संबंधीन्यवमार्जनान्यंगसंशोधकानि स्थानानीमेमान्युक्तानि द्युलोकादीनि किंच सनितुर्यागसंभक्तुस्तवेमेमानि शफानां निधानानि स्थानानि।
> संचारप्रदेशा इत्यर्थः । किंचात्रैव ते तव भद्रा भंधनीयाः कल्याणा रशना ग्रीवादिषु संयुक्ता रज्जूरत्रापश्यं । एषूक्तेषु स्थानेष्टपश्यं । रशना विशेष्यंते । या
> गोपा गोपयित्र्यो रशना ऋतस्य सत्यभूतस्य यज्ञसाधनस्य वाश्वस्य॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी॥
> ऋतमभिरक्षंति अभितः पालयंति ता अपश्यं । दृष्टवानस्मि । यद्वा । इमा ते तवावमार्जनान्यंगप्रक्षालनसाधनान्युदकानि । इमानि सनितुस्तव शफानां निधानानि देवयजनभूमयः संचारप्रदेशा वा । अत्रास्मिन्देवयजनस्थाने या रशना गोपास्तव
> पालयितारो राजपुरुषा ऋत्विजो वा ऋतस्य संबंधिन्नोऽभिरक्षंति ता अपश्यं ॥
*Gist:* The purifying places (heaven etc.), the hoof-resting places and the auspicious guarding reins are seen; alternatively: waters, sacrificial grounds, and the royal men or priests as guards.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
वाजिन् (*vājin*)—O horse; ते (*te*)—your; अवमार्जनानि (*avamārjanāni*)—the places that purify the limbs; इमा (*imā*)—these heaven-world etc., (and); सनितुः (*sanituḥ*)—of you who take part in the sacrifice; शफानां (*śaphānāṃ*)—the hooves; निधाना (*nidhānā*)—where they are set down; इमा (*imā*)—these regions (and); याः (*yāḥ*)—which; गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—protecting; ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—the horse that is a means of the sacrifice, or that is of the nature of truth; अभिरक्षंति (*abhirakṣaṃti*)—guard, such ones and; भद्राः (*bhadrāḥ*)—auspicious; ते (*te*)—your; रशनाः (*raśanāḥ*)—bridles; अत्र (*atra*)—here; अपश्यं (*apaśyaṃ*)—I have seen.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, I have seen here these heaven-world etc., which are the places that purify your limbs; these regions where you, who take part in the sacrifice, set down your hooves; and your bridles, which guard the horse that is a means of the sacrifice and are auspicious.

**Printed English**
I have beheld, Horse, these your purifying (regions); these impressions
of the feet of you, who share in the sacrifice; and here your auspicious reins,
which are the protectors of the rite that preserve it.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
Two kinds of construction have been made for this Ṛk: first, with reference to the heaven-world etc., and second, with reference to waters etc. The first construction is given in the Pratipadārtha.

### Printed p. 250
*Running head: 250 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

Secondly, the word अवमार्जनानि (*avamārjanāni*) is explained by अंगप्रक्षालनसाधनान्युदकानि (*aṃgaprakṣālanasādhanānyudakāni*), that is, waters that wash and purify the limbs; शफानां निधाना इमा (*śaphānāṃ nidhānā imā*) as the sacrificial ground where the hooves are set down; and गोपाः रशनाः (*gopāḥ raśanāḥ*) as the priests who guard the sacrifice, or the royal men who guard the horse.

For the same mantra in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā Sāyaṇa has given a somewhat different meaning. There (Tai. Saṃ. 4-6-7) for अवमार्जनानि (*avamārjanāni*) he says शरीरशोधनसाधनानि लोहमयानि (*śarīraśodhanasādhanāni lohamayāni*), that is, iron instruments for scrubbing the body; and for शफानां निधाना (*śaphānāṃ nidhānā*) he says लोहमयान्याभरणविशेषरूपाणि (*lohamayānyābharaṇaviśeṣarūpāṇi*), that is, since it is the custom to put an iron cover on the hooves of royal horses so that no kind of trouble befalls them as they walk, the word is explained as indicating such a covering device here too.

भद्रा (*bhadrā*):—भद्रं भगेन व्याख्यातं । भजनीयं, भूतानामभिद्रवणीयं भवद्रमयतीति वा भाजनवद्वा (*bhadraṃ bhagena vyākhyātaṃ | bhajanīyaṃ, bhūtānāmabhidravaṇīyaṃ bhavadramayatīti vā bhājanavadvā*) (Ni. 4-9). The word भद्र (*bhadra*) is used in the sense of "conducive to welfare" or "auspicious". To explain further: the word भद्र (*bhadra*) is used in the senses of that which is fit for enjoyment, that which is most desired by living beings, or that which causes the enjoyment of happiness, or that which belongs only to one who is deserving. When explaining this word the Nirukta-writers explained one meaning suited to the context through the quotation of a Śruti passage:

अत्रा सखायः सख्यानि जानते भद्रैषां लक्ष्मीर्निहिताधि वाचि (*atrā sakhāyaḥ sakhyāni jānate bhadraiṣāṃ lakṣmīrnihitādhi vāci*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-21-2)

In this passage, explaining the word भद्र (*bhadra*), they gave the meaning "auspicious"; accordingly here too the meaning is "auspicious".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

इमा (*imā*)—in the neuter plural, by शेश्छंदसि बहुलम् (*śeśchaṃdasi bahulam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70) the loss of śi.

अवमार्जनानि (*avamārjanāni*)—मृजू (*mṛjū*) शुद्धौ (*śuddhau*) (in the sense of purifying), a root. The suffix lyuṭ; by युवोरनाकौ (*yuvoranākau*) the substitute ana. Caused by the suffix, by मृजेर्वृद्दिः (*mṛjervṛddiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-114) vṛddhi for the light penultimate vowel of the root, with a following r. By लिति (*liti*) (Pā. Sū. 9-1-193) *[number as supplied]* the udātta accent comes on the syllable before the suffix. When it forms a compound with the prefix etc., the *kṛd*-final member keeps its own accent (*prakṛti-svara*).

सनितुः (*sanituḥ*)—षण (*ṣaṇa*) संभक्तौ (*saṃbhaktau*) (in the sense of sharing), a root. By धात्वादेः षः सः (*dhātvādeḥ ṣaḥ saḥ*) the change of the initial to s. In the agent sense, the suffix tṛc. By आर्धधातुक (*ārdhadhātuka*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-35) the iṭ augment for it. A form ending in the genitive singular. By the suffix accent it becomes final-acute.

### Printed p. 251
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೨.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 251] (as supplied).*

निधाना (*nidhānā*)—डुधाग् (*ḍudhāg*) धारणपोषणयोः (*dhāraṇapoṣaṇayoḥ*) (in the senses of holding and nourishing), a root. By करणाधिकरणयोश्च (*karaṇādhikaraṇayośca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-117) the suffix lyuṭ in the locative sense; the substitute ana for it. By गतिकारकोपपदात्कृत् (*gatikārakopapadātkṛt*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-139) the *kṛd*-final member keeps its own accent. In the neuter plural, by शेश्शृंदसि बहुलम् (*śeśśṛṃdasi bahulam*) (Pā. Sū. 9-1-70) *[number and letters as supplied]* the loss of śi.

अत्र (*atra*)—by यजितुनुघ (*yajitunugha*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-133) lengthening occurs in the Saṃhitā text.

भद्रा (*bhadrā*)—भवि (*bhavi*) कल्याणे सुखे च (*kalyāṇe sukhe ca*) (in the sense of welfare and happiness). By यजेंद्राग्र (*yajeṃdrāgra*) (U. Sū. 1-186) it is irregularly formed with the ending ra.

अपश्यम् (*apaśyam*)—दृशिर् (*dṛśir*) प्रेक्षणे (*prekṣaṇe*) (in the sense of seeing), a root. लज् (*laj*) (as supplied), a form ending in the first person singular. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

यशस्सु (*yaśassu*)—In the sense of the object the पक्कि (*pakki*) has come. *[as supplied: the print reads thus; not settled here.]*

अभिरक्षंति (*abhirakṣaṃti*)—रक्ष (*rakṣa*) पालने (*pālane*) (in the sense of protecting). *Laṭ*, a form ending in the third person plural. By यद्वृत्तान्नित्यं (*yadvṛttānnityaṃ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-66) the prohibition of the *nighāta* accent results. By त्यास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tyāsyanudāttet*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-186) the *lasārvadhātuka* is unaccented, so the root accent remains; by तिजिर्चोदात्तवति (*tijircodāttavati*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71) the *nighāta* accent comes on the gati (abhi).

गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—गुपू (*gupū*) रक्षणे (*rakṣaṇe*) (in the sense of guarding), a root. By नंदिग्रहपचादिभ्यः (*naṃdigrahapacādibhyaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-134) the suffix ac in the agent sense. By पुगंतलघूपधस्य च (*pugaṃtalaghūpadhasya ca*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-89) guṇa for the light penultimate vowel of the root, caused by the suffix. By चितः (*citaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-163) the final-acute accent results. ‖ 5 ‖

#### Ṛk 6
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> आ॒त्मानं॑ ते॒ मन॑सा॒राद॑जानाम॒वो दि॒वा प॒तयं॑तं पतं॒गं।
> शिरो॑ अपश्यं प॒थिभिः॑ सु॒गेभि॑ररे॒णुभि॒र्जॆह॑मानं पत॒त्रि ॥ ६ ॥
*[as supplied: the label "ಚದಮಾರು" stands here, where the Pada-pāṭha label belongs; the script replaced it.]*
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> आत्मानं । ते । मनसा । आरात् । अजानां । अवः । दिवा । पतयंतं । पतंगं ।
> शिरः । अपश्यं । पथिभिः । । सुगेभिः । अरेणुभिः । जेहमानं । पतत्रि ॥ ६ ॥

> *ātmānaṃ | te | manasā | ārāt | ajānāṃ | avaḥ | divā | patayaṃtaṃ | pataṃgaṃ |*
> *śiraḥ | apaśyaṃ | pathibhiḥ | | sugebhiḥ | areṇubhiḥ | jehamānaṃ | patatri ‖ 6 ‖*

### Printed p. 252
*Running head: 252 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> हे अश्व ते तवात्मानं शरीरं मनसास्मदीयेन प्रकृष्टगमनवता चेतसारादतिदूरे स्थितमजानां । जानामि । अतिव्याप्तं भावयामीत्यर्थः । कथं । अवोऽ-वस्तात् । सामर्थ्याद्दूमेः । भूमिमारभ्य दिवांतरिक्षेण मार्गेण पतंगमादित्यं
> पतयंतं गच्छंतं प्राप्पुवंतं । यद्वा । यतो दिवांतरिक्षेण मार्गेण दिवि वा
> पतयंतं गच्छंतं पतंगमादित्यमवः आवः व्याप्योषि अतो व्याप्तं जानामि । किंच
> शिरोऽपश्यं । तव शिर एवं पश्यामि । कथं । सुगेभिः शोभनगमनसाधनैररेणुभिरपापैः पथिभिर्मार्गैः । आदित्यमंडलादप्युपरिभूतैः सत्यलोकमार्गैरित्यर्थः । तत्र
> प्रतिबंधाभावान्मार्गाणामुक्तरूपत्वं । तथाविध्यॆस्तैर्जॆहमानमुपर्युपरि व्याप्पुवत् पतत्रि पतनवच्छिरोऽपश्यं । भावयामि । एवं लोकत्रयव्याप्तं भावयामीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । त आत्मानमेवं भावयामि मनसा दिवा द्योतनात्मकेनांतरिक्षमार्गीण दिवः सकाशाद्वावोऽवस्तादवाङ्मुखं पतयंतं यागदेशं प्रति व्याप्प्पुवत् आत एव पतंगं पतनशीलमधःपतदादित्यसदृशं वा । एवं तव शिरो भावयामीत्यर्थः । शिरश्चैवमपश्यं । उक्तरूपैर्देवयजनमार्गैर्जेहमानं यज्ञदेशं
> व्याप्नुवत् पतत्रि शीघ्रगामि शिरः पश्यामि । यद्यप्यात्मशब्देन सशिरस्कं शरीरमुक्तं
> तथापि देहात्पूर्वं शिरसो दृश्यमानत्वात् पृथगभिधीयते ॥
*Gist:* I perceive by mind your form far off, flying from earth through the sky toward the sun, and your head moving swiftly on dustless, excellent paths.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
(अश्व (*aśva*)—O horse); अवः (*avaḥ*)—(starting) from the earth; दिवा (*divā*)—by the path of the firmament; पतंगं (*pataṃgaṃ*)—aiming at the sun; पतयंतं (*patayaṃtaṃ*)—flying; ते (*te*)—your; आत्मानं (*ātmānaṃ*)—form; आरात् (*ārāt*)—from very far; मनसा (*manasā*)—with my faith-filled mind; अजानां (*ajānāṃ*)—I knew; (and) अरेणुभिः (*areṇubhiḥ*)—dustless (pure); सुगेभिः (*sugebhiḥ*)—excellent, fit for travel; पथिभिः (*pathibhiḥ*)—by the path; जेहमानं (*jehamānaṃ*)—pervading higher and higher; पतत्रि (*patatri*)—flying; शिरः (*śiraḥ*)—your head also; अपश्यं (*apaśyaṃ*)—I see.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, starting from the earth, aiming at the sun, flying by the path of the firmament, your form I knew from very far with my faith-filled mind. And I see also your head, flying, pervading higher and higher by a path that is free of impurity, pure, and excellent and fit for travel.

### Printed p. 253
*Running head: [ಅ.೨.ಆ. ೩ ವ. ೧೨. ] ಸುಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 253] (as supplied; the word ಸುಗ್ವೇದ is printed so).*

**Printed English**
I recognise in my mind your form afar off, going from (the earth)below, by way of heaven, to the sun. I behold your head soaring aloft, and mount-ing quickly by unobstructed paths, unsullied by dust.

*[as supplied: the label "ವಿಶೇಷವಿವರಗಳು" stands here, where the Special Topics label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
Two kinds of construction have been made for this Ṛk. First, for the word अवः (*avaḥ*): अवस्तात् (*avastāt*), "from the lower part", that is, from the earth; and second, अवः (*avaḥ*): अवस्तात् (*avastāt*), "to the lower part", that is, aiming at the earth. The first meaning is given in the Pratipadārtha.

According to the second construction: दिवः सकाशात् (*divaḥ sakāśāt*), starting from the firmament; अवः (*avaḥ*), facing the earth; पतयंतं (*patayaṃtaṃ*), flying and coming down; पतंगं (*pataṃgaṃ*), resembling the Āditya; आत्मानं (*ātmānaṃ*), the form of the horse. And in the latter half: सुगेभिः पथिभिः (*sugebhiḥ pathibhiḥ*), on excellent paths for the divine sacrifice; जेहमानं (*jehamānaṃ*), pervading; पतत्रि (*patatri*), swift-going; शिरः पश्यामि (*śiraḥ paśyāmi*), "I see the head"—this is the meaning given. If the objection arises: when the word आत्मानं (*ātmānaṃ*) already means the body together with the head, why use the word शिरः (*śiraḥ*) again and describe it?—the answer is given as यद्यप्यात्मशब्देन सशिरस्कं शरीरमुक्तं तथापि देहात्पूर्वं शिरसो दृश्यमानत्वात् पृथगभिधीयते (*yadyapyātmaśabdena saśiraskaṃ śarīramuktaṃ tathāpi dehātpūrvaṃ śiraso dṛśyamānatvāt pṛthagabhidhīyate*). That is, although the word आत्म (*ātma*) itself means the body including the head, since in motion the head is seen first, this word has been stated separately. Even then an objection may arise: in such a context one ought to have followed the order, saying first "I saw the head" and afterwards "I saw the body"—this objection is natural. But in the speech of a ṛṣi there is no room for such faults. Since Sāyaṇa has stated first the meaning given in the Pratipadārtha, it appears that he has taken that as the main one. If that is followed, then for सुगेभिः अरेणुभिः पथिभिः (*sugebhiḥ areṇubhiḥ pathibhiḥ*) he says आदित्यमंडलादपि उपरिभूतैः सत्यलोकमार्गैः (*ādityamaṃḍalādapi uparibhūtaiḥ satyalokamārgaiḥ*), that is, by the paths of the Satya-loka, which lie above even the orb of Āditya. This description of the Satya-loka is given in the Upaniṣads thus:

सत्यमेव जयति नानृतं सत्येन पंथा विततो देवयानः । येनाक्रमंत्यृषयो ह्याप्तकामा यत्र तत्सत्यस्य परमं निधानं (*satyameva jayati nānṛtaṃ satyena paṃthā vitato devayānaḥ | yenākramaṃtyṛṣayo hyāptakāmā yatra tatsatyasya paramaṃ nidhānaṃ*)
(Muṇḍaka 3-1.-6) *[reference as printed]*

It is described that the ṛṣis who have attained their desires and know the highest truth reach this most excellent Satya-loka, and that this is the supreme place. This very thing:

### Printed p. 254
*Running head: 254 [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩. (as supplied; the head is printed without its closing bracket).*

यस्तु विज्ञानवान्भवति समनस्कः सदा शुचिः । स तु तत्पदमाप्नोति यस्माद्भूयो न जायते (*yastu vijñānavānbhavati samanaskaḥ sadā śuciḥ | sa tu tatpadamāpnoti yasmādbhūyo na jāyate*)
(Kara. 3-8-9) *[abbreviation as printed: ಕರ.]*

Having acquired spiritual knowledge, the self-controlled person endowed with the riches of tranquillity, self-restraint and the rest attains this Satya-loka and does not become a transmigrating being again: thus there is a description of the Satya-loka which goes beyond even the world of Āditya.

जेहमानं (*jehamānaṃ*)—the word जेहते (*jehate*) is read among the 122 names of motion (Ni. 3-9). It is derived from the root जेहृ (*jehṛ*), which gives the meaning "strive, arise"; and the meaning of this word must be stated according to the context.

अस्य शुष्मासो ददृशानपवेर्जॆहमानस्य स्वनयन्नियुद्भिः । प्रत्नेभिरो रुशद्भिर्देवतमो वि रेभद्भिररतिर्भाति विभ्वा (*asya śuṣmāso dadṛśānapaverjèhamānasya svanayanniyudbhiḥ | pratnebhiro ruśadbhirdevatamo vi rebhadbhiraratirbhāti vibhvā*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-3-6)

In this passage it has the meaning of mere motion, that is, "going towards the gods"; and

ये तातृषुर्देवत्रा जेहमाना होत्राविदः स्तोमतष्टासो अर्कैः । अग्ने याहि सुविदत्रेभिरर्वाज् सत्यैः कव्यैः पितृभिर्घर्मसद्भिः (*ye tātṛṣurdevatrā jehamānā hotrāvidaḥ stomataṣṭāso arkaiḥ | agne yāhi suvidatrebhirarvāj satyaiḥ kavyaiḥ pitṛbhirgharmasadbhiḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-15-9)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-15-9) *[the reference is printed twice, as supplied]*

in this passage it has the meaning "attained". In some contexts it shows an extremely intense desire. In the present Ṛk it has the meaning "pervade".

*[as supplied: the label "ವ್ಯಾಕರಣಕ್ರಿಯಾ" stands here, where the Grammar label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

अजानाम् (*ajānām*)—ज्ञा (*jñā*) अवबोधने (*avabodhane*) (in the sense of knowing), a root. लज् (*laj*) (as supplied), first person singular, mip. By तस्थस् थमिपां (*tasthas thamipāṃ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-101) the substitute am for it. By क्र्यादिभ्यः श्ना (*kryādibhyaḥ śnā*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-81) the śnā infix. By ज्ञाजनोर्जा (*jñājanorjā*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-79) the substitute jā for the root. The stem takes the aṭ augment. Because it follows a non-finite verb, the *nighāta* accent results.

अवः (*avaḥ*)—by पूर्वाधरावराणामसिपुरधवश्चैषाम् (*pūrvādharāvarāṇāmasipuradhavaścaiṣām*) (Pā. Sū. 5-3-39) the suffix asi in the sense of "from below". The substitute av for the stem. By the suffix accent it becomes final-acute.

पतॆयंतम् (*patèyaṃtam*) *[as supplied]*—पत (*pata*) गत् (*gat*) *[as supplied]*, a root of the *curādi* class. By सत्यापपाश (*satyāpapāśa*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-15) the suffix ṇic in its own sense. Because it ends in a, the loss caused by ṇic. After the ṇijanta, for laṭ *[the entry is cut off at the end of the window and continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 255
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨ ಅ ೩. ಪ. ೧೨] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 255] (as supplied).*

*(Grammar of Ṛk 6, continuing the entry on the word printed ಪತೆಯಂತಮ್ from the previous page:)* the substitute śatṛ. The śap infix. Caused by śap, guṇa for ṇic; the substitute aya. When the second-case विकवचन (*vikavacana*) *[as supplied: ದ್ವಿತೀಯಾವಿಕವಚನ]* ending follows, because it is ug-marked the num augment. Since the *lasārvadhātuka* is unaccented, the root accent remains.

पतंगम् (*pataṃgam*)—पत्ल्य (*patlya*) *[as supplied]* गत् (*gat*) *[as supplied]*, a root. By पतेरंगच् (*pateraṃgac*) (U. Sū. 1-116) the suffix aṅgac. By चितः (*citaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-163) the final-acute accent results. A form ending in the accusative singular.

अपश्यम् (*apaśyam*)—दृशिर् (*dṛśir*) प्रेक्षणे (*prekṣaṇe*) (in the sense of seeing), a root. In the Veda लज् (*laj*) (as supplied) in the present sense. A form ending in the first person singular. The *tiṅanta* *nighāta* accent results.

सुगेभिः (*sugebhiḥ*)—गम्‌ल्य (*gamlya*) *[as supplied]* गतौ (*gatau*) (in the sense of going), a root. By सुदुरोरधिकरणे (*suduroradhikaraṇe*) the suffix ḍa. By the force of its being ḍ-marked, the loss of the ṭi (av) of the root. The suffix accent results. By बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*), because the substitution of ais for bhis is optional, the e-substitution.

अरेणुभिः (*areṇubhiḥ*)—न विद्यंते रेणवः येषु ते (*na vidyaṃte reṇavaḥ yeṣu te*) (those in which there are no dusts). अरेणवः (*areṇavaḥ*), तैः (*taiḥ*). By नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) the final-acute accent of the latter member results.

जेहमानम् (*jehamānam*)—जेहृ (*jehṛ*) प्रयत्ने (*prayatne*) (in the sense of striving), a root of the *bhvādi* class. Because it is anudātta-marked (it has anudātta ṭ), by अनुदात्तजित आत्मनेपदम् (*anudāttajita ātmanepadam*) *[as supplied]* (Pā. Sū. 1-3-12) the ātmanepada results. For laṭ the substitute śānac. The śap infix. By आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-82) the muk augment. By तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-189) *[number as supplied]* the *lasārvadhātuka* is unaccented, so the root accent remains. ‖ 6 ‖
