#### Ṛk 7
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> अत्रा॑ ते रू॒पमु॑त्त॒मम॑पश्यं॒ जिगी॑षमाणमि॒ष आ प॒दे
> गोः ।
> य॒दा ते॒ मर्तो॒ अनु॒ भोग॒मान॒ळादिद्ग्रसि॑ष्ठ॒ ओष॑धीरजीगः ॥ ७ ॥

> *atrā te rūpamuttamamapaśyaṃ jigīṣamāṇamiṣa ā pade*
> *goḥ |*
> *yadā te marto anu bhogamānaḷādidgrasiṣṭha oṣadhīrajīgaḥ ‖ 7 ‖*

### Printed p. 256
*Running head: 256 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ಮಂ. ೧. ೮. ೨೨, ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

*[as supplied: the label "ಟಡದಿ" stands here, where the Pada-pāṭha label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> अत्र । ते । रूपं । उत्कतमं । अपश्यं । जिगीषमाणं । इषः । आ । पदे । गोः ।
> यदा । ते । मर्तः । अनु । भोगं । आनट् । आत् । इत् । ग्रसिष्यः । ओषधीः ।
> अजीगरिति ॥ ७ ॥
*[as supplied: the label "ಶಾಯುಘಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" stands here, where the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> एवं गमनानंतरं हे अश्व ते तवोत्तममुक्तरूपेणोत्कृष्टतमं रूपमत्रास्मिन्यागदेशेऽपश्यं । पश्यामि । कीदृशं । गोर्भूम्याः पदे स्थाने देवस्थाने
> देवयजनप्रदेश आजिधावनस्थाने वेषः ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठि ॥ इषमिष्यमाणमन्नमन्नानि
> वा आ सर्वतो जिगीषमाणं गंतुमिच्छंतं । हे अश्व ते तव यदा मर्तो
> मनुष्टो भोगं भोक्तव्यमन्नमन्वानट् ॥ नशतिर्गतिकर्मा ॥ अनुक्रमेण
> समीपं गमयति । आदिदनंतरं तदानीमेव तदनुग्रहाद्विलंबमकृत्वा ग्रसिष्टो
> महातृष्णयेव ग्रसितृतमो भक्षयितृतमः सन्नोषधीरन्नान्यजीगः । गिरसि । भक्षयसि ।
> महानुभावोऽपि त्वं लौकिकाश्ववत्तुच्छेन पुरुषेण दीयमानं स्वल्पं भक्ष्यमनुग्रहात्स्वीकरोषीत्यर्थः ॥
*Gist:* I see here at the sacrificial ground your finest form, eager to get food; when a man brings you food in order, you at once devour it greedily.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
(अश्व (*aśva*)-O horse) गोः (*goḥ*)-of the earth; पदे (*pade*)-in the sacrificial ground; इषः (*iṣaḥ*)-food; आ (*ā*)-all around; जिगीषमाणं (*jigīṣamāṇaṃ*)-desirous of obtaining; ते (*te*)-your; उत्तमं (*uttamaṃ*)-excellent; रूपं (*rūpaṃ*)-form; अत्र (*atra*)-in this sacrificial place; अपश्यं (*apaśyaṃ*)-I see (and); ते (*te*)-your; मर्तः (*martaḥ*)-(servant) man; यदा (*yadā*)-when; भोगं (*bhogaṃ*)-the enjoyable food; अनु आनट् (*anu ānaṭ*)-brings and offers in order; आदित् (*ādit*)-at that very moment; ग्रसिष्ठः (*grasiṣṭhaḥ*) (it), in eager haste to eat; ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)-the foods; अजीगः (*ajīgaḥ*)-you devour.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, I see in this sacrificial place your excellent form, desirous of obtaining food all around the sacrificial ground of the earth; and when your attendant, the man, brings you in order the food that is enjoyable to you and offers it to you, at that very moment you devour those foods in eager haste to eat them.

### Printed p. 257
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ಪ. ೧೨] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 257] (as supplied).*

**Printed English**
I behold your most excellent form coming eagerly to (receive) your food in your (holy) place of earth: when your attendant brings you nigh to the enjoyment (of the provender), therefore greedy, you devour the fodder.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
उत्तमं रूपं अपश्यं (*uttamaṃ rūpaṃ apaśyaṃ*)—this is a mantra to be recited at the time of the Prakramāhuti. In the Prakramāhuti the form of the horse, and the oblation corresponding to it, is to be offered. Therefore he announces "I behold your excellent form". Since it is established that the sacrifice is not complete unless an oblation corresponding to this form of the horse is offered, this Prakramāhuti is a prescribed rite. First the nature of this oblation is described, and afterwards it is examined what the form of the horse is that is fit to receive this oblation.

यथा वै हविषोऽहुतस्य स्कंदेत् । एवमेतत्पशोः स्कंदति यं प्रोक्षितमनालब्धमुत्सृजंति यद्रूपाणि जुहोति सर्वहुतमेवैनं जुहोत्यस्कंदायस्कन्नग्ं .... .... .... .... .... एतानि वा अश्वस्य रूपाणि तान्येवावरुंधे (*yathā vai haviṣo 'hutasya skaṃdet | evametatpaśoḥ skaṃdati yaṃ prokṣitamanālabdhamutsṛjaṃti yadrūpāṇi juhoti sarvahutamevainaṃ juhotyaskaṃdāyaskannagṃ .... .... .... .... .... etāni vā aśvasya rūpāṇi tānyevāvaruṃdhe*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-5) *[abbreviation as printed: ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ.]*

If, at the time of offering the oblation, some part falls outside the altar, the oblation is not complete. If this "form-oblation" is then offered, that deficiency is made good. The oblation is to be offered thus: हिंकाराय स्वाहा ; हिंकृताय स्वाहा (*hiṃkārāya svāhā ; hiṃkṛtāya svāhā*) and so on. These are the forms of the horse. There is also a discussion on this matter:

तदाहुः । अनाहुतिर्वै रूपाणि नैना होतव्या इत्यथो खल्वाहुरत्र वा अश्वमेधः संतिष्ठते यद्रूपाणि जुहोति (*tadāhuḥ | anāhutirvai rūpāṇi nainā hotavyā ityatho khalvāhuratra vā aśvamedhaḥ saṃtiṣṭhate yadrūpāṇi juhoti*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-3-6)

Even those who say that this form-oblation is not needed at all, having learned its importance, admit that the Aśvamedha is completed only by performing it. But if it is asked what this form of the horse is, its description is as follows. This form of the horse, which is of the nature of the universe, was earlier described in

असि यमो अस्यादित्यो अर्वन्नसि त्रितो गुह्येन व्रतेन । असि सोमेन समया विपृक्त आहुस्ते त्रीणि दिवि बंधनानि (*asi yamo asyādityo arvannasi trito guhyena vratena | asi somena samayā vipṛkta āhuste trīṇi divi baṃdhanāni*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-163-3)
*[printed here: "[17]" before the reference, and "Vol. 12]" as a stray footer line, as supplied.]*

### Printed p. 258
*Running head: 258 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

and so on; it was thus shown that the horse has a form pervading the three worlds in the forms of Agni and the others. Not only that, but later also:

तव शरीरं पतॆयिष्णर्वण्तव चित्तं वात इव ध्रजीमान् । तव शृंगाणि विष्ठिता पुरुत्रारण्येषु जर्भुराणा चरंति (*tava śarīraṃ patèyiṣṇarvaṇtava cittaṃ vāta iva dhrajīmān | tava śṛṃgāṇi viṣṭhitā purutrāraṇyeṣu jarbhurāṇā caraṃti*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-163-11)

In this passage the body of the horse, its mind, and the hairs that stand in the place of horns are described, and its form is praised. The nature of this description will be explained later.

यदा ते मर्तः अनु भोगमानट् (*yadā te martaḥ anu bhogamānaṭ*)—"when the human attendant places before you the food that is enjoyable to you": the order of this is described in

प्राजापत्योश्वः स्वयैवैनं देवतया समर्धयंति लाजी३इं ची ३ न्यव्ये गव्य इत्यतिरिक्तमन्नमश्वायोपावहरति (*prājāpatyośvaḥ svayaivainaṃ devatayā samardhayaṃti lājī3iṃ cī 3 nyavye gavya ityatiriktamannamaśvāyopāvaharati*)

and similar passages, in which the foods to be offered to the horse are described.
(Śa. Brā. 13-2-68)

जिगीषमाणं (*jigīṣamāṇaṃ*)—this word has the conventional meaning जेतुमिच्छा (*jetumicchā*), "desirous of conquering". But that meaning is not here. Whenever this word occurs in the Ṛk-saṃhitā, the meaning is "desirous of obtaining" or "fit". For example:

स्तुतासो नो मरुतो मृळयंतूत स्तुतो मघवा शंभविष्ठः । ऊर्ध्वा नः संतु कोम्या वनान्यहानि विश्वा मरुतो जिगीषा (*stutāso no maruto mṛḷayaṃtūta stuto maghavā śaṃbhaviṣṭhaḥ | ūrdhvā naḥ saṃtu komyā vanānyahāni viśvā maruto jigīṣā*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-171-3)

In this the favour of the Maruts is sought. Here for the word जिगीषा (*jigīṣā*) the explanation जिगीषितव्यानि प्राप्तव्यानि उत्तरत्रानुभूयमानानि (*jigīṣitavyāni prāptavyāni uttaratrānubhūyamānāni*) is given; the prayer is "give us all the desired objects that we are to obtain and to experience". Likewise in the present Ṛk also the intention is जिगीषमाणं गंतुमिच्छंतं (*jigīṣamāṇaṃ gaṃtumicchaṃtaṃ*), "desirous of obtaining".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

अत्र (*atra*)—by ऋचितुनुघ (*ṛcitunugha*) *[letters as supplied]* (Pā. Sū. 6-3-133) lengthening occurs in the Saṃhitā text.

जिगीषमाणम् (*jigīṣamāṇam*)—जि (*ji*) जये (*jaye*) (in the sense of conquering), a root. By धातोः कर्मणः (*dhātoḥ karmaṇaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-7) the suffix san in the sense of desire. By अज्ञनग (*ajñanaga*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-16) lengthening of the root when san follows. By the sūtra सन्यजोः (*sanyajoḥ*) (Pā. Sū 6-1-9) reduplication of the root. Shortening of the reduplicative syllable. San. By लिटोर्जेः (*liṭorjeḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-57) the change to k of the root's j. By inversion, when laṭ comes after san, the substitute śānac. By आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*) the muk augment. The ṣatva, ṇatva and so on. By अभ्यस्तानामादिः (*abhyastānāmādiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-189) the initial-acute accent results. A form ending in the accusative singular.

### Printed p. 259
*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೧೨ ] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 259] (as supplied).*

इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—a form ending in the genitive singular. By सावेकाचॆ (*sāvekācè*) *[as supplied]* (Pā Sū. 6-1-168) the udātta accent comes on the case-ending. The genitive has come in the sense of the object.

आनट् (*ānaṭ*)—णश (*ṇaśa*) आदर्शने (*ādarśane*) (in the sense of non-appearance), a root. Here it is used in the sense of going. By छंदसि लुज् लज् लिटः (*chaṃdasi luj laj liṭaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-7) लज् (*laj*) (as supplied) in the present sense. Third person singular, tip. Loss of the i. By बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the luk-elision of the vikaraṇa. By the sūtra व्रश्चभृस्थ (*vraścabhṛstha*) the ṣa-substitution for the root's ś. By the sūtra हल् ज्याभ्यो (*hal jyābhyo*) the loss of the suffix. In the Veda the stem takes the āṭ augment. For the word-final ś the jaś- and car-substitutions. Because of the connection with yat, the *nighāta* accent does not result. Since the āṭ augment is udātta, it becomes initial-acute.

ग्रसिष्ठः (*grasiṣṭhaḥ*)—ग्रसु (*grasu*) अदने (*adane*) (in the sense of eating), a root. When tṛc comes in the agent sense, the form grasitṛ results. आतिशयेन ग्रसिता ग्रसिष्ठः (*ātiśayena grasitā grasiṣṭhaḥ*) (one who devours most is grasiṣṭha). By तुष्पंदसि (*tuṣpaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 5-3-59) the suffix iṣṭhan in the sense of the superlative after a tṛ-ending. By तुरिष्टे मेयस्सु (*turiṣṭe meyassu*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-154) the loss of tṛc when iṣṭhan follows. By इत्यादिर्नित्यम् (*ityādirnityam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-169) the initial-acute accent results.

ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—ओषः पाकः आसु धीयते इति ओषधयः (*oṣaḥ pākaḥ āsu dhīyate iti oṣadhayaḥ*) (those in which ripening is held are *oṣadhis*). By कर्मण्यधिकरणे च (*karmaṇyadhikaraṇe ca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-93) the suffix ki after the root धाञ् (*dhāñ*). By आतो लोप इट च (*āto lopa iṭa ca*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-64) the loss of the root's ā. When the feminine is intended, by कृदिकारादक्तिनः (*kṛdikārādaktinaḥ*) the suffix ṅīp. When śas follows it, the lengthening by homogeneous coalescence with the preceding vowel. Because it is read in the *dāsībhārādi* group, the accent of the base of the former member results. Since it is "ಘಇಂತ" *[as supplied]*, it becomes initial-acute.

अजीगः (*ajīgaḥ*)—गृ (*gṛ*) निगणे (*nigaṇe*) *[as supplied]*, a root. In the Veda लज् (*laj*) (as supplied). By बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the substitute ślu for śap. Caused by ślu, reduplication of the root. By बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the i-substitution for the reduplicative syllable. Caused by sip, guṇa for the ik of the root, with a following r. By the sūtra हल् ज्याभ्यो (*hal jyābhyo*) the loss of sip. Visarga for the repha. By तुजादीनां दीर्घोS (*tujādīnāṃ dīrghoS*) (Pā Sū. 6-1-7) lengthening of the reduplicative syllable. For the stem, because it follows a non-finite verb, the *nighāta* accent results. ‖ 7 ‖

*[as supplied: the label "ಕಂಠಪಾಠ. ಈ" stands here, where the Saṃhitā-pāṭha label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

#### Ṛk 8
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> अनु॑ त्वा॒ रथो॒ अनु॒ मर्यो॑ अर्व॒न्ननु॒ गावोऽनु॒ भगः॒॑॑
> कनीनां ।
> अनु॒ व्राता॑स॒स्तव॑ स॒ख्यमा॑यु॒रनु॑ दे॒वा म॑मिरे वी॒र्यं॑ ते।
> ॥ ८ ॥

> *anu tvā ratho anu maryo arvannanu gāvo 'nu bhagaḥ*
> *kanīnāṃ |*
> *anu vrātāsastava sakhyamāyuranu devā mamire vīryaṃ te|*
> *‖ 8 ‖*

### Printed p. 260
*Running head: 260 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

*[as supplied: the label "ಪವಪಾಠ" stands here, where the Pada-pāṭha label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> अनु । त्वा । रथः । अनु । मर्यः । अर्वन् । अनु । गावः । अनु । भगः । कनीनां ।
> अनु । व्रातासः । तव । सख्यं । ईयुः । अनु । देवाः । ममिरे । वीर्यं । ते ॥ ८ ॥
*[as supplied: the label "ಶಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ" stands here, where the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya label belongs; the script replaced it.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> इदानीं कृत्स्नस्य लोकस्यापि मुख्य इति प्रतिपादयति । हे अर्वन् गमनशीलाश्व त्वा त्वामन्वनुसृत्य रथो गच्छति । ततः पुरतोऽश्वस्य गमनात् । तथा मर्त्यो
> मर्त्यस्त्वा त्वामनु ॥ जात्येकवचनं ॥ अनु गावः । गावोऽपि त्वामनुगच्छंति । तथा
> कनीनां कन्यकानां स्त्रीणां भगो भाग्यं सौंदर्यं त्वद्भगमनु । तथा व्रातासो
> व्राताः संघात्मका अन्येऽश्वसमूहा वस्वादिदेवगणा वा त्वामन्वनुसृत्य तव सख्यमायुः । सखिभावं गच्छंति । देवा ऋत्विजो द्योतमाना देवा ना त्वामनुसृत्य
> ते वीर्यं वीरकर्म ममिरे । स्तुत्या मांति । अतः सर्वोऽपि त्वामनुसरः त्वं तु
> सर्वस्य श्रेष्ठ इत्यर्थः ॥
*Gist:* Chariot, man, cattle, maidens' beauty, troops of horses or gods all follow you; the priests praise your valour; so you are the foremost of all.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
आर्वन् (*ārvan*) *[as supplied]*—O horse; त्वा (*tvā*)—you; रथः (*rathaḥ*)—the excellent chariot; अनु (*anu*)—follows; मर्यः (*maryaḥ*)—man; अनु (*anu*)—comes following; गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—cows; अनु (*anu*)—follow behind; कनीनां (*kanīnāṃ*)—of maidens; भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—the loveliness *[as supplied: ಸೌಂದಯ್ಯವು]*; अनु (*anu*)—follows; व्रातासः (*vrātāsaḥ*)—other troops of horses of the nature of bands, or the groups of gods such as the Vasus; अनु (*anu*)—following you; तव (*tava*)—your; सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—friendship; ईयुः (*īyuḥ*)—obtain; देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the resplendent priests or the gods; अनु (*anu*)—(you) following; ते (*te*)—your; वीर्यं (*vīryaṃ*)—heroic act; ममिरे (*mamire*)—praise with hymns.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
O horse, the excellent chariot follows you. In the same way the man, the cows, the lovely maidens and the band-like groups of gods follow you and obtain your friendship. The resplendent priests, following you, praise your heroic act with hymns.

**Printed English**
The car follows you, O Horse : men attend you ; cattle follow you ;
the loveliness of maidens (waits) upon you ; troops of demigods following
you have sought your friendship ; the gods themselves have been admirers
of your vigour.

*(The Special Topics of this Ṛk follow in the next window.)*

### Printed p. 261
*Running head: 261 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 12] (as supplied).*

**Special Topics** *(continuing Ṛk 8)*

त्वं तु सर्वस्य श्रेष्ठ इत्यर्थः (*tvaṃ tu sarvasya śreṣṭha ityarthaḥ*)—"You are the best of all, that is the meaning": thus Sāyaṇa has stated the purport of this Ṛk, meaning "you are better than all, gods, men and the rest."

देवा ममिरे वीर्यं ते (*devā mamire vīryaṃ te*)—Here the word देवाः (*devāḥ*) is explained as the priests or the gods. They praise the heroic deeds of the horse with hymns. The greatness of the horse, praised with those hymns, is described in the following manner.

देवस्य त्वा सवितुः प्रसवेऽश्विनोर्बाहुभ्यां पूष्णो हस्ताभ्यामा दद इमामगृभ्नाश्रशनामृतस्य पूर्व आयुषि विदथेषु कव्या । तया देवास्सुतमा बभूवुर्यतस्य सामन्थरमारपंती (*devasya tvā savituḥ prasave 'śvinorbāhubhyāṃ pūṣṇo hastābhyāmā dada imāmagṛbhnāśraśanāmṛtasya pūrva āyuṣi vidatheṣu kavyā | tayā devāssutamā babhūvuryatasya sāmantharamārapaṃtī*)

(Śrī Saṃ. 2-1-10-1) *[as supplied: reference abbreviation and numbers as printed]*
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-11-1)

O horse, by the impulse of the god Savitṛ, I take you with the arms of the Aśvins and with the hands of Pūṣan and lead you along. The poets of old took up this sacrificial bond at the times of sacrifice. Therefore it became possible for the gods to obtain the pressed Soma juice. The satisfaction of the gods and the others is to be obtained only through your favour.

अभिधा असि भुवनमसि यंतासि धर्तासि सोऽग्निं वैश्वानरग्ं सप्रथसं गच्छ स्वाहाकृतः पृथिव्यां यंता राड्यंतासि यमनो धर्तासि धरुणः कृष्यै त्वा क्षेमाय त्वारय्यै त्वा पोषाय त्वा पृथिव्यै त्वांतरिक्षाय त्वा दिवे त्वा सते त्वासते त्वाद्भ्यस्यषधीभ्यस्त्वा विश्वेभ्यस्त्वा भूतेभ्यः (*abhidhā asi bhuvanamasi yaṃtāsi dhartāsi so 'gniṃ vaiśvānaragṃ saprathasaṃ gaccha svāhākṛtaḥ pṛthivyāṃ yaṃtā rāḍyaṃtāsi yamano dhartāsi dharuṇaḥ kṛṣyai tvā kṣemāya tvārayyai tvā poṣāya tvā pṛthivyai tvāṃtarikṣāya tvā dive tvā sate tvāsate tvādbhyasyaṣadhībhyastvā viśvebhyastvā bhūtebhyaḥ*)

(Trai. Saṃ. 7-8-19) *[as supplied: abbreviation "ತ್ರೈ. ಸಂ."]*
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-11-1)

O horse, you yourself are the all-enveloping covering. You are also the whole world, the controller and the support. Go to Vaiśvānara as a victor. You are the controller of the earth, and you are the support and the foundation of the earth. In the same way you alone are the foundation and the support of the atmosphere, of the heavenly world, of the plants, of the waters *[as supplied: the print reads "ಅಪ್ಪಿಗೂ"]* and of all kinds of beings. For all that exists, and likewise for all that does not exist, that is, for the entire universe made of being and non-being, you alone are the foundation.

विभूर्मात्रा प्रभोः पित्राश्वोऽसि हयोऽस्यत्योऽसि नरोऽस्यॆर्वासि सप्तिरसि वाज्यसि वृषासि नृमणा आसि ययुर्नामास्यादित्यानां पत्वान्विहि (*vibhūrmātrā prabhoḥ pitrāśvo 'si hayo 'syatyo 'si naro 'syèrvāsi saptirasi vājyasi vṛṣāsi nṛmaṇā āsi yayurnāmāsyādityānāṃ patvānvihi*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-12-1)

You are the horse (aśva) that pervades everything; you are the haya and the atya that conquer all kinds of animals; you are the man (nara) who leads those who keep you as their carrier; you are the ārva skilled in going, the sapti who shows skill in battles, the swift-going vājin, and one bearing the name yayu, which is the dearest of all the names of horses; and by your greatness you reach the Āditya himself.

### Printed p. 262
*Running head: 262 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

उपयामगृहीतोऽसि प्रजापतये त्वा जुष्टं गृह्णामि तस्य ते द्यौर्महिमा नक्षत्राणि रूपमादित्यस्ते तेजः (*upayāmagṛhīto 'si prajāpataye tvā juṣṭaṃ gṛhṇāmi tasya te dyaurmahimā nakṣatrāṇi rūpamādityaste tejaḥ*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 7-5-17-1)

तस्य ते पृथिवी महिमौषधयो वनस्पतयो रूपमग्निस्तीतेजः (*tasya te pṛthivī mahimauṣadhayo vanaspatayo rūpamagnistītejaḥ*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 7-5-16-1)

When you are in the heavenly world, the sky is your greatness, the stars are your form and the Āditya is your brilliance. When you are on the earth, the earth is your greatness, the plants and trees are your form and Agni is your brilliance. Since the priests praise the nature of the sacrificial horse in this and similar ways, it is said देवा ममिरे वीर्यं ते (*devā mamire vīryaṃ te*).

व्रातासः (*vrātāsaḥ*)—व्रात (*vrāta*) means a group. The Nirukta-author has included this word among the names of men (Ni. 3-7). For this word, in the present Ṛk, Sāyaṇa has explained संघात्मकाः अन्ये अश्वसमूहाः वस्वादिदेवगणा ना (*saṃghātmakāḥ anye aśvasamūhāḥ vasvādidevagaṇā nā*), that is, other bands of horses, and groups of gods such as the Vasus. It is natural that other horses should follow this horse. But if the question arises why the other gods should follow it, (the answer is that) this horse has made it possible for the gods to obtain many things that were not available to them. To take one example—

देवा ना उदंचः । स्वर्गं लोकं न प्राजानंस्तमश्वः प्राजानद्यदश्वेनोदंचो यंति स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य प्रजात्यै (*devā nā udaṃcaḥ | svargaṃ lokaṃ na prājānaṃstamaśvaḥ prājānadyadaśvenodaṃco yaṃti svargasya lokasya prajātyai*)

(Śa. Brā. 13-2-8-1)

The gods, going upward, did not know the way to the world of heaven. Then, following the horse, they learnt that way with the help of the horse that knew it; and many such occasions are described. Therefore it is said व्रातासः अनु तव सख्यमीयुः (*vrātāsaḥ anu tava sakhyamīyuḥ*).

Moreover, in तया देवास्सुतमा बभूवुर्यतस्य सामन्त्सरमारंपती (*tayā devāssutamā babhūvuryatasya sāmantsaramāraṃpatī*) (Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-11) it is stated that the gods obtained the pressed Soma juice with the help of the sacrificial horse; so it is also shown that friendship with the horse must be made for the sake of Soma and the other foods that are their support.

मर्यः (*maryaḥ*)—मर्यो मनुष्यो मरणधर्मा (*maryo manuṣyo maraṇadharmā*): "marya is man, he who is subject to death." Because he has the nature of dying, man is called marya (Ni. 3-15). With the help of the sacrificial horse man can obtain all good; therefore he follows this horse—this is one meaning. Secondly, when this horse is let loose on a campaign of conquest preceding the sacrifice, it is explained that all preparations are made for its protection; and it is described that, since protecting this sacrificial horse, which can bring welfare to the whole world, brings good to all, gods, men and the rest, everyone follows it for its protection.

### Printed p. 263
*Running head: 263 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 12] (as supplied).*

कनीनां भगः अनु (*kanīnāṃ bhagaḥ anu*)—When the horse is adorned with various ornaments, there is a custom that the chief queen and her women friends do it. One opinion is that the horse has a natural feminine beauty; another opinion is that when it is adorned by beautiful women, the horse too acquires that beauty.

गायत्रेण छंदसेति महिष्यभ्यनक्ति । .... .... .... पत्न्योऽभ्यंजंति श्रियै वा एत द्रूपं यत्पत्न्यः श्रियमेवास्मिन्दधाति (*gāyatreṇa chaṃdaseti mahiṣyabhyanakti | .... .... .... patnyo 'bhyaṃjaṃti śriyai vā eta drūpaṃ yatpatnyaḥ śriyamevāsmindadhāti*)

(Śa. Brā. 13-2-6-4)

Since it is described thus and in similar ways, the meaning is that when the horse is adorned by the chief queens and others, its feminine beauty increases. Or there may be a still deeper, hidden meaning in this.

*Grammatical Derivation (transcribed, not explained):*
मर्यः (*maryaḥ*)—root मृज् (*mṛj*) in the sense of abandoning life *[as supplied]*; suffix ṇyat; by Pā. Sū. 3-1-123 (निष्कर्क्यदेवहूयॆ (*niṣkarkyadevahūyè*), preceded by छंदसि (*chaṃdasi*)) the suffix yat; guṇa with r following by सार्वधातुकार्थधातुकयोः (*sārvadhātukārthadhātukayoḥ*); by यतोनावः (*yatonāvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-213) initial acute; singular used because it is a word denoting a class.
व्रातासः (*vrātāsaḥ*)—when jas follows, āsuk-augment by आज्जसेरसुक् (*ājjaserasuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50).
सख्यम् (*sakhyam*)—सख्युर्भावः सख्यम् (*sakhyurbhāvaḥ sakhyam*); suffix ya by सख्युर्यः (*sakhyuryaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 5-1-126); elision of i by यस्येति च (*yasyeti ca*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-148); final acute by the suffix accent.
ईयुः (*īyuḥ*)—root इण् (*iṇ*) in the sense of going; by छंदसि लुज्लज्लिटः (*chaṃdasi lujlajliṭaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-6) liṭ in the present sense; third person plural form; being after a non-finite word, the nighāta accent arises.
ममिरे (*mamire*)—root माज् (*māj*) in the sense of measuring; liṭ in the Vedic manner as before; third person plural form; the tiṅanta nighāta accent arises.
वीर्यम् (*vīryam*)—वीरस्य कर्म (*vīrasya karma*), vīryam; suffix yat in the sense of relation; as explained before, the initial acute by यतोनावः (*yatonāvaḥ*) does not arise in the case of the word vīra; by तित्‌स्वरितम् (*titsvaritam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-185) the svarita accent arises. ॥ 8 ॥

### Printed p. 264
*Running head: 264 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 9
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> हिर॑ण्यशृं॒गोऽयो॑ अस्य॒ पादा॒ मनो॑जवा॒ अव॑र॒ इंद्र॑
> आसीत् ।
> दे॒वा इद॑स्य हवि॒रद्य॑माय॒न्नो अर्वं॑तं प्रथ॒मो अ॒ध्यति॑ष्ठत् ॥ ९ ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> हिरण्यशृंगः । अयः । अस्य । पादाः । मनःजवाः । अवरः । इंद्रः । आसीत् ।
> देवाः । इत् । अस्य । हविःअद्यं । आयन् । यः । अर्वंतं । प्रथमः । अधिअतिष्ठत् ।
> ॥ ९ ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> अयमश्वो हिरण्यशृंगो हितरमणीयशृंगो वा उन्नतशिरस्को हृदयरमणशृंगस्थानीयशिरोरुहो वा । अस्य पादा अयोऽयोमया. । अयःपिंडसदृता इत्यर्थः । तथा मनोजवा मनोवेगाः । अथवैतदिंद्रविशेषणं । ईदृशस्याश्वस्य सामर्थ्यं प्रति मनोवेग इंद्रोऽप्यवरो निकृष्ट आसीत् । किंचास्याश्वस्य
> हविरद्यं हविषोdनं भक्षणं ॥ स्वार्थिको यत् ॥ आदनयोग्यं हनिर्वापेक्ष्य देवा
> इत् सर्वॆऽपि देवा आयन् । प्राप्ताः । य इंद्रोऽर्वंतं प्रथमः प्रथमभावी
> सन्नध्यतिष्ठत् अधिष्ठितवान् । सहविष्टेन स्वकीयत्वेन वाश्रितवानित्यर्थः । इंद्र एणं
> प्रथमो अध्यतिष्ठदिति ह्युक्तं ॥
*Gist:* This horse has golden or lovely horn-like mane and iron-firm feet; Indra is inferior to it in speed; all gods come for its oblation; Indra mounted it first.
**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
अयं अश्वः (*ayaṃ aśvaḥ*)—this horse; हिरण्यशृंगः (*hiraṇyaśṛṃgaḥ*)—possessing manes that are golden (lit. "golden-toothed", as supplied) and stand in the place of horns; अस्य (*asya*)—of this horse; पादाः (*pādāḥ*)—the feet; अयः (*ayaḥ*)—firm like steel; मनोजवाः (*manojavāḥ*)—swift like the speed of mind; इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra; अवरः (*avaraḥ*)—inferior to this horse; आसीत् (*āsīt*)—is; अस्य (*asya*)—of this horse; आद्यं (*ādyaṃ*)—fit to be eaten; हविः (*haviḥ*)—desiring the oblation-food; देवा इत् (*devā it*)—all the gods also; आयन् (*āyan*)—have come; यः (*yaḥ*)—that same Indra who; अर्वंतं (*arvaṃtaṃ*)—this horse; प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—before all; अध्यतिष्ठत् (*adhyatiṣṭhat*)—mounted and sat.

### Printed p. 265
*Running head: 265 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 12] (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
The mane-hairs of this horse, which are like horns, are golden-coloured and attractive. Its feet are firm like steel. Indra, who is swift as the speed of mind, is inferior to this horse in speed. Desiring the edible oblation of this horse, all the gods have come. Before all others Indra mounted and sat on this horse.

**Printed English**
His name is of gold ; his feet are of iron; and fleet as thought, Indra
is his inferior (in speed). The gods have come to partake of his (being offered
as) oblations : the first who mounted the horse was Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
मनोजवाः (*manojavāḥ*)—Two kinds of construction have been made for this word. First, explaining it as मनोवेगाः (*manovegāḥ*) with reference to the horse: the speed of this horse is swift like the speed of the mind, and Indra's speed is less than its speed. Secondly, taking this word as an epithet of Indra, it is explained: अथवा एतदिंद्रविशेषणं । ईदृशस्याश्वस्य सामर्थ्यं प्रति मनोवेगः इंद्रः अपि निकृष्टः असीत् (*athavā etadiṃdraviśeṣaṇaṃ | īdṛśasyāśvasya sāmarthyaṃ prati manovegaḥ iṃdraḥ api nikṛṣṭaḥ asīt*) *[as supplied: "ಅಸೀತ್" in the Kannada comment, against "ಆಸೀತ್" in the Ṛk]*—that is, even Indra, who is swift like the speed of the mind, appeared inferior.

यस्ते रथो मनसो जवीयानेंद्र तेन सोमपेयाय याहि तूयमा ते हरयः प्र द्रवंतु येभिर्यासि वृषभिर्मंदमानः (*yaste ratho manaso javīyāneṃdra tena somapeyāya yāhi tūyamā te harayaḥ pra dravaṃtu yebhiryāsi vṛṣabhirmaṃdamānaḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-112-2)
Since in Ṛks such as this the chariot of Indra is described as having a speed greater than the speed of the mind, the second meaning also suits here.

हिरण्यशृंगः (*hiraṇyaśṛṃgaḥ*)—हितरमणीयशृंगो वा हृदयरमणशृंगस्थानीयशिरोरुहो वा (*hitaramaṇīyaśṛṃgo vā hṛdayaramaṇaśṛṃgasthānīyaśiroruho vā*). It means either a horn made of gold, or a mane that is attractive and stands in the place of a horn; for the word हिरण्य (*hiraṇya*) Sāyaṇa has explained "beneficial and delightful." The Nirukta-author also, for this word (gives the explanation):

हिरण्यं कस्माद्ब्रियत आयम्यमानमिति वा । ह्रियिते जनाज्जनमिति वा । हितरमणं भवतीति वा । हृदयरमणं भवतीति वा । हर्यतीर्वा स्यात्प्रॆप्साकर्मणः (*hiraṇyaṃ kasmādbriyata āyamyamānamiti vā | hriyite janājjanamiti vā | hitaramaṇaṃ bhavatīti vā | hṛdayaramaṇaṃ bhavatīti vā | haryatīrvā syātprèpsākarmaṇaḥ*)
(Ni. 2-80) *[as supplied: the reference is printed with a stray Telugu-script letter, "సి. ೨-೮೦"]*

### Printed p. 266
*Running head: 266 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

*(The Nirukta explanation continues:)* he has shown the etymology that gold has this name because it spreads widely in the form of ornaments and the like, or because it moves from one person to another, or because it is useful and gladdening, or because it is desired with a most intense wish. Just so here also, it may be a mane attractive to the sight and standing in the place of a horn, or it may be a horn made of gold.

That gold was created from this very horse is also stated in many places.

अश्वस्य वा आलब्धस्य । रेत उदक्रामत्तत्सुवर्णग्ं हिरण्यमभवद्यत्सुवर्णग्ं हिरण्यं ददात्यश्वमेव रेतसा समर्धयति (*aśvasya vā ālabdhasya | reta udakrāmattatsuvarṇagṃ hiraṇyamabhavadyatsuvarṇagṃ hiraṇyaṃ dadātyaśvameva retasā samardhayati*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-1-3)

In sentences like this it is said that gold was created from the seed of the horse; therefore the limbs of the horse are in the form of gold, and here हिरण्यशृंग (*hiraṇyaśṛṃga*) may mean a horn made of gold.

अद्यं हविः देवा इत् आयन् (*adyaṃ haviḥ devā it āyan*)—For the description that the gods came desiring this edible oblation of the Aśvamedha there is a narrative setting.

प्रजापतिर्देवेभ्यो यज्ञान्त्यादिशत् । स आत्मन्नश्वमेधमधत्त ते देवाः प्रजापतिमब्रुवन्नेष वै यज्ञो यदश्वमेधोपि नोऽत्रास्तु भाग इति तेभ्य तेभ्य एतानन्नहोमानकल्पयद्यदन्न होमाञ्जु होति देवानेव तत्प्रीणाति (*prajāpatirdevebhyo yajñāntyādiśat | sa ātmannaśvamedhamadhatta te devāḥ prajāpatimabruvanneṣa vai yajño yadaśvamedhopi no 'trāstu bhāga iti tebhya tebhya etānannahomānakalpayadyadanna homāñju hoti devāneva tatprīṇāti*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-2-1-1)

Prajāpati assigned sacrifices to the gods, but kept the Aśvamedha for himself. Then the gods said, "Since this Aśvamedha is also a principal sacrifice, let us also have a share in it"; whereupon Prajāpati devised the anna-homa (food-oblations). Therefore, since the anna-homa is performed at the time of the Aśvamedha, the gods are satisfied: this matter is conveyed by the sentence "देवा इदस्य हविरदम्यायन् (*devā idasya haviradamyāyan*)" *[as supplied: so printed, "ಹವಿರದಮ್ಯಾಯನ್"]*.

अर्वंतं प्रथमोऽध्यतिष्ठत् (*arvaṃtaṃ prathamo 'dhyatiṣṭhat*)—When it is said that Indra first took possession, it can be taken, like the sentence "इंद्र एणं प्रथमोऽध्यतिष्ठत् (*iṃdra eṇaṃ prathamo 'dhyatiṣṭhat*)", to mean that Indra first mounted this horse; or:

दिशस्सुषुवाणेनाभिजित्या इत्याहुर्यदेतानि हवीग्गंषि निर्वपति दिशामभिजित्या एतयावा इंद्रं देवा अयाजयन्तस्मादिंद्रसव एतया मनुं मदुष्यास्तस्मान्मनुसवो यथेंद्रो देवानां यथा मनुर्मनुष्याणामेवं भवति (*diśassuṣuvāṇenābhijityā ityāhuryadetāni havīggaṃṣi nirvapati diśāmabhijityā etayāvā iṃdraṃ devā ayājayantasmādiṃdrasava etayā manuṃ maduṣyāstasmānmanusavo yatheṃdro devānāṃ yathā manurmanuṣyāṇāmevaṃ bhavati*)
(Trai. Saṃ. 7-5-12-2, 3) *[as supplied: abbreviation "ತ್ರೈ ಸಂ."]*

### Printed p. 267
*Running head: 267 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 12] (as supplied).*

*[Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 9:]* In the (passage) just quoted, it may have been said "first mounted" (प्रथमोध्यतिष्ठत् (*prathamodhyatiṣṭhat*)) because in the homa that is the means of conquest of the directions the gods gave Indra the first place, and also because, that being a chief part of the Aśvamedha, the foremost place in it fell to Indra. Sāyaṇa too has explained: स्वहविष्टेन स्वकीयत्तेन वा आश्रितवान् इत्यर्थः (*svahaviṣṭena svakīyattena vā āśritavān ityarthaḥ*), "he took possession of it with his own oblation or as his own."

*Grammatical Derivation (transcribed, not explained):*
हिरण्यशृंगः (*hiraṇyaśṛṃgaḥ*)—हिरण्ये शृंगे यस्य सः (*hiraṇye śṛṃge yasya saḥ*); by बहुप्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् (*bahuprīhau prakṛtyā pūrvapadam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-1) the accent of the first member's own base arises. Likewise in मनोजवाः (*manojavāḥ*), being a bahuvrīhi compound, the accent of the first member's base arises.
आसीत् (*āsīt*)—root अस भुवि (*asa bhuvi*); laṅ, third person singular form; being after a word that is not a finite verb, the nighāta accent arises.
हविरद्यम् (*haviradyam*)—हविषः अद्यम् हविरद्यम् (*haviṣaḥ adyam haviradyam*); root अद भक्षणे (*ada bhakṣaṇe*); suffix yat in its own sense or in the sense of fitness; by यतोऽनावः (*yato 'nāvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-213) the initial acute arises; in the compound, by गतिकारकोपपदात् (*gatikārakopapadāt*)… (Pā. Sū. 6-1-139) the accent of the base of the latter member ending in kṛt arises.
आयन् (*āyan*)—root इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*); laṅ, third person plural, suffix jhi; by रोंतः (*roṃtaḥ*) the substitute anta; by इतश्च (*itaśca*) elision of the final i; by आडजादीनां (*āḍajādīnāṃ*) the aṭ-augment to the stem; by इणोयण् (*iṇoyaṇ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-81) yaṇ for the i of the root; being after a word that is not a finite verb, the nighāta accent arises.
अध्यतिष्ठत् (*adhyatiṣṭhat*)—adhi is the prefix; root ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ (*ṣṭhā gatinivṛttau*); laṅ, third person singular form; because of the connection with the word यः (*yaḥ*), by यद्वृत्तांतित्यम् (*yadvṛttāṃtityam*) (printed "ಪಾ. ಸವ. ೮-೧-೬೬", i.e. Pā. Sū. 8-1-66 *[as supplied: "ಪಾ. ಸವ."]*) the nighāta accent is prohibited; the augment aṭ being udātta, it has the initial acute; by तिजिचोदात्तवति (*tijicodāttavati*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71) the nighāta accent arises for the preverb (adhi). ॥ 9 ॥
*[stray line as supplied: :C:]*

### Printed p. 268
*Running head: 268 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 10
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> ई॒र्मांता॑सः॒ सिलि॑कमध्यमासः॒ सं शूर॑णासो दि॒व्यासो॒॑
> अत्याः॑ ।
> हं॒सा इ॑व श्रेणि॒शो य॑तंते॒ यदाक्षि॑षुर्दि॒न्यमज्म॒मश्वाः॑ ।
> ॥ १० ॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> ईर्मअंतासः । सिलिकमध्यमासः । सं । शूरणासः । दिव्यासः । अत्याः ।
> हंसाः इव । श्रेणिशः । यतंते । यत् । आक्षिषुः । दिव्यं । अज्मं । अश्वाः ॥ १० ॥
**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**
> अत्राश्वसमूहस्तुतिद्वारेणायं स्तूयते । ईर्मांतास ईरिताः प्रेरिता अंता
> येषां ते तथोक्ताः । विरलांता इत्यर्थः । सिलिकमध्यमासः संस्कृताः संगता मध्यप्रदेशा येषां ते तथोक्ताः । मध्यॆ निविडा इत्यर्थः । अथवा शरीरस्यैते विशेषणे
> ईर्म ईरितः । पुरतो नुन्न इत्यर्थः । तादृशः पृथुरंतो जघनप्रदेशो येषां
> ते तादृशाः । तथा सिलिकमध्यमास । संस्कृतः संगतः संलग्नो मध्यभागो येषां ते
> तादृशाः। पुष्टत्वात्पौढपृष्ठाः संलग्नमध्याश्चेत्यर्थः । जघनपुरोभागावपेक्ष्यसिलिकमध्यमा इत्युक्तं । समित्ययमुपसर्गो यतंत इत्याख्यातेन संबंधयितव्यः ।
> शूरणासो विक्रमशीला दिव्यासो दिवि भवा अत्या आतनशीलाः । यद्यप्ययमश्वनाम
> तथाप्यश्वशब्दस्य वक्ष्यमाणत्पादयं यौगिकोऽवगंतव्यः । ईदृशाः अश्वाः श्रेणिशः ।
> एकव्यापाराणां बहूनां समूहः श्रेणिरित्युच्यते । शीघ्रधावनाय श्रेणिशः पंक्तिभूय सं यतंते । सम्यग्यत्नं कुर्वंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः । हंसा इव । ते यथा
> श्रेणिभूता ईर्मांतास इत्याद्युक्तरूपाः संतॊऽंतरिक्षे गमनाय यतंते तद्वत् ।
> कदा । उच्यते । यद्यदाश्वा दिव्यमज्मं गमनं गंतव्यं वा मार्गमाक्षिषुः व्याप्प्पुवंति तदा ॥ अयं मंत्रो यास्केनैवं व्याख्यातः । ईर्मांताः समारितांताः
> पृथ्वंता ना सिलकमध्यमाः संस्कृतमध्यमाः शीर्षमध्यमा वा । अपि वा शिर आदित्यो
> भवति यदनुशेते सर्वाणि भूतानि मध्यॆ चैषां तिष्ठति । इदमहीतरच्छिर एतस्मादेव समाश्रितान्येतदिंद्रियाणि भवंति । सं शूरणासो दिव्यासो अत्या! शूरः शवते-

> *atrāśvasamūhastutidvāreṇāyaṃ stūyate | īrmāṃtāsa īritāḥ preritā aṃtā*
> *yeṣāṃ te tathoktāḥ | viralāṃtā ityarthaḥ | silikamadhyamāsaḥ saṃskṛtāḥ saṃgatā madhyapradeśā yeṣāṃ te tathoktāḥ | madhyè niviḍā ityarthaḥ | athavā śarīrasyaite viśeṣaṇe*
> *īrma īritaḥ | purato nunna ityarthaḥ | tādṛśaḥ pṛthuraṃto jaghanapradeśo yeṣāṃ*
> *te tādṛśāḥ | tathā silikamadhyamāsa | saṃskṛtaḥ saṃgataḥ saṃlagno madhyabhāgo yeṣāṃ te*
> *tādṛśāḥ| puṣṭatvātpauḍhapṛṣṭhāḥ saṃlagnamadhyāścetyarthaḥ | jaghanapurobhāgāvapekṣyasilikamadhyamā ityuktaṃ | samityayamupasargo yataṃta ityākhyātena saṃbaṃdhayitavyaḥ |*
> *śūraṇāso vikramaśīlā divyāso divi bhavā atyā ātanaśīlāḥ | yadyapyayamaśvanāma*
> *tathāpyaśvaśabdasya vakṣyamāṇatpādayaṃ yaugiko 'vagaṃtavyaḥ | īdṛśāḥ aśvāḥ śreṇiśaḥ |*
> *ekavyāpārāṇāṃ bahūnāṃ samūhaḥ śreṇirityucyate | śīghradhāvanāya śreṇiśaḥ paṃktibhūya saṃ yataṃte | samyagyatnaṃ kurvaṃti | tatra dṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | haṃsā iva | te yathā*
> *śreṇibhūtā īrmāṃtāsa ityādyuktarūpāḥ saṃtò 'ṃtarikṣe gamanāya yataṃte tadvat |*
> *kadā | ucyate | yadyadāśvā divyamajmaṃ gamanaṃ gaṃtavyaṃ vā mārgamākṣiṣuḥ vyāpppuvaṃti tadā ‖ ayaṃ maṃtro yāskenaivaṃ vyākhyātaḥ | īrmāṃtāḥ samāritāṃtāḥ*
> *pṛthvaṃtā nā silakamadhyamāḥ saṃskṛtamadhyamāḥ śīrṣamadhyamā vā | api vā śira ādityo*
> *bhavati yadanuśete sarvāṇi bhūtāni madhyè caiṣāṃ tiṣṭhati | idamahītaracchira etasmādeva samāśritānyetadiṃdriyāṇi bhavaṃti | saṃ śūraṇāso divyāso atyā! śūraḥ śavate-*

### Printed p. 269
*Running head: 269 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 12] (as supplied; the print has a stray full stop before "೧೨").*

> र्गतिकर्मणो दिव्या दिविजा अत्या अतनाः । हंसा इव श्रेणिशो यतंते । हंसा
> हंतेर्घ्नंत्यध्वानं ! श्रेणिश इति श्रेणिः श्रयतेः समाश्रिता भवंति । यदाक्षिषुर्यदापुर्दिव्यमज्ममजनिमाजिमश्वाः । नि- ४-१३ । इति ॥
*Gist:* The sun's horses are praised: full-hipped, slender-waisted, brave, celestial, they strive in rows like swans when they traverse the heavenly path; Yāska's Nirukta explanation is cited.
**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**
यत् (*yat*)—when; अश्वाः (*aśvāḥ*)—the horses (of the Sun); दिव्यं अज्मं (*divyaṃ ajmaṃ*)—the path of the world of the gods; आक्षिषुः (*ākṣiṣuḥ*)—traverse (then); ईर्मांतासः (*īrmāṃtāsaḥ*)—having full-grown haunch-girths; सिलिकमध्यमासः (*silikamadhyamāsaḥ*)—having evenly formed sides of the waist; शूरणासः (*śūraṇāsaḥ*)—possessing vigour; दिव्यासः (*divyāsaḥ*)—born in the world of the gods; अत्याः (*atyāḥ*)—the horses, given to movement; श्रेणिशः (*śreṇiśaḥ*)—forming rows; हंसा इव (*haṃsā iva*)—like swans; संयतंते (*saṃyataṃte*)—strive (to fly).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**
When the horses of the Sun traverse the path of the world of the gods, those horses, with full-grown haunch-girths, evenly formed sides of the waist, vigour, and birth in the world of the gods, strive to fly, forming rows like flying swans.

**Printed English**
The full-haunched, slender-waisted, high-spirited, and celestial coursers
(of the sun), gallop along like swans in rows, when the horses spread
along the heavenly path.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**
Here the movement of the horses of the Āditya in the heavenly world is compared to a garland of swans flying in a flock, and in this simile, besides describing the beauty of the horses' going, their supernatural form and powers are also described. The author of the Nirukta has commented on this Ṛk; it is quoted in the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (Ni. 4-13).

ईर्मांतासः (*īrmāṃtāsaḥ*)—ईर्मांताः समीरितांताः पृथ्व्यंता वा (*īrmāṃtāḥ samīritāṃtāḥ pṛthvyaṃtā vā*): the meaning is "having well-formed, or fully stretched, middles (girths)."

सिलिकमध्यमासः (*silikamadhyamāsaḥ*)—the Kannada comment quotes the Nirukta: शीर्षमध्यमा वा अपि ना शिर आदित्यो भवति यदनुशेते सर्वाणि भूतानि मध्यॆ चैषां तिष्ठति (*śīrṣamadhyamā vā api nā śira ādityo bhavati yadanuśete sarvāṇi bhūtāni madhyè caiṣāṃ tiṣṭhati*), that is, "well-formed in the middle, or having the head in the middle; for the head is the Āditya, because all beings lie (follow) under him, and he stands in the midst of them." *[as supplied: the comment begins with the head-word and a dash, and has "ನಾ" where the bhāṣya has "ವಾ"]*

### Printed p. 270
*Running head: 270 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ.೧೬೩.] (as supplied).*

*(Continuing सिलिकमध्यमासः (*silikamadhyamāsaḥ*):)* It means either "with evenly pressed-in sides of the waist" or "with the head in the middle." Here the word śīrṣa (śiras) applies to the Sun. The author of the Nirukta explains that this epithet is used because he pervades all beings and stands in the midst of them. Durgācārya, commenting on this, says यस्माच्चासौ अनुप्रविश्य प्राणभावेन सर्व भूतानि शीते । अस्ते तस्मात्सर्वभूताश्रयणात् शिर आदित्यः (*yasmāccāsau anupraviśya prāṇabhāvena sarva bhūtāni śīte | aste tasmātsarvabhūtāśrayaṇāt śira ādityaḥ*), and explains that, since he enters all beings in the form of breath and pervades everything, he is indicated by the epithet "head" (śiras).

चित्रं देवानामुदगादनीकं चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्याग्नेः । आप्रा द्यावापृथिवी अंतरिक्षं सूर्य आत्मा जगतस्तस्थुषश्च (*citraṃ devānāmudagādanīkaṃ cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasyāgneḥ | āprā dyāvāpṛthivī aṃtarikṣaṃ sūrya ātmā jagatastasthuṣaśca*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-115-1)
In Ṛks such as this, the Sun, who is described as the self of the whole world, has here the synonymous name "head" (śiras). This is the purport of Durgācārya's commentary. स चैतेषामश्वानां मध्यॆ तिष्ठति (*sa caiteṣāmaśvānāṃ madhyè tiṣṭhati*): since this Āditya, who is of the form of the head, is in the midst of the horses, it should be understood that सिलिकमध्यमाः (*silikamadhyamāḥ*) is an epithet of the horses.

संशूरणासः (*saṃśūraṇāsaḥ*)—शूरः शवतेर्गतिकर्मणः (*śūraḥ śavatergatikarmaṇaḥ*): it is formed from the root शु (*śu*) in the sense of going.
दिविजाः (*divijāḥ*)—दिव्याः दिविजाः (*divyāḥ divijāḥ*): born in the heavenly world.
अत्याः (*atyāḥ*)—अपेक्षितप्रदेशं प्रति आतनशीलाः (*apekṣitapradeśaṃ prati ātanaśīlāḥ*): it means one having the nature and power of travelling to whatever place he wishes. This word is an epithet of Agni, of the horse and of the Āditya.

एष प्र पूर्वीरव तस्य चम्रिषोऽत्यो न योषामुदयंस्त भुर्वणः । दक्षं महे पाययते हिरण्ययं रथमावृत्या हरियोगमृभ्वसं (*eṣa pra pūrvīrava tasya camriṣo 'tyo na yoṣāmudayaṃsta bhurvaṇaḥ | dakṣaṃ mahe pāyayate hiraṇyayaṃ rathamāvṛtyā hariyogamṛbhvasaṃ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-56-1)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-56-1) *[as supplied: the reference is printed twice]*
In the Ṛk *[as supplied: printed "ಮಕ್ಕಿನಲ್ಲಿ"]* (just quoted) अत्यः (*atyaḥ*) is a synonym of "horse"; and in

अस्य श्लोको दिवीयते पृथिव्यामत्तो न यंसद्यक्षभृद्विचेताः । मृगाणां न हेतयो यंति चेमा बृहस्पतेरहिमाया अभि द्यून् (*asya śloko divīyate pṛthivyāmatto na yaṃsadyakṣabhṛdvicetāḥ | mṛgāṇāṃ na hetayo yaṃti cemā bṛhaspaterahimāyā abhi dyūn*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-190-4)
and other Ṛks, आतनशीलः आदित्यः (*ātanaśīlaḥ ādityaḥ*) ("the Āditya, who has the nature of travelling") is also understood. In the present Ṛk it is an epithet of the horses.

### Printed p. 271
*Running head: 271 Ṛgveda-saṃhitā [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 13] (as supplied; the preceding pages have "Varga 12").*

अज्जं (*ajjaṃ*) *[as supplied: so printed here; the Ṛk has अज्मं (*ajmaṃ*)]*—This word is used in many senses. It is read among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of houses (Ni. 3-13). Besides the usages in these two senses,

उचथ्येण वपुषि यः स्वराळुत वायो धृतस्नाः । अश्वेषितं रजेषितं शुनेषितं प्राज्ञ तदिदं नु तत् (*ucathyeṇa vapuṣi yaḥ svarāḷuta vāyo dhṛtasnāḥ | aśveṣitaṃ rajeṣitaṃ śuneṣitaṃ prājña tadidaṃ nu tat*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-46-28)
there the word ajma is also explained as "food".

अच्युता चिद्वो अज्मन्ना नानदति पर्वतासो वनस्पतिः। भूमिर्यामेषु रेजते (*acyutā cidvo ajmannā nānadati parvatāso vanaspatiḥ| bhūmiryāmeṣu rejate*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-20-5)
There it is explained, अज्मनि गमने सति (*ajmani gamane sati*)—"when there is going", that is, when there is movement—as a usage in the sense of going.

ये पातॆयंते अज्जभिर्गिरीणां स्नुभिरेषां । यज्ञं महिष्वणीनां सुम्मंतुविष्वणीनां प्राध्वरे (*ye pātèyaṃte ajjabhirgirīṇāṃ snubhireṣāṃ | yajñaṃ mahiṣvaṇīnāṃ summaṃtuviṣvaṇīnāṃ prādhvare*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-46-18)
There अज्जभिः बलैः बलकरैरुदकैः सह (*ajjabhiḥ balaiḥ balakarairudakaiḥ saha*) has been explained as "strength-giving water." In the present Ṛk, अज्मं अजनिं । अज्यतेऽस्यां गम्यत इति अजनिः वाहिकां स्वर्गपथ इत्यर्थः (*ajmaṃ ajaniṃ | ajyate 'syāṃ gamyata iti ajaniḥ vāhikāṃ svargapatha ityarthaḥ*); Durgācārya has explained the view of the author of the Nirukta that it is the road to heaven.

हंसाः (*haṃsāḥ*)—हंतेर्घन्त्यध्यानं (*haṃterghantyadhyānaṃ*): gathering in flocks they cover the roads. It is formed from the root हन् (*han*) *[as supplied: printed "ನಿಷ್ಪನ್ನವಾಗಿನೆ"]*.
श्रेणयः (*śreṇayaḥ*)—formed from the root श्री (*śrī*), which gives the sense "bring together"; the meaning is that which is a collective.

*Grammatical Derivation (transcribed, not explained):*
ईर्मांतासः (*īrmāṃtāsaḥ*)—ईर्माः ईरिताः अंताः येषां ते ईर्मांतासः (*īrmāḥ īritāḥ aṃtāḥ yeṣāṃ te īrmāṃtāsaḥ*); root ईर गतौ प्रेरणे च (*īra gatau preraṇe ca*); by the inclusion of bahula, the suffix mak prescribed by इषियुधींधि (*iṣiyudhīṃdhi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-142) comes here too; by the suffix accent ईर्म (*īrma*) has the final acute; in the bahuvrīhi compound the accent of the first member's base arises. From this word onward, in four words, when jas follows, by आज्जसेरसुक् (*ājjaserasuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50) it takes the augment asuk. In सिलिकमध्यमासः (*silikamadhyamāsaḥ*) also, being a bahuvrīhi compound, the accent of the first member's base arises.

### Printed p. 272
*Running head: 272 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೩] (as supplied).*

दिव्यासः (*divyāsaḥ*)—दिवि भवाः दिव्यासः (*divi bhavāḥ divyāsaḥ*); being included among daṇḍādi words, by दंडादिभ्यो यः (*daṃḍādibhyo yaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 5-1-66) the suffix ya; by the suffix accent it has the final acute; as before, when jas follows, it takes the augment asuk.
श्रेणिशः (*śreṇiśaḥ*)—by संख्यैकवचनाच्च वीप्सायाम् (*saṃkhyaikavacanācca vīpsāyām*) (Pā. Sū. 5-4-43) the suffix śas; by the suffix accent it has the final acute.
यतंते (*yataṃte*)—root यती प्रयत्ने (*yatī prayatne*); laṭ, third person plural form; the tiṅanta nighāta accent arises.
आक्षिपुः (*ākṣipuḥ*) *[as supplied: so printed; the Ṛk has आक्षिषुः (*ākṣiṣuḥ*)]*—root अक्षू व्याप्तौ (*akṣū vyāptau*), of the first (bhvādi) class; by छंदसिलुज्लिज्लिटः (*chaṃdasilujlijliṭaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-6) luṅ in the present sense; third person plural form; because of the connection with यत् (*yat*) the nighāta accent does not arise; the augment āṭ being udātta, it has the initial acute.
अज्मम् (*ajmam*)—root अज गतिक्षेपणयोः (*aja gatikṣepaṇayoḥ*); since bahula is included among the Uṇādi (suffixes), the suffix man; by the statement वलादावार्धधातुके वेष्यते (*valādāvārdhadhātuke veṣyate*) (Kā. 2-4-56-2), the vī-substitution that should come by अजेव्यघञपोः (*ajevyaghañapoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-56 *[as supplied: so printed]*) does not come; by इत्यादिर्नित्यम् (*ityādirnityam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-169), the suffix having the marker n, the initial acute arises. ॥ 10 ॥

#### Ṛk 11
**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**
> तव॒ शरी॑रं पतयि॒ष्णुव॑र्न॒न्तव॑ चि॒त्तं वात॑ इव॒ ध्रजी॑मान् ।
> तव॒ शृंगा॑णि॒ विष्ठि॑ता पुरु॒त्रार॑ण्येषु॒ जर्भु॑राणा चरंति ॥११॥
**॥ पदपाठः ॥**
> तव । शरीरं । पतयिष्णु । अर्वन् । तव । चित्तं । वातःइव । ध्रजीमान् ।
> तव । शृंगाणि । विस्थिता । पुरुत्रा । अरण्येषु । जर्भुराणा । चरंति ॥ ११ ॥

*(The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya and the rest of this Ṛk follow in the next window.)*
